# IDP-Cargo · Go + DDD + Bento-Go · V2.29 integration candidate

**IDP-Cargo is one product repository and one Go module. Business code is organized by DDD bounded context; IDP-Bento-Go and IDP-Platform-Go stay separate repositories referenced as Go module versions.**

- Product module: `go.idp.xyz/idp-cargo` (candidate). The `go.idp.xyz` vanity host currently serves only `idp-bento-go`, so this path does not resolve outside the repository yet.
- Framework module: `go.idp.xyz/idp-bento-go@v0.1.0-rc.2` (release candidate, not stable), resolved from the public repository `github.com/idp-xyz/idp-bento-go`.
- Go `1.26.5`; PostgreSQL 16.

## Layout

```
cmd/cargo-migrate/              operator-only migrations: Bento schema, framework migration, then each context's migrations in manifest order
cmd/cargo-worker/               Outbox relay: claim, publish to an HTTP sink, settle; at least once
cmd/cargo-api/                  the HTTP API (paths under /v1): bearer tokens, RFC 9457 problems; reaches Cargo through internal/bootstrap
cmd/cargo-demo/                 seeds the test tenant eii-test through the contexts' commands (tools/demo/eii-test.sh runs it with the API)
cmd/cargo-mcp/                  FIN's MCP server for the IDP TELOS gateway: three read-only tools, the caller known only from the gateway's signed assertion (tools/demo/fin-mcp.sh runs it)
internal/transport/http/        the server: routing, correlation id, panic recovery, access log, authentication of the bearer token
internal/transport/mcp/         the MCP server's transport: the verifier of the gateway's caller assertion, a middleware over the raw JSON-RPC body, in front of the official Go SDK's stateless Streamable HTTP handler
internal/foundation/identity/   the verified principal, the token Verifier port (HS256 for development and tests) and permission checks
internal/foundation/httpapi/    what context adapters share: problems, error kinds to status codes, strict JSON, Idempotency-Key, If-Match, ETag
internal/contexts/org/          ORG bounded context
  module.go                     assembles ORG; the only constructor other code uses
  api/                          ORG's contract: commands, PartyRef v1 and the party-role query, party identity and address book commands and queries, published events, error kinds, Authorizer port
  internal/                     private to ORG (Go internal rule)
    domain/                     BusinessParty (with typed identifiers and English name), LegalEntity, PartnerMergeCase, AddressBook (a party's addresses and contacts)
    application/                ApproveRole, the party identity and address book commands and queries, and their ports
    adapters/postgres/          tenant-scoped persistence, receipts, Outbox, Bento repository ports
    integration/                PG16 tests (build tag integration)
internal/contexts/rate/         RATE bounded context (Rate, Quote & Commercial Rules)
  module.go                     assembles RATE; ORG is passed in as its query port
  api/                          RATE's contract: quote commands, QuoteSnapshot v1, events, error kinds, Authorizer and MarginPolicy ports
  internal/domain/              Quote: margin floor, price approval, acceptance within validity
  internal/application/         CreateQuote, ApproveQuotePrice, AcceptQuote, GetQuoteSnapshot and their ports
  internal/adapters/postgres/   quotes, receipts and Outbox in cargo_rate
  internal/adapters/org/        customer check through ORG's api (anticorruption layer)
  internal/adapters/http/       inbound HTTP: the charge-code catalogue, rate cards, rating and snapshots, quotes and service levels
  internal/integration/         PG16 tests with a stand-in for ORG
internal/contexts/com/          COM bounded context (Commercial Commitment)
  module.go                     assembles COM; ORG and RATE are passed in as query ports
  api/                          COM's contract: order commands, ObligationReservation v1 for FUL, ObligationSnapshot v1 and v2, events, error kinds, Authorizer port
  internal/domain/              CustomerOrder: lines in packs, gross weight and volume; obligation versions; reservations; the frozen customer and quote snapshots
  internal/application/         record, allocate, revoke, amend, cancel, reserve and release use cases, GetObligationSnapshot, and their ports
  internal/adapters/postgres/   orders, receipts and Outbox in cargo_com
  internal/adapters/org/        customer snapshot from ORG's api (anticorruption layer)
  internal/adapters/rate/       accepted quote and terms hash from RATE's api (anticorruption layer)
  internal/integration/         PG16 tests with stand-ins for ORG and RATE, including races of allocations and of reservation against revocation
internal/contexts/ful/          FUL bounded context (Freight Fulfillment): the shipment plan and, since V0.4, its consol attachment and verified load evidence
  module.go                     assembles FUL; COM, and PHY's ConsolMembership v1 and PhysicalLoadEvidence v1, are passed in as query ports
  api/                          FUL's contract: shipment commands, ShipmentPlanSnapshot v1 and v2, LoadIntent v1, events, error kinds, Authorizer port
  internal/domain/              Shipment: planned obligation versions of one customer, immutable plan versions, confirmations, consol attachments
  internal/application/         create, add, amend, confirm, release, cancel and record-attachment use cases, the plan query, and their ports
  internal/adapters/postgres/   shipments, receipts and Outbox in cargo_ful
  internal/adapters/com/        obligation versions from COM's ObligationSnapshot v3 (anticorruption layer)
  internal/adapters/phy/        a shipment's membership and load evidence from PHY's ConsolMembership v1 and PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 (anticorruption layer)
  internal/integration/         PG16 tests with a stand-in for COM, including shipments racing for one obligation
internal/contexts/phy/          PHY bounded context (Physical Logistics); consolidations and their houses (V0.1), cargo lots and tenders (V0.2)
  module.go                     assembles PHY; FUL's LoadIntent v1 and the location reference data are passed in
  api/                          PHY's contract: consolidation, house, cargo lot and load execution commands, ConsolMembership v1, PhysicalLoadEvidence v1, events, error kinds, Authorizer port
  internal/domain/              Consolidation with immutable versions; Membership: a shipment's ledger of immutable entries, one live at most; CargoLot; LoadExecution
  internal/application/         consolidation, house, lot and tender use cases, their lock order, the queries, and their ports
  internal/adapters/postgres/   consolidations, membership ledgers, cargo lots, load executions, receipts and Outbox in cargo_phy
  internal/adapters/ful/        a shipment's LoadIntent v1 from FUL (anticorruption layer)
  internal/adapters/reference/  routes resolved against the location reference data
  internal/integration/         PG16 tests with a stand-in for FUL, including attaches racing a close and loads racing for one lot
internal/contexts/air/          AIR bounded context (Air Freight): the air movement of an air consolidation, MAWB, flights and house waybills (V0.1), with measured house facts (V0.2)
  module.go                     assembles AIR; BOOK's CarrierBooking v1 and FUL's AirOperationPlan v1 are passed in as query ports
  api/                          AIR's contract: movement, house waybill and HAWB series commands, queries, events, error kinds, Authorizer port
  internal/domain/              AirMovement: booking links with flights, MAWB, immutable house waybill entries; HawbSeries
  internal/application/         command and query use cases, the HAWB a house takes, and their ports
  internal/adapters/postgres/   movements, series, the HAWB register, receipts and Outbox in cargo_air
  internal/adapters/book/       the booking's MAWB, airline and flights from BOOK (anticorruption layer)
  internal/adapters/ful/        a house's plan and consolidation from FUL (anticorruption layer)
  internal/integration/         PG16 tests with stand-ins for BOOK and FUL, including concurrent draws from one series
internal/contexts/sea/          SEA bounded context (Sea Freight): the ocean movement of a sea LCL consolidation, voyage legs, master bill reference and house bills (V0.1); container visits, the verified gross mass and container events (V0.2)
  module.go                     assembles SEA; BOOK's CarrierBooking v1 and FUL's OceanOperationPlan v1 are passed in as query ports
  api/                          SEA's contract: movement, master bill, house bill, HBL series and container visit commands, queries, events, error kinds, Authorizer port
  internal/domain/              OceanMovement: booking links with voyage legs and cut-offs, master bill references, immutable house bill entries; HblSeries; ContainerVisit with its seals
  internal/application/         command and query use cases, the HBL a house takes, and their ports
  internal/adapters/postgres/   movements, container visits, series, the HBL register, receipts and Outbox in cargo_sea
  internal/adapters/book/       the booking's carrier or co-loader, port route, voyage legs and cut-offs from BOOK (anticorruption layer)
  internal/adapters/ful/        a house's plan, consolidation and sealed containers from FUL (anticorruption layer)
  internal/adapters/http/       inbound HTTP: ocean movements, house bills, the HBL series, published facts, carrier facts, container visits and the read of a VGM signatory
  internal/integration/         PG16 tests with stand-ins for BOOK and FUL, including concurrent draws from one series and movements racing for one master bill number
internal/contexts/book/         BOOK bounded context (Carrier Booking); in V0.1 bookings for consolidations, recorded as sent and answered
  module.go                     assembles BOOK; ORG's query side and the location reference data are passed in
  api/                          BOOK's contract: booking commands, CarrierBooking v1, events, error kinds, Authorizer port
  internal/domain/              CarrierBooking: request versions, steps sent and answered with evidence, capacity versions, allocations
  internal/application/         command and query use cases and their ports
  internal/adapters/postgres/   bookings, receipts and Outbox in cargo_book
  internal/adapters/org/        the carrier's role and airline codes from ORG (anticorruption layer)
  internal/adapters/reference/  legs resolved against the location reference data
  internal/integration/         PG16 tests with stand-ins for ORG, including bookings racing for one MAWB number
internal/bootstrap/             composition root: one connection pool, a Bento transaction owner per context and for the relay
internal/kernel/                value objects shared by every context (ValidityInterval, Money, Quantity, LocationCode); standard library only
internal/reference/             read-only reference data: api (the Locations contract contexts use) and locations (cargo_ref)
internal/foundation/bento/      context-agnostic IDP-Bento-Go adaptation (tenant-bound transactions, clock)
internal/foundation/messaging/  reliable messaging: Outbox relay, HTTP webhook publisher
migrations/org/                 immutable ORG migrations 000001–000010
migrations/rate/                RATE migrations, starting with 000001_rate_quotes
migrations/com/                 COM migrations 000001–000008
migrations/ful/                 FUL migrations 000001–000004
migrations/phy/                 PHY migrations, starting with 000001_phy_consolidation_membership
migrations/book/                BOOK migrations, starting with 000001_book_carrier_bookings
migrations/air/                 AIR migrations, starting with 000001_air_movement_waybills
migrations/sea/                 SEA migrations: 000001_sea_ocean_movement_house_bills, 000002_sea_container_visits
migrations/ref/                 reference data: location tables and the S1 location subset
migrations/manifest/            order in which the contexts' migrations run
tools/dependency-check/         package rules of the structure baseline; cross-context allowlist generated from the Context Map
.github/CODEOWNERS              an owner for every context directory and the kernel
```

- Other contexts, transports, cross-context processes and the AI capability layer import `internal/contexts/org/api` only; the compiler rejects imports of `internal/contexts/org/internal/...` from outside ORG, and `tools/dependency-check` rejects the rest of what the layering and the Context Map do not allow.
- Further contexts (CUS, EVT, …), `internal/processes/` and `internal/ai/` get directories when their first real code lands; no empty scaffolding.
- Design documents, review reports and evidence logs are kept in the IDP-Cargo master archive and are not published in this repository.

## Status of evidence

GitHub Actions, `ubuntu-24.04` runner, workflow `org-pg16-contract.yml`, on every push and pull request. `main` is protected: changes land through pull requests that pass `org-pg16-contract` and are up to date with `main`; administrators are included; force pushes and branch deletion are blocked. No approving review is required yet.

- Runs 42201396450 (`3973a51`, failed: torn read in `PartyStore.Load`, defect 4), 42202747912 and 42203416561 (32 tests), 42204649979, 42205465991, 42205477004 and 42205609990 (35 tests plus 13 Bento testkit contract scenarios).
- V2.8 moves ORG into `internal/contexts/org/{module.go, api, internal}` and the Bento seam into `internal/foundation/bento`, moves migrations to `migrations/org`, drops the obsolete `bento` build tag, and adds the ORG module with api error classification. Migration files and their checksums are unchanged.
- V2.9 adds `cmd/cargo-worker` and the Outbox `Relay` (in `internal/foundation/messaging`, as the repository structure baseline V1.1 places it). PG16 tests cover a failed publish retried with backoff, abandonment after the failure budget without blocking the partition, and a process-level crash drill: the worker is killed with SIGKILL while the sink holds a delivery, a second worker re-delivers it after the lease expires, and SIGTERM stops it cleanly. Each publish is bounded by the sink timeout and a batch must fit inside its lease, so a slow sink cannot push queued deliveries past the lease and have them re-claimed while still waiting.
- V2.10 starts S1. `internal/kernel` holds `ValidityInterval`. ORG's api gains PartyRef v1 and `ResolvePartyRole`, which confirms that an active party holds an approved role for a verified legal entity at an instant. `internal/bootstrap` gives every context and the relay its own Bento transaction owner; a PG16 test shows that ORG refuses commands and queries issued inside another context's transaction (AC4). `go run ./tools/dependency-check` runs as a step of the required check: it enforces rules D1–D6 (AC2, AC3) and CODEOWNERS coverage (AC7), and warns on D7.
- V2.11 adds RATE, step 2 of the S1 slice. A quote is priced in one currency. If its margin is below the floor that `MarginPolicy` supplies, someone other than its author must approve the price, with a reason. It is accepted within its validity, and ORG confirms the CUSTOMER role both when the quote is created and when it is accepted. `GetQuoteSnapshot` returns QuoteSnapshot v1 for accepted quotes, without costs. `cargo_rate` keeps accepted quotes and all quote lines immutable through triggers and FORCE RLS. `cargo-migrate` applies contexts in `migrations/manifest` order, and the allowlist gains `rate org` from Context Map V1.3. A PG16 test in `internal/bootstrap` runs ORG and RATE together.
- V2.12 adds COM, step 3 of the S1 slice.
  - A customer order is recorded against a quote that RATE has accepted, for the quote's customer, and ORG must confirm the CUSTOMER role at that instant. The order freezes the customer's transaction snapshot and the accepted quote, including the SHA-256 of the exact terms.
  - Allocation commits part or all of one order line to a fulfillment obligation at version 1. A line's obligations never exceed its quantity. `GetObligationSnapshot` returns ObligationSnapshot v1 for FUL.
  - `cargo_com` freezes the commitment through triggers, grants and FORCE RLS. It also rejects any obligation that does not fit its line. One accepted quote backs at most one order.
  - The kernel gains `Quantity`. A property test checks allocations; a PG16 test races eight allocations against one revision. A test in `internal/bootstrap` runs ORG, RATE and COM together.
- V2.15 implements COM V0.3, the part of the S1 business review that FUL needs first (decisions C3 and C6).
  - Order lines and obligations are measured in packs of a UN/ECE Recommendation 21 package type, gross weight and an optional volume. A line's active obligations never exceed its packs, gross weight or volume; a line is fully allocated when all its packs are.
  - `RevokeObligation` writes a new obligation version with status REVOKED. `AmendObligation` revokes one obligation and allocates its replacement in one command. `CancelCustomerOrder` revokes what is still active and cancels the order for good.
  - FUL reserves an obligation version for a shipment, and releases it, through ObligationReservation v1 (`ReserveObligationForFulfillment`, `ReleaseObligationReservation`). While a shipment holds an obligation, COM refuses to revoke it or to cancel its order. COM never queries FUL.
  - `GetObligationSnapshotV2` adds packs, package type, gross weight, volume, the version's status, the latest version and the holding shipment. ObligationSnapshot v1 stays published, with the packs as a Recommendation 20 package unit (`XPX` for pallets).
  - Migration `000002_com_obligation_lifecycle` replaces the line and obligation quantities, adds cancellation and `obligation_reservation`, and replaces the triggers. It refuses to run on a `cargo_com` that already holds V0.1 orders. The triggers lock the order row, so a reservation and a revocation of one obligation never both commit; PG16 tests race them through the api and in raw SQL. A model-based property test runs random sequences of all six commands.
- V2.15 also adds FUL, step 4 of the S1 slice: the shipment plan (FUL tactical candidate V0.1).
  - A shipment plans exact COM obligation versions of one customer party and legal entity. COM must report each version ACTIVE, the obligation's latest, and reserved for the shipment; FUL asks before its own transaction. The shipment has its own identity, never the order's.
  - Every change writes an immutable plan version: transport mode, origin and destination UN/LOCODEs, departure window, and the cargo scope summed from the planned obligations. Confirmation needs a complete route and re-checks every obligation with COM; a confirmed plan that changes must be confirmed again.
  - Releasing an obligation or cancelling the shipment comes first; the caller then releases COM's reservation. FUL never writes to COM and never asserts loading or delivery.
  - `GetShipmentPlanSnapshot` returns ShipmentPlanSnapshot v1 for BOOK in step 5. The kernel gains `LocationCode`, which keeps UN/LOCODE and IATA airport codes apart.
  - `cargo_ful` keeps plan versions and confirmations immutable, lets a planned obligation be released only once, and plans an obligation in at most one live shipment through a unique index. PG16 tests race eight shipments for one obligation and eight amendments against one revision; a test in `internal/bootstrap` runs ORG, RATE, COM and FUL through the C6 handshake.
- V2.16 implements owner decision F1 (FUL tactical candidate V0.2): a shipment plans a part of an obligation version, and the parts of one obligation may be planned in several shipments. A carrier's shortload or a rollover is replanned in FUL, without a COM amendment.
  - A part has packs and gross weight, and volume exactly when the obligation declares one, in its units. The live parts of an obligation never exceed it; the part with its last packs takes all the weight and volume left, and a part that leaves packs leaves weight and volume for them.
  - `CreateShipmentFromObligation` and `AddObligationToShipment` take an optional part; without one they plan all that other shipments leave. `ChangeShipmentPart` replans a part with a reason, as a `PART_CHANGED` plan version and `ShipmentPartChanged.v1`; a confirmed plan that changes must be confirmed again. `GetShipmentPlanSnapshotV2` lists the parts with what COM committed.
  - FUL takes the obligation's transaction lock before it reads what other shipments plan of it, in the command's transaction, and the `shipment_obligation` trigger takes the same lock and re-checks the sum, so concurrent commands cannot exceed an obligation. PG16 tests race eight shipments for parts of one obligation.
  - COM V0.4: several shipments may each hold a reservation on one obligation version, one per shipment; COM still refuses to revoke an obligation, or to cancel its order, while any shipment holds it. `GetObligationSnapshotV3` lists every holder; in v2 `HeldByShipmentID` names the holder only when there is exactly one. FUL reads v3.
  - Migrations `com/000003_com_reservation_per_shipment` and `ful/000002_ful_shipment_parts`. The FUL migration converts V0.1 entries, which planned whole obligations, into parts equal to the whole. A test in `internal/bootstrap` replans a shortload of 8 of 30 pallets across two shipments with the real contexts.
- V2.17 implements RATE V0.3: the business review decisions R3, R4, R5 and R7, the owner decisions R8, R9 and T1 of 2026-11-09, and the quote side of C8.
  - R8: each line's sell and cost carry their currency. A quote totals in its own currency at frozen exchange rates, one per other currency, with source, date and the ISO 4217 minor digits used; lines convert one by one, halves away from zero. The margin floor is checked in the quote currency. The rates price the quote; invoicing rates belong to FIN.
  - R9: a zero line is a charge included elsewhere; a discount is a negative line without cost, and makes the quote need a price approval. The total never falls below zero.
  - R3: the quote's author may approve its price only when the `Authorizer` allows `ApproveOwnQuotePrice`, and the approval is flagged as a self-approval. R4: `ExpireQuote` ends a quote not accepted once its validity has passed (the scheduled job follows in S2); expired quotes are frozen. R5: acceptance evidence has a kind: signed document, e-mail or customer portal. R7: quotes go to customers, overseas agents, shippers or consignees, whom ORG confirms in that role.
  - T1: `kernel.RecordWindow` admits a business time up to seven days before the time RATE records the command (configurable with `WithRecordWindow`) and five minutes after it; the acceptance's recording time is kept. A command committed earlier still replays later.
  - C8: optional Incoterms rule, edition (2010 or 2020), named place and service level on the quote.
  - `GetQuoteSnapshotV2` returns all of it, without costs or margins; v1 keeps each line's own currency and totals in the quote currency.
  - Migration `rate/000002_rate_quote_v03` converts V0.2 quotes without loss: lines take the quote currency, recipients are customers, and accepted quotes get the evidence kind UNRECORDED and no recording time. New table `cargo_rate.quote_exchange_rate`, immutable.
- V2.21 implements COM V0.5 and RATE V0.4: the owner decisions C10, C1, C2 and the amendment of C8 of 2026-11-09, T1 in COM, and the ordering-party side of R7.
  - C10: a customer's correction of what it declared is `AmendOrderLine`, the line's next version with a reason, without a new quote; `AddOrderLine` adds a line. A line never falls below what its active obligations commit, and keeps its package type and units while it has any. A difference measured at receipt is not a correction; FUL records it.
  - C1, C2: an order policy per legal entity, with a tenant default, bounds how many orders one accepted quote backs (1 to 999, built in 1; cancelled orders do not count) and the grace after the quote's validity (0 to 30 days, built in 0). `SetOrderPolicy` records versions that take effect when recorded; each order freezes the policy it followed. COM locks the quote before it counts, and the database checks both again.
  - C8: an order freezes the commercial terms of the quote and may complete what the quote left open, never change it. A service level is a code from RATE's service-level list (`GetServiceLevel`, `ListServiceLevels`; S1 lists STANDARD, EXPRESS, ECONOMY and DEFERRED); free text recorded before the list is kept and marked unlisted.
  - R7: the ordering party is the quote's recipient, confirmed by ORG in the role the quote went to. COM reads `QuoteSnapshotV2` and hashes its canonical form.
  - T1: every COM command's business time lies within `kernel.RecordWindow` before the time COM records it (configurable with `WithRecordWindow`); orders and line changes keep that time.
  - Migrations `rate/000003_rate_service_level_list` and `com/000004_com_order_correction_policy_terms` convert existing data without loss: orders become orders of a v1 snapshot by a customer under the built-in policy, without terms or recording time. New tables `cargo_com.order_line_amendment` and `cargo_com.order_policy`, immutable.
- V2.22 makes the database check an order's status (COM V0.5.1). The status is derived from the order's lines and obligations: CANCELLED once cancelled, RECORDED while no obligation is active, ALLOCATED when every line's active obligations take all its packs, otherwise PARTIALLY_ALLOCATED. COM writes it with every change; until now a write past COM, such as a revocation written in SQL, could leave a status COM then refused to restore. Deferred constraint triggers now check every order a transaction touches when it commits. Migration `com/000005_com_order_status_derived` derives stale statuses again; status is derived data, so nothing is lost.
- V2.23 implements ORG V0.3: ORG keeps each party's addresses and contacts (decision C9 as amended on 2026-11-09, privacy assessment V1.1), so COM V0.6 can carry them onto orders by role.
  - An address has a short code unique among the party's active addresses, purposes (OFFICE for documents and the office, PICKUP, DELIVERY, BILLING), the name and up to four lines as printed on documents, city, state or province, postcode and country, optionally a UN/LOCODE in that country, a residential flag, and optionally the same address in a local language such as Chinese. A contact has purposes (BOOKING, DOCUMENTS, ACCOUNTS), a name and at least one of phone, mobile and e-mail, and nothing else about the person.
  - Each purpose has at most one default address and one default contact. An address or contact that is deactivated, erased or loses a purpose stops being its default.
  - The address book is an aggregate of its own with its own revision; the party's identity revision, which PartyRef carries, does not change. Every command is one revision with a change record (who, why, when); every change of an address or contact is a new immutable version, and defaults are kept per revision. Changes take effect when ORG records them.
  - Commands `AddPartyAddress`, `ChangePartyAddress`, `DeactivatePartyAddress`, `SetDefaultPartyAddress`, `AddPartyContact`, `ChangePartyContact`, `DeactivatePartyContact`, `ErasePartyContact`, `SetDefaultPartyContact`; queries `GetPartyAddresses`, `GetPartyAddress` (any version) and `GetPartyContacts`. Reading contacts needs its own authorization (`ReadPartyContacts`). Events carry identifiers only. An archived party's address book is closed.
  - Erasing a contact clears its name, phone, mobile and e-mail in every version and keeps purposes, statuses and the change records.
  - Migration `org/000007_org_party_address_book` replaces the encrypted `party_address` and `party_contact` candidates of migration 000001, which no code ever wrote, and refuses to run if they hold rows. Versions, defaults and change records cannot be rewritten; a contact version can only be erased. Deferred constraint triggers check every address book a transaction touches when it commits. The application role gets only the grants it needs on the new tables.
  - PG16 tests race eight commands on one address book revision (one commits) and show that the database refuses eight kinds of writes past ORG; a model-based property test runs random sequences of all nine commands.
- V2.24 implements ORG V0.4, the owner's decision O1 of 2026-11-10 after benchmarking party data against CargoWise and Chinese rules: customs declarations, manifests and invoices name parties by official codes, which ORG did not keep.
  - A party has typed identifiers (MD-06): `CN_USCC`, the 18-character unified social credit code checked against the GB 32100 check character; `CN_CUSTOMS`, the 10-character customs code; `AEO` (issuing country and code, as declarations print it); `EU_EORI`; `US_EIN`; and `REGISTRATION` for other national registration codes. Each entry has its issuing country and evidence; one active entry per type and country; an entry may record that the party has none (declarations then print NO). A code belongs to one party of a tenant; recording it for a second party is a conflict that names the holder, to be merged. Retiring keeps the entry as history; a correction retires and records again.
  - A party has an optional English name, printable ASCII, as international documents print it; the legal name stays the registered one.
  - Identity is part of the party, so `RecordPartyIdentifier`, `RetirePartyIdentifier` and `SetPartyEnglishName` advance the revision PartyRef carries; `GetPartyIdentity` returns names, status, identifiers and revision. `ResolvePartyRole` now returns the English name and identifiers in the same view as the revision, for COM V0.6 to freeze. Events name the identifier's type and country, never the code.
  - An address may carry the organization's phone and e-mail at that address, such as a switchboard (manifests require the shipper's phone); a residential address carries neither.
  - Migration `org/000008_org_party_identifiers` replaces the encrypted `party_identifier` candidate of migration 000001, which no code ever wrote, and refuses to run if it holds rows. The database checks each code's form, including the GB 32100 check character (`cargo_org.uscc_valid`), one active entry per type and country, one active holder per code, and lets an identifier only be retired, once. The application role gets INSERT and the retirement columns only.
  - PG16 tests race eight parties recording one code (one commits), and show the database refusing a wrong check character, a second active entry, rewrites and deletions of identifiers, an undone retirement and a non-ASCII English name.
- V2.25 implements COM V0.6: the order side of C9, with the decisions C11–C14 of 2026-11-10 (CargoWise practice, delegated by the owner).
  - An order may carry the requested transport mode (AIR, SEA, ROAD, RAIL) and movement type (door-to-door, door-to-port, port-to-door, port-to-port), as CargoWise shipments carry a transport mode and pickup and delivery modes (C11). `ChangeOrderTransport` records the next version with a reason.
  - Per role, the order freezes its shipper, consignee and notify party: the ORG party at its revision with legal and English names, registration country and identifiers (ORG V0.4), and a documentary address. The shipper and the consignee must hold that role in ORG for the quote's legal entity (C9); the notify party need only be active (C13). The consignee of a SEA order may be TO ORDER or TO ORDER OF a holder, as text, with a notify party; an AIR order needs a named consignee (C11).
  - Addresses come from ORG: the party's default OFFICE address, or another active address it names. A movement that starts at a door carries the shipper's PICKUP address, one that ends at a door the consignee's DELIVERY address (C14). Without a default the order must name or give one. An address given on the order records who gave it, why, and the ORG address it replaced; nothing is written back to ORG.
  - A contact is optional: by default the party's DOCUMENTS contact, read from ORG with the caller's own authorization. Contacts are stored apart from the order, never in events or receipts, and read through `GetOrderParties` only by callers authorized for `ReadOrderContacts`.
  - `RecordCustomerOrder` takes the transport and parties; `SetOrderParty` sets or replaces one role as a new version, with a reason when it replaces, until the order is cancelled (C12). Each version publishes `OrderPartySet.v1` with the role, version, kind and whether it carries anything given on the order: the trigger later screening needs (X1). ORG changes never alter a frozen order.
  - Migration `com/000006_com_order_parties` adds `order_transport`, `order_party` and `order_party_contact`. Versions are immutable; a frozen contact can only be erased. Deferred constraint triggers check at commit that versions are numbered from 1, contact rows match, and the current parties keep the rules of C9, C11 and C14. Orders recorded before V0.6 have neither transport nor parties.
  - A PG16 test runs ORG, RATE and COM together through defaults, door pickup, a delivery override, a TO ORDER consignee and a refused change to AIR, contact authorization, events without personal data, and writes past COM refused by privileges, triggers and the commit check.
- V2.26 implements FUL V0.3: decision F2 (option 4), T1 in FUL, and the check of C11 against COM's requested transport mode.
  - Location reference data lives where the repository structure baseline puts it: `internal/reference` (contract `internal/reference/api`, implementation `internal/reference/locations`) and schema `cargo_ref`, read-only for the application. Editions never change and one is current. Migration `ref/000002` loads the S1 subset: 43 locations copied from the UNECE UN/LOCODE release of 2026-11-01 (via github.com/datasets/un-locode `0fb7229b6447`, file checksum recorded), 22 IATA airport codes and 4 carrier aliases.
  - `ResolveLocation` takes a UN/LOCODE, or for AIR an IATA airport code. AIR needs the airport function and exactly one IATA airport code, so cities of several airports (USNYC, JPTYO, USCHI) and IATA city codes (NYC) are refused; SEA needs the port function; ROAD and RAIL take any known location. A code that does not serve the mode resolves through the mode's alias: CNSHA, which the current release gives to Hongqiao airport, is the port of Shanghai (CNSHG) for SEA. IATA codes are never derived from a UN/LOCODE's last three letters.
  - `AmendShipmentPlan` resolves the route before FUL's transaction; plan versions freeze the UN/LOCODEs, for AIR the IATA airport codes, and the edition, and a plan is confirmed only once resolved. `ShipmentPlanSnapshotV2` publishes the IATA codes, the edition and the recording time.
  - C11: COM's `ObligationSnapshotV3` carries the order's requested mode; FUL refuses an obligation into a shipment of another mode, and an amendment or confirmation whose mode the orders do not request.
  - T1: every FUL command's business time lies within `kernel.RecordWindow` before the time FUL records it (`WithRecordWindow`); plan versions and confirmations keep both times.
  - Migration `ful/000003_ful_location_resolution` adds the frozen codes, the edition and the recording times; versions written before V0.3 stay as they are, those written since must be resolved.
  - Tests: the S1 subset resolved in PG16 (19 cases), reference data read-only for the application, FUL's rules in the domain, and a PG16 slice from an AIR order to a resolved air plan, refused SEA and city codes, a change of the order to SEA and the replan by sea through the carriers' alias.
- V2.27 implements COM V0.7: decision P1, the retention and erasure of the contacts frozen on orders, with the owner's rules of 2026-11-10.
  - `CloseCustomerOrder` records that the work on an order is finished. Only an order whose lines are all allocated closes. A closed order no longer changes: lines, obligations, parties and transport stay, it cannot be cancelled, and shipments may no longer reserve its obligations, though those that hold them may release them. It publishes `CustomerOrderClosed.v1`.
  - Once an order is closed or cancelled, its contacts are restricted: `GetOrderParties` returns them only to callers authorized for `ReadRestrictedOrderContacts`, and `OrderParties.Contacts` says until when they are kept. They are kept for the months `com.WithContactRetention` sets (36 to 120; default 36, three years after the business time of the closure or cancellation, to be confirmed by legal counsel), counted in UTC calendar months as PostgreSQL adds them; each order keeps the end it was given.
  - `EraseOrderContacts` erases the contacts of every party version of an order at once, once COM's clock has passed the end; the order, its revision and ORG's own contacts stay. It publishes `CustomerOrderContactsErased.v1`, which names the order only. `ListOrdersDueForContactErasure` lists the orders due, earliest first, for a job allowed to erase them.
  - Migration `com/000007_com_order_closure_contact_retention` adds the CLOSED status and `order_contact_retention` and `order_contact_erasure`, both immutable. The database closes only ALLOCATED orders, keeps a closed one closed, refuses lines, obligations and reservations for closed orders and party and transport versions for closed or cancelled ones, refuses an erasure before the retention's end, and clears a contact only together with the record of the order's erasure. Deferred constraint triggers check at commit that an order has a retention exactly when it is closed or cancelled, starting then, and that an erasure leaves no contact. Orders cancelled before V0.7 keep their contacts 36 months from their cancellation.
  - Tests: closure, retention and erasure in the domain and application; a PG16 slice with ORG, RATE and COM through closure, restricted reads, the due list, a refused early erasure, the erasure three years on by a movable clock, ORG's contact kept, events without contact data, and ten writes past COM refused.
- V2.29 implements ORG V0.5 and COM V0.8: the access log of contact reads and the notice and consent of contacts, the first controls of privacy assessment V1.2 (decisions P4, P6 and P7 of 2026-11-10). The tenant is the personal information handler and the software company its entrusted party (P3), so these are tools for the tenant.
  - Every read of contacts states its purpose (`kernel.AccessPurpose`: DOCUMENTS, BOOKING, OPERATIONS, ACCOUNTS, ORDER_PARTY, CUSTOMS_AUDIT, LEGAL_CLAIM, DATA_SUBJECT_REQUEST) and is logged in the reading transaction, so a read that cannot be logged returns nothing. ORG logs `GetPartyContacts` with the contacts returned, COM `GetOrderParties` with contacts, whether they were restricted and how many it returned; COM carrying a contact onto an order reads ORG for the purpose ORDER_PARTY under the caller's identity. Restricted contacts, those of a closed or cancelled order, are read only for a customs audit, a legal claim or the person's own request (PIPL article 47). `ListContactAccess` in ORG and COM shows the log to callers authorized for `ReadContactAccessLog`, such as the tenant's data protection officer. The log holds no contact details, is timed by the database, never rewritten and kept three years.
  - `RecordContactConsent` records, as a revision of the address book, the version of the tenant's personal information rules a contact was told of, the separate consents to the contact being provided to others (article 23) and abroad (article 39), how they were obtained (the customer's undertaking in its contract, or the person's confirmation) and the evidence. Recording a narrower consent withdraws; an erased contact keeps its last consent, which holds no personal data. `GetPartyContacts` returns the consent; COM freezes it with a contact carried onto an order, or takes one with a contact given on the order. DOC and BOOK will check it before they send a contact out.
  - Migrations `org/000009_org_contact_consent_access` (`party_contact_consent`, `party_contact_access`) and `com/000008_com_contact_consent_access` (consent columns on `order_party_contact`, `order_contact_access`). The database checks at commit that every consent has its change record and follows no erasure, logs a COM read as restricted exactly when the order's contacts are retained, stamps every read with its own clock, refuses purposes not listed, and refuses to rewrite the logs and consents; a logged read is deleted only once three years old. Erasing an order's contacts leaves the frozen consent as it was.
  - Tests: consent in the ORG domain; purposes, restriction and fail-closed logging in both applications; consent on order contacts; a PG16 slice with ORG, RATE and COM through a consent, a contact carried with it, a contact given on the order with its own, reads for each purpose, a closed order's restricted read, both logs for the data protection officer, ten writes past the contexts refused, a backdated read stamped by the database, and events without contact data or evidence.
- V2.31 implements PHY V0.1 and the first part of FUL V0.4: consolidations and the attach and detach of houses, step 1 of the N1 forwarder daily loop (China export air consolidation and ocean LCL), with the decisions N1-PHY and N1-FUL of 2026-11-10. As in CargoWise, a consolidation (consol) is shared by houses of several customers under one master; its identity is its own, never a MAWB, MBL or booking number.
  - PHY: `CreateConsolidation` for AIR_CONSOL, SEA_LCL_OWN or SEA_LCL_COLOAD (OD-6), run by an operating legal entity, on a route resolved against the location reference data (UN/LOCODEs, for AIR the IATA airport codes, and the edition), with a planned departure and a house cut-off; `AmendConsolidation` moves the schedule at any time before reconciliation and the route only while no house is attached; `CloseConsolidation` is the house cut-off and freezes the membership until `ReopenConsolidation` (OD-5); `CancelConsolidation` without houses. Every change writes an immutable version, so the original plan stays visible after a rollover.
  - A house is a FUL shipment, attached whole. `AttachHouse` reads FUL's LoadIntent v1 first: the shipment must be PLANNED, of the consolidation's mode, origin and destination (OD-8) and legal entity (OD-10), and its departure window must contain the planned departure. `DetachHouse` and `TransferHouse` (a rollover or a flight change, in one step) need a reason; `RefreshHouseIntent` takes a replanned shipment (a shortload, F1) into a new entry; `EndMembershipForCancelledShipment` ends a cancelled shipment's membership, even after the cut-off. Each shipment has a ledger of immutable entries with the FUL revision and plan version PHY verified and the parts it froze; at most one entry is live, so a shipment is never in two consolidations at once.
  - PHY holds the membership and commits first; FUL records it after asking PHY (OD-1). FUL's `RecordConsolAttachment` and `RecordConsolDetachment` read PHY's ConsolMembership v1 and record the consolidation and entry on the shipment's main carriage; a transfer or refresh ends the previous attachment, and `CancelShipment` ends the shipment's own. `CloseConsolidation` re-confirms every house with FUL: PLANNED at the plan version PHY verified and attached to this consolidation in FUL. The S1 API client runs the steps (T6); `bootstrap.FULAndPHY` assembles the two contexts, which read each other's query sides (relationships 7 and 8 of Context Map V1.4; the allowlist is regenerated from it).
  - Locks: a consolidation command locks the consolidation FOR UPDATE, a house command the consolidations it touches FOR SHARE in id order and then the house's ledger FOR UPDATE, so houses of one consolidation attach concurrently and attach and close run one after another. A close that finds the houses changed while it asked FUL is a conflict and is retried.
  - Migrations `ful/000004_ful_consol_attachment` and `phy/000001_phy_consolidation_membership`. The database keeps versions, entries and parts immutable, ends an entry only once, freezes a closed consolidation's houses except a cancelled shipment's, checks at commit that a house joins only an OPEN consolidation and a cancelled consolidation keeps none, and that a cancelled shipment keeps no current attachment.
  - Tests: the consolidation and membership rules in the domain, a property test of random attaches, detaches, transfers, refreshes, closes and reopens; PG16 tests with a stand-in for FUL, writes past PHY refused, attaches racing a close (a closed consolidation holds only houses its close confirmed with FUL), and eight consolidations racing for one shipment (one wins); a PG16 slice with the real contexts in which two customers' AIR houses share a consolidation, one is shut out to the next flight, the other is shortloaded and refreshed, a cancelled shipment leaves, and the carriers' CNSHA resolves to the port of Shanghai.
  - Not yet: load units, cargo receipt, stuffing and loading (PHY), MAWB/HAWB and MBL/HBL facts (AIR, SEA), carrier booking (BOOK) and the manifest-change cause once a house is declared (CUS).
- V2.32 implements BOOK V0.1 and ORG V0.6: the carrier booking of a consolidation, step 2 of the N1 forwarder daily loop, with the decisions N1-BOOK of 2026-11-10. Following N1-PHY OD-7, an air consolidation's MAWB number comes from the airline's booking confirmation recorded here; AIR takes it from CarrierBooking v1 next.
  - The operator sends the request through the carrier's own channel and BOOK records it (OD-1): `DraftCarrierBooking` for AIR_CONSOL, SEA_LCL_CONSOL_EQUIPMENT or SEA_LCL_CO_LOAD, `AmendBookingRequest`, `RecordBookingRequestSent`, and the carrier's answers `RecordCarrierAcknowledgement`, `RecordCarrierConfirmation`, `RecordCarrierRejection`, `RecordCarrierChange`, `RecordCarrierDecline`; the forwarder's `RequestBookingChange` (answered by `RecordChangeConfirmed` or `RecordChangeRefused`), `RequestBookingCancellation` (`RecordCancellationConfirmed`), `WithdrawDraftBooking` and `ReassignBookingCapacity`. A request is never a confirmation, and a confirmation never loading or departure.
  - Every sent request and every answer keeps its evidence: the SHA-256 and storage id of the e-mail, PDF or screenshot (OD-2); the same operator may record both, and both are recorded (OD-6). In V0.1 requests carry no personal contacts (OD-5, option C as the default content).
  - Each confirmation, carrier change (schedule change, rollover, reduction, split) or confirmed change writes an immutable capacity version, at most what was requested (PARTIAL_CONFIRMATION when less), with legs from the request's first port to its last, flights of the airline or vessels and voyages (IMO check digit), estimated departure and arrival, and cut-offs as instants with their IANA time zone. The current version is allocated to the booking's one consolidation (OD-3); a change moves the allocation, a decline or cancellation releases it.
  - The carrier must hold the role CARRIER in ORG for the legal entity at the business time, CO_LOADER for a co-load (OD-4); the party revision and role decision are frozen. ORG V0.6 records carrier codes as party identifiers issued internationally (country ZZ): the airline's IATA designator, its AWB prefix, and the SCAC; an airline needs the first two. An air consolidation's confirmation states exactly one MAWB number of the airline's prefix with a valid modulus-7 check digit; a carrier reference is unique among the tenant's live bookings of that carrier and type.
  - Queries: `GetCarrierBooking` (CarrierBooking v1), `ListCapacityForConsumer`, `FindBookingByCarrierReference`, `ListBookingsAwaitingCarrier`, `ListCutOffsDue`.
  - Migrations `org/000010_org_carrier_codes` and `book/000001_book_carrier_bookings`. The database keeps request versions, steps, capacity and allocation versions immutable, lets a reference only stop being live, never changes an ended booking, and checks at commit that the pointers name the latest versions, that exactly one allocation is active on the current capacity while the booking is in force, and that an air consolidation's confirmed booking has its MAWB number.
  - Tests: the booking rules and a property test of random command sequences (the status follows the steps, at most one allocation active, never two MAWB numbers); PG16 tests with stand-ins for ORG, writes past BOOK refused, eight bookings racing for one MAWB number (one wins) and eight reassignments of one booking (one wins); a PG16 slice with ORG, PHY and BOOK in which an airline gets its codes and role, an air consolidation is booked and confirmed with its MAWB, found again by the MAWB, and a co-load needs a CO_LOADER.
  - Not yet: AIR (MAWB/HAWB, linking the booking's flights), electronic booking channels, booking contacts and their provision records (P5), direct bookings for a shipment, and FUL's capacity links.
- V2.33 implements AIR V0.1 and FUL's AirOperationPlan v1: the master and house air waybills of an air consolidation, step 3 of the N1 forwarder daily loop (PHY tactical candidate V0.1, backlog PB5 without the document facts), with the decisions N1-PHY OD-2, OD-7, OD-8, OD-10 and N1-HAWB of 2026-11-10.
  - `OpenAirMovement` opens the air movement of a consolidation from its booking: BOOK must report the booking AIR_CONSOL, in force with confirmed capacity, allocated to the consolidation and holding its MAWB number. The movement takes the airline with its IATA designator and AWB prefix, the operating legal entity, the airport route, the MAWB and the flights from CarrierBooking v1 (OD-7). One open movement per consolidation and one per MAWB. `LinkCarrierBooking` takes a newer capacity version of the same booking (a retimed flight under the same MAWB): the flights are replaced and kept as superseded, the houses stay. Another airline or MAWB is another movement.
  - `AssociateHouseWaybill` associates a house: FUL's AirOperationPlan v1 must report the shipment PLANNED by AIR, attached to the consolidation (membership reaches AIR through FUL, OD-2), on the movement's route (OD-8) and of its legal entity (OD-10). The house takes the HAWB number it holds under the legal entity, the one given, or the next of the entity's series (`RegisterHawbSeries`), drawn under the series' lock; a number belongs to one shipment for good and moves with it to another movement (N1-HAWB). Its planned pieces, gross weight and volume and its documented chargeable weight (6,000 cm3 per kg, rounded up to the half kilogram) are frozen; master totals and the master chargeable weight are computed on the live houses.
  - `RefreshHouseWaybillFacts` takes a newer FUL plan version into a new entry with the same HAWB; `EndHouseWaybillAssociation` ends an entry once FUL reports the shipment detached, transferred or cancelled; `CancelAirMovement` cancels a movement without houses and voids its MAWB. A house whose departure window no longer contains the first flight's ETD is flagged.
  - Queries: `GetAirMovement`, `FindAirMovementByMAWB`, `FindHouseWaybill`, `GetHawbSeries`. FUL adds `GetAirOperationPlan` (AirOperationPlan v1 with the PHY membership revision of the attachment).
  - Migration `air/000001_air_movement_waybills`. The database keeps links, flights, parts and the HAWB register immutable, lets a series only move forward and an entry end only once, never changes a cancelled movement, and checks at commit that a live entry belongs to an open movement and carries its shipment's registered HAWB, and that a cancelled movement keeps no house.
  - Tests: the chargeable weight, movement and house rules in the domain and a property test of random associations, refreshes and ends; PG16 tests with stand-ins for BOOK and FUL, writes past AIR refused, eight houses drawing from one series at once (eight numbers in order, none lost) and eight movements racing for one consolidation (one wins); a PG16 slice with every context from two customers' orders to their houses under the airline's MAWB, a retimed flight, and a house rolled to another consolidation's MAWB with its HAWB.
  - Not yet: AirDocFacts v1 for DOC (FINAL needs PHY's measures and screening, backlog PB3), carrier status facts, split bookings across flights, and reference data for chargeable-weight rules (PB8).
- V2.34 implements PHY V0.2: receipt, measurement, screening and the tender of an air consolidation's houses, step 4 of the N1 forwarder daily loop (PHY tactical candidate V0.1, backlog PB3 for tenders and the PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 query of PB4), with the decisions N1-PHY OD-3, OD-4 (option C: carrier facts entered by hand) and OD-5 of 2026-11-10. As decided for this step, the AIR line runs end to end first; container stuffing, ULD build-up and their load units come with SEA.
  - A cargo lot is a house's cargo at a facility (the forwarder's warehouse, a CFS, the airline terminal: UN/LOCODE and facility code), one in force per shipment and facility. `ReceiveCargo` opens it for a shipment FUL reports PLANNED, or adds a receipt line (packs, package type, condition GOOD, DAMAGED, WET or OPENED); `RecordCargoMeasurement` adds a measurement version (gross weight, volume, package groups, method SCALE, DIMENSIONER, CFS_TALLY or TERMINAL_WEIGHT); `RecordScreeningResult` records the screening party's outcome for AIR cargo (PASSED, FAILED, PENDING, NOT_REQUIRED). Every fact carries its evidence: source, reference, time and the SHA-256 of the raw payload where there is one.
  - `CompleteReceipt` compares the lot with FUL's current LoadIntent v1: short, over and damaged packs and a gross weight beyond 5 % or a volume beyond 10 % are discrepancies, never a change of FUL's plan (C4). `ResolveCargoDiscrepancy` resolves one as FUL_REPLANNED (FUL's plan now equals the packs received, F1), ACCEPTED with a reason, or CLAIM_OPENED with the claim's reference. `CloseCargoLot` closes a lot without open discrepancies once its packs are loaded or accounted for by FUL; a cancelled shipment's lot closes with nothing loaded, even while still receiving.
  - A load execution tenders an AIR_CONSOL to the airline terminal or a SEA_LCL_COLOAD to the co-loader's CFS, at the consolidation's origin, while the consolidation is OPEN or CLOSED: `OpenLoadExecution`, `StartLoading`, `RecordCargoLoaded` (a lot's packs under the house's live membership; weight and volume measured for the line, or apportioned from the lot's measurement, the packs that complete the lot taking what the live lines leave of it), `RecordCargoUnloaded` (reversal lines with a reason; after the tender only packs taken back from the carrier, and never out of a closed lot), `PlaceLoadExecutionOnHold` and `ReleaseLoadExecutionHold` (the last release resumes loading), and `CloseLoadExecution` with the tender receipt. A lot's live loaded packs never exceed its received packs, nor a house's its planned packs (CONSERVATION_VIOLATION); loads and reversals of one house run one after another under its ledger's lock. A FAILED screening holds every tender with live packs of the lot, and packs of a failed lot are recorded and hold the tender; the hold is released once the lot is cleared or its packs are out.
  - `RecordCarrierLoadEvidence` records the airline's or co-loader's status of a closed tender by hand (OD-4 C): ACCEPTED, MANIFESTED, DEPARTED, OFFLOADED, ARRIVED or LOADED_ON_BOARD, of the master or one house, on a flight or voyage. A source reference is recorded once, and the same message entered again changes nothing. A fact reported with more pieces than tendered, or departed and offloaded together beyond them, is recorded and raises a LOAD_CONFLICT discrepancy (each fact counts its largest report, so one departure reported by two sources is one departure); offloaded pieces raise an OFFLOADED one (G06-05); `ResolveLoadingDiscrepancy` accepts or claims them.
  - A house leaves its consolidation (detach, transfer, cancellation) only once nothing of it is loaded there, and a refreshed plan keeps at least the packs loaded. A consolidation reopens only before any pack is tendered and is cancelled only with no tender open (OD-5). `ReconcileConsolidation` ends a CLOSED consolidation once FUL reports every live house PLANNED at the frozen plan version and allocated to it, its live loaded packs equal the plan, no tender is open and no discrepancy of the houses' lots or of the tenders is open; a reconciled consolidation never changes again.
  - Queries: `GetCargoLot`, `GetLoadExecution`, `ListOpenDiscrepancies` (of a consolidation, a shipment or the tenant) and `GetPhysicalLoadEvidence` (PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 for FUL: a shipment's received packs, the latest measurement and screening of each lot, tendered packs and lines, tender receipts, carrier evidence and open discrepancies).
  - Migration `phy/000002_phy_cargo_lots_tender`. The database keeps receipt lines, measurements, screening results, load lines, reversals and carrier evidence immutable, lets a discrepancy or hold be resolved or released only once, keeps a lot's and an execution's identity, and refuses any change to a closed lot or execution. At commit, under the lot's and the membership ledger's locks, it checks that a lot's live loaded packs stay within its received packs and a house's within its live entry's planned packs, that a house leaving a consolidation has nothing loaded there, and that a consolidation reopens before any tender and is cancelled or reconciled with no tender open.
  - Tests: the lot, tender and reconciliation rules in the domain, and a property test of random loads and reversals over three tenders (live packs within the lot; apportioned weights adding up to the lot's). PG16 tests with a stand-in for FUL: the air day from receipt to reconciliation, conservation, a failed screening holding the tender, writes past PHY refused, eight tenders racing for one lot (three loads of two packs commit; the lot never exceeds its six), eight tenders racing to load one house from two lots (one load of four commits, the others are refused under the house's lock), and a tendered house whose shipment is cancelled, taken back from the terminal, ended and its lot closed. A PG16 slice with every context runs scenario CW-GAP-E2E-01: a house one carton short at receipt is replanned in FUL, refreshed in PHY and AIR, tendered, departed and reconciled.
  - Not yet: FUL consuming PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 and relaying PHY's measures to AIR, AirDocFacts v1, PhysicalCostBasis v1 for FIN, container stuffing, ULD build-up and planned load units (with SEA and reference data PB8), and a carrier-message adapter (OD-4 A before the pilot).
- V2.35 implements the second part of FUL V0.4 and AIR V0.2: FUL takes in PHY's verified evidence and passes the measures to AIR, step 5 of the N1 forwarder daily loop (FUL tactical candidate V0.4, backlog FU4 for RecordVerifiedLoadEvidence and the execution status; PHY/AIR/SEA candidate, OD-2 option A: measures reach AIR through FUL).
  - `RecordVerifiedLoadEvidence` reads PHY's PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 before FUL's transaction (T3), for the consolidation FUL records the shipment attached to, and keeps a new evidence version when PHY's facts changed: received packs and the lots' latest measures, whether every lot completed receipt, what was tendered in the consolidation, the pieces the carrier's evidence shows departed, arrived and offloaded, the security state of AIR cargo and the open discrepancies. The same facts again change nothing and keep the revision, unless the plan changed since (the status and the packs final for planning are derived again); a cancelled shipment records nothing (SHIPMENT_CANCELLED). The evidence stays beside the plan, never in it (UL-13).
  - The execution status (NOT_STARTED, PARTIALLY_DEPARTED, DEPARTED, PARTIALLY_ARRIVED, ARRIVED) is derived from the evidence alone. Departed packs (up to what the plan held) are final for planning: the shipment is then not cancelled, its route not amended, and its plan not cut below them; the database checks the same at commit.
  - AirOperationPlan v1 relays the measures: TENDERED with what was handed over in the attached consolidation (tenders with their receipt) once weighed, else RECEIVED with the lots' latest measures once every lot completed receipt; a volume missing for a lot or tender makes the volume unknown rather than partial; with the security state (CLEARED, PENDING, FAILED), the evidence version and the execution status.
  - AIR V0.2: a house freezes the relayed measures (basis RECEIVED or TENDERED) with the security state and FUL's evidence version, and its documented chargeable weight is computed on them; `RefreshHouseWaybillFacts` takes new measures as well as a new plan version, and refuses when neither changed. Parts stay as planned. Master totals follow the houses.
  - PHY's PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 now tells, per tender, the house's departed, arrived and offloaded pieces (its own reports, largest per flight and summed over flights, or the master's when they cover the tender or the tender less the pieces offloaded from named houses; an offload reported for the master alone names no house) and its quantities at each lot's latest measurement (lines weighed for themselves keep their weight, the lot's other packs take the rest), so a terminal weight taken after the tender counts; and per lot whether receipt was completed.
  - Migrations `ful/000005_ful_load_evidence` (immutable evidence versions; departed packs final at commit) and `air/000002_air_measured_facts` (basis RECEIVED and TENDERED, evidence version, security state; existing entries stay PLANNED).
  - Tests: evidence versions, the derived status and departure finality in FUL's domain; FUL's summing of PHY's evidence (units, unmeasured lots, security, tenders of the attached consolidation); PHY's attribution of pieces and its measures at a terminal weight; AIR's measured facts and refreshes; a PG16 test of FUL with stand-ins for PHY (versions, a no-change record keeping the revision, the relayed measures, cancel refused after departure, writes past FUL refused); a PG16 slice with every context in which a house goes from planned to received (303 kg, 2 m3: 333.5 kg chargeable) to tendered at the terminal's 318 kg, departs, and can no longer be cancelled.
  - Not yet: FUL's leg allocations, capacity links and rebooking of offloaded cargo; execution discrepancies and cost drivers; AirDocFacts v1 for DOC; a house received at two facilities.
- V2.36 implements AIR V0.3 with the third part of FUL V0.4: AIR finalizes the master's and houses' facts for DOC as AirDocFacts v1 (relationship 11), step 6 of the N1 forwarder daily loop (PHY/AIR/SEA candidate, backlog PB5; DOC candidate 24).
  - FUL keeps the goods description of the COM order line on every obligation entry it plans and relays it with each part in LoadIntent v1 and AirOperationPlan v1. Each evidence version of an AIR shipment keeps the lots' latest screening results (outcome, screening party, method, reference, and the time of the screening party's evidence), and AirOperationPlan v1 relays them. Evidence without screenings keeps its V2.35 digest (pinned by a test), so earlier versions and SEA shipments are not recorded again.
  - AIR freezes the goods descriptions and the screening evidence on each house entry; a new screening reference is something to refresh. `FinalizeAirDocFacts` first reads FUL's AirOperationPlan v1 of every live house (T3) and publishes the next AirDocFacts version only when each still matches what AIR froze (STALE_HOUSE otherwise), every house is on PHY's measures (RECEIVED or TENDERED) with a measured volume, cleared by screening with its evidence (security status SPX), every part has its goods description and every house departs within its window. The movement becomes FINAL and the receipt names the version.
  - A version is immutable: the master (airline, MAWB from the booking confirmation, airports, confirmed flights, totals with the chargeable weight, SPX) and every live house in HAWB order (FUL plan and evidence versions, measures, chargeable weight, screening evidence, parts with order ids, package types and goods descriptions). It is stored as the exact JSON published, with its SHA-256, which the database checks on insert and AIR checks on the stored bytes whenever it loads the movement, so a stored version reads whatever AIR's code becomes. It carries no parties, charges, handling codes or carrier facts.
  - Any later change (a relinked booking, a house associated, refreshed or ended) returns a FINAL movement to OPEN, publishes `AirDocFactsSuperseded.v1`, and the version reads SUPERSEDED; the next finalization publishes the next version. `GetAirDocFacts` reads the latest or a given version with its bytes; the two events carry the version number and identifiers only. FINAL speaks for AIR: until AIR consumes FUL's events, a house takes FUL's newer facts when it is refreshed (T6).
  - Migrations `ful/000006_ful_goods_and_screening` and `air/000003_air_doc_facts`: state FINAL, one movement in force per consolidation, immutable screening and version rows. At commit the database checks that versions are numbered without gaps and written at the revision of the movement they make FINAL, that a FINAL movement has a live house and is still at that revision (no clocks compared), and that a house's screenings and parts, and FUL's screening rows, are written with their entry or evidence version. Existing obligation entries and parts have no goods description, so houses of shipments FUL planned before V2.36 are refused for DOC.
  - Tests: the finalize rules, superseding, and restore checks (content that does not match its digest, a FINAL movement changed since) in AIR's domain; a PG16 test of AIR with stand-ins (versions, replay, a stale house, a FINAL movement holding its consolidation, a house associated and ended from FINAL, the same reading on a session in another time zone, writes past AIR refused); FUL's goods descriptions on PG16; the PG16 slice with every context now finalizes version 1 after the terminal weight, supersedes it when the airline retimes the flight, finalizes version 2 and keeps it current after departure.
  - Not yet: DOC itself (HAWB and MAWB issued from AirDocFacts v1), handling codes and dangerous goods, the airline's status facts, and SEA's OceanDocFacts v1.
- V2.37 implements DOC V0.1, the transport documents of the N1 air line: the house air waybill (HAWB) and master air waybill (MAWB) of an air consolidation, step 7 of the N1 forwarder daily loop (DOC candidate 24, backlog DB1 and DB2 for air and DB3; decisions N1-DOC OD-1, OD-2, OD-3, OD-7, OD-10), with COM V0.9 (the C12 party lock) and ORG's LegalEntityRef v1.
  - `DraftTransportDocument` builds a version from AIR's latest AirDocFacts v1, which must be FINAL: a HAWB from the house of one shipment, with the shipper, consignee and notify party COM froze on every order of its parts (the orders must name the same parties, else DOC_PARTIES_DIVERGE; air waybills need a named consignee); a MAWB from the master with a line per house, the issuing legal entity as shipper and the destination agent (an ORG party in the OVERSEAS_AGENT role, with its office address) as consignee. Numbers are AIR's: the HAWB AIR drew for the shipment, the MAWB from the booking confirmation. An issued number belongs to its shipment (HAWB) or movement (MAWB) under its legal entity for ever: the shipment's next HAWB after a cancellation issues it again, another shipment is refused at draft (DOC_NUMBER_ISSUED). One document of a kind that is not cancelled per shipment or consolidation.
  - Every version is kept as the JSON written with its SHA-256; approval, issue and discard are records beside it. `RefreshDocumentDraft` writes the next draft from newer facts or parties; `ApproveTransportDocument` checks the document is complete (a Chinese shipper's credit or customs code, the consignee's identifier) and flags an approver who drafted it, who needs ApproveOwnTransportDocument (OD-7 B).
  - `IssueTransportDocument` reads AIR, COM and FIN first (T3): the facts must still be AIR's latest FINAL version (DOC_SOURCE_STALE); for a HAWB, COM must report every order's parties locked for the document and unchanged since the draft (DOC_PARTIES_NOT_LOCKED, C12); FIN is not built, so a house document needs a credit override with a reason from an actor allowed OverrideCreditCheck (OD-3 B), recorded on the issue. The version must be approved and complete and the issuer still VERIFIED in ORG. An issuance authority must be in force for the legal entity, kind, issue place and, for a MAWB, airline (`RecordIssuanceAuthority`, `WithdrawIssuanceAuthority`; OD-10 A); DOC holds it while it issues.
  - `OpenDocumentAmendment`, `IssueDocumentAmendment` and `AbandonDocumentAmendment` correct an issued document as its next version under the same number; the amendment's draft is approved before it is issued, and abandoning needs the parties locked again and unchanged. A HAWB whose shipment AIR rolled to another movement is amended to name the new master (MovementID). `CancelTransportDocument` cancels a draft, approved, issued or amending document. Queries: `GetTransportDocument`, `GetDocumentVersion` (the content as written and read), `ListDocumentsForShipment`, `ListDocumentsForConsolidation`, `GetIssuanceAuthority`. Events carry identifiers and the version only.
  - COM V0.9: `LockOrderParties` and `ReleaseOrderPartyLock` (OrderPartyLock v1) hold an order's parties and transport for a document; COM refuses `SetOrderParty` and `ChangeOrderTransport` while a lock is held, and `GetOrderParties` lists the locks; a closed order is locked too, a cancelled one is not. As with FUL's reservations, the API client locks and releases (T6); COM never asks DOC. ORG adds `GetLegalEntity` (LegalEntityRef v1) and the OVERSEAS_AGENT role constant.
  - Migrations `com/000009_com_order_party_lock` and `doc/000001_doc_transport_documents`. The database keeps versions, approvals, issues, discards and issued numbers immutable, checks each version's digest, lets a document change only its state and an authority only be withdrawn, and checks at commit that a document's state matches its versions, that its records are written with it, and that no party or transport version is written while the order is locked.
  - Tests: content, lifecycle, every issue gate, version statuses and restore from bytes in DOC's domain; COM's locks in the domain; a PG16 test of DOC with stand-ins (lock, override, authority, stale facts, amendments approved before issue, the shipment's next HAWB after a cancellation, a rollover to another master, another shipment refused the number, writes past DOC refused) and eight HAWBs racing for one shipment (one wins); a PG16 slice with every context from the order to the issued HAWB and MAWB, a party change refused under the lock, and a notify party added by amendment while setting the party races the lock.
  - Not yet: PDF rendering, sea documents, number stocks, an airline-executed MAWB, release cases, dispatch with consent and cross-border records (P5), the HTTP API, the screening gate (until CUS screening is in force).
- V2.38 implements DOC V0.2, printing and sending the air waybills (DOC candidate 24, backlog DB6 and DB7 in part; decisions N1-DOC OD-4 A and OD-5 A, privacy decision P5), with ORG V0.7 (ContactTransferRegister v1), COM's ordering party on OrderParties v1 and the kernel's contact keys.
  - `RenderDocumentVersion` renders a version to PDF under template IDP-AWB-V1, written by DOC's own small PDF 1.4 writer: Helvetica (WinAnsi, so accented Latin letters, the euro sign and typographic quotes and dashes too) and the non-embedded STSong-Light font for Chinese, no date in the file, so the same version, status and contacts give the same bytes. Boxes grow with their text up to a limit, and what does not fit continues after the cargo; long words break, an item never runs into the execution box, weights are rounded half up. A version that is not issued carries a diagonal "DRAFT - NOT VALID" mark beneath the text. DOC stores no PDF.
  - `PrepareDocumentDispatch` records one copy of a version for an ORG party over a channel (EMAIL_PDF, PRINT_HANDOVER, PORTAL): an issued version to anyone, a draft only to the ordering party COM names. DOC reads the recipient's registration country from ORG, the contacts of the house's first order from COM for the purpose DOCUMENTS (with the actor's own authorization; COM logs the read), only those of the party versions the version froze, ORG's current consent of each contact from ORG's address book (also logged; a consent withdrawn after the order counts, the narrower of the two applies), and ORG's register. Each contact is decided and recorded with its key, party, role, source, classification, legal path and consent, never its name, phone, e-mail or consent evidence: the ordering party gets back the contacts it gave on the order and its own (NOT_PROVIDED); any other contact goes to a party in mainland China only with consent to be provided (PROVISION), and to a party abroad, Hong Kong, Macao and Taiwan included, only with consent to go abroad too (CROSS_BORDER), by the threshold exemption or the tenant's standard contract or certification, and none while the register is BLOCKED (DOC_TRANSFER_BLOCKED). A contact without the consent needed, or of a closed or erased order, is left off with the reason; one person on two roles goes by the narrower decision. The copy's SHA-256 is recorded.
  - The API client sends (T6): it reserves the dispatch's cross-border contact keys in ORG (`ReserveContactTransfers`), fetches the copy (`GetDispatchCopy`, which renders it again and refuses with DOC_COPY_CHANGED when a contact changed, lost its consent or was erased since, and with DOC_TRANSFER_NOT_RESERVED while ORG does not hold its keys), sends it and records the outcome. `RecordDispatchOutcome` records SENT or UNKNOWN only while ORG holds the keys, FAILED with evidence, after which the client releases them (`ReleaseContactTransfers`). Queries: `GetDispatch`, `ListDocumentDispatches`, `ListContactProvisions` (the records of a period, for an actor allowed ReadProvisionRecords). Events carry the dispatch, document and version only.
  - Contact keys (kernel): `ORG:<party>:<contact id>` for a contact of ORG's address book (contact ids are a party's own), `COM:<order>:<role>:<party version>` for one given on an order, and `SHA:<digest>` of either when an id holds anything else, so a key never carries an e-mail or phone used as an id.
  - ORG V0.7: ContactTransferRegister v1 counts, per tenant and calendar year in China time by ORG's clock, the distinct persons whose contacts went abroad, from each dispatch's reservations, under the year's row lock: a warning from 80,000 persons, a refusal from 100,000 without a standard contract or certification in force (`RecordTransferSafeguard`), and from 1,000,000 always. A person is counted once a year; a dispatch reserves once ever and is released once; a release may bring the count to none; a replay answers the persons and state the command answered. `GetContactTransferRegister` answers the persons, the state, the safeguard and a dispatch's keys. The thresholds are an option of the module (`WithTransferThresholds`).
  - COM's `GetOrderParties` names the ordering party (CustomerPartyID).
  - Migrations `org/000011_org_contact_transfer_register` and `doc/000002_doc_rendering_dispatch`. The database keeps contact decisions and outcomes immutable, checks each contact's key, its legal path against its consent and, at commit, its classification against the recipient and that one person is left off for every role or none; it lets a dispatch change only by outcomes until SENT or FAILED, sends a draft only to a known ordering party, and checks that the dispatch's state matches its outcomes and that its contacts were written by the transaction that prepared it. In ORG a dispatch's marker and its reservations are written under the register's lock, a reservation is only released, once, after it was reserved on ORG's clock, and markers and safeguards never change.
  - Tests: the contact keys; the PDF's structure (xref offsets, stream lengths), determinism, Chinese and accented Latin text, widths, wrapping, growing boxes, rounding and continuation pages; the contact decisions (the ordering party, a person on two roles, a blocked register) and the dispatch lifecycle in DOC's domain; a PG16 test of DOC with stand-ins (a draft to the ordering party, the copy to an agent abroad, copies and SENT only once ORG holds the keys, a consent withdrawn in ORG, the register blocked, a standard contract, a closed order's contacts, no personal data in DOC's rows or events, writes past DOC refused); PG16 tests of ORG's register (counting once, warning, block, safeguard, assessment threshold, release to none, reserving once, replay, writes past ORG refused) and eight reservations racing at the block (three admitted); the PG16 slice with every context now sends the HAWB to the agent in the US with the shipper's consenting contact and without the consignee's.
  - Not yet: ORG settling reservations from DOC's DocumentDispatched events (until then the client must not release a dispatch recorded SENT or UNKNOWN), counting a person given on several orders once (each order's contact counts), the three-year purge of the records (with the platform scheduler), the sending channels themselves, BOOK's use of the register, sea documents, carrier messages (FWB, FHL), the HTTP API.

- V2.39 implements FIN V0.1, the first part of charges and billing (N1 step 8; FIN candidate 26, backlog FB1 in part, FB2 without rating, FB8; decisions N1-FIN OD-1 A, OD-5 B, OD-7 A, OD-8 A), with RATE's PricingBasis v1, FUL's CostDriver v1 and DOC's credit check against FIN (C7).
  - New context FIN (`internal/contexts/fin`). `OpenFinancePolicy` records a legal entity's functional currency and book once (FIN is the authority for the functional currency; the legal entity must be VERIFIED in ORG); `RecordExchangeRate` adds rate entries per type (ESTIMATE, INVOICE, ACCOUNTING), currency and period of at most a year that never overlap, with the ISO 4217 minor digits the kernel lists; `EndExchangeRate` ends a wrong entry's period earlier once, unless a charge was converted at it after the new end. Conversion halves away from zero per line, in the kernel (`kernel.ConvertMinor`, which RATE's quote conversion now uses too).
