  - `OpenFreightJob` opens the job of a house shipment from FUL's CostDriver v1: one job per shipment and legal entity that is not cancelled (OD-1 A), freezing the legal entity, functional currency, book and customer. `ApplyPricingBasis` prices the job from the accepted quote (RATE's PricingBasis v1, read before FIN's transaction): each quote line becomes a charge line with a sell estimate for the customer and a cost estimate when it has one, converted at FIN's own ESTIMATE rate (never the quote's); the quote must back an order of the shipment's parts at the revision FUL planned; a quote is priced once per job and a quote and order on one job only (FIN_BASIS_APPLIED). `RecordChargeLine` adds a line by hand with a reason, the debtor and creditor confirmed in their roles by ORG; `ReviseChargeSide` writes the next immutable version with a reason, keeping the side's party unless another is named; amounts are bounded by 10^15 minor units; `CancelFreightJob` frees the job's quotes. `GetJobProfit` answers the estimated revenue, cost and profit per charge code in the functional currency.
  - Credit: `SetCreditTerms` opens or changes a debtor's account with a legal entity (limit, payment days and basis); `PlaceCreditHold` and `ReleaseCreditHold` are manual (OD-8 A); `ListCreditExposure` lists the debtors with an account or with exposure (the estimated sell of their open jobs) against the limit. `GetCreditStatus` answers CreditStatus v1 (OK, ON_HOLD or NO_ACCOUNT, with a decision id naming the account revision).
  - DOC asks CreditStatus v1 for the ordering parties of a house document's orders at issue, amendments included: a hold refuses the issue even with an override reason (DOC_CREDIT_HOLD); all OK issues with credit basis FIN_OK and the decision ids, which the database requires (`doc/000003_doc_credit_decision`); a debtor without an account still needs the override (OD-3 B).
  - RATE: `GetPricingBasis` answers PricingBasis v1 of purpose QUOTE_ACCEPTED, an accepted quote's sell and cost lines (costs leave RATE only here). FUL: a planned obligation entry keeps the accepted quote COM's obligation snapshot named, and `GetShipmentCostDriver` answers CostDriver v1 of kind SHIPMENT_PLANNED (plan version, status, mode, route, scope, legal entity, customer, and the orders of the live parts with their quotes).
  - Migrations `ful/000007_ful_cost_driver`, `fin/000001_fin_jobs_credit` and `doc/000003_doc_credit_decision`. The database keeps rate entries, charge lines, side versions and terms immutable, lets a job change only its state, a basis only stop being live with its job's cancellation, and a hold only be released once; one job per shipment and legal entity and one live basis per quote and order are unique; at commit it checks that rate entries do not overlap and were not ended before a use, that a side's local amount is its amount or recomputes exactly from an entry of the job's policy in force when it was recorded, that versions are numbered without gaps, that rows are written with their parent and that an account's state matches its open hold.
  - Tests: the kernel's conversion; policy rates, job charges and profit, and credit accounts in FIN's domain; a PG16 test of FIN with stand-ins (policy, rates and their overlap, a job priced from the quote and by hand, a debtor without a billing role refused, revisions, profit, the quote refused on a second job of the order until the first job is cancelled, credit terms, a hold, CreditStatus v1, exposure, writes past FIN refused) and eight jobs of one shipment racing (one wins); RATE's pricing basis and FUL's cost driver on PG16; DOC issues on FIN's decision (a hold refuses, OK issues as FIN_OK); the PG16 slice with every context now prices the job of SH-A from the accepted quote, and the HAWB is refused while FIN holds the customer's credit and issued on FIN's decision once released.
  - Known limits: a hold placed after DOC read FIN does not stop that issue (the decision id names the revision checked); every SEA job is LCL until CostDriver v1 carries the load type; two copies of one command sent at once may answer a conflict instead of a replay, as elsewhere.
  - Not yet: debit and credit notes on issued documents, billing profiles and numbering (FIN V0.2), rating through RATE, consol cost pools, creditor invoices, fapiao, tax treatments, recognition, closure, postings, FinanceEvent v1 to EVT, PaymentTerms v1 to RATE, the HTTP API.
- V2.40 implements FIN V0.2, billing, the second part of N1 step 8 (FIN candidate 26, backlog FB5 and FB1's tax treatments; decisions N1-FIN OD-2 B in part, OD-5 B), with DOC V0.4's TransportDocumentSnapshot v1 (DOC -> FIN, registered in Context Map V1.4).
  - DOC: `GetHouseDocumentSnapshot` answers the valid issued version of a shipment's HAWB (number, master number, legal entity, issue time, route, weights); a draft, approved or cancelled document is not found, so FIN never bills a draft.
  - Policy: `SetChargeTaxTreatment` adds immutable versions of a charge code's treatment (EXEMPT_INTL_FREIGHT_FORWARDING, TAXABLE with a rate in basis points, PASS_THROUGH_DISBURSEMENT) in force from a date; a version is refused on or before a date an unreversed debit note line billed the code (FIN_TAX_TREATMENT_IN_USE). Amounts are what the debtor pays; VAT is split out on the fapiao later. `RegisterInvoiceSeries` fixes one number series per kind (debit, credit note): prefix and zero-padded sequence, drawn from the series row in the issuing transaction, so numbers have no gaps and a failed issue takes none; `GetFinancePolicy` shows the next number.
  - `SetBillingProfile` freezes the invoice title and taxpayer number from ORG's party identity (identifiers stay in ORG, invoice data in FIN, MD-21): a Chinese enterprise needs ORG's unified social credit code, a foreign buyer is titled with its English name; the enterprise's registered address, phone, bank and account are optional (none of a sole trader's phone or account); the evidence is a document reference. A debit note is refused while the profile no longer matches ORG (FIN_BILLING_PROFILE_STALE).
  - `IssueDebitNote` bills a debtor's estimated sell sides of a job: before FIN's transaction DOC reports the HAWB issued, not after the note's time (FIN_NO_ISSUED_DOCUMENT), and ORG confirms the debtor's role and identity. The note is in the functional currency (foreign sides at the INVOICE rate) or in its lines' one currency (booked at the ACCOUNTING rate), freezes the profile version, the credit account's terms with the due date (due at once without an account) and the document, and takes the next number. Billed sides become INVOICED: not revised, not billed again, and the job not cancelled (FIN_INVOICED). Several notes per job and debtor.
  - `IssueCreditNote` reverses whole lines of a debit note, each once, negated at its conversion and tax; it lowers what the debtor owes and leaves the remaining lines above zero, so a discount line is never stranded. The sides return to ESTIMATED at their amount (ESTIMATE rate) or at zero with `WriteOff`; a debit note with every line reversed is CANCELLED_BY_CREDIT_NOTE. `GetDebtorInvoice`, `ListOpenDebtorItems` and `GetBillingProfile` read them; credit exposure splits into Unbilled and Billed, and job profit shows the invoiced part. Events (identifiers only): DebtorInvoiceIssued, DebtorInvoiceCancelled, CreditNoteIssued, BillingProfileSet, ChargeTaxTreatmentSet, InvoiceSeriesRegistered, and ChargeSideRevised for every side a note writes.
  - FIN and DOC ask each other, so the composition root assembles FIN with a `DocumentSnapshots` binding and binds it to DOC once DOC exists.
  - Migration `fin/000002_fin_billing`. Notes, lines, profiles and treatments are immutable; a debit note changes only from ISSUED to CANCELLED_BY_CREDIT_NOTE, a series only its next number forward. At commit the database checks that a note totals its lines, carries its series' number, is dated on its issue's date and not before its document, and follows its terms; that a debit note line bills the INVOICED side written with it at the same amount and conversion, recomputed from the rate entry in force, under the tax version in force among those entered by then; that a credit note line negates its debit note line and a returned side has its amount (zero for a write-off); that a cancelled job has no issued debit note; that a series drew exactly the notes' numbers; and that an INVOICED side converts at an INVOICE or ACCOUNTING rate, an estimate at ESTIMATE. CI grants add only `UPDATE (state)` on notes and `UPDATE (next_seq)` on series.
  - Tests: unit tests of treatments, series, notes, profiles and dates; a PG16 test of FIN with stand-ins (every refusal, CNY and USD notes, credit notes including a USD note after the ESTIMATE rate ended and a write-off, a renamed debtor, open items, a treatment corrected after reversal, the rate in use, writes past FIN including a forged note and a cancelled billed job); eight debit notes at once drawing eight numbers without gaps, two at one job revision (one wins), an exhausted series drawing nothing, and a treatment racing six notes (consistent in both orders); DOC's snapshot; the PG16 slice with every context bills the HAWB issued on FIN's decision (SHADN000001) and waives the document fee (SHACN000001).
  - Known limits: a HAWB cancelled after billing is not noticed (no DOC event consumed yet; a credit note reverses the bill); SEA jobs wait for DOC's sea house bills; returning a foreign side at its amount needs an ESTIMATE rate; every debit note stays open until settlement exists; one series per kind for good.
  - Not yet: settlement and statements, fapiao and red-letter fapiao, tax amounts, individual buyers and delivery contacts, printing debit notes, rating through RATE, consol cost pools, creditor invoices, recognition, closure, postings, FinanceEvent v1 to EVT, PaymentTerms v1 to RATE, the HTTP API.
- V2.41 takes document numbers from idp-platform-go's numbering (master data rule MD-28: the platform owns the template language and the sequence allocation, Cargo owns its document types, templates and issued numbers), for FIN's debit and credit notes (FIN V0.2.1) and AIR's HAWB numbers (AIR V0.3.1), which V2.40 and V2.33 had numbered with series of their own.
  - New dependency `go.idp.xyz/idp-platform-go v0.1.0-rc.1` (public, built on the same Bento candidate rc.2); CI pins its checksum beside Bento's. `internal/foundation/numbering` is Cargo's seam: Cargo's subset of the template language (a literal prefix, literals, `{yyyy}`, `{yy}`, `{mm}`, `{q}` — a month or quarter only with its year, or its numbers would recur a year later — and one `{seq:N}` with N from 4 to 10; no random, scrambled or time-of-day tokens), the reset period of a business date (year, quarter, month or none, by the finest date token), exhaustion when a sequence outgrows its width, and the platform's sequence table in a context's own schema.
  - `cargo-migrate` applies the platform's numbering asset in the schema of each context the manifest marks `numbering` (`air numbering`, `fin numbering`; `-numbering` with `-directory`), recorded as `platform:<schema>:<id>`. Migrations `air/000004_air_hawb_numbering` and `fin/000003_fin_numbering` scope the sequence tables by tenant (FORCE RLS); a sequence starts at 1 or at its series' seed, only for a registered series, moves one number at a time and is never deleted. Existing series become continuous templates `<prefix>{seq:<width>}` with their sequences after the last number drawn, so issued numbers stay and new ones continue. CI grants on the sequence tables only `UPDATE (next_val, updated_at)`.
  - `RegisterHawbSeries` takes a continuous template rendering at most twelve capital letters and digits and a first number: HAWB templates do not reset yet, as AIR does not know the issuing office's time zone. `RegisterInvoiceSeries` takes a template rendering five to twenty capital letters, digits and hyphens (the debit and credit note prefixes never start one another), a first number and, for a template that resets with a first number above 1, the date of the first number's period. A note takes the period of its invoice date; numbers are taken in the command's transaction, so a failed command releases them. A note keeps its period; at commit the database renders Cargo's template subset itself and checks that a note's number is its template at its sequence value and invoice date, its period the template's period of that date, and that the sequence drew exactly the notes' numbers. `GetHawbSeries` and `GetFinancePolicy` show the template, first number and last number per period.
  - Tests: the template subset (a month without its year refused), periods and exhaustion; the manifest's capabilities and the migration order; PG16: templates refused (no HAWB form, too long, a token Cargo does not take, a HAWB template that resets), eight houses and eight debit notes drawing concurrently without gaps, a monthly note template whose first number continues in one month and restarts in the next, an exhausted sequence drawing nothing, a note numbered off its template refused at commit, and the sequence tables refusing to move back or two at a time, a stray or out-of-seed start, another tenant's row, deletion and a skipped number.
  - Known limits: HAWB templates do not reset until AIR knows the issuing office's time zone; Cargo reads and writes the platform's sequence table itself (seed, last values, checks), as the platform has no seed, peek or assign-at-date call yet.
- V2.42 follows billing up (FIN V0.2.2, DOC V0.4.1), removing two of V2.40's known limits.
  - DOC: `GetHouseDocumentSnapshot` takes an optional instant `At` and answers the version of the shipment's valid HAWB issued last at or before it (ISSUED, or SUPERSEDED by a later amendment); with no version issued by then, or the HAWB cancelled, it is not found. `IssueDebitNote` bills the version valid at the note's business time, so a note entered after an amendment for an earlier date bills the version it was issued on.
  - `ListDebitNotesOnChangedDocuments` lists a legal entity's open debit notes whose HAWB changed after billing, for a person to decide: DOCUMENT_AMENDED (a later version issued), DOCUMENT_CANCELLED (no valid HAWB now) or DOCUMENT_REPLACED (another HAWB holds the shipment), with the document billed and the valid one. FIN asks DOC when asked, as it consumes no DOC event yet; a credit note closes a note's entry.
  - `IssueCreditNote` returns a reversed side to the estimate it had before billing, at that estimate's own conversion, instead of converting it again at the ESTIMATE rate in force: a foreign side can be credited after its ESTIMATE rate ended. The returned version keeps when its rate was applied (`ConvertedAt` in `GetFreightJob`); `WriteOff` still returns it at zero.
  - Migration `fin/000004_fin_returned_estimates`: side versions gain `converted_at`, set only on an estimate a credit note returned. At commit the database recomputes such a local amount at the entry in force when it was converted, counts that time as the entry's use when the entry is ended, and checks that the returned version copies the estimate billed (amount, local amount, conversion and its time) or is zero for a write-off. The returns V2.40 and V2.41 wrote (converted again when they were recorded, no conversion time) stay as they are and stay valid, so their jobs go on; a trigger refuses new returns of that shape. The migration goes in with the V2.42 binary: once it is applied, a V2.41 binary's credit note that returns a foreign side is refused.
  - Tests: DOC's snapshot at instants (before the first issue, between versions, while amending, after a cancellation and reissue); FIN with stand-ins: a note entered after an amendment billing version 1, the review list after an amendment, a cancellation and a replacement until a credit note closes it, a USD note credited after the ESTIMATE rate ended (CNY 1,424.68 at the estimate's rate) and then billed again and written off, the ESTIMATE rate's end refused at the estimates' conversion, and the database refusing an earlier conversion on a billed or revised side. The return check itself is tested on stored rows rewritten as the migrator role (the tests need `IDP_CARGO_MIGRATOR_DSN`): a return converted at another time, at another amount or at zero without a write-off, and a write-off at its amount or keeping a rate, are refused at the job's next commit; a return rewritten to V2.40's shape passes and its side is billed and credited again; a new return of that shape is refused.
  - Known limits: the review list is computed when called, over every open debit note of the legal entity with one DOC read per shipment, not from DOC's events, and has no paging; it has no acknowledgement, so a note stays listed until a credit note closes it, also when the amendment changed nothing billed; SEA jobs still wait for DOC's sea house bills.
- V2.43 implements FIN V0.3, settlement (FIN candidate 26: decision N1-FIN OD-2 B, the second half of backlog FB5): FIN keeps the settlement status of debit notes from bank or ERP evidence; the cash book, bank reconciliation, payment runs and exchange differences stay in the ERP.
  - `RecordDebtorReceipt` records money received from a debtor (ORG confirms its debtor role; a debtor whose roles have ended is still recorded while a debit note of the legal entity is open): the amount credited to the bank, optional bank charges deducted on the way and borne by the legal entity (not above the amount; the debtor is credited with both), the date received (not after the day recorded, not more than 366 days before) and the evidence as a source (BANK or ERP) and a reference, never free text. A legal entity records a piece of evidence once (FIN_EVIDENCE_USED) until that receipt is cancelled. A payment in another currency than the notes is entered at the amount it settles in the notes' currency.
  - `AllocateDebtorReceipt` settles debit notes from a receipt, in part or across several: each an issued debit note of the receipt's legal entity, debtor and currency (FIN_NOT_ALLOCATABLE), within what the receipt has unallocated and the note has open (FIN_OVER_ALLOCATED); what is not allocated stays on account. `ReverseDebtorReceiptAllocation` reverses one allocation once, with a reason; `CancelDebtorReceipt` cancels a receipt that settles nothing (ENTERED_IN_ERROR or PAYMENT_RETURNED). Nothing is deleted.
  - A debit note's state stays about the document; what is open of it is its unreversed lines less its live allocations. `GetDebtorInvoice` shows what is settled, by which receipts, and what is open; `ListOpenDebtorItems` lists the notes with an open amount. `IssueCreditNote` is refused when it would leave less on the debit note than receipts settle (FIN_SETTLED): reverse the allocation, issue the credit note, allocate again.
  - Credit exposure counts what is open of a debtor's debit notes, in the functional currency in proportion to the notes' local amounts. Money on account does not lower it: `GetCreditAccount` and `ListCreditExposure` show it per currency beside the exposure, also for a debtor with no credit account and no exposure.
  - `GetDebtorStatement` states a debtor's account over a period, per currency and by business dates: the opening balance, the entries (debit and credit notes on their invoice date, receipts on the date received, cancelled receipts on the date cancelled) with the running balance, the closing balance and, as of the end date, the open items with days overdue, the money on account and the ageing (not due, 1 to 30, 31 to 60, 61 to 90, over 90 days). Allocations and reversals count from their dates, so the closing balance is the open items less the money on account at every end date. `GetDebtorReceipt` and `ListDebtorReceipts` read receipts. Events (identifiers only): DebtorReceiptRecorded, DebtorReceiptAllocated, DebtorReceiptAllocationReversed, DebtorReceiptCancelled.
  - An allocation and every command of the notes' jobs take turns: allocating locks the receipt, then the jobs in order of job id, then reads what is open; the jobs' revisions do not change.
  - Migration `fin/000005_fin_settlement`: `debtor_receipt` (changes only to be cancelled, once) and `debtor_receipt_allocation` (changes only to be reversed, once), under row-level security, never deleted. At commit the database checks that an allocation is written with its receipt, to a debit note of the receipt's legal entity, debtor and currency dated by then; that no debit note has more settled than it still bills (also when its job is written, locking the note's job row first so transactions past FIN racing to one note wait and see each other); that a receipt's allocations are numbered without gaps and their live total is within amount plus bank charges; and that a cancelled receipt settles nothing — the receipt's checks run both when the receipt and when an allocation is written, so `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` cannot slip an allocation past them. Date windows are in hours (8820 hours = 366 days and 36 hours), independent of the session time zone across daylight-saving changes. CI grants add only the cancellation columns of receipts and the reversal columns of allocations.
  - Tests: unit tests of receipts, allocations, the proportion of a note open, the credit note guard and statements (balanced at every end date of two months); PG16 with stand-ins: every refusal, partial and split allocations, bank charges, exposure and money on account, a credit note waiting for a reversal, cancellation and the evidence entered again, statements, writes past FIN refused (rows, allocations behind the receipt, beyond the note or the receipt, to another debtor's note or a credit note, a cancelled receipt that settles), and stored rows rewritten as the migrator role refused at the job's and the receipt's next commit; six receipts racing for one debit note (one settles it) and an allocation racing a credit note (consistent in both orders); receipts of a debtor whose roles ended (recorded while a note is open, refused with nothing open) and money on account listed without an account; a stored allocation re-dated before its receipt refused at the receipt's next commit; the PG16 slice with every context records the customer's payment of SHADN000001 and settles it.
  - Known limits: no write-off of small balances or short payments other than bank charges, and no refund of money on account (the accountant's rules); no receipts in another currency than the notes; allocation by hand, no bank statement import; statements are not acknowledged by the debtor; settled debit notes stay on the review list of changed house documents; a credit note backdated before an allocation's reversal can leave a negative open item on statements as of the dates in between (the balances still add up); creditor payments come with creditor invoices (FB4), posting instructions for receipts with FB7.
- V2.44 implements FIN V0.4, consol cost pools (backlog FB3, OD-6 A): PHY's PhysicalCostBasis v1, one `ConsolCostPool` per consolidation and operating legal entity, versioned ACCRUED cost lines, and apportionment to the house jobs by chargeable weight, W/M, volume, pieces or by hand, conserving every minor unit (migration `fin/000006_fin_consol_pool`).
- V2.45 implements FIN V0.5, creditor invoices and the closing of pools (FIN candidate 26: backlog FB4; decisions N1-FIN OD-9 B, OD-10 A, OD-5 B).
  - `RecordCreditorInvoice` records a supplier's invoice to a legal entity as received: the creditor confirmed by ORG in a CARRIER, CO_LOADER or OVERSEAS_AGENT role, the supplier's number (once per legal entity and creditor among invoices not cancelled, FIN_SUPPLIER_INVOICE_USED), a civil invoice date not after the day recorded and not more than 366 days before, one currency, the incoming tax document (special or ordinary fapiao, or a foreign invoice) with its number, and its lines.
  - `MatchCreditorInvoice` matches every line in full, each accrual once, to job cost sides and pool cost lines of open jobs and pools of the invoice's legal entity: in the invoice's currency (FIN_MATCH_CURRENCY: revise the accrual first), naming no creditor or the invoice's (FIN_MATCH_CREDITOR), of the line's charge code or with a reason, neither matched final nor released (FIN_ACCRUAL_CLOSED). A match that is not final consumes at most what the accrual has open and leaves the rest accrued for a later invoice (FIN_MATCH_EXCEEDS); a final match closes the accrual and carries the variance of all live matches against it, in the currency and in the functional currency. Each match converts at the ACCOUNTING rate in force (OD-5 B). Accruals keep only their estimates and releases: what is matched is the sum of the live matches of invoices not cancelled, read under the job's or pool's lock.
  - Variance (OD-9 B): `SetCostVarianceThreshold` versions a legal entity's threshold, an amount in the functional currency and a rate of the accrual's local amount, in force after the legal entity's latest matching (an invoice keeps the threshold it was matched under); a final variance beyond the greater of the two needs `ApproveCostVariance` with a reason, by another person than the matcher unless the Authorizer also allows `ApproveOwnCostVariance` (the approval keeps the flag). Without a threshold every variance needs approval.
  - `DisputeCreditorInvoice` interrupts a recorded or matched invoice and `ResolveCreditorInvoiceDispute` returns it to that state; `CancelCreditorInvoice` cancels a recorded or disputed one, whose matches then consume nothing, unless another invoice holds a final match of an accrual it matched in part (FIN_FINAL_MATCH_BEHIND: that match's variance counted it; cancel that invoice first). Disputing and cancelling lock the pools and jobs of the matches and need them open (FIN_TARGET_CLOSED).
  - `ReleaseJobAccrual` and `ReleasePoolAccrual` release what no invoice will consume, with a reason: a RELEASED version of amount zero; the accrual then costs what was matched and takes no further match or revision. A cost side is revised only above what invoices consume, in their currency and naming no other creditor than theirs (FIN_ACCRUAL_MATCHED); a job with a matched or released cost side is not cancelled.
  - Pools: an apportionment now divides each line's current cost (what is matched plus what is still accrued; once final or released, what is matched) and records it. `CloseConsolCostPool` closes a pool once every line is matched final or released, by invoices neither disputed nor awaiting approval, and apportioned at its final cost on PHY's current basis with every house loaded, naming the live houses and their current quantities (FIN_POOL_NOT_CLOSABLE); a line of zero cost needs no apportionment, and once PHY cancelled the consolidation or no house is left on it the apportionments at the final costs stand. A cost that no house can bear any more (a no-show or dead-freight charge matched after PHY cancelled the consolidation) is apportioned RETAINED with a reason: it stays on the consolidation as its own cost, as CargoWise leaves a consol charge on the consol when it is not apportioned, and `GetConsolProfit` shows it as retained; RETAINED is refused while PHY reports houses on the consolidation, so house jobs bear what they can. A closed pool takes no change until `ReopenConsolCostPool` reopens it with a reason (OD-10 A).
  - Queries: `GetCreditorInvoice`, `ListCreditorInvoices` (also those awaiting approval) and `ListOpenAccruals` (a legal entity's accruals still expecting an invoice, by creditor); job lines show their accrual status and what is matched; `GetJobProfit` splits cost into matched and accrued; pool lines show status, matched and current cost, the pool its closures, and `GetConsolProfit` the pooled current cost and what is matched.
  - Reads and isolation: FIN's queries run in one snapshot (`TenantTransactor{Snapshot: true}`: REPEATABLE READ, READ ONLY, set before the first statement), so a view reading an aggregate, what is matched of it and its pool shares in several statements never mixes rows from before and after a commit, and cannot write. Every write to a `cargo_fin` table, of this version or an earlier one, runs at READ COMMITTED (a statement-level trigger per table refuses others, fin_read_committed): FIN's commit checks lock the rows other transactions write and then read what they committed, which a REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE snapshot would miss; this covers the settlement, pool share, tax treatment and rate end checks of earlier versions as well.
  - Lock order: a matching locks the invoice, then the pools, then the jobs of its accruals (each in id order), then holds the policy FOR SHARE; job and pool commands read what is matched under their own lock, so matches, revisions, releases, apportionments and closing take turns. Every command now takes the policy last: `ApportionConsolCosts` locks the house jobs before it (since V2.44 it took the policy first, so a rate change waiting for the policy could sit between an apportionment and a job command waiting for each other).
  - Migration `fin/000007_fin_creditor_invoices`: `creditor_invoice` (state, match, approval and cancellation columns change once each along the states), its lines and matches (immutable), disputes (resolved once), `cost_variance_threshold` (immutable, written with the policy, numbered and set in order), `consol_cost_pool_closure` (reopened once); cost and pool versions may be RELEASED (amount zero, no conversion); an apportionment line records the cost it divided (NULL on lines of FIN V0.4, which divided their version's figures). At commit the database checks that an invoice's lines total it, its matches cover every line in full once matched, its disputes follow its state, each match converts at the ACCOUNTING entry in force when matched, and the approval is required exactly when a final variance is beyond the threshold then in force; that a match consumes the accrual's latest version as it stands (owner locked first, open, same legal entity, currency and creditor, charge code or reason, its figures and what other invoices consume recorded, no other final match, within the accrual unless final); that disputing or cancelling an invoice finds its pools and jobs open; that a cost or pool version stands against what invoices consume (nothing after a final match's version or after a release); that a cancelled job has nothing matched or released; that an apportionment of FIN V0.5 divides its line's cost as it stands; and that a CLOSED pool's lines are final, not held, and apportioned at their final cost on the ACTUAL basis, while no line, version, apportionment or share is written into a pool that is not OPEN nor a cost version onto a job that is not. A match locks the policy FOR SHARE and names the threshold in force when matched; a threshold is not set at or before a matching. The commit checks that lock a row and then read what others committed refuse to run at another isolation level than READ COMMITTED (fin_read_committed): at REPEATABLE READ the read would miss what was committed after the transaction's snapshot. Ending a rate entry now also counts pool cost versions (left out by fin/000006) and matches. The migration goes in with the V2.45 binaries: a V2.44 binary cannot apportion afterwards (it writes no line cost). CI grants add only the invoice's changing columns, the dispute's resolution and the closure's reopening.
  - Tests: unit tests of invoices, matching (every refusal, conversion, variance), thresholds, approval, disputes, cancellation, accrual cost, job and pool releases, revisions above matches, and closing (houses added, re-weighed or not loaded, a cancelled consolidation, manual apportionments); PG16 with stand-ins: the whole cycle of two MU invoices and a trucker's invoice over a pool and a job, every refusal, approval by another person and by the controller allowed to approve their own, a dispute holding the pool, closing, reopening, cancelling and releasing, the number freed by a cancellation, and writes past FIN refused; races, each with both orders observed: two invoices matching one accrual in part (one wins, the other FIN_MATCH_EXCEEDS), a dispute against closing the pool, and lowering an accrual against a match. An independent review found a threshold set back in time stranding matched invoices, a cancellation behind another invoice's final match, closed pools and cancelled jobs taking records past FIN, checks bypassed at REPEATABLE READ, a rounding difference between Go and SQL above 10^8 minor units and an approval accepted on a recorded invoice; each is fixed and tested (`TestReviewFindingsOnPostgres`). Further tests: a no-show charge retained on a cancelled consolidation and the pool closed (`TestRetainedOnPostgres`), snapshot reads and writes refused at REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE on tables of V0.3 and V0.5 (`TestSnapshotsAndIsolationOnPostgres`).
  - Known limits: creditor payments (AP settlement) come next; no posting instructions (FB7), so closing a pool waits for no acknowledgement and a variance approval prepares no AP posting; no creditor credit notes (a disputed invoice is cancelled and the supplier's corrected one recorded); an accrual in another currency than its invoice is revised first; a retained cost is not recharged to anyone by FIN (a person adds a sell line to the job of the customer who caused it, while that job is open); job closure and recognition (FB9) are not here.
- V2.46 implements FIN V0.6, creditor payments (FIN candidate 26: decision N1-FIN OD-2 B, the payable side of V2.43's settlement): FIN keeps what is paid of creditor invoices from bank or ERP evidence; the cash book, payment runs, bank reconciliation and exchange differences stay in the ERP.
  - `RecordCreditorPayment` records money a legal entity paid a creditor: the creditor in a CARRIER, CO_LOADER or OVERSEAS_AGENT role (one whose roles ended is still paid what it is owed and no more: ORG still reports the party ACTIVE and the legal entity VERIFIED, and the payment, in a currency of the creditor's matched invoices, disputed or not, is not more than they have open less what earlier payments have on account in that currency; it is recorded at the revision the latest such invoice names), a positive amount, the civil date paid (not after the day recorded, not more than 366 days before) and the evidence (BANK or ERP and a reference, once per legal entity among payments not cancelled, FIN_EVIDENCE_USED).
  - `AllocateCreditorPayment` pays the creditor's invoices: each MATCHED, not awaiting a variance approval (an invoice recorded only or disputed is held from payment, FIN_NOT_PAYABLE), of the payment's legal entity, creditor and currency, within what the payment has unallocated and the invoice has open (FIN_OVER_ALLOCATED); what is not allocated stays on account (paid in advance). `ReverseCreditorPaymentAllocation` reverses an allocation once; `CancelCreditorPayment` cancels a payment paying nothing (ENTERED_IN_ERROR or PAYMENT_RETURNED). A creditor invoice that payments pay is not cancelled (FIN_INVOICE_PAID).
  - Queries: `GetCreditorPayment`, `ListCreditorPayments`, `GetCreditorAccount` (a creditor's open items as they stand when asked, with what is paid, open and payable and the days since each invoice, what is open per currency and what is on account, read in one snapshot; it and `ListCreditorPayments` name a legal entity and a creditor, FIN_INVALID_QUERY otherwise); `GetCreditorInvoice` shows what is paid and by which payments.
  - Lock order: an allocation locks the payment, then its invoices in id order (as invoice commands lock them), so allocations, disputes and cancellations of an invoice take turns.
  - An independent review found commit checks passing when a transaction rebinds its tenant before committing (a paying payment cancelled, a paid invoice cancelled), a former creditor paid in any currency and amount while one minor unit was open, and the account query reading every creditor's invoices when no creditor was named; each is fixed and tested. Receipts of a debtor whose roles ended (V2.43) now likewise need ORG to report the party ACTIVE and the legal entity VERIFIED.
  - Migration `fin/000008_fin_creditor_payments`: `creditor_payment` (changes only to be cancelled, once) and `creditor_payment_allocation` (changes only to be reversed, once), under row-level security, never deleted, written at READ COMMITTED only. At commit the database checks that an allocation is written with its payment, to an invoice of its legal entity, creditor and currency dated by then and, when written, MATCHED and not awaiting approval; that no invoice has more paid than it totals (locking the invoice row first); that a payment's allocations are numbered without gaps and their live total is within its amount, and a cancelled payment pays nothing (a payment it cannot read refuses the commit); and that a cancelled invoice has nothing paid. A deferred guard on every `cargo_fin` table with a tenant, run before a row's other checks, refuses to commit a row of another tenant than the transaction is bound to at commit (`fin_tenant_scope`), so a transaction that rebinds `app.tenant_id` before committing cannot make the row-level-security-bound checks read nothing; roles bypassing row-level security (the migrator) are not bound. CI grants add only the payment's cancellation columns and the allocation's reversal columns.
  - Tests: unit tests of payments, allocations, reversals, cancellations and the paid invoice guard; PG16 with stand-ins: the cycle with every refusal, a creditor whose roles ended (paid what it is owed; beyond it, in another currency, once on account or while suspended refused), the creditor's account (and the queries naming no creditor refused), a paid invoice cancelled only after its allocations are reversed, a payment returned and its evidence entered again, and writes past FIN refused (a tenant rebound before commit among them); two payments racing for one invoice (one wins, the other FIN_OVER_ALLOCATED) and an allocation racing a dispute, with head starts alternated so that both orders can occur; the test asserts that exactly one wins and logs the orders seen.
  - Known limits: no due dates or payment terms of creditors, so no ageing beyond the days since the invoice; no creditor statement; no payment runs; no creditor credit notes (an overpayment stays on account); postings (FB7) and recognition (FB9) are not here.
- V2.47 implements RATE V0.5 part 1, the charge-code catalogue, rate cards and rate evaluation (RATE candidate 11: backlog RB12, RB14 and the engine of RB15; decisions R6, MD-27, N1-RATE OD-1 A, OD-2 A, OD-3 A, OD-8 A). Part 2 builds FIN's pricing bases on it, part 3 the quote side.
  - Catalogue: every tenant starts with the built-in starter list for the launch lines (air freight, fuel and security surcharges, origin terminal handling, handling, CFS, documents, customs clearing, pickup, LCL ocean freight, destination charges, discount; version 0). `DefineChargeCode`, `ReviseChargeCode`, `DeactivateChargeCode` and `ReactivateChargeCode` write immutable versions (codes of two to sixteen upper-case letters, digits or underscores; names in Chinese and English; group, default calculator and unit, modes). `GetChargeCode`, `ListChargeCodes`. Quote lines keep free-text codes until part 3.
  - Rate cards: a SELL `TARIFF` for any customer, a SELL `CUSTOMER` card of a party ORG confirms in a role a quote goes to (R7), a BUY `SUPPLIER` card of a party ORG confirms as CARRIER, CO_LOADER or OVERSEAS_AGENT. `DraftRateCardVersion` (one draft at a time, optionally copying a version), `SetRateCardEntries` (lanes: AIR LOOSE or SEA LCL, origin, destination and via resolved against the location reference data, so the carriers' CNSHA becomes the port CNSHG for SEA; carrier, listed service level, GENERAL commodity, transit days, frequency, a narrower validity; lines per charge code with FLAT, PER_UNIT or PERCENT_OF, rates at a scale finer than minor units, minimum, maximum, quantity step, minimum quantity, volume factor with the OD-8 defaults by mode, breaks, call for pricing; quantities bounded so that no arithmetic overflows), `PublishRateCardVersion` (the entries checked again, codes listed, active, for the entry's mode and not discounts, no overlap with another version as it rates, a start within the record window; a tariff approved by the pricing manager who publishes it, and, when the approver drafted or set the version, self-approved by an allowed role and flagged), `WithdrawRateCardVersion` (with a reason: the version ends when the withdrawal is recorded, still rating earlier business times, and a successor may start then). A CUSTOMER card is published by accepting its AGREEMENT quote (part 3). `GetRateCard`, `GetRateCardVersion`, `ListRateCards`.
  - Rating (`EvaluateRates`, purpose QUOTE_LINE; `GetRateSnapshot`): SELL takes the customer's card first and the tariff for the codes it lacks; BUY the given carrier's cards or the lowest total among supplier cards rated in full in one currency (RATE converts nothing; other cases are recorded). Per charge code the most specific entry (via, then carrier). The band the quantity falls in prices the whole quantity, a higher band's lower bound is charged when cheaper, then minimum and maximum, rounded half away from zero once per line. Chargeable weight is the greater of gross weight and volume over the factor; W/M the greater of tonnes and cubic metres. A missing rate, a call-for-pricing line, a percentage without its base or a PER_UNIT line whose quantity the cargo does not state is a gap, never zero. A snapshot records the input, every candidate and why it was taken or passed over, every line with quantity, band, rate and bounds, and the engine version; it prices nothing.
  - Migration `rate/000004_rate_cards`: the starter list, code versions (in sequence, immutable), cards (only the revision changes), versions (numbered in sequence, DRAFT to PUBLISHED to WITHDRAWN, nothing they state changes once published, their preparers only grow while a DRAFT, the approver is the publisher), entries, lines and breaks (written and replaced only while their version is a DRAFT, holding it FOR SHARE), snapshots (immutable, candidates and lines written with the snapshot and of a side it was asked for; a gap carries no amount), all under row-level security. At commit, with the card row locked first and at READ COMMITTED only: no two versions of a card rate at the same instant (a withdrawn one up to its withdrawal); a published version has entries with lines, a tariff its approval, and no CUSTOMER version is published past RATE. The tenant guard of fin/000008 now covers every `cargo_rate` table (`rate_tenant_scope`). Command receipts name what they changed (Quote, ChargeCode, RateCard or RateSnapshot). Events: `ChargeCodeDefined`, `ChargeCodeRevised`, `ChargeCodeDeactivated`, `ChargeCodeReactivated`, `RateCardVersionPublished`, `RateCardVersionWithdrawn` and `RatesEvaluated` (v1, identifiers and the version only). CI grants: SELECT on the starter list; SELECT, INSERT on code versions, cards, versions and snapshots; UPDATE of the card's revision and of a version's status, preparers, publication, approval and withdrawal columns; SELECT, INSERT, DELETE on entries, lines and breaks.
  - Tests: unit tests of the catalogue, cards and lines (every refusal), the engine (breaks, chargeable weight, W/M, minimum, maximum, rounding, scale, overflow, percentages, gaps, SELL precedence, specificity, validity, the BUY choice) with a property test that a charge never falls as the weight rises and a determinism test over shuffled inputs; PG16 with stand-ins: the catalogue, the card lifecycle with every refusal, rating both sides, and writes past RATE refused (a published version changed, content added or deleted, a customer or empty version published, a second draft, a tenant rebound before commit, a withdrawal at REPEATABLE READ); two definitions of one code and two drafts of one card racing (exactly one commits, orders logged); the composition root with the real ORG and location reference data.
  - An independent review found the SEA chargeable-weight default taken from AIR, recorded snapshots extensible past RATE, ORG and the location data asked before the Authorizer (so an actor without rights could learn roles), a withdrawal erasing the version's past, a self-approval laundered by a colleague re-setting the entries, quantity overflow, open-ended entry validity and odd sides failing as raw database errors, versions written out of sequence, missing cargo priced at zero and copying from a missing version; each is fixed and tested (`TestTariffApprovalAndStarterListOnPostgres` and the card, rating and domain tests). By choice, the BUY lowest total compares what each supplier card charges: a charge one supplier does not bill is not a gap.
  - Known limits: FCL, road and parcel tariffs, zones, conditional lines, fuel indexes, carrier spot quotes and rate ingestion are out of S2; SEA SELL filing references are recorded, not exported; no HTTP API yet (RB18).
- V2.48 implements RATE V0.5 part 2 and FIN's rated estimates: PricingBasis v1 of purposes SHIPMENT_ESTIMATE and CONSOL_COST_ESTIMATE (RATE candidate 11: the PricingBasis of RB15; FIN candidate 26: rating in FB2 and FB3; decisions R6 and N1-RATE OD-1 A, OD-2 A). Part 3 is the quote side.
  - RATE: `RateShipmentEstimate` (both sides, for a customer ORG confirms in a role a quote goes to) and `RateConsolCost` (the BUY side, the given carrier's cards or the lowest total) rate a shipment or a consolidation at the business time, record the rate snapshot and issue an immutable basis built from it in one transaction: per charge code the sell estimate, the cost estimate and the supplier whose card priced it (a side not rated is absent, never zero), and the gaps. The id is the caller's; a replay answers the same basis. `GetPricingBasis` reads a rated basis by `BasisID` (quote bases unchanged, their lines now marked QUOTE). Snapshot lines name the party of the card that priced them. Event `PricingBasisIssued.v1`.
  - FIN: `RateFreightJob` asks RATE to rate the job's shipment with FUL's CostDriver v1 route and cargo as planned (FIN's view of the cost driver now keeps them) at the service level given, and turns every rated line into a RATED charge line with a sell side for the customer and a cost side for the supplier, ORG confirming both, at the ESTIMATE rate. `RateConsolCostPool` rates the pool's consolidation for its carrier, lane and service level with the cargo of PHY's live houses and records RATED pool cost lines. A job is priced by its accepted quotes or by one rated estimate, a pool by one rated estimate (FIN_BASIS_APPLIED); a shipment RATE cannot rate, or without a planned route, is FIN_NOT_PRICEABLE. FIN derives the basis id and RATE's command id from its own command, so a retried command applies the basis RATE issued; retried after FUL's plan or PHY's consolidation changed, it is an idempotency conflict (a new command id rates the new facts). FIN checks that RATE answered for the plan version or consolidation revision it asked about. The charges RATE could not rate are listed by RATE's basis under the id the job and pool show. Job views show the basis purpose and plan version, pool lines their source and basis.
  - Migrations: `rate/000005_rate_pricing_basis` (bases, lines and gaps, immutable, under row-level security and the tenant guard; at commit a basis is issued in its snapshot's transaction from a snapshot of its purpose and the sides it rates, legal entity, customer and subject, and equals it line for line, every snapshot line a basis line or a gap and no gap the snapshot lacks, a cost always naming its creditor; the check also runs when the snapshot's lines are written, so making checks immediate cannot slip a line past it; snapshot lines gain the card's party, checked against the card); `fin/000009_fin_rated_estimates` (bases with a purpose and plan version, a job's live bases of one purpose and at most one estimate, RATED charge lines only from an estimate and QUOTE lines only from a quote, a basis line one charge line, RATED pool lines naming their basis line, one estimate per pool checked with the pool locked, a basis line applied once and a basis to one job or one pool; constraints added NOT VALID and validated after). CI grants: SELECT, INSERT on the basis tables.
  - Tests: unit tests of issuing a basis and of FIN's rated job and pool; PG16 with stand-ins: RATE rating a shipment and a consolidation into bases, the replay, the gaps, and writes past RATE refused (a basis changed, a line added, a basis unlike its snapshot); FIN rating a job and a pool, the request it sends, the replay, a second estimate, a quote on a rated job and an estimate on a quoted one refused, a customer as supplier, a shipment without a route and RATE refusing, and writes past FIN refused; the composition root with the real ORG, RATE and location reference data (FUL and PHY stand in).
  - An independent review found RATE's command-id conflict reaching FIN's caller as a retryable revision conflict, the basis check slipped past by making checks immediate, a pool's one-estimate rule without a lock, a NULL plan version passing, gaps, sides and creditors the basis check missed, and one basis line becoming several charge lines; each is fixed and tested.
  - Known limits: the service level, and for a pool the carrier and lane, are given with the command (FUL's cost driver and PHY's cost basis do not carry them); ONE_OFF quotes re-evaluated on actual quantities (OD-7) and AGREEMENT quotes come with part 3; a new estimate of a priced job is entered by revising its sides.
- V2.49 implements RATE V0.5 part 3a, quote lines entered by hand or rated (RATE candidate 11: RB13 and the rest of RB12; decisions R6, R2 option 4, N1-RATE OD-6 A). Part 3b brings AGREEMENT quotes, their customer cards, AGREEMENT_BELOW_FLOOR (OD-4), the re-evaluation of per-unit lines on the quantity shipped (OD-7) and COM's change (OD-5).
  - `CreateQuote` lines are MANUAL, RATED or RATED_OVERRIDDEN. A rated line names a RateSnapshot of purpose QUOTE_LINE recorded for the quote's legal entity, customer, scope and service level within the record window before the quote, and for no other subject than the quote; a snapshot's SELL line prices one line of a quote. RATE takes the line's sell, its cost when a BUY line of the code priced one (naming that line), and its basis from the snapshot. Only a RATED line from a PER_UNIT snapshot line that no minimum or maximum bounded is priced per unit (the unit, rate and charged quantity, whose product is the sell); a manual line, an overridden one and a bounded one are lump sums. An overridden line gives its sell in the rated currency, not below zero, and a reason; selling below the rated sell by more than the legal entity's threshold (an optional `DiscountPolicy` of the margin policy, 0 to 10000 basis points; none allowed without it) needs a price approval with reason RATE_CARD_DISCOUNT. A quote takes an optional scope (lane and cargo), and one with rated lines and no scope takes its snapshot's, as it takes the snapshot's service level when it names none. A snapshot that cannot be read fails the command with its cause; only one not recorded is a rejection.
  - New quote lines take listed, active codes of the catalogue applying to the scope's mode (RATE_CHARGE_CODE_UNLISTED otherwise, inactive codes included) and keep the catalogue version; codes of lines priced before the catalogue that it does not list are kept and marked unlisted. Tests now quote catalogue codes (AIR_FREIGHT for FREIGHT, DISCOUNT for LOYALTY_DISCOUNT). A command without the V0.5 line fields hashes as before, so commands recorded before the upgrade replay their receipts.
  - `GetQuoteSnapshotV3` (QuoteSnapshotV3Queries), from one read of the quote: QuoteSnapshotV2 with the kind (ONE_OFF), the scope and per line the source, the snapshot of a rated line, the basis, the catalogue version (none for a line priced before the catalogue) and the unlisted mark; no costs, and no rated sell or override reason (they stay in RATE). v2 and v1 unchanged.
  - Migration `rate/000006_rate_quote_lines`:
    - Quotes gain a kind, a scope and the threshold they were priced under; quote lines gain the source, snapshot lines, rated sell, override reason, basis, catalogue version and unlisted mark. Constraints are added NOT VALID and validated, and agree with RATE's own checks (bounds of the scope's cargo, a reason blank by Go's TrimSpace).
    - Old lines are marked tenant by tenant, so a migrator that does not bypass row-level security marks them correctly too.
    - At commit: a quote's lines and exchange rates are written in the transaction that created the quote, so none is added once it is priced, approved or accepted; a new line takes a code at a version the catalogue lists; a rated line equals its snapshot as rated (purpose, legal entity, customer, subject, mode, container mode, lane, carrier, cargo, service level, the SELL line and the basis it gives, and the BUY line whenever the snapshot priced one). A snapshot's SELL line prices one line of a quote (unique index).
    - A quote keeps what it was priced on: a trigger refuses any change but its status, approval, acceptance, expiry, revision and update time. CI grants UPDATE on `cargo_rate.quote` for those columns only, revoking the table-wide UPDATE first, as a deployment upgrading from V2.48 must too (grants only add).
  - Tests: unit tests of rated, overridden and manual lines, the basis, the threshold, an old quote's free-text code, the request hash of a command without the new fields, and a snapshot read failure. PG16: a quote rated from the tariff with a line overridden beyond the threshold, approved, accepted and read as QuoteSnapshotV3; overrides 4%, 5% and 6% below the rated sell under a 5% threshold; an override and a minimum as lump sums; a cost from the BUY side; every refusal by RATE (an unlisted or inactive code, another customer's snapshot, a shipment's snapshot, one snapshot line on two lines, a code the snapshot did not rate, an override without a reason, an unknown snapshot, a scope unlike the snapshot's) and by the database (17 rows unlike their snapshot or rules, lines and an exchange rate added after the quote, and the quote's pricing changed by the application role or the migrator).
  - Not yet: a MANUAL line of a code the quote's snapshot rated is not compared with the rated sell, so only BELOW_MARGIN_FLOOR (and DISCOUNT for a negative line) can hold it for approval; the scope keeps the cargo, and each per-unit line the quantity it was charged on, but not one chargeable quantity for the quote as the design lists. Both are open for part 3b.
  - An independent review found 2 major and 7 minor defects and some nits, all reproduced on PostgreSQL 16 and fixed before merge: lines could be added to a quote later, even an accepted one; a per-unit basis could disagree with the sell (overrides, minimums); the database's rated-line check covered only part of what RATE copies; the application role could change a quote's threshold, scope or customer; the old-line marking needed a migrator that bypasses row-level security; every CreateQuote request hash had changed; snapshot read failures were rejections; a snapshot line could price two lines, and a shipment's snapshot a quote; and the PG test never used a non-zero threshold.
- V2.50 implements RATE V0.5 part 3b-1, AGREEMENT quotes, and COM's orders on them (RATE candidate 11: RB16 and the rest of RB17; decisions R6, C1 option 3, N1-RATE OD-4 A, OD-5 A). Part 3b-2 brings the re-evaluation of a ONE_OFF quote's per-unit lines on the quantity shipped (OD-7).
  - `CreateQuote` of kind AGREEMENT has no lines: it names a DRAFT version of the customer's CUSTOMER SELL card valid exactly over the quote's validity, one entry per lane (as a CargoWise quotation carries one rate entry per trade lane), every entry of the scope's mode (the scope names the mode only) and publishable. Each lane is checked at a reference quantity (100 kg chargeable for AIR, 1 W/M for LCL) at the later of the quote's business time and the lane's start (a lane that no longer rates then is refused): its sell from the entry against the valid supplier cards, each card rated on its own (only the entry's carrier's when it names one) and the lowest total taken across currencies, every line converted at the quote's exchange rates. A lane below the margin floor, or without a buy rate rated in full, needs a price approval (AGREEMENT_BELOW_FLOOR). Naming a draft needs MaintainSellRates as well as CreateQuote. No lane of an agreement may meet, at an instant both rate, a lane another of the customer's CUSTOMER versions rates or another agreement that has not expired quotes (the same lane, service level and commodity, via and carrier equal where both name one; RATE_LANE_TAKEN), and no other version of the card may rate during it (RATE_VERSION_OVERLAP): rating would otherwise leave one card's rate unused.
  - While an AGREEMENT quote that has not expired names the draft, its entries cannot be set (RATE_VERSION_QUOTED), and no other agreement names it; once the quote expired, the draft changes and is quoted again.
  - `AcceptQuote` of an AGREEMENT publishes the version in the acceptance's transaction, by whoever recorded the acceptance, keeping the validity the customer accepted, and records `QuoteAgreementPublished.v1`; later shipments of the customer are rated with it (OD-1 A). The catalogue was checked when the agreement was priced, as at a publication, so a code deactivated since does not stop the acceptance; the customer's lanes are checked again. QuoteSnapshotV3 answers for an agreement with its kind, card version and mode, no lines and an empty total; QuoteSnapshot v1, v2 and a QUOTE_ACCEPTED basis refuse it.
  - COM reads QuoteSnapshotV3. A ONE_OFF order keeps the v2 contract and terms hash, its total and the policy's orders per quote; an AGREEMENT order relies on QuoteSnapshotV3 (a terms hash of the v2 encoding with the kind, card version and mode), has no total and is not counted (OD-5 A); the agreement's validity and the policy's grace bound it. A quote backs orders of one kind.
  - Migration `rate/000007_rate_agreements`: quote kind AGREEMENT with its card version; the scope of an agreement is its mode, never NULL; one agreement that has not expired per version; lane checks (written with the quote, immutable, row-level security and the tenant guard). As each row is written, at once: a quote line is of a ONE_OFF quote, a lane check of an AGREEMENT and an entry of its version. At commit, with the version row locked: an agreement names a DRAFT customer version of its legal entity and customer over its validity, every entry of its mode, has no lines and checks each entry once. A quoted draft's entries do not change. A CUSTOMER version is published only together with the acceptance of its agreement, and none of its lanes meets one another of the customer's versions rates then (publications for one customer take turns). CI grants: SELECT, INSERT on `quote_agreement_check`.
  - Migration `com/000010_com_agreement_orders`: orders record the quote's kind; an agreement order is under QuoteSnapshot.v3 without a total, a ONE_OFF order under v1 or v2 with one; only ONE_OFF orders are counted against orders per quote; a quote backs orders of one kind.
  - Tests: domain tests of the agreement version, the lane checks (lowest buy rate, a USD buy rate converted, the cheapest card across currencies, no buy rate, one not in full, a tariff that is no buy rate, a lane that has ended), the customer's other lanes, the floor at and around 20%, the publication and its event; COM's domain and translation tests; PG16: an agreement of two lanes awaiting approval (one without a buy rate), its draft fixed, approved, accepted, published, read as QuoteSnapshotV3 and rating the customer's shipments, an agreement above the floor, an expired agreement's draft changed, a lane already rated or quoted for the customer, a version of the card rating during the agreement, an actor without MaintainSellRates, an acceptance after a code was deactivated, ten other refusals by RATE and thirteen by the database (among them SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE, an agreement without its mode, a sea agreement over an air draft, and an accepted agreement on a lane taken); a race of an agreement and an entry change (both outcomes, each consistent); COM's agreement orders beyond one per quote and five refusals by the database; the real ORG, RATE, COM and FIN together.
  - Not yet: an AGREEMENT quote cannot be withdrawn or declined before its validity ends, so its draft stays fixed until then (the design has no such command); the database does not recompute a lane check's amounts, which RATE computes (as it does not recompute a ONE_OFF quote's margin).
  - An independent review found 1 major and 8 minor defects and some nits, reproduced on PostgreSQL 16 and fixed before merge: two accepted agreements of a customer could rate one lane, leaving one unused; a quoted draft could change after its agreement committed, through the database; SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE put off the agreement's consistency; an agreement without a mode, or of another mode than its entries, passed the database; a version of the card rating during the agreement, or a code deactivated since, was found only at acceptance and froze the card; the cheapest buy card in another currency was passed over; QuoteAgreementPublished.v1 was not recorded; any quoter could fix a customer's draft; a lane that had ended was checked; and a command naming ONE_OFF hashed unlike one leaving it out.
- V2.51 implements RATE V0.5 part 3b-2: an accepted ONE_OFF quote re-evaluated on the cargo a shipment carries (RATE candidate 11; decision N1-RATE OD-7 A). RATE V0.5 is complete with it, apart from the HTTP API (RB18).
  - `GetPricingBasis` of an accepted ONE_OFF quote takes an optional `Shipment` (shipment, plan version, cargo). RATE then re-evaluates the quote's RATED lines on the cargo the shipment carries by the card lines they were rated from, which never change once published: a per-unit line is rated again by its card line (its rate and weight breaks, its quantity rule — unit, chargeable weight at its volume factor, step, minimum quantity — and its minimum and maximum), as RATE rated it; a percentage line (such as a fuel surcharge) again on its re-evaluated base from the same snapshot; a cost from a per-unit or percentage BUY line likewise. On the cargo quoted each line is the quoted line. Flat, manual and overridden lines, lines in documents (a shipment's cost driver reports none) and lines whose unit the cargo lacks are as quoted. The basis is `QUOTE:<quote>@<revision>/<shipment>@<plan version>` and names the shipment as its subject; its lines say which were re-evaluated, in what unit and on what quantity. A shipment id with blanks, `/` or `@`, cargo outside RATE's bounds, or a basis id with a shipment are refused; a snapshot or card line RATE cannot read back is an internal failure.
  - FIN: `ApplyPricingBasis` on a shipment whose order parts are all under that quote's revision asks RATE for the quote on the shipment's planned cargo (pounds and cubic feet converted exactly), and the job's basis names the plan version; such a shipment is priced only once FUL planned it. A shipment shared with other quotes, or measured in a unit RATE cannot take, keeps the quoted amounts. `RateFreightJob` refuses a shipment that carries an order under an accepted ONE_OFF quote (the job is priced by the quote) and still rates the cards for shipments without a quote or under an agreement. The design names this the SHIPMENT_ESTIMATE path; it is delivered on the quote's basis because a SHIPMENT_ESTIMATE basis equals a rate snapshot line for line, while a quote's lines are not card evaluations, and the job keeps its lines tied to the accepted quote revision.
  - Migration `fin/000010_fin_quote_reevaluation`: a job's QUOTE_ACCEPTED basis may name a plan version.
  - Not yet: a job priced on a plan version keeps it when FUL plans the shipment again (its lines are revised by hand, as before); the cargo is FUL's planned scope, not the weights measured at receipt; RATE does not store a re-evaluation, so it is read again with the shipment's cargo, not by its basis id alone; FIN's quote lines keep their amounts, not the quantity they were re-evaluated on, and a re-evaluated BUY cost names no creditor (as quote lines never did).
  - Tests: domain tests of the re-evaluation (on the cargo quoted for 150 kg, 90 kg charged at the 100 kg break, 5 kg at the minimum, 10 m3 by volume and 2 t, every line the quoted line; heavier, lighter in another break and under the minimum, the fuel surcharge following its freight up to its maximum; no weight or volume shipped, documents, a quote not accepted, cargo out of bounds); PG16: a quote rated from the tariff and a supplier card, accepted, read as quoted, on its own cargo (the quote itself) and on 1,500 kg (its fuel surcharge 10% of the freight re-evaluated), and four refusals; FIN: a job's quote basis on a shipment of one quote and on one shared with another quote, a shipment planned again, one in pounds and one in ounces, and an estimate refused for a shipment under an accepted quote; the real ORG, RATE and FIN: a quote for 100 kg priced on the 300 kg shipped.
  - An independent review found 5 major and 3 minor defects and some nits, fixed before merge: the re-evaluation did not give the quote back on its own cargo (a cheaper break) and ignored the card line's minimum and maximum; a percentage line stayed as quoted while its base moved; `RateFreightJob` could price a shipment under an accepted quote at current cards; a quote applied while its shipment was a draft stayed as quoted; a shipment weighed in pounds was refused; lookup failures were rejections; shipment ids, documents and a basis id with a shipment were not checked.
- V2.52 implements FIN V0.7, fapiao records (FIN candidate 26: backlog FB6, entered by hand from the platform's evidence; the platform adapter comes later). FIN asks for a fapiao on issued debit notes and keeps what the national e-invoice service platform answered, as an authorized operator records it; FIN does not call the platform and is not the statutory tax record (the platform and the ERP are). CargoWise sends eligible invoices to RongJin's Tax Easy portal as Pending and issues the fully digital e-fapiao from there (the archive's comparison, public material); a record covering several debit notes of one debtor follows the design (monthly billing is common in China; no public CargoWise material on it).
  - `RequestFapiao` covers 1 to 200 ISSUED debit notes, each named once, of one legal entity to one debtor that ORG confirms in a debtor role, with a CN_ENTERPRISE billing profile whose current version still names it as ORG does (FIN_BILLING_PROFILE_STALE); the record freezes that version's buyer data (title, taxpayer number, address, phone, bank and account). It is ORDINARY or SPECIAL, in CNY, the legal entity's functional currency. Each note puts on the fapiao its lines not reversed by credit notes per tax treatment and rate (a line in another currency at its CNY amount), leaving its pass-through disbursements off; the record states a line per treatment and rate with the VAT a taxable amount includes (gross × rate / (1 + rate), rounded half up once per rate). Refused (FIN_NOT_FAPIAOABLE): a note with nothing but disbursements, a SPECIAL fapiao with exempt international freight forwarding, a treatment below zero, notes of another legal entity or debtor, a foreign buyer, another functional currency.
  - A debit note is covered by one REQUESTED or ISSUED record at a time (FIN_FAPIAO_COVERS; the design asks at most one ISSUED, a second request is refused too), and no credit note reverses a line of it the fapiao states while it is: the red-letter fapiao comes first. Its disbursements, not on the fapiao, may be credited meanwhile. A request retried while the first is still committing hears ErrConflict and replays its receipt when sent again.
  - `RecordFapiaoIssued` records the platform's number (the 20 digits of a fully digital e-invoice, the platform's only kind since 2024-12-01), date (not before the notes, not after the day it is recorded, not more than 366 days before) and evidence reference; `RecordFapiaoFailed` the platform's refusal or the operator abandoning it, with a reason, and the notes are covered no longer; `RecordRedLetter` the red-letter fapiao of an ISSUED record with its own number, dated not before it, the reference of its red-letter information confirmation form (filled in for every fully digital red-letter fapiao) and a reason; credit notes may then reverse the notes and a new record cover what they still bill. The platform's numbers are unique nationwide, so a tenant records a number once, issued or red-letter alike (FIN_FAPIAO_NUMBER_USED). Events `FapiaoIssued.v1` and `FapiaoRedLettered.v1` (identifiers only). Queries: `GetFapiaoRecord`, `ListFapiaoRecords` (a legal entity and debtor, FIN_INVALID_QUERY otherwise).
  - Lock order: a request locks the notes' jobs in job id order before it reads them, as job commands lock a job, so requests and credit notes on a note take turns; the coverage of other records is answered only for notes of the request's legal entity and debtor.
  - Migration `fin/000011_fin_fapiao`: `fapiao_record` (the issue, failure and red letter are added once each, in order; nothing it was requested on changes; its billing profile version a foreign key), `fapiao_covered_note` (only released, once its record failed or was red-lettered; one live record per note by a partial unique index), `fapiao_covered_amount` (no disbursements) and `fapiao_number` (immutable, one number per tenant), under row-level security, never deleted, written at READ COMMITTED only; command receipts of kind `FapiaoRecord`. At commit the database checks that a record is requested on the debtor's latest billing profile version, which it equals, in the policy's functional currency, with totals and VAT those of its covered amounts; that each covered note is an ISSUED debit note of its legal entity and debtor putting exactly what its lines not reversed bill, disbursements left off, and something, on the fapiao (locking the note's job first); that an issued record is dated on or after its notes, and a moved record recorded its number with the move and released its notes; and that no credit note line reverses a line on the fapiao of a covered note. A commit check lost to a concurrent change is a conflict. CI grants add only the record's issue, failure and red-letter columns and the covered note's release.
  - Not yet: the platform adapter (requests are recorded, not sent); CN_INDIVIDUAL buyers and delivery contacts (with the privacy controls); fapiao for overseas debtors and the treatment of disbursements, which wait for the tax adviser, as does which CNY amount (the note's conversion) a foreign-currency line goes on the fapiao at; the platform's own amounts (it rounds by item, the record states FIN's lines and keeps the platform's number, date and evidence); fapiao issued before 2024-12-01 with an invoice code; a record of a debtor whose roles ended; incoming fapiao of creditors (kept by the ERP); postings (FB7).
  - Tests: domain tests of requests (the lines, VAT rounded half up per rate, disbursements left off and a note of disbursements only refused, each refusal, amounts over the largest), the issue, failure and red letter with their refusals (numbers not of 20 digits, no confirmation form) and stored records that do not hold together; PG16 with stand-ins: the cycle on party-a's notes (exempt freight, 6% and a USD line at 9%) with every refusal (a special fapiao on exempt freight, another debtor's note, a foreign debtor, a disbursement only alone and beside another note, an unknown note, no notes, a note named twice, a stale profile), a covered note neither covered again nor credited until red-lettered while its disbursement is credited, the replay, a reused command id and the record requested by another command, a fapiao dated before its notes, numbers recorded once, a red letter without its form, a failed record, a note credited in full, the outbox, and what the database refuses past FIN (a buyer rewritten, a record deleted, a failed record issued, a note covered again, an amount rewritten, a note or number added past its move, totals or amounts not the notes', a disbursement stored, a record issued before its notes, without its number or with a number recorded already, failed without releasing its notes, a covered note credited, a record on a superseded profile version); races of two requests on one note, of a request and a credit note reversing the note in full, each won by both sides, and of one request sent twice at once.
  - An independent review found 1 major and 3 minor defects and some nits, reproduced on PostgreSQL 16 where it could and fixed before merge: a note of disbursements only was covered, so crediting a disbursement, which is not on the fapiao, needed the whole fapiao red-lettered; a request retried while the first was committing was refused as covered by its own record; the database did not check an issued date against the notes or that the profile version was the latest; and 8-digit numbers, unique only with their invoice code, were taken as unique per legal entity.
- V2.53 adds the HTTP API's foundation and RATE's charge-code catalogue on it (backlog RB18, first part; repository structure baseline V1.1: `cmd/cargo-api`, `internal/transport/http`, `RegisterHTTP`, inbound adapters in `internal/adapters/http`; contract conventions of Engineering Contracts V2.0 §05). The rate card, rating and quote routes come next.
  - `cmd/cargo-api` serves `/v1` through the composition root (entry points import only `internal/bootstrap`), with server timeouts and a graceful shutdown on SIGTERM that cancels the requests still running when `-shutdown-timeout` ends (a second signal ends the process). It starts only with `-dev-tokens`; `-mint-token` prints a development token.
  - Authentication: a `Verifier` port turns the bearer token into the principal (tenant, actor, permissions); the HS256 verifier (standard library only) is for development and tests, until the IDP identity platform's issuer and keys are known. It reads a token strictly: canonical base64 without blanks, one object of known keys, each once and in its case. Authorization: each context's authorizer port is answered from the principal's permissions, which must be the tenant and actor the command names; queries need `<context>:Read`.
  - `internal/transport/http`: a correlation id per request, panic recovery as a 500 problem (a connection aborted when the response had begun), an access log of ids only (never bodies, tokens or query strings), 401 without a valid token, and problems for routes or methods that do not exist. `internal/foundation/httpapi`: problems from a context's error kinds (rule C5) with the business code a sentinel error of the context names (never one quoted from what the caller sent), PostgreSQL's own messages kept from callers, strict JSON bodies (one object, snake_case keys unique in each object, no unknown fields, 1 MiB), `Idempotency-Key` as the command id, `If-Match` as the expected version, `ETag`, and 503 `COMMIT_UNCERTAIN` when Bento cannot confirm a commit.
  - RATE: `GET /v1/charge-codes` (`include_inactive`), `GET /v1/charge-codes/{code}` (ETag its version), `POST /v1/charge-codes` (201, Location), `POST /v1/charge-codes/{code}/revise|deactivate|reactivate` (If-Match the version; 0 for a starter code).
  - A command's body states `occurred_at`: a time the server filled in would differ on each attempt, and a retry with the same `Idempotency-Key` would be refused as another command. RATE holds the command id for the transaction (an advisory lock), so the same command sent twice at once commits once and both answer its receipt; a charge-code command is authorized before its replay is answered.
  - RATE's margin policy for the API is one floor and one rate-card discount for every legal entity, from flags (`-margin-floor-bp`, default 2000; `-rate-card-discount-bp`, default 0), until the legal entities' commercial policy is configurable.
  - Not yet: the rate card, rating, pricing basis and quote routes (next); the other contexts' routes, each with the same command-id lock and authorization before replay; the IDP identity platform and policy service; rate limits (429); the OpenAPI contract in `contracts/` (rule C2) and its publication, which wait for the owner; masking and export controls (P7).
  - Tests: unit tests of the verifier (issuer, audience, expiry and leeway, lifetime, `alg`, a critical header, another key, altered claims, malformed tenants and permissions), of permission checks, of the server (tokens, health, correlation ids, problems for routes and methods, panics, the log) and of the helpers (each error kind, business codes, commit uncertainty, media types, unknown fields, two objects, body size, If-Match and Idempotency-Key forms); `cmd/cargo-api`'s configuration and tokens; and on PG16 the catalogue through the API end to end: reads and permissions, a define replayed and its key reused, a code defined twice, a bad code, no business time or one outside the record window, a stale or missing If-Match, deactivation and reactivation, a starter code revised, tenant isolation and a token of another key; eight identical defines and revisions at once (one commit, eight equal receipts; without the lock seven heard 409), a replay without the permission, and a used key sent by an actor not allowed.
  - An independent review found 1 major and 6 minor defects and some nits, reproduced where it could and fixed before merge: the same command sent twice at once answered 409 instead of its receipt; a replay was answered before authorization, and a used key told another actor it existed; a business code could be quoted from the request; a problem could carry PostgreSQL's message; a panic after a partial write sent a short response that looked complete, without an access-log line; the token parser took non-canonical base64, data after the claims and case-folded or repeated keys; and a shutdown could outlast its timeout. The nits (case-folded and repeated body keys, 413 after the first object, Go type names in details, `If-Match` forms, `include_inactive` values, the opt-in for development tokens) are fixed too.
- V2.54 adds RATE's rate cards and rating to the HTTP API (backlog RB18, second part). Quotes and pricing bases come next.
  - Routes: `GET /v1/rate-cards` (`side`, `scope`, `legal_entity_id`, `party_id`, `valid_at`), `POST /v1/rate-cards` (201, Location), `GET /v1/rate-cards/{card}` (ETag the card's revision), `POST /v1/rate-cards/{card}/versions` (a DRAFT, `copy_from` optional), `GET /v1/rate-cards/{card}/versions/{n}` (with its entries), `PUT /v1/rate-cards/{card}/versions/{n}/entries` (replaces a DRAFT's entries; entries and lines are numbered in the order sent, locations resolved and their edition named), `POST .../publish`, `POST .../withdraw` (a reason), `POST /v1/rate-evaluations` (201, the snapshot's Location) and `GET /v1/rate-snapshots/{id}`. A command on a card names the card's revision in If-Match (a version's GET carries it as ETag). Money is `{"currency","minor"}`; a gap line has no amount (what RATE cannot rate is never zero). A version the card does not have is 404.
