  - Supplier costs stay with those who need them (costs and margins stay in RATE): `rate:Read` shows SELL cards and the SELL side of snapshots; BUY cards and the BUY lines and candidates of snapshots need `rate:ReadBuyRates` (or `rate:MaintainBuyRates`). CargoWise grants cost visibility apart from sell visibility too.
  - RATE authorizes every card and rating command before it answers a replay: a change reads the card outside its transaction to know its side (MaintainSellRates or MaintainBuyRates), publishing a tariff asks ApproveTariff first, and ApproveOwnTariff when the actor prepared the version (both asked again inside the transaction), rating asks EvaluateRates.
  - Not yet: quotes and pricing bases over HTTP (next); the other contexts' routes; paging of the card list; a draft's Location names the card, whose versions list the new one (the receipt keeps no version number); who may withdraw a tariff the manager approved (anyone maintaining SELL rates may, as before) is for the owner; what V2.53 lists.
  - Tests: on PG16 with the real ORG and location data, through the API: a tariff and a co-loader's card created, drafted, filled (CNSHA resolved to CNSHG for SEA, lines numbered) and published, each by the permission its side and step need; a tariff refused to its preparer without approval, its publication replayed by the manager and refused to one who may not approve; entries of a published version refused; versions `0`, `01` and `x` not found; cards listed by side and a bad `valid_at`; an evaluation without permission refused and one rated into a snapshot of four lines (2.5 W/M); a version withdrawn; If-Match missing (428) or stale (409); a BUY card drafted by a SELL maintainer; entries of a version the card does not have (404); a create and an evaluation replayed by one who lost the permission; a tariff self-approved with ApproveOwnTariff, flagged, and its replay refused once that permission is withdrawn; rate breaks; cards and snapshots read with `rate:Read` only (SELL only, BUY cards refused), by an auditor with `rate:ReadBuyRates` and without `rate:Read`; unknown and other tenants' cards and snapshots (404); gap lines without amounts.
  - An independent review found 1 major and 4 minor defects and some nits, reproduced and fixed before merge: supplier costs (BUY cards, BUY snapshot lines and candidates) were readable with `rate:Read`; a replayed self-approved tariff publication skipped ApproveOwnTariff; gap lines showed a zero amount; a version's GET had no ETag to send back; a version the card does not have answered 422 on a command and 404 on a read. The list filters are validated and an absent customer is left out.
- V2.55 adds RATE's quotes to the HTTP API (backlog RB18, third part). With it RATE V0.5 is complete over HTTP; pricing bases stay in-process, FIN's.
  - Routes: `POST /v1/quotes` (201, Location; a ONE_OFF quote of MANUAL, RATED or RATED_OVERRIDDEN lines, or an AGREEMENT naming a customer card version), `GET /v1/quotes/{id}` (the quote as it stands, ETag its revision), `POST /v1/quotes/{id}/approve-price`, `/accept` and `/expire` (If-Match the revision), `GET /v1/quotes/{id}/snapshot` (QuoteSnapshotV3 of an accepted quote, no costs or margin) and `GET /v1/service-levels`.
  - RATE gains `GetQuote`: a quote in any status with its lines, costs, totals in the quote currency, approval reasons, approval and acceptance, for RATE's own users (other contexts keep reading snapshots). Over HTTP the cost total and the margin go to who may see supplier costs (`rate:ReadBuyRates`, `rate:MaintainBuyRates`) or approves prices (`rate:ApproveQuotePrice`, who decides on the margin); line costs, which can be a supplier's exact rate, to the former only; the creator of a quote reads it without them. Legacy quotes show their unlisted codes and service level as such.
  - Supplier costs stay out of what may be created without seeing them: rating the BUY side (`POST /v1/rate-evaluations` with BUY) and creating a quote whose rated lines name a snapshot that rated the BUY side need `rate:ReadBuyRates` (or `rate:MaintainBuyRates`). Otherwise whether such a quote needs approval would give its creator the costs, one quote at a time. Sales without it quote from SELL evaluations: their rated lines carry no cost, and the rate-card discount threshold, not the margin floor, holds them for approval.
  - The quote commands hold the command id for their transaction (the same command sent twice at once commits once) and are authorized before a replay is answered; a quote's author approving it without ApproveOwnQuotePrice is refused up front as unauthorized (403), a replay of an earlier self-approval too.
  - Not yet: lists of quotes (by customer, status); an AGREEMENT's lane checks in the read model, and AGREEMENT quotes over HTTP beyond what RATE checks (one who maintains SELL rates could probe its lane checks against buy rates, at the cost of a new DRAFT version each time); pricing bases over HTTP (FIN reads them in-process); what V2.53 and V2.54 list.
  - Tests: on PG16 with the real ORG through the API: the service levels; a quote refused to who may not create and with a tenant in its body; the same create sent eight times at once (one commit, eight equal receipts; without the lock, 409); the creator's view without costs and the approver's with a 10% margin below the 20% floor; a snapshot refused before acceptance; the author's approval refused without ApproveOwnQuotePrice; an approval refused to who may not, made, replayed and refused once the permission is withdrawn; an acceptance at a stale revision (409) and accepted; QuoteSnapshotV3 without costs or margin; a quote expired after its validity and the expiry replayed without the permission refused; unknown and other tenants' quotes (404) and reads without `rate:Read`; the approver's view with the margin but no line costs, an analyst's and a buyer's with line costs; an approval without If-Match (428); a snapshot of an unknown quote (404); a self-approval with ApproveOwnQuotePrice, flagged, and its replay refused once that permission is withdrawn; the BUY side rated by sales refused and by an analyst recorded; a quote taking costs from that snapshot refused to sales.
  - An independent review found 1 major and 3 minor defects and nits, reproduced and fixed before merge: a sales user could find a supplier's exact cost by creating quotes from a BUY snapshot and reading whether each needed approval (16 quotes in a binary search); approvers saw line costs the card routes denied them; a replayed self-approval by a token that lost ApproveOwnQuotePrice answered "the author may not approve" although the approval had happened; legacy quotes' unlisted codes and service level looked listed. The margin in basis points is computed without overflow.
- V2.56 starts the sea freight line with the container mode (decision N1-PHY OD-9, option A, of 2026-11-10: requested on the COM order with the transport mode, like C11, and planned in FUL, so that an LCL consolidation can refuse an FCL house).
  - COM: an order's transport version carries an optional container mode: LCL or FCL on SEA, LOOSE on AIR (RATE's term), none on ROAD or RAIL yet, and none without a transport mode (`RecordCustomerOrder`, `ChangeOrderTransport` with a reason, refused while DOC holds the parties locked). OrderParties v1 and ObligationSnapshotV3 (`RequestedContainerMode`) report it. The design gives no values beyond "LCL or FCL" for SEA; CargoWise records a container mode on the shipment at registration (Logixboard's CargoWise One mapping guide, moderate), its own code list was not found in public sources.
  - FUL: a plan's route carries the container mode (`AmendShipmentPlan`, of its transport mode; it may change alone), checked against what the orders request as the mode is (C11): once the route names its transport mode, an order that requests a container mode needs the plan to plan that one, so adding a part, amending and confirming refuse another container mode or none (FUL_MODE_MISMATCH), and an amendment cannot drop it. LoadIntent v1 and ShipmentPlanSnapshotV2 carry it (additive).
  - PHY: an LCL consolidation (SEA_LCL_OWN, SEA_LCL_COLOAD) refuses a shipment FUL plans as FCL (PHY_FCL_HOUSE); one whose container mode is not planned is taken, as before.
  - Migrations `com/000011_com_container_mode` and `ful/000008_ful_container_mode`: a column of the transport version and of the plan version, checked against the transport mode, a missing one included (NOT VALID, then validated); rows written before request and plan none, nothing is converted. Commands recorded before it hash as they did (the field is left out when empty).
  - Not yet: an order whose container mode changes after FUL confirmed its plan keeps the plan's until FUL amends it (as the transport mode, C11, is checked at adding, amending and confirming only); FCL fulfilment (containers, VGM) and an FCL job kind in FIN, which still takes every SEA job as SEA_EXPORT_LCL_HOUSE (CostDriver v1 does not carry the container mode yet); a plan confirmed without a container mode; the container mode of a quote's scope against the order's; ROAD and RAIL modes (FTL, LTL); a shipper-built ULD on AIR. Reference data for container size-types and ULD types (PB8) comes with PHY's load units.
  - Tests: domain tests of the order's container mode (FCL on AIR, LOOSE on SEA, none without a mode, ROAD, unknown and lower-case values refused; LCL, FCL, LOOSE recorded), of FUL's (LOOSE on SEA refused, an FCL order into an LCL plan refused, an LCL order and one requesting none taken, fresh references checked, a container mode without a mode refused, kept by later plan versions) and of PHY's attach (FCL refused in an LCL consolidation, LCL and none taken); on PG16 with the real COM, FUL and PHY: an LCL and an FCL order planned as SEA shipments, ObligationSnapshotV3 and OrderParties v1, LoadIntent v1 of each, an FCL or LOOSE plan for the LCL order refused (the mismatch itself asserted), a plan dropping the FCL its order requests refused, a plan of an order requesting none given LCL alone, FCL on an AIR order refused by COM and LOOSE taken, the database refusing FCL on AIR and a container mode without a transport mode on both tables, and the LCL consolidation taking the LCL house and refusing the FCL one; domain tests of co-load consolidations too, and of request hashes unchanged without a container mode.
  - An independent review found 2 major and 3 minor defects and nits, reproduced on PostgreSQL 16 and fixed before merge: a plan's container mode could not change alone (the route comparison left it out), so the test's mismatch case passed for the wrong reason; a SEA plan without a container mode let an FCL order through to an LCL consolidation; the database checks passed rows without a transport mode; and tests were missing. An order changed after confirmation is documented above.
- V2.57 is sea freight part 1b-i: PHY V0.3 plans load units on consolidations, against new equipment reference data (backlog PB8).
  - Reference data: `cargo_ref.equipment_edition` and `cargo_ref.container_size_type` hold ISO 6346 size-types with their TEU and default maximum gross, tare and internal capacity, kept by edition as the location data is (F2, MD-26). Edition EQUIPMENT-2026-11-S1 loads 22G1, 42G1 and 45G1. The limits are defaults: a container's own tare and maximum gross are on its CSC plate, which SEA records when it assigns the container. `internal/reference/equipment` resolves a size-type against the current edition.
  - PHY: `PlanLoadUnit` plans a CONTAINER of a size-type on an OPEN or CLOSED SEA_LCL_OWN consolidation. PHY resolves the type outside its transaction and freezes its TEU and limits with the edition; the receipt's ItemNo is the unit's number, counted from 1. `RemovePlannedLoadUnit` removes a planned unit with a reason; the unit keeps its number. A co-load consolidation (SEA_LCL_COLOAD) has no units, its stuffing being the co-loader's; an AIR consolidation's ULD and loose units come with ULD build-up (PHY_LOAD_UNIT_KIND). An unknown size-type is refused (PHY_EQUIPMENT_UNKNOWN). Planning or removing a unit is a change of the consolidation: nothing later, a unit or a version (amend, close, reopen, cancel, reconcile), may be dated before it. Units are planned or removed; whether one is in use or closed will be a fact of its load executions.
  - Published: the consolidation view and PhysicalCostBasis v1 (the live units, additive) carry the units; LoadUnitPlanned.v1 and LoadUnitRemoved.v1 carry the unit as the item and no free text.
  - Migrations `ref/000003_ref_equipment`, `ref/000004_ref_equipment_s1` and `phy/000003_phy_load_units`: the unit table, whose trigger checks that a unit follows the previous one, on an own-container LCL consolidation that is OPEN or CLOSED, with the limits its type has in the edition named (a foreign key to the reference row too, so a referenced edition can never be removed, as no edition is), dated not before the consolidation's latest version or unit change, and that only its removal is written later, once; a second trigger dates a consolidation's versions not before its units' planning and removal. The application role may insert units and update the removal columns only. Receipts of consolidation commands may name an item. The S1 maximum gross is 30,480 kg, the long-standing ISO 668 rating; later amendments allow 36,000 kg, so the plate governs, and S1 is to be verified against carrier data before the pilot.
  - Not yet: stuffing into a unit, an overweight hold against the unit's limits, the seal as closing evidence and the envelope check at close (part 1b-ii); refusing the removal of a unit with cargo in it, which comes with stuffing; ULD types and build-up; container numbers, seals and plate tare (SEA); TEU apportionment in FIN.
  - Tests: domain tests of planning (a container on own-container LCL, OPEN and CLOSED; refused on a co-load, on AIR, as ULD or no kind, with invalid limits, without an actor, before the current version, and once cancelled), of removal (a reason, a planned unit, not before its planning, once, and a unit planned and removed together), of nothing (cancel, close, amend, another unit, a removal) dated before a unit's planning or removal, and of restoring stored units; on PG16: planning, replay and a reused command id, a stale revision, unknown and malformed size-types, a co-load, AIR, ULD, an intruder, an unknown consolidation (whatever the type) and a plan dated before the latest change refused, a stand-in answering a wrong tare refused by the database, removal and its replay, the view and the cost basis, the events, a cancel dated before a removal refused, and nothing planned or removed once cancelled; a unit planned and removed in one transaction written through the store; a committed plan replayed by a module without the equipment data; the database rules (limits and type fixed, no delete, a removal needs its reason and is never undone, the next unit only, the reference limits and TEU, an unknown edition, no units on a co-load or a cancelled consolidation, a unit, a removal or a version dated before the latest change, the trigger refusing rewrites and deletes even from the migrator, tenant isolation); the S1 size-types resolved and unknown ones refused; and, with the real reference data, a 45G1 and a 22G1 planned on a consolidation whose route was resolved through the CNSHA alias.
  - An independent review found no blocker or major defect, and 2 minor defects and 4 nits, reproduced on PostgreSQL 16 and fixed before merge: a unit planned and removed in one transaction was refused by the database (no command did this yet); a cancel or reconcile could be dated before a unit's planning or removal; the trigger's immutability branches were never tested past the grants; a replay on a module without equipment answered a missing dependency; an unknown consolidation with an unknown type answered "rejected"; and the S1 note called 30,480 kg the ISO 668 rating without its later amendments.
- V2.58 is sea freight part 1b-ii: PHY stuffs the planned containers of own-container LCL consolidations (PHY V0.3).
  - A load execution of kind STUFFING (`OpenLoadExecution` with `LoadUnitNo`; the receipt's ItemNo is the unit) fills one planned container of a CLOSED SEA_LCL_OWN consolidation, its houses frozen, at the forwarder's CFS at the origin, dated not before the close; a unit takes one stuffing (PHY_LOAD_UNIT_IN_USE, also when two are opened at once), and a co-load or AIR consolidation is not stuffed (PHY_NOT_STUFFED). Lines come, and the container is sealed, while the consolidation is CLOSED (PHY_HOUSES_NOT_FROZEN) and dated not before its latest version: a reopen, dated not before the stuffing's last change, pauses it until the house list is closed again, and nothing is dated inside the reopened period. Conservation and carrier evidence are as for tenders.
  - The container is checked against its type's limits as frozen with the unit: tare and live cargo against the default maximum gross, live cargo against the capacity, the cargo measured as PHY publishes it (lines measured for themselves as measured, apportioned lines from their lot's latest measurement; volumes not given are not counted; GRM, KGM, TNE and LBR, CMQ, DMQ, LTR, MTQ and FTQ converted exactly). The check runs after each stuffed line and when a lot with packs in a loading stuffing is weighed again (under the stuffing's lock, as a failed screening holds a tender; tenders with packs of the lot are not locked). Over the limits, or with a measure in a unit PHY does not convert, the line or measurement is recorded and the stuffing held with OVERWEIGHT (G06-05), its text giving the figures; the limits are defaults, so releasing the hold with its evidence, such as the CSC plate, lets the stuffing go on, and a later line or weighing over them holds it again.
  - A stuffing is sealed with the container's seal as evidence (source SEAL, the seal number as reference) and cargo in it; the seal's party and history stay SEA's. A sealed container is not unstuffed (a new seal comes later) (built in V2.85), and its consolidation does not reopen. A unit is IN_USE while its stuffing is open and CLOSED once sealed (derived, in the consolidation view and the cost basis); a unit with a stuffing is not removed. A lot weighed again after its container was sealed changes the measures PHY publishes; the container's verified gross mass is SEA's (VGM).
  - Published, additively: the execution view and PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 carry the load unit, and the opened event the unit as its item; FUL takes a sealed container's measures as tendered, its basis for documents (OD-2).
  - Migration `phy/000004_phy_stuffing`: the execution's kind STUFFING and unit (a foreign key to the planned unit, one per unit by a unique index, kept), the seal as a stuffing's closing evidence, the stuffing opened OPEN on a PLANNED unit of a CLOSED SEA_LCL_OWN consolidation and dated not before its latest change, its lines and its seal only while the consolidation is CLOSED and not dated before its latest version, no reversal of a sealed container, no reopen once a container is sealed nor dated before the stuffings' changes, and no removal of a unit with a stuffing. Existing executions are tenders and name no unit. The limits check is PHY's domain rule.
  - Not yet: the envelope of the planned units checked when the consolidation closes, with an overrun reason (part 1b-iii); the container's number, plate tare and maximum gross, the seal's party and the VGM (SEA, part 1e); unstuffing and resealing a container (built in V2.85); per-unit loaded measures in the cost basis and TEU apportionment in FIN.
  - Tests: domain tests of opening (a planned container of a closed own LCL; refused once taken, for an unknown or removed unit, elsewhere, before the unit was planned or the consolidation closed, with the houses not frozen, on a co-load or AIR, and through the tender constructor), of the limits (27,000 kg in a 22G1 taken; 30,000 kg recorded and held, the hold's text, no second hold, no seal while held, a reversal lowering the checked weight, the hold released with the plate, a later line held again, a tender receipt and a seal without number refused, the seal taken, no reversal and no reopen after it; the published measure after a weighing during stuffing; a weighing over the limits holding; measured and apportioned lines together; 33.200001 m3 over 33.2 m3; the unit conversions, ounces held as not checked), of the frozen houses (a reopen dated before the stuffing refused, no line and no seal after a reopen, nor dated in the reopened period once closed again), an empty container not sealed and restoring; on PG16: a stuffing opened (its receipt naming the unit) and replayed, a second one of the unit, no unit, an unknown unit, a tender of an own LCL and a tender naming a unit refused, the unit IN_USE and not removed, a 28,000 kg line over the default maximum gross held and released with the plate, a receipt refused and the seal taken, the unit CLOSED, the cost basis and PhysicalLoadEvidence v1, no reopen, the events; a lot weighed again holding the stuffing; a reopen pausing the stuffing in the domain and the database; a seal on an open house list, a line or seal dated in a reopened period, a reversal of a sealed container and a reopen after the seal (an emptied container too) refused in the domain and the database, a reopen dated before the stuffing refused in both; a lot weighed again while a tender with its packs is locked, not waiting for it; eight stuffings of one unit opened at once, one opened and the others refused as the unit's; a stuffing meeting the one-per-unit index while another commits, refused the same way; the database rules (one stuffing per unit, a stuffing's unit, no tender naming a unit or of an own LCL, a co-load, an unknown unit, an OPEN consolidation, a REMOVED unit, dated before the close, opened loading, the unit fixed even for the migrator, no removal of a unit in use, the seal); and, with the real COM, FUL and PHY and reference data, two LCL orders stuffed into a 22G1 and sealed, FUL taking the measures as tendered.
- V2.59 is sea freight part 1b-iii: the physical envelope at the house cut-off (PHY V0.3; design: CloseConsolidation needs "planned totals within the physical envelope or an accepted overrun reason").
  - With load units planned, `CloseConsolidation` checks the live houses' planned gross weight and volume against the live units' envelope: their default maximum gross less tare, and their capacity (volumes not planned are not counted; weights and volumes converted as for stuffing, LBR and FTQ included). A plan over it, or in a unit PHY does not convert, closes only with an `OverrunReason` (PHY_ENVELOPE_EXCEEDED, its text giving the totals), which the CLOSED version keeps as its reason; a plan within it, or a consolidation without units (AIR, co-loads, an own LCL without units), takes none. The reason records the plan against the units live at the close: a unit planned, or removed with its own removal reason, while the consolidation is CLOSED is not checked again until the next close (a product choice; the utilisation query will show the current fill). The ConsolidationClosed event carries no reason (free text, C3).
  - The command's hash leaves the reason out when empty, so closes recorded before replay as they did.
  - Migration `phy/000005_phy_envelope`: a CLOSED version may keep a reason; `cargo_phy.grams` and `cargo_phy.cubic_cm` convert as PHY does (rounded up); a trigger writes the reason exactly when the live houses' plan exceeds the live units' envelope at the close, under the consolidation's lock, after the version rules. Versions written before keep no reason on their close.
  - Not yet: the utilisation query of the design (planned, received and loaded totals per unit against its limits); per-unit loaded measures in the cost basis and TEU apportionment in FIN; SEA's container facts (part 1e).
  - Tests: domain tests of the close (within; one kilogram over refused; an accepted overrun kept on the version; over in volume; volumes not planned; pounds over and within; a unit not converted; a reason within the envelope or without units refused; a removed unit not counted; two units; a blank stored reason not restored); request hashes of closes and tender openings unchanged by the new fields; on PG16: 30,000 kg planned into a 22G1 refused without a reason with the totals in the text, closed with one, replayed and kept on the version; a reason within the envelope and without units refused, the plain close taken; and the database refusing a close over the envelope without a reason and a reason within it; and 25 plans put through the domain's close and the database trigger, which must both find each over or within as expected (kilograms and grams at the limit, pounds and cubic feet at their rounding boundaries, rounding per house, litres and cubic decimetres, volumes missing, ounces and gallons not converted, int64-sized amounts, two units, a removed unit, 42G1 and 45G1 together, no units).
  - An independent review found no blocker or major defect, and 2 minor findings and 2 nits, fixed before merge: the stuffing tests closed on empty scopes, which the application cannot (now the house's scope, with a reason when over); the trigger fired before the version rules (renamed to run after them); the parity of the Go and SQL conversions had no committed test (now the 25 cases above); and that a unit planned or removed while CLOSED is not checked again is now stated as the reason's meaning rather than as an invariant of stored data.
- V2.60 is sea freight part 1c: FUL publishes OceanOperationPlan v1 for SEA (relationship 10; decision N1-PHY OD-2, option A: membership, measures and stuffing reach SEA through FUL).
  - `GetOceanOperationPlan` answers a shipment's plan with its transport and container mode, parts (package types and goods descriptions), the consolidation FUL records it attached to with the PHY membership entry and revision, the execution status and, once FUL recorded PHY's evidence, the measures (TENDERED or RECEIVED, as for AIR) and the sealed containers of the attached consolidation with the shipment's packs in them: PHY's stuffing and planned unit with its ISO 6346 size-type, the shipment's packs, gross weight and volume in it as PHY measures them, PHY's load lines of the shipment with live packs in it (with the stuffing, the PHY evidence ids), and the seal PHY recorded (its number, the SHA-256 of its document when given, and when it was applied). Like AirOperationPlan v1 it answers any shipment with its mode, so that SEA can end the association of a house planned elsewhere. The container's number and plate data are SEA's (part 1e). It carries no names.
  - FUL keeps the sealed containers with every load evidence version it records from PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 (a stuffing still loading, of another consolidation, or without live packs of the shipment is not kept); they are part of the version's digest, and left out of it while there are none, so versions recorded before, and AIR shipments, keep their digests. Versions recorded before V2.60 carry no containers: recording the evidence again after the upgrade records a sealed container as a new version (only test data predates it, as with goods descriptions in V2.36). PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 now also gives a stuffing's unit size-type (additive), and PHY's per-house measures no longer depend on map order.
  - Migration `ful/000009_ful_stuffing_evidence`: the stuffing rows of a load evidence version, immutable, written with their version (checked at commit), the size-type, units, lines and seal digest checked, with row level security; the application role may select and insert them.
  - Not yet: SEA's ocean movement, house bills and container visits, which read this plan (parts 1d and 1e); container numbers, seals' parties and VGM (SEA); package types per container; FUL's FCL job kind for FIN.
  - Tests: domain tests of the stuffing evidence (a sealed container; refused without a consolidation, packs, unit, seal, time, execution, size-type, lines, with a line zero, a malformed seal digest or a negative weight) and of the digest (the V2.35 digest unchanged without stuffings, changed with one); the relay from PhysicalLoadEvidence v1 (a sealed container of the attached consolidation kept with its live lines, the seal's own time to the microsecond and its digest; a stuffing loading, one of another consolidation and one emptied not); on PG16: the plan before any evidence (container mode, consolidation, goods description), a shipment without a mode and one planned by AIR answered with their modes, an unknown shipment refused, the evidence of a sealed and a loading stuffing recorded and the sealed one relayed with its size-type, packs, weight, lines, seal, digest and time and the tendered measures, and the database keeping the rows (no update or delete, even for the migrator; none written apart from its version; the size-type and lines checked); PHY's evidence giving the size-type; and, with the real COM, FUL and PHY, the sealed 22G1 of two LCL orders in SH-L's OceanOperationPlan v1.
  - An independent review found no blocker or major defect, and 5 minor findings and 4 nits, fixed before merge: the seal's time relayed was the close's; a house re-planned by AIR could no longer be read by SEA (the plan now answers any mode); the PHY evidence ids and the seal's digest were dropped; a test meant to check the size-type did not; the immutability past the grants, an AIR shipment and the digest's time precision were untested or unhandled; and the upgrade of sealed SEA shipments is documented above. PHY's per-house weight unit no longer depends on map order.
- V2.61 is sea freight part 1d: SEA V0.1, the ocean movement of a sea LCL consolidation with its master bill reference and house bills (PHY tactical candidate V0.1, backlog PB6 without container visits, VGM and OceanDocFacts v1; decisions N1-PHY OD-2, OD-6, OD-8 and OD-10 of 2026-11-10). SEA is a new context beside AIR, built like AIR V0.1 (V2.33), and reads the OceanOperationPlan v1 of V2.60.
  - `OpenOceanMovement` opens the movement of a consolidation from its booking: BOOK's CarrierBooking v1 must report a SEA_LCL_CONSOL_EQUIPMENT booking (movement kind SEA_LCL_OWN, the shipping line in role CARRIER) or a SEA_LCL_CO_LOAD one (SEA_LCL_COLOAD, the co-loader in role CO_LOADER), in force with confirmed capacity, allocated to the consolidation, with one capacity line. The movement takes the carrier with its party revision as BOOK froze it from ORG, the operating legal entity, the port route (UN/LOCODEs and the edition) and the voyage legs (vessel, its IMO number when given, voyage, ports, the carrier's ETD and ETA) with their cut-offs (kind, instant, IANA zone, place) as BOOK recorded them. One open movement per consolidation. SEA does not ask PHY: the consolidation's kind is the kind of its booking. `LinkCarrierBooking` takes a newer capacity version of the same booking (a schedule change, or a roll to another vessel): the legs are replaced and the old ones kept as superseded; houses and the master bill stay. Another booking, carrier, route or legal entity is another movement.
  - `RecordMasterBill` records the master bill reference as the carrier or co-loader states it, with its evidence (SHA-256, storage id, media type, capture time): CARRIER_MBL or CARRIER_SEA_WAYBILL on an own container, COLOAD_BILL on a co-load; the issuer is the movement's carrier or co-loader. A further number or kind supersedes the current one, which stays readable; a number is current on one movement of the tenant per issuer; the current reference of a cancelled movement is VOIDED. Numbers are trimmed and written in capital letters. It is a reference, not the bill: issuing and releasing bills of lading stay with DOC.
  - `RegisterHblSeries`, `AssociateHouseBill`, `RefreshHouseBillFacts` and `EndHouseBillAssociation` follow AIR's houses. FUL's OceanOperationPlan v1 must report the shipment PLANNED by SEA and not as FCL (a plan without a container mode is taken, as PHY takes it), attached to the movement's consolidation (OD-2), on its route (OD-8) and of its legal entity (OD-10). The house takes the HBL number it holds under the legal entity, the one given, or the next of the entity's series (a numbering template of idp-platform-go, MD-28, which does not reset yet), drawn in the association's transaction; a number of the series that was given by hand before the sequence reached it is passed over, so that it cannot stop the series. A number belongs to one shipment for good and moves with it to another movement. Each entry freezes the packs, gross weight and volume (FUL's plan, or PHY's measures as FUL relays them: basis RECEIVED or TENDERED with FUL's evidence version), the parts with their package types and goods descriptions, and the per-container breakdown: the sealed containers of the consolidation with the house's packs in them as FUL relays them from PHY (the stuffing and its load lines, which are the PHY evidence ids; the unit and its ISO 6346 size-type; packs, gross weight and volume; the seal, its document's digest and when it was applied). A refresh writes a new entry with the same HBL for a newer plan version, other measures or other containers; the same again, or an older plan version or FUL revision than the entry's, is refused. An entry ends once FUL reports the shipment transferred, detached or cancelled.
  - Quantities stay in the units FUL relays. The movement states its total gross weight (GRM) and volume (CMQ) only while every live house's unit is one SEA converts (KGM, GRM, TNE; MTQ, DMQ, LTR, CMQ); houses without a volume add none. SEA documents no chargeable weight: freight tonnes are RATE's and FIN's. A house whose departure window no longer contains the first leg's ETD is flagged (WindowDrift), as in AIR.
  - `CancelOceanMovement` cancels an open movement without houses, with a reason. Queries: `GetOceanMovement`, `FindOceanMovementByMasterBill` (by issuer and number), `FindHouseBill`, `GetHblSeries`. Events carry identifiers only: OceanMovementOpened.v1, VoyageLegsLinked.v1, MasterBillRecorded.v1, HouseBillAssociated.v1, HouseBillFactsRefreshed.v1, HouseBillAssociationEnded.v1, OceanMovementCancelled.v1 and HblSeriesRegistered.v1. A change is dated not before the movement's last change, and within the record window (T1). A command is replayed by its id when its name and fields are the same; the request hash covers the command's name, so that a refresh and an end of one house under one id are not taken for each other (AIR's hash does not yet). The anticorruption layers keep a vessel's name without the spaces BOOK may hold around it and instants to the microsecond, as they are stored; take capacity allocated to a consolidation only; and answer BOOK's and FUL's refusals in SEA's own error kinds, never theirs.
  - Migration `sea/000001_sea_ocean_movement_house_bills` (schema `cargo_sea`; the manifest entry `sea numbering` creates the platform's sequence table first): the movement, booking links, voyage legs and cut-offs, master bill references, house bill entries with parts and containers, the HBL series and register, command receipts; row level security on every table. Links, legs, cut-offs, parts, containers, series and register rows are immutable; a movement changes only its state and cancellation, a master bill only leaves CURRENT, an entry is only ended, each once. Checked at commit: an entry is written to an open movement, with a part and its shipment's registered HBL; a link or a master bill reference is written to an open movement, so that nothing is added to a cancelled one; the movement is read under a share lock, which a cancellation waits for, so that an entry and a cancellation cannot pass each other; a cancelled movement keeps no live entry; booking links are of the movement's booking, numbered without gaps, each of a later capacity version and with a leg; master bill references are the movement's carrier's, of a kind of its kind, numbered without gaps, the latest CURRENT (VOIDED once cancelled) and the earlier ones SUPERSEDED; legs and cut-offs are accepted only from the transaction that wrote their link, parts and containers only from the one that wrote their entry (kept on the row, since ending an entry rewrites it). The sequence table is scoped by tenant, starts only for a registered series and moves one number at a time. The CI workflow grants the application role SELECT and INSERT on `cargo_sea` and UPDATE on the columns that change.
  - Values and their sources: the design gives no form for bill numbers. An HBL number is one to sixteen capital letters and digits: SEA's own bound, deliberately narrow and not yet checked against the manifest and filing formats (to verify before the pilot; a later migration can widen it, while numbers once given are kept for ever). A master bill number is capital letters and digits with single hyphens or slashes inside, 35 characters at most, the length BOOK keeps carrier references at. The voyage form and the IMO check digit are BOOK's.
  - Not yet: container visits with container numbers, seals' parties and VGM (part 1e); `FinalizeOceanDocFacts`, the state FINAL and OceanDocFacts v1 for DOC, with its guards (every house stuffed in the movement's containers, VGM before its cut-off); ocean carrier facts (loaded on board, departed, rolled, discharged); a booking split over several capacity lines; SEA's routes on the HTTP API; SEA does not follow FUL's or BOOK's events, the API client refreshes and relinks (T6); the NVOCC filing that an HBL issued in the forwarder's name needs (DOC's issuance authority); FCL; house ports beyond the master's (OD-8, option B).
  - Tests: domain tests of the number forms (HBL, master bill, IMO check digit), the booking checks (20 refusals, a transhipment of two legs), the link (the same version, another booking, carrier, legal entity, route or kind, the window drift after a retiming), the master bill references (kinds per movement kind, evidence, supersession, the same again, voided with the movement), the houses (7 refusals of FUL's report and 10 of its content, a plan without a container mode, an HBL or a house twice, refreshes for a sealed container, another seal and another plan version, the three ends, cancellation), the totals with a house in pounds and cubic feet, and 21 stored states a restore refuses, each for its reason; the two anticorruption layers (the fields SEA takes, a vessel's name trimmed, instants to the microsecond, another tenant's, booking's, shipment's or stage's answer, and each of BOOK's and FUL's error kinds answered in SEA's); module tests of the event payloads and ids, the published event types and vocabulary, the view's totals and the error kinds. On PG16 with stand-ins for BOOK and FUL: a movement opened, replayed and refused (unknown booking, another consolidation, an actor without the right, a business time outside the record window, a command id with another payload, a second movement, another tenant); houses with a drawn and a given HBL; a shipment FUL does not know; an FCL house; master bill references recorded, normalized, found by issuer and number, refused while current on another movement and free again once superseded; a roll to another vessel; a house refreshed with its sealed container and read back; a house moved to another movement with its HBL; ends, cancellation with the reference voided, a new movement for the consolidation afterwards; the Outbox events in order; the same reading on a session in Asia/Shanghai; a co-load movement; a series from 41 with numbers keyed by hand at and ahead of the sequence, each draw giving the next free number; a refresh and an end under one command id (the end refused, the refresh replayed). Past SEA: 16 changes of what never changes, refused to the application role for want of the privilege and to the migrator by the triggers; 36 writes the application role may make, each refused by its rule, at once or at commit (among them a part added by the transaction that ends its entry, a house without a part, links without a leg, out of number or of another booking, and a link, a master bill reference and a house, live or ended, for a cancelled movement); a live house written past SEA whose checks ran before its commit, for which SEA's cancellation waits and is then refused; another tenant reading and changing nothing. Eight houses drawing from one series at once (numbers in order, none lost), eight movements racing for one consolidation and eight for one master bill number (one wins each). With the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and BOOK (`TestHouseBillsUnderTheOceanMovementWithEveryContext`, scenario CW-GAP-E2E-02): two LCL orders as houses of an own-container consolidation booked with a shipping line; HBLs SHAL0000001 and SHAL0000002; house M shut out at the CFS, transferred to the next consolidation and associated in its movement with the same HBL; house L received, stuffed into the 22G1 and sealed, its entry refreshed with the container as FUL relays it; the line's master bill number; and the container rolled to the next vessel under the same booking. BOOK holds the second booking's vessel name with a space after it; SEA keeps the name.
  - An independent review of `91889c6` found no blocker, 1 major and 6 minor findings and 7 nits, most of them reproduced; they are fixed before merge except where stated. Major: an HBL number of the series given by hand before the sequence reached it stopped the series for good (the draw failed on the taken number and its transaction took the sequence's step back with it); a draw now passes over a taken number. Minor: the checks at commit could be passed by writes past SEA on the application role, leaving movements SEA could not read (a cancellation committing between a live house's write and its commit; parts added by the transaction that ends an entry; links without a leg, out of number or of another booking; a house without a part; rows added to a cancelled movement); the checks are now as described above. A refresh and an end of one house under one command id were answered with each other's receipt; the hash now covers the command's name. A vessel's name BOOK holds with spaces around it was refused; it is trimmed. BOOK's and FUL's error kinds reached SEA's caller; they are SEA's now. A shipment named like an HBL made a valid movement unreadable. Eleven database refusals were refused for want of the privilege, never by the trigger (now tested as the migrator too), and a restore case was refused for another reason than its name (each case now states its reason). Nits fixed: instants finer than a microsecond from upstream, capacity allocated to another kind of consumer, an older plan taken on refresh, and what the series' last number states before a number is taken. Left as they are, as in AIR: a replayed command id is answered before authorization; queries of more than one statement do not read one snapshot; a stored movement that does not restore is answered as rejected; the route's edition is the booking request's. The stopped series, the request hash and the adapters' error kinds are the same in AIR's code, which this change does not touch.
- V2.62 takes three corrections of SEA's review (V2.61) into AIR, whose code had the same defects. No migration.
  - A HAWB number of the series that was given by hand before the sequence reached it stopped the series for good: the draw failed on the taken number and its transaction took the sequence's step back with it. A draw now passes over a taken number and gives the next free one.
  - A command's request hash now covers its name, so that a refresh and an end of one house under one command id are not answered with each other's receipt (the second is an idempotency conflict). Receipts written before keep the hash of the fields alone: a command recorded then is still replayed, under either hash; for those receipts only, two commands of the same fields remain indistinguishable.
  - BOOK's and FUL's error kinds no longer reach AIR's caller: a booking or shipment the upstream does not know, or a question it rejects, is AIR's unknown booking or shipment (rejected); its other kinds are kept as text; a cancelled context or a failing database passes unchanged.
  - Not examined: whether the other contexts' command receipts hash the fields alone as AIR's did.
  - Tests: the two adapters with each of BOOK's and FUL's error kinds; on PG16, HAWB numbers keyed by hand at and ahead of the sequence with each draw giving the next free number; a refresh and an end under one command id (the end refused, the refresh replayed, the end taken under its own id); and a receipt as AIR wrote it before, replayed by the same command and refused to the id with other fields.
- V2.63 is sea freight part 1e-i: SEA V0.2 records container visits (PHY tactical candidate V0.1, aggregate ContainerVisit; backlog PB6 without VGM, container events and OceanDocFacts v1). A container visit is one use of one container for one ocean movement; the same equipment number recurs in later visits.
  - `AssignContainer` assigns a container to a planned load unit of an OPEN movement of the consolidator's own container (SEA_LCL_OWN; a co-loader's container is the co-loader's): its ISO 6346 number, whose check digit is validated, its size-type and, when known, its own tare and maximum gross from the CSC plate or as the carrier states them (PHY's limits of the unit are its type's defaults, V2.57). A container number has one live visit in the tenant and a load unit of a movement one live container; both are looked up in the command's transaction, under the movement's lock, and held by unique indexes. The unit's number is the one PHY gave the planned unit: SEA does not ask PHY (no relationship in Context Map V1.4), and `RecordStuffingDone` checks it against what FUL relays. The assignment is dated not before the movement was opened; the visit then has its own history and revision, and the movement's revision does not move.
  - `RecordContainerSeal` records a seal applied to the container: its number, the party that applied it (CARRIER, SHIPPER or CUSTOMS), when, and the source it was read from. A further seal replaces the current one, which stays as history (a customs inspection seals again), also on a stuffed container; the current number again is refused.
  - `RecordStuffingDone` records that an assigned container is stuffed and sealed: a seal is recorded, and the movement's live houses carry, as FUL relays it from PHY (OD-2), one sealed stuffing of the visit's load unit. The visit keeps PHY's stuffing and is STUFFED, and the houses' per-container breakdown then names the container. It is dated not before PHY sealed. PHY keeps the seal it closed the stuffing with as it was keyed (any text) and the size-type of the unit it planned, and corrects neither once the container is sealed: when that seal is none of the visit's seals (compared by letters and digits alone, whatever the case and the separators) or that size-type is not the visit's, the command is refused unless it states a `DifferenceReason`, which the visit keeps, as PHY's close takes an overrun of the envelope only with a reason (V2.59). A container assigned to the wrong unit is released and assigned again instead; without a difference a reason is refused.
  - `ReleaseContainer` gives an assigned container back unused, with a reason; its number and its load unit are free for another visit. A stuffed container is not released. `CancelOceanMovement` now refuses a movement that holds a container, and is dated not before its last container was released.
  - Queries: `GetContainerVisit`, `ListContainerVisits` (a movement's visits in the order they were assigned, released ones included) and `FindLiveContainerVisit` (by container number). `GetOceanMovement` and `FindOceanMovementByMasterBill` list the movement's live visits and name, on each house's container, the number of the stuffed visit that keeps its stuffing. Events carry identifiers only, the visit as subject with its movement: ContainerAssigned.v1, SealRecorded.v1 (the seal's number in the visit's history), ContainerEventRecorded.v1 (reason STUFFED) and ContainerReleased.v1; never a container or seal number, nor a reason's text. The event ids of movements are as before.
  - Migration `sea/000002_sea_container_visits`: `container_visit` and `container_seal` under row level security, and a receipt that may name a visit and a seal. The check digit is a CHECK; one live visit per container number and per load unit are unique indexes. Seals are immutable. A visit starts ASSIGNED at revision 1, leaves ASSIGNED once, for STUFFED or RELEASED, writing only what that step records; otherwise only its revision moves, by one; a released visit never changes; nothing is deleted. Checked at commit, with the movement read under a share lock that a cancellation and the movement's house commands wait for: a visit is of an own-container movement and names its consolidation; a live visit is of an OPEN movement; seals are numbered without gaps, each of another number than the one before and recorded not before it or before the assignment, and each written by a transaction that steps its visit's revision; a stuffed visit has a seal; and the step to STUFFED finds, among the live houses of the movement, a sealed stuffing of the visit's load unit with the execution it keeps, sealed not after. A movement is not cancelled while it holds a container. The CI workflow grants the application role UPDATE on a visit's status, stuffing, release, revision and `updated_at` only.
  - Values and their sources: the design names the seal's fields, not its form. A seal number is capital letters and digits with single spaces, hyphens or slashes inside, 35 characters at most (the length of a seal identifier in UN/EDIFACT's SEL segment, data element 9308, from general knowledge and not re-checked); to verify before the pilot, like the bill numbers of V2.61. The parties CARRIER, SHIPPER and CUSTOMS and the plate sources are the design's. The container number's form and check digit are ISO 6346's as the design states them; serial numbers whose check sum leaves 10 take the digit 0.
  - Not yet: VGM assertions with their method, responsible party and signatory (personal data, to be kept apart with restricted reads and the access log, as the owner decided on 2026-11-10: P4, P6, P7) and the carrier's response; the container's events before and after stuffing (empty released and picked up, arrived at the CFS, gate-in, loaded on board, rolled) and the states VGM_SUBMITTED, GATED_IN and LOADED; `FinalizeOceanDocFacts` and OceanDocFacts v1; SEA's routes on the HTTP API; a correction of plate data or of a size-type keyed wrongly, other than by releasing an assigned container; a container whose cargo is taken out again (PHY does not unstuff a sealed container yet); what V2.61 lists.
  - Tests: domain tests of container numbers (check digits 0 for the remainders 0 and 10, wrong digits, forms), the assignment (15 refusals, a container assigned before the movement's last change), the seals (13 refusals, replacement, a seal on a stuffed and on a released container), stuffing done (the same seal keyed otherwise or replaced since, each difference refused and taken with its reason, a reason without a difference, a time before PHY sealed, two houses in one container, two stuffings named for one unit, a house ended), the release, and 23 stored visits a restore refuses, each for its reason; module tests of the events' payloads and ids (a movement's event id pinned to its form before visits), the published event types and vocabulary, and the error kinds. On PG16 with stand-ins for BOOK and FUL (`TestContainerVisitsOnPostgres`): 13 assignments refused, an assignment replayed and its command id reused, the container on another movement, another container on the unit, a visit id twice; seals refused, recorded in small letters, replayed; stuffing done refused before FUL relays the stuffing, with a reason without a difference and before PHY sealed, then recorded and replayed; a release under a stuffing's command id; a stuffed container not released; a customs seal after stuffing; a second container whose record in PHY differs (the seal a sentence, another size-type), refused and then taken with its reason; a container released, its movement cancelled only after that and not dated before the release, the number assigned again; the queries, another tenant, the events in order with identifiers only, and the same reading on a session in Asia/Shanghai. Past SEA (`TestContainerVisitDatabaseRules`): 7 changes of what never changes, refused to the application role for want of the privilege and to the migrator by the triggers; 41 writes the application role may make, each refused by its rule; another tenant reading and changing nothing. Eight movements racing for one container and eight containers for one load unit (one wins each); a container written past SEA whose checks ran before its commit, for which SEA's cancellation waits and is then refused, and a cancellation written past SEA for which SEA's assignment waits and is then refused. With the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and BOOK (`TestHouseBillsUnderTheOceanMovementWithEveryContext`): the container CSQU3054383 assigned to PHY's unit 1, the CFS's seal recorded with the shipper as its party, stuffing done against PHY's stuffing as FUL relays it, and house L naming its container.
- V2.64 is sea freight part 1e-ii: the verified gross mass of a stuffed container (SOLAS). `AssertVgm` takes a visit from STUFFED, or from VGM_SUBMITTED, to VGM_SUBMITTED. The mass is a positive weight in grams, kilograms or tonnes, stored in grams, and where the plate states a tare it is not below that tare (equal is allowed). It is not the cargo's net weight and not a sum of declared weights. Method 1 is the packed container weighed; method 2 is cargo, packing and tare. The responsible party is the one the command names — the shipper on the master bill, which for the consolidator's own container is the forwarder — and SEA does not ask ORG. The business time is the submission. After the current voyage's first-leg VGM cut-off a late reason is required; without a cut-off, or while still before it, a reason is refused. A further assertion while one is already submitted adds the next version. `RecordVgmResponse` records the carrier's acceptance or rejection of the latest unanswered version, once, from a source and not before the submission; the visit stays VGM_SUBMITTED. The cut-off is checked when the assertion is inserted, not again when the carrier answers, so a later roll of the vessel does not reopen a stored assertion.
  - The authorised signatory's name is personal data (privacy assessment V1.2, decisions P4, P6 and P7 of 2026-11-10). It is stored in `vgm_signatory`, apart from the assertion. The application role may insert a name and may not select the table. A read is `ReadVgmSignatory`, which calls `cargo_sea.read_vgm_signatory`: the function takes the tenant from the transaction, accepts only a customs audit, a legal claim or the person's own request, writes the access log in the same call and returns the name. An unknown assertion returns nothing and writes no log. A refused purpose or an actor without the right is turned away before the function is called, so it writes nothing either. The log is insert-only through the function (the application role cannot insert a row of its own), the database stamps the time, a row is kept three years and never rewritten, and a listing of it (`ReadVgmSignatoryAccess`) carries no name. `GetContainerVisit` lists the assertions, latest first, with the mass, the method, the party and the carrier's answer, and no name. Events `VgmAsserted.v1` and `VgmResponseRecorded.v1` carry the assertion's version only. A house of a submitted container still names that container.
  - Migration `sea/000003_sea_vgm`. `000001` and `000002` are unchanged. A visit may be VGM_SUBMITTED and then still holds its stuffing, its seal and any difference reason; it leaves STUFFED only for VGM_SUBMITTED, changing its status, and afterwards only its revision moves. Assertions are immutable except the carrier's answer, which is filled once from null. At commit an assertion belongs to a submitted visit written in the same transaction, the versions have no gaps, each has its signatory, the mass is not below the tare, and a late reason is present exactly when the submission is after the cut-off. The commit check counts signatories through `vgm_signatory_count`, which sees the caller's tenant only, because the application role cannot select the names. A receipt may name the assertion's version (`result_assertion_no`), and only a visit's receipt may. The CI workflow grants UPDATE of the answer columns, revokes SELECT on `vgm_signatory` and INSERT on `vgm_signatory_access`, and grants EXECUTE on the two functions.
  - Not yet: the container's terminal and vessel events and the states GATED_IN and LOADED (gate-in requires a submitted VGM); `FinalizeOceanDocFacts` and OceanDocFacts v1, which carry the assertion's id and not the name; SEA's routes on the HTTP API; what V2.63 lists besides the VGM.
  - Tests: domain tests of a mass below the tare, no weight, an unknown method, a determination after the submission, a reason while still in time, a late submission with no reason, a name with a surrounding space, another movement, a tonne equal to the tare, an answer before the submission and an answer twice, a second version that leaves the first answer, and a reason where the voyage has no VGM cut-off; a restored visit keeps no name. The published event types include the two VGM events, and an event id covers the assertion's version. On PG16 (`TestVGMOnPostgres`): a mass below the tare and a reason before the cut-off refused; an assertion replayed; the visit and the outbox without the name or the mass; the house still naming its container; the application role refused SELECT on the name and INSERT on the log; the function refusing a documents purpose; an actor without the right, a documents purpose and an unknown version refused with nothing logged; two restricted reads logged, newest first, without the name; a gram written past SEA refused at commit because it is below the tare, and the stored assertion left as it was; a late assertion refused without a reason and taken with one; the carrier's answer once. The container-visit suite and `TestHouseBillsUnderTheOceanMovementWithEveryContext` still pass.
- V2.65 is sea freight part 1e-iii: `RecordContainerEvent` records a carrier or terminal fact about a container. The kinds are EMPTY_RELEASED, EMPTY_PICKED_UP, ARRIVED_AT_CFS, GATE_IN_TERMINAL, LOADED_ON_BOARD and ROLLED. Stuffing stays with `RecordStuffingDone`. A fact never moves a house. The place is a UN/LOCODE. The source reference is recorded once in the tenant: the same message again returns the event already recorded and leaves the revision, and the same reference with other content is refused. An empty pickup, an arrival and a roll do not change the visit's state. A gate-in is taken from VGM_SUBMITTED and moves the visit to GATED_IN; a further gate report leaves it. A loading is taken once the container is gated in and moves it to LOADED. A roll after loading leaves it loaded: the voyage itself changes by `LinkCarrierBooking`, not by this fact.
  - The published event is still ContainerEventRecorded.v1. Its reason is the kind, and it names the event's number. It carries neither the place nor the source reference. A stuffing recorded before this version has no event number, so its event id is unchanged.
  - Migration `sea/000004_sea_container_events`. `000001`, `000002` and `000003` are unchanged. A visit leaves VGM_SUBMITTED only for GATED_IN, and GATED_IN only for LOADED, changing its status; otherwise only its revision moves. A gated-in or loaded container still holds its stuffing, its seal and its VGM. At commit a gate-in is recorded exactly when the visit is gated in or loaded, and a loading exactly when it is loaded. Events are immutable and numbered without gaps, and each is written by a transaction that steps its visit. A receipt may name the event (`result_event_no`).
  - Not yet: `FinalizeOceanDocFacts` and OceanDocFacts v1, which carry the assertion's id and not the signatory's name; SEA's routes on the HTTP API; what V2.63 and V2.64 list besides these events.
  - Tests: domain tests of a gate before the VGM, stuffing by this command, a fact dated after it is recorded, an empty pickup that leaves the visit assigned, the same message again, the same reference with other content, a loading before the gate, a second gate report, a roll that leaves the visit loaded, and an event on a released container. On PG16 (`TestContainerEventsOnPostgres`): the same, with the replay, the house still naming its container, the outbox carrying the kind and the event number only, a status of LOADED written past SEA without the fact refused at commit, and an event written without a step of its visit refused. The visit suite, the VGM suite and `TestHouseBillsUnderTheOceanMovementWithEveryContext` still pass.
- V2.66 is sea freight part 1e-iv: `FinalizeOceanDocFacts` publishes the next immutable version of OceanDocFacts v1 and makes the movement FINAL. FUL's plan of every live house is read before the transaction; a house FUL no longer reports as SEA froze it is refused until it is refreshed or its association ended. An own container needs a current voyage, the carrier's master bill reference, every live visit sealed, a VGM submitted by the cut-off or accepted by the carrier, and every live house's packs stuffed in those containers. A co-load needs its co-load bill. The published JSON names each VGM assertion and its mass, method and responsible party, and has no signatory. A later link, refresh or end returns the movement to OPEN (`OceanDocFactsSuperseded.v1`) and leaves the published version as it was. A master bill, a new house, a cancellation and a change of a container are refused while the movement is FINAL.
  - Migration `sea/000005_sea_ocean_doc_facts`. One movement in force, OPEN or FINAL, per consolidation. A FINAL movement's latest version is written at its current revision. Versions are immutable, numbered from one, and their SHA-256 is the database's hash of the stored JSON. A receipt may name the version (`result_facts_version`). `GetOceanDocFacts` reads the latest or a given version; its status is FINAL only while the movement is FINAL and the version is the latest.
  - Not yet: `RecordOceanCarrierFact` (the on-board date in the facts is taken from a container's LOADED_ON_BOARD event when one is recorded); SEA's routes on the HTTP API; marks, which PHY has not given SEA; what V2.63 to V2.65 list besides these facts.
  - Tests: domain tests of a finalization before the VGM, the published bytes without the signatory and with the assertion id, a master bill refused once FINAL, and a refresh that reopens the movement and supersedes version 1. On PG16 (`TestOceanDocFactsOnPostgres`): the same, a seal refused after finalization, the outbox without the name, and version 1 SUPERSEDED after the refresh. The visit, VGM and container-event suites and `TestHouseBillsUnderTheOceanMovementWithEveryContext` still pass.
- V2.67 records container facts after the movement is FINAL. A gate-in, a loading, an empty release or pickup, an arrival, a roll, and the carrier's acceptance of a VGM are written on the visit and leave the movement FINAL. The published OceanDocFacts version is the snapshot taken at finalization and is not rewritten, so a loading recorded afterwards does not fill its on-board date. A new seal, a new VGM assertion, or the carrier's rejection returns the movement to OPEN in the same transaction (`OceanDocFactsSuperseded.v1`): a rejection means the published facts no longer hold. Assigning a container, recording stuffing, releasing it, a master bill, a new house and a cancellation stay refused while FINAL. A new VGM assertion is still taken only from STUFFED or VGM_SUBMITTED, and the carrier's answer only while the visit is VGM_SUBMITTED.
  - Migration `sea/000006_sea_facts_after_final`. `000001` through `000005` are unchanged. A live container may sit on an OPEN or FINAL movement; a cancelled movement still holds none. A seal, a new VGM assertion and a rejection commit only while the movement is OPEN, so a write that skips the reopening is refused. The carrier's acceptance may be written while the movement is FINAL.
  - Not yet: `RecordOceanCarrierFact`; SEA's routes on the HTTP API. The archive versions for V2.64 through V2.67 are not registered.
  - Tests: a rejection, a further VGM assertion and a gate-in while FINAL in the domain, then `Supersede` returns the movement to OPEN. On PG16 (`TestOceanDocFactsOnPostgres`): an acceptance, a gate-in and a loading leave the movement FINAL and the published version current, with no on-board date; a seal, a VGM assertion or a rejection written past SEA without reopening is refused; a seal through the module reopens the movement. `TestANewVGMReopensTheFacts` and `TestARejectedVGMReopensTheFacts` record the assertion and the rejection through the module. The visit, VGM and container-event suites and `TestHouseBillsUnderTheOceanMovementWithEveryContext` still pass.
- V2.68 records a carrier's report on the movement: loaded on board, departed, rolled or discharged. It names one live container, or the current master bill, or a live house's HBL, with the vessel and voyage the carrier stated. The same source reference again returns the fact already recorded. The movement's state and revision do not change, so a report after finalization leaves the published OceanDocFacts version current. The event carries the fact's number and its kind, not the bill, the container number or the vessel.
  - Migration `sea/000007_sea_ocean_carrier_fact`. `000001` through `000006` are unchanged.
  - Not yet: SEA's routes on the HTTP API.
  - Tests: a departure while the movement is final in the domain, the same report again, a container the movement does not have, and a fact on a cancelled movement. On PG16 (`TestOceanCarrierFactsOnPostgres`): a departure and a loading after finalization leave the revision and the published version, a replay and the same reference return the fact, another kind on that reference is refused, and a container written past SEA that the movement does not have is refused at commit.
- V2.69 corrects how the on-board date reaches the published facts, and three defects found when V2.67 and V2.68 were reviewed. DOC issues an ocean house bill after the on-board date and reads that date from OceanDocFacts, so a loading recorded after finalization has to reach a published version.
  - The on-board date is the earliest loading recorded: a container's LOADED_ON_BOARD event, or the carrier's LOADED_ON_BOARD fact, which until now the facts did not read. A co-load has no container of its own, so the co-loader's report is its only source.
  - A loading that changes the on-board date the published facts state (they state none, or a later one) returns a FINAL movement to OPEN in the same transaction (`OceanDocFactsSuperseded.v1`), as a new seal does; finalizing again publishes the next version with the date. A loading that leaves the published date, a gate-in, a departure, a roll and a discharge still leave the movement FINAL. This replaces V2.67's rule that a loading leaves it FINAL, and V2.68's that no carrier fact changes the state.
  - Facts without a loading no longer state one. V2.66 to V2.68 published `"on_board_at":"0001-01-01T00:00:00Z"`, because `omitempty` does not omit a zero time; the field is now left out (`omitzero`). Versions already published keep their bytes, and a reader takes the year 1 as no date.
  - Migration `sea/000008_sea_on_board_date`. `000001` through `000007` are unchanged. A loading that changes the published date commits only while the movement is OPEN, for an event and for a carrier fact, so a write that skips the reopening is refused. The check of a VGM assertion reads the movement under its share lock: before, an assertion written past SEA while a finalization held the movement, with the assertion written before its visit's step, was accepted on the movement that became FINAL.
  - A carrier fact is still recorded without a step of the movement's revision, unless it reopens the movement. `GetOceanMovement` therefore returns `CarrierFacts`, like `Containers`, that change while `Revision` stays; the HTTP routes must not serve the movement's revision alone as its ETag.
  - Not yet: SEA's routes on the HTTP API. A loading reported for a vessel the movement has since left (a later `LinkCarrierBooking`) still counts. A carrier's LOADED_ON_BOARD fact about a container does not move that container's visit to LOADED.
  - Tests: in the domain, a loading after finalization (recorded before it, refused; then superseding), the second version with the date, a later loading and a departure that leave it, the carrier's earlier loading that changes it, and a co-load's date from the co-loader. On PG16: `TestOceanDocFactsOnPostgres` (the stored JSON without the field, a loading written past SEA refused, the loading reopening, the second version with the date and the new seal, the carrier's later report leaving it), `TestOceanCarrierFactsOnPostgres` (a house bill named by its HBL, a roll and a discharge, six refusals through SEA, the same reference on another movement, a cancelled movement, five writes past SEA each refused by its rule, the loading reopening), `TestACoLoadTakesItsOnBoardDateFromTheCarrier`, and `TestAnAssertionPastSEACannotPassAFinalization` (both orders of the write wait for the finalization and are refused).
- V2.70 adds SEA's routes to the HTTP API. Every command and query of SEA's `api` has a route; the adapter (`internal/contexts/sea/internal/adapters/http`) takes the tenant and the actor from the verified principal and holds no business rule. No migration.
  - Movements: `POST /v1/ocean-movements` (201 with `Location`), `GET /v1/ocean-movements/{movement}`, `GET /v1/ocean-movements?issuer_party_id=&master_bill_number=`, and under a movement `POST link-carrier-booking`, `master-bills`, `house-bills`, `house-bills/{shipment}/refresh`, `house-bills/{shipment}/end`, `finalize-doc-facts`, `carrier-facts` and `cancel`, `GET doc-facts`, `doc-facts/{version}`, `carrier-facts` and `container-visits`. Series and register: `POST /v1/hbl-series`, `GET /v1/hbl-series/{legal_entity}`, `GET /v1/house-bills/{legal_entity}/{hbl}`. Visits: `POST /v1/container-visits` (201), `GET /v1/container-visits/{visit}`, `GET /v1/container-visits?container_number=`, and under a visit `POST seals`, `stuffing-done`, `release`, `vgm-assertions`, `vgm-response` and `events`. A change names the revision it expects in `If-Match`; a receipt names what the command wrote (`entry_no`, `hbl`, `bill_no`, `seal_no`, `assertion_no`, `event_no`, `fact_no`, `facts_version`).
  - An ETag covers everything its resource states. A movement's revision does not move when a container visit changes or a carrier fact is recorded (V2.69), so the movement resource states neither: the movement's visits and its carrier facts are lists of their own, without an ETag, and a house's containers are given without the container number, which is the visit's. A movement's ETag does move when a visit's command returns a FINAL movement to OPEN. A container visit's ETag is its own revision, which covers its seals, VGM assertions and events. The HBL series has no ETag: its sequence moves with every number drawn.
  - `GET doc-facts` returns the version's status, digest, who finalized it and when, and as `facts` the published JSON byte for byte: the stored bytes are written into the response, not encoded again, so `sha256` is the digest of the bytes a client receives as `facts`. `api.OceanDocFacts` carries them as `Content`.
  - Permissions are `sea:<Action>` for a command and `sea:Read` for a query. The signatory's name is read with `POST /v1/container-visits/{visit}/vgm-assertions/{version}/signatory-reads`, whose body states the purpose; it needs `sea:ReadVgmSignatory`, and the log of those reads (`GET` on the same path, without the name) needs `sea:ReadVgmSignatoryAccess`. `sea:Read` and the command permissions grant neither. The wildcards `sea:*` and `*` grant both, as they grant every action: whether a wildcard should include the name is not decided here.
  - `bootstrap.API` now assembles COM, FUL, PHY and BOOK as well, because SEA reads BOOK's bookings and FUL's plans. Those four have no routes yet, so a client cannot run the whole chain over HTTP: a booking, a plan and PHY's stuffing still have to exist before SEA's routes can use them.
  - Not yet: a list of movements (SEA has no query for one), conditional reads (`If-None-Match`), and routes for COM, FUL, PHY, BOOK, AIR, DOC and FIN.
  - Tests: on PG16 `TestHTTPSeaOnPostgres` runs ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and BOOK in process and every SEA step through the handler: the series, the movement, a replay and a reused key, the house, a container released unused and the one stuffed, the master bill, the VGM, two published versions around a loading with the digest checked over the bytes received, a carrier fact that leaves the revision, a roll and a cancellation; 401, 403, 404, 409, 422 and 428 answers; a movement's ETag staying while its visits change and moving when a loading reopens it; the name refused to the operator, to `sea:Read` and to the log's reader, read once by the auditor and found in the log, and in no other response; and another tenant holding `sea:*` seeing nothing.
- V2.71 corrects SEA in the three ways found when V2.70 was registered, as the owner decided on 2026-11-18, and in six ways an independent review of V2.64 to V2.70 reproduced the same day with writes past SEA on the application role. Migration `sea/000009_sea_final_holds`; `000001` through `000008` are unchanged.
  - A command is authorized before its replay is answered (`application.begin`). Before, SEA looked the command id up first, as AIR does: an actor whose permission was gone was still answered the receipt of a command it had run, and a principal without the permission was answered 409 for a key in use and 403 for one that was not, so it could tell which keys were in use. Each of these is now 403. This is what V2.53 asks of every context's routes; for SEA it replaces the order V2.61's review had left. AIR keeps its order until it has routes.
  - A command's transaction holds its command id first (an advisory lock, `CommandReceipts.Lock`), so the same command sent twice at once commits once and both answer its receipt. Before, a copy that lost the race answered 409 although the command had committed. A command that reads BOOK, FUL or the movement's revision before its transaction may prepare against the state the first copy has already changed; it then answers the receipt as well (`committedMeanwhile`).
  - The read of a VGM signatory's name (`sea:ReadVgmSignatory`) and of the log of those reads (`sea:ReadVgmSignatoryAccess`) are granted by name only: `sea:*` and `*` no longer grant them (`identity.AuthorizeNamed`, which `bootstrap`'s permissions use for these two actions). A token that relied on a wildcard to read the name must now name the permission.
  - From the review, in the database (each of these committed before, past SEA; SEA's own commands could do none of them): a house is ended only while its movement is OPEN at commit, so the only live house of a FINAL movement can no longer be ended under its published facts; a container is assigned, stuffed or released only while its movement is not FINAL; every change of a movement is a step of its revision by one, so a movement cannot be made FINAL again at the revision its facts were published at, nor opened, changed and made FINAL with its revision standing still (made FINAL at a later revision it needs a facts version written at it).
  - From the review, the signatory's name: `cargo_sea.read_vgm_signatory` logged the read and returned the name in the caller's transaction, so a caller on the application role that rolled the transaction or a savepoint back kept the name and left no log. The read is now two steps in two transactions: `log_vgm_signatory_read` logs it and returns the entry's id, and `read_logged_vgm_signatory` returns the name only for an entry another transaction wrote (which is therefore committed), of the caller's tenant, less than a minute old. `ReadVgmSignatory` commits the entry and then reads against it. An entry may be read against more than once within its minute, and an entry whose second step fails stays: the log never has fewer reads than there were. The earlier function is dropped; the CI workflow grants the two new ones.
  - From the review, the request hash: `AssertVgm`'s hash covered the signatory's name and is kept on the receipt, which the application role reads beside every other field of the command, so a guessed name could be confirmed offline. The hash no longer covers the name. A retry that states another name is therefore answered the first receipt (the name stored is the first); a receipt written before V2.71 keeps its hash and is still answered.
  - From the review, published facts: the database refused the word "signatory" anywhere in a facts version's text, so a house whose goods description contained it could never be finalized, and the finalization failed with the database's error. The check now refuses a member named for a signatory at any depth (`cargo_sea.json_names_a_signatory`) and leaves the text of values alone.
  - The review was not finished: the reviewing agent ran seven probes and was cut off before it wrote its report or reached OceanDocFacts against DOC's needs and the tests' reasons for passing. What it reproduced is corrected here.
  - Not yet: AIR's commands still answer a replay before they authorize (AIR has no routes); a write past SEA that forges a whole finalization (a movement made FINAL with a facts version of its own making) is not something the database can tell from SEA's; a FINAL movement returned to OPEN past SEA publishes no `OceanDocFactsSuperseded.v1`; the log of an assertion that does not exist is an empty list, not 404; the other contexts' routes; what V2.70 lists.
  - Tests: on PG16 in SEA, `TestACommandIsAuthorizedBeforeItsReplay` (another actor with the id in use and with an id not in use, the same actor once its right is gone with the same and with another payload, a visit's command) and `TestTheSameCommandAtOnceCommitsOnce` (eight copies each of an association, a master bill and a container assignment answer one receipt; one house, one number drawn, one event each); through the handler, `TestHTTPSeaRetriesOnPostgres` (the replay refused to the actor without the permission, a key in use and a key not in use answered alike, eight registrations at once answering one receipt); `TestAuthorizeNamed`; and `TestHTTPSeaOnPostgres` now refuses the name and the log to `sea:*` and to `*`. Run against V2.70's code, each of the four fails for the defect it names.
