For the review's findings, on PG16 with the statements of its probes: `TestAHouseIsNotEndedPastSEAOnAFinalMovement` (and SEA's own end still returning the movement to OPEN), `TestAContainerIsNotAssignedPastSEAOnAFinalMovement`, `TestAMovementIsNotMadeFinalAgainPastSEAOnFactsThatNoLongerHold` (back to the published revision; opened and made final at one revision), `TestASignatoryIsReadOnlyAgainstACommittedLogEntry` (logged and read in one transaction, in a savepoint rolled back, against an entry rolled back, in another tenant's scope; then against a committed entry), `TestAVgmReceiptDoesNotConfirmTheSignatory`, `TestAVgmReceiptHashedWithTheNameIsStillAnswered` and `TestAGoodsDescriptionMayContainTheWordSignatory` (a member named for a signatory still refused at the top and nested).
- V2.72 issues the house bill of a sea shipment from OceanDocFacts v1 (DOC candidate 24, backlog DB4 without the originals, the sea waybill and the recorded master bill). Migration `doc/000004_doc_house_bill`. The number is the HBL SEA drew, in the form V2.61 set and the owner kept until the pilot (2026-11-18): one to sixteen capital letters and digits. The issuing authority is recorded from simulated evidence, for the legal entity, the kind and the port of loading; whether an NVOCC filing applies to LCL waits for counsel before the pilot.
  - `DraftTransportDocument` with kind HBL reads SEA's latest FINAL version and COM's parties of the house's orders. The consignee is named and the release type is EXPRESS. A draft may exist before an on-board date; issuing, and issuing an amendment, is refused until the facts the version was built on state one. The same gates as a HAWB then apply: the order parties locked for the document (C12), FIN's credit or an override (C7), and the authority in force. An amendment and a cancellation are the commands a HAWB already has. A cancelled HBL's number stays with its shipment.
  - Not yet: a consignee to order, the originals and their surrender, a house sea waybill, recording the carrier's master bill, rendering an HBL to PDF, and DOC's HTTP routes.
  - Tests: on PG16 `TestAHouseBillOnPostgres` drafts before an on-board date and is refused at issue, refreshes onto the version that states the date and issues (the number, the vessel, the master bill, the seal and the on-board date), amends when the goods description changes, cancels, issues the number again for the same shipment and refuses it to another.
  - Found after the merge and corrected in V2.73: a second HBL drafted for a shipment that has one was answered the database's unique violation, not a conflict; FIN's snapshot of an HBL had no master number; `api.DocumentContent` did not read an HBL's content back to the same bytes. No separate review of V2.72 was finished.
- V2.73 adds the originals of an HBL, their surrender at origin, the house sea waybill and the consignee to order (DOC candidate 24: OriginalBill, the release types, RECORD_ORIGINALS_SURRENDERED, C11). Migration `doc/000005_doc_originals`.
  - Release. `DraftTransportDocument` takes `Release` and `Originals` for an HBL: EXPRESS (also when empty), or ORIGINALS with one to nine originals. Kind `HOUSE_SEA_WAYBILL` is the other sea house document: release SEA_WAYBILL, a named consignee, no originals, an issuance authority of its own kind. A shipment has one sea house document that is not cancelled, an HBL or a sea waybill. `RefreshDocumentDraft` and `OpenDocumentAmendment` take `Release` and `Originals` as well: a release replaces both the release and the number, a number alone replaces only the count, so a draft EXPRESS bill whose order's consignee becomes to order is refreshed as ORIGINALS, and an issued ORIGINALS bill is amended to EXPRESS, which issues no originals. An air waybill takes neither.
  - Originals. Issuing a version of originals writes one record per original; each change of an original is its next record, numbered from 1, and the last is its status. `ReturnOriginal` records one original back with the issuer, with evidence. `OpenDocumentAmendment` is refused until every original of the issued version is returned, and `CancelTransportDocument` while any is out (`DOC_ORIGINALS_OUT`). Abandoning the amendment records the returned originals as handed back (ISSUED again, the reason as evidence); issuing it gives the new version originals of its own and leaves the old ones returned. `ListOriginals` reads the records.
  - Surrender. `SurrenderOriginals` records every original of the issued version surrendered at origin, with evidence (telex release), whether each original is out or already back with the issuer: the issuer ends up holding the whole set either way. The document becomes SURRENDERED and its version stays the issued one (`OriginalBillsSurrendered.v1`). FIN's snapshot answers a surrendered document, with the master bill as the master number of a sea document. A surrendered document is cancelled, its originals being with the issuer; it is not amended.
  - To order. The consignee is the order's. Where COM holds a consignee to order (TO ORDER, or TO ORDER OF a holder), the document freezes that text as `consignee.to_order` with no party; only an HBL issued as originals whose orders name a notify party carries it (`DOC_INCOMPLETE` otherwise). Orders of one house with different texts diverge.
  - Database. `cargo_doc.original` has forced row security like every table of the schema; its records are immutable, written only by a transaction that writes the document, only for an HBL's version issued last and the originals its content counts, and only in order (ISSUED to RETURNED or SURRENDERED_AT_ORIGIN, RETURNED to ISSUED or SURRENDERED_AT_ORIGIN). A version's content has the member `originals` exactly when it is released as ORIGINALS, and it is then one digit from 1 to 9 on an HBL's content (constraint `document_version_originals` of `cargo_doc.document_version`). At commit a document's state agrees with its originals: ISSUED with none surrendered, SURRENDERED with all (a surrendered document may have an abandoned amendment's draft after its issued version), AMENDING with all returned, CANCELLED with none out; and every original of a version that is no longer the last issued one is RETURNED.
  - Not yet: amending a surrendered document; an original presented at destination, lost originals (OD-6) and VOID; number stock for a house series; recording the carrier's master bill; rendering an HBL to PDF (until its template exists, rendering or dispatching a sea document is refused with `DOC_NO_TEMPLATE`; V2.72 laid it out as an air waybill; V2.74 adds the template); DOC's HTTP routes. Cancelling a surrendered bill checks nothing about the cargo having been released on it, because release cases are not built (the design's "no release case AUTHORIZED"). A dispatch of a sea document prepared or sent under V2.72 can no longer have its copy produced, and one still PREPARED can only be recorded FAILED (development data only). A returned original is handed back only by abandoning an amendment. Once every original of the issued version is returned, a write past DOC that writes a whole issued version with its originals, or a single valid record, is not something the database can tell from DOC's.
  - Tests: in the model, `TestSeaHouseReleaseAndConsignee`, `TestOriginalsSurrendered`, `TestOriginalsReturnedForAnAmendment`, `TestOriginalsSurrenderedAfterAnAbandonedAmendment`, `TestOriginalsSurrenderedWithOneAlreadyReturned`, `TestOriginalsAreNotDatedBeforeTheirLatestRecord` (a return and a surrender dated before the latest record are refused, the same instant is accepted), `TestAHouseSeaDocumentIsCompleteOrNot` (release and number of originals held together, the text to order, `complete()` called on wrong contents and the build refusing the same texts), `TestAnAirWaybillTakesNoOriginals` and `TestABillWithoutOriginalsHasNone`. On PG16: `TestOriginalsSurrenderedAtOriginOnPostgres` (refused while out, surrendered with a replay, FIN's snapshot, cancelled, the shipment's next bill), `TestOriginalsReturnedForAnAmendmentOnPostgres` (one by one, abandoned and handed back, amended with a new set, the amended bill surrendered), `TestOriginalsSurrenderedAfterAnAbandonedAmendmentOnPostgres` (returned, amended, abandoned and handed back, then surrendered, with FIN's snapshot on version 1, and cancelled), `TestOriginalsSurrenderedWithOneAlreadyReturnedOnPostgres` (three originals, one returned, all three surrendered, the snapshot answers, cancelled), `TestARecordBesideAnAmendmentIssuedAtOnceOnPostgres` (a record written while DOC issues the amendment in another transaction is refused at commit), `TestReturnOriginalIsReplayedOnPostgres` (the same receipt again, an idempotency conflict on another serial, a conflict at a stale revision, also for a surrender), `TestReleaseAndOriginalsChangeOnPostgres` (a refresh as ORIGINALS of three and issued with three, a count changed alone, an issued bill amended to EXPRESS, two originals on an EXPRESS draft refused), `TestOriginalsOfOtherKindsAndCountsPastDOCOnPostgres` (seven versions of the wrong release, count or kind written past DOC are refused), `TestAConsigneeToOrderOnPostgres` (COM's text on the bill; refused on EXPRESS, on a sea waybill and without a notify party), `TestAHouseSeaWaybillOnPostgres` (its own authority, no originals, an HBL of the same shipment a conflict), `TestSeaHouseDocumentsRaceForOneShipment` (eight drafts at once, one commits) and `TestOriginalsPastDOCOnPostgres` (another tenant's scope sees and writes none, every table of `cargo_doc` has forced row security and every policy a USING and a WITH CHECK expression that name the tenant, the records are immutable by a trigger, and twenty-six writes on the application role are refused, among them a second issued version over originals that are out, over a surrendered bill and over an original handed back, records on an amending and on a surrendered document, and originals of a house sea waybill).
- V2.74 renders and dispatches the house bill and the house sea waybill (DOC candidate 24, DB7's rendering for sea documents). No migration.
  - Template. The renderer names a template per kind of document: `IDP-AWB-V1` for a HAWB and a MAWB, `IDP-HBL-V1` (`api.TemplateHBL`) for an HBL and a house sea waybill; a kind with none is refused with `DOC_NO_TEMPLATE`. The page is titled BILL OF LADING or SEA WAYBILL and says, by release, `COPY - NOT NEGOTIABLE. Original bills of lading issued: <n>` for ORIGINALS and `EXPRESS RELEASE - NOT NEGOTIABLE. No original bill of lading is issued.` for EXPRESS, and `NOT NEGOTIABLE. Delivery to the named consignee.` for a sea waybill; a version that is not issued says `to be issued` and `will be issued` instead, and prints `Not issued` with no signature line. The master bill has a line of its own under the release line. A release the template does not know (an empty or unknown one, or one that does not go with the kind) is refused with `DOC_NO_TEMPLATE`. It shows the places as UN/LOCODEs, the vessel and voyage, the on-board date (`Not stated` on a draft made before loading), the totals, a line per cargo line, printed once when a house of several orders gives the same goods for each order, one per container with its seal, and the execution box. The PDF of a bill issued as originals is a copy: it is evidence of what was printed, never the document, and the system prints no originals. A consignee to order prints the order's text and nothing else. Layout rules, continuation pages, the draft mark and the contacts of a copy are the air waybill's.
  - Dispatch. A sea house document is dispatched like a HAWB: the contacts frozen on the first order, of the party versions the version froze; a consignee to order has no contact. `DispatchCopy` compares the template recorded with the template of the version's kind now.
  - The air waybill's bytes are unchanged, pinned by `TestAirWaybillBytesAreUnchanged` (seven renderings, their SHA-256 recorded before the change): a dispatch records the digest of its copy and a copy is rendered again to compare.
  - Not yet: the PDF does not show that originals were surrendered; printing originals; charges and clauses on the bill; a place of delivery other than the port of discharge; marks and numbers; bilingual templates; the carrier's master bill; DOC's HTTP routes. Dates are printed in UTC+8, the zone of a port of loading in China, which is the scope of N1 (a port of loading elsewhere would need its own zone). The label says `Master bill of lading` also when the carrier's master is a sea waybill (OceanDocFacts does not say which). A single text longer than a page, and a text too wide for its line outside a box, are not clipped, as on the air waybill. A dispatch of a sea document recorded by V2.72 under the air template answers `DOC_COPY_CHANGED`; no test covers a copy recorded under another template.
  - Tests: `TestAKindWithoutATemplateIsRefused`, `TestAnIssuedBillOfLadingRendersToAValidDeterministicPDF` (the title, number, release line, master bill, vessel, places, on-board date, totals, a cargo line, a container with its seal, the template), `TestTheLineUnderTheTitleFollowsTheRelease`, `TestAConsigneeToOrderPrintsTheOrderOnly`, `TestADraftBillSaysWhatIsNotStated`, `TestALongBillContinuesOnFurtherPages`, `TestACargoLineWithoutACountAndABareContainer` and `TestABillCopyListsItsContacts`, `TestAHouseOfSeveralOrdersPrintsItsGoodsOnce`, `TestAReleaseTheTemplateDoesNotKnowIsRefused` and `TestTheMasterBillHasALineOfItsOwn`. On PG16: `TestAHouseBillIsRenderedOnPostgres` (a draft preview, the issued bill twice to the same bytes, a sea waybill) and `TestAHouseBillIsDispatchedOnPostgres` (a copy to the ordering party and one to an agent abroad with only the consenting contact, sent once ORG holds the reservation, the provision records, a bill to order without a consignee contact, a house sea waybill to the ordering party); `TestAHouseSeaWaybillOnPostgres` now renders the waybill.
- V2.75 records the carrier's master bill (DOC candidate 24, RECORD_CARRIER_DOCUMENT; backlog DB4's recorded MBL). Migration `doc/000006`.
  - Recorded. The carrier's master bill of lading, or its master sea waybill, is issued by the carrier: DOC records it, it does not issue, render or dispatch it. A `CarrierDocument` is an aggregate of its own beside the transport document and the dispatch, with its own table; it has no drafts, versions, approvals, originals, rendering or dispatch. `RecordCarrierDocument` reads SEA's latest `OceanDocFacts v1` of the movement, which must be FINAL (`DOC_SOURCE_NOT_FINAL`), and takes from it the kind, the issuer capacity, the carrier (party and revision), the number, the legal entity, the vessel, the voyage and the ports: `CARRIER_MBL` with the carrier in role `CARRIER` is an MBL, `CARRIER_SEA_WAYBILL` a `MASTER_SEA_WAYBILL`, and any other pair is refused. The person recording enters the release (ORIGINALS with one to nine originals, or EXPRESS, for an MBL; SEA_WAYBILL, or empty, for a sea waybill), the issue place (a UN/LOCODE), the carrier's issue date (a real date, not after tomorrow in UTC+8) and the evidence of the carrier's issuance: its SHA-256, a storage id, a media type and the time it was captured. The content is one immutable JSON object with its SHA-256 and no name of a person or party. The existing commands refuse the kinds `MBL` and `MASTER_SEA_WAYBILL`.
  - Verified by a second person. One person records the document with the reference to the evidence, another verifies the record (`VerifyCarrierDocument`); DOC records who did, not that the evidence was checked. The person who recorded it is refused with `DOC_SELF_VERIFICATION`, ids are compared as they are (an id with surrounding space is refused, nothing else is normalised), and a document is verified once. A recorded document, verified or not, is cancelled with a reason (`CancelCarrierDocument`); a verified one keeps its verification. Events `CarrierDocumentRecorded.v1`, `CarrierDocumentVerified.v1` and `CarrierDocumentCancelled.v1` carry identifiers only. `GetCarrierDocument` and `FindCarrierDocumentByMovement` (the one that is not cancelled) read it; like DOC's other queries they carry no actor.
  - The co-loader's bill. On a co-load movement SEA's facts say `COLOAD_BILL` and the co-loader in role `CO_LOADER`; DOC records it as an MBL whose issuer capacity is `CO_LOADER`. A `COLOAD_BILL` with the carrier in role `CARRIER` is refused.
  - Database. `cargo_doc.carrier_document` has forced row security like every table of the schema. The database keeps the content the bytes its digest covers and, for the members it lists, saying what the columns say; kind, carrier's role, issuer capacity, release and originals together; a verification by a second person and not before the record; a cancellation not before the record or the verification and set exactly when the document is CANCELLED; ids and the number without surrounding space; and a row inserted as a fresh record (ISSUED, revision 1, not verified, not cancelled). There is one document that is not cancelled per movement, and for a tenant and a carrier a number is on at most one document that is not cancelled: a cancelled record frees it, and another movement may then record it (`DOC_NUMBER_ISSUED` otherwise, also for two commands racing for one number). A document is never deleted; it changes only its state, verification, cancellation and revision, by exactly one at a time; nothing changes once it is cancelled; a verification is never changed or removed. Command receipts may name a `CarrierDocument`. CI grants: SELECT, INSERT on the table and UPDATE of the columns that change.
  - Not yet: the master's shipper, consignee (the destination agent) and notify party; the carrier's originals one by one and the carrier's release obtained (they come with release cases); amending a recorded document (it is cancelled and recorded again); telling that the facts were superseded after the record (a roll-over to another vessel with a new master bill: the record stays as it was until it is cancelled); the evidence file itself (DOC keeps its digest and storage reference; storage is BOOK's and not built); a MAWB the airline executes itself; that ORG still holds the carrier in its role (DOC takes SEA's facts for it); a document id may be used once among carrier documents but is not checked against the other documents' ids (a carrier document may take the id of a transport document); DOC's HTTP routes. Also: nothing ties the evidence entered to the evidence SEA holds for the same bill (`OceanDocFacts v1` does not publish it); the content check does not refuse members it does not know or a member written twice; the reads are not authorized, as DOC's other reads (until DOC has HTTP routes); an event's `version` is the document's revision; the number's form is not checked beyond SEA's; a co-loader's capacity and a separate aggregate are not in design candidate 24 as written (the design is amended at registration).
  - Tests: in the model, `TestACarrierDocumentTakesItsKindAndCapacityFromTheFacts`, `TestTheOtherDocumentsStillKnowFourKindsOnly`, `TestCarrierReleaseAndOriginals`, `TestACarrierDocumentIsEnteredCorrectly`, `TestACarrierDocumentNeedsFinalCompleteFacts`, `TestACarrierDocumentsContent`, `TestACarrierDocumentIsVerifiedBySecondPerson`, `TestACarrierDocumentIsCancelled` and `TestACarrierDocumentsIdsCarryNoSurroundingSpace`; on PostgreSQL, `TestACarrierMasterBillIsRecordedOnPostgres`, `TestACarrierSeaWaybillAndACoLoadBillOnPostgres`, `TestACarrierDocumentIsCancelledAndRecordedAgainOnPostgres` (a cancelled record's number goes to another movement; a replayed record answers its first receipt), `TestACarrierDocumentNeedsFinalFactsOnPostgres` (with an authorizer that refuses by action and keeps what it was asked), `TestCarrierDocumentsRaceForOneMovement`, `TestCarrierDocumentsRaceForOneNumber` (eight movements, one number: one commits, seven `DOC_NUMBER_ISSUED`) and `TestCarrierDocumentsPastDOCOnPostgres` (writes past DOC on the application role, the triggers again as the migrator, and another tenant's scope), and the migration list of `cmd/cargo-migrate`.
- V2.76 bills the ocean line from its issued house document (FIN candidate 26, the debit note's document for SEA jobs; plan stage 2a). No migration and no change to production code.
  - Found. Nothing in FIN compares a document kind: a job of a FUL shipment by SEA opens as `SEA_EXPORT_LCL_HOUSE`, and `IssueDebitNote` takes what DOC's `GetHouseDocumentSnapshot` answers, whether it is a HAWB, an HBL or a house sea waybill. No test proved that a SEA job could be billed: every FIN test used an air shipment and a stand-in snapshot of kind HAWB, and the one cross-context ocean test stopped before the facts were finalized and had neither DOC nor FIN. The comment of `IssueDebitNote` still said that DOC issues HAWBs only and a SEA job cannot be billed yet; it now says what is true.
  - Proved, stage by stage, with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY, BOOK, SEA, DOC and FIN on PostgreSQL (AIR only because DOC takes it): (1) two customers' LCL orders by sea, consolidated in one own container, booked, stuffed with a seal, weighed, gated in and loaded on board, with the carrier's master bill reference, take the movement to FINAL `OceanDocFacts v1`; (2) DOC issues shipper A's HBL as three originals on a second person's approval, under COM's party lock and FIN's credit decision, with the number SEA drew and the real vessel, voyage, ports, master bill and container with its seal; (3) DOC issues shipper B's house sea waybill under its own authority; (4) DOC records the carrier's master bill on the facts the real SEA published (kind MBL, issuer capacity CARRIER, the master bill number and the carrier party) and a second person verifies it; (5) FIN opens shipment A's job as `SEA_EXPORT_LCL_HOUSE` from the order's quote, refuses a debit note while the HBL is a draft and while it is approved (`FIN_NO_ISSUED_DOCUMENT`), and one dated before the HBL's issue (DOC's answer for that instant is "none issued by then"; FIN's own comparison of the note's time with the issue time is not reachable, with the real DOC or with FIN's stand-in), and once it is issued bills it: the note carries the HBL's document id, version 1, number, the master bill number as master number and the issue time, and the job's profit follows the quote's OCEAN_FREIGHT line; (6) after the originals are surrendered at origin the HBL is SURRENDERED and still answers its issued version, so a second debit note, for a document fee recorded after the surrender, finds it; (7) shipment B's job is billed on the sea waybill, with its number under the same master bill; (8) DOC cancels the waybill, a further note is refused with `FIN_NO_ISSUED_DOCUMENT` and the note already issued is unchanged; (9) a third shipment with a declared volume (800 kg and 2 m3) and an order under an agreement (a customer's SELL card and the line's BUY card for SEA LCL, OCEAN_FREIGHT per FREIGHT_TONNE and CFS_HANDLING per CBM) is rated by `RateFreightJob` from the cards by W/M and per CBM: the measurement 2.0 is charged, not the weight 0.8, so 2.0 freight tonnes and 2 CBM, and the RATED lines carry the amounts of a hand calculation (OCEAN_FREIGHT sell USD 100.00 and cost USD 50.00, CFS_HANDLING sell USD 50.00 and cost USD 30.00, at the estimate rate); RATE's snapshot holds the cargo and quantities FIN sent; the job is then billed on an HBL released EXPRESS with its own number under the same master bill. An order under a one-off quote is priced by its quote, and `RateFreightJob` refuses its job with `FIN_NOT_PRICEABLE`. What follows the note is shown with stand-ins only, because it does not depend on the document's kind: an HBL number of 16 characters and a master number of 35 are kept, a credit note, a receipt allocated to the note and the debtor statement showing the note under the HBL's number.
  - Not yet: the cost pools of an own-container consolidation (FIN opens pools for AIR_CONSOL and SEA_LCL_COLOAD only; V2.77 opens them for SEA_LCL_OWN) and apportionment by container; the end-to-end scenario with apportionment, consolidation profit and a shut-out; an FCL job kind (CostDriver v1 carries no container mode); the debit note does not store the document's kind; a shipment with two valid house documents is answered as an outage, not as `FIN_NO_ISSUED_DOCUMENT` (DOC allows one, so it cannot arise through DOC); billing while an HBL is being amended is not tested; the recorded carrier document (V2.75) plays no part in billing: the note's master number is the house document's own.
  - Tests: `TestTheOceanLineIsBilledWithEveryContext` (`internal/bootstrap`, stages 1 to 9 above) and, on PG16 with stand-ins, `TestSeaJobsAreBilledOnPostgres` (a SEA job opens as `SEA_EXPORT_LCL_HOUSE`; a note on a snapshot of kind HBL and one of kind HOUSE_SEA_WAYBILL keep the document id, version, number, master number and issue time; no house document, a DRAFT or APPROVED version, a document of another shipment or legal entity and a note before the document's issue time are refused with `FIN_NO_ISSUED_DOCUMENT`; the last case is the stand-in's answer, like DOC's, of not found, and does not exercise FIN's own comparison; an HBL number of 16 characters and a master number of 35 are kept, and a credit note, a receipt allocated to the note and the debtor statement follow it).
- V2.77 gives an own-container ocean consolidation its cost pool (FIN candidate 26, backlog FB3 for SEA_LCL_OWN; plan stage 2b-1). No migration.
  - Changed. Two lines of production code. `OpenPool` took the pool's mode from the kind PHY reports for the consolidation and knew `AIR_CONSOL` (mode AIR) and `SEA_LCL_COLOAD` (mode SEA_LCL); it now reads `SEA_LCL_OWN`, the consolidation the forwarder stuffs into its own container at a CFS, as mode SEA_LCL too. `RatePool`'s kind-to-mode map gets `SEA_LCL_OWN` as SEA, as `SEA_LCL_COLOAD` has. Before, the ocean line the cross-context tests run had no cost pool, no apportionment and no consolidation profit (`FIN_NOT_OPENABLE`). The comments that list the kinds a pool accepts say so. The owner decided on 2026-11-18 that such a pool takes the methods that exist, W/M by default as in CargoWise; PHY's `PhysicalCostBasis v1` already gave the quantities the methods need (planned and loaded packs, weight and volume per house); it does not let FIN tell a finished stuffing from one in progress, or a house shut out from one not yet loaded, and FIN does not read the load units it reports.
  - Proved. With stand-ins on PostgreSQL: the pool of a `SEA_LCL_OWN` consolidation opens in mode SEA_LCL; a line by CHARGEABLE is stage PLANNED while the houses are not loaded and is apportioned again at stage ACTUAL once they are, with W/M shares in milli freight tonnes (`MWM`) summing to the line; lines by VOLUME, PIECES and MANUAL; a house without volume (VOLUME gives it nothing, CHARGEABLE falls back to its weight) and consolidations whose houses have no volume at all (VOLUME is refused with `FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS`, CHARGEABLE still divides by weight); each job carries its shares and `GetConsolProfit` agrees with the jobs; a cancelled consolidation and another legal entity's are refused; a house loaded in part (stage ACTUAL on what is loaded so far; the pool is refused on closing until its lines are apportioned again on the completed basis, then closes); and RATE is asked in mode SEA for the planned scope of the houses. With the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY, BOOK, SEA, DOC and FIN: the ocean line of three LCL orders in one own container opens its pool before any lot is stuffed from PHY's cost basis (kind `SEA_LCL_OWN`), apportions the line's ocean freight at stage PLANNED on the planned scope and at stage ACTUAL on the loaded weights and volumes (hand calculations in the test, largest remainder in both USD and CNY), apportions a CFS charge by volume and a lashing charge by pieces, shows each job's pool cost and profit and the consolidation's agreeing (the houses' jobs carry the sell side only and the cost of the charges comes from the pool alone, so no charge is counted twice), rates a charge the pool does not carry by hand from the line's BUY card (`RateConsolCostPool`), and matches the line's invoice, apportions at the final cost and closes the pool. A second test with a consolidation of two planned containers, one house wholly in each and one split 4 + 2 over both, shows PHY's cost basis giving the split house's measures as the sums over the containers (each stuffing line takes its share of the lot's measure rounded down, so 799 kg and 1,999,999 cm³ of a lot of 800 kg and 2 m³), both load units, and the same apportionment as for one container.
  - Not yet: apportionment by container or by TEU and the per-unit loaded measures in `PhysicalCostBasis v1` (they wait for the list of methods CargoWise offers; the existing methods apportion over the whole consolidation, so two containers of unequal load factor share one pool line by W/M across all houses); a buy rate per container in RATE (RATE has no per-container unit; a container's cost is recorded as a flat line or rated per W/M or CBM); the scenario with a shut-out (2c); an FCL job kind.
  - Known limits found in review. A house received but left out of a sealed own container cannot leave the consolidation in PHY: a stuffing needs the consolidation CLOSED, and a sealed container forbids reopening it, while an air or co-load consolidation stays open while it is tendered, so a house can be detached there. FIN then keeps the stage PLANNED, the house left behind keeps a share by its planned measures, and the pool cannot be closed (`FIN_POOL_NOT_CLOSABLE`); MANUAL meets the same check and RETAINED is for a cancelled or empty consolidation only. (V2.78 lets the apportionment leave the house out.) The shut-out scenario (plan stage 2c) has to solve this first. The stage is ACTUAL as soon as every house has some packs loaded, also while a stuffing is still in progress (the rule of the design, the same for air); re-apportioning after loading completes corrects the shares, and a pool does not close on a stale basis. A cost line belongs to the consolidation, not to a container: with two containers the existing methods spread a container's cost over all houses (V2.78: unless the houses of the other container are left out of the line), MANUAL is the only way to put it on the houses in that container (V2.78: leaving the other houses out of the line does it with any method), and a house split over containers is counted by its summed measures. A job's own cost line and a pool line for the same charge are both counted: FIN does not reconcile a house's estimated cost with its share of the pool, so the books must carry the charge in one place. PLANNED uses the planned scope even after the CFS has weighed and measured the cargo.
  - Tests: in the model, `TestOpenPoolByConsolidationKind` (the three kinds with their modes; an empty and an invented kind refused) and `TestApportionWMOwnContainer`; on PostgreSQL with stand-ins, `TestOwnContainerPoolOnPostgres` and `TestOwnContainerPoolIsRatedOnPostgres`; with every context, `TestTheOceanConsolidationIsCostedWithEveryContext` and `TestTwoOwnContainersAndASplitHouseAreCostedWithPHYAndFIN` (`internal/bootstrap`).
- V2.78 lets an apportionment leave houses out, and adds the methods by gross weight and by shipment (FIN candidate 26, FB3; CargoWise's Is Used and its methods Gross Weight and Shipment; plan stage 2b-2). Migration fin/000012.
  - Changed. CargoWise apportions a consol cost line by its Apportionment Method over the sub-shipments whose Is Used box is ticked on that line, and a cost of one container is recorded as a line of its own and put on the houses in that container by unticking the others. FIN gave every live house a share of every line, so a cost that belongs to some houses only could be divided by MANUAL amounts only. `ApportionConsolCosts` now takes `NotUsed`, the shipments of live houses that take no part in the lines the command apportions (empty: every house takes part, as before; to leave different houses out of different lines, apportion the lines in separate commands). A shipment named twice, one that is not a live house of PHY's basis, every house left out, and `NotUsed` together with RETAINED are refused (`FIN_INVALID`). A house left out still has a share row on each line, marked not used, with quantity 0, no unit and amounts 0, so a line still names every live house once and the record shows who was left out; its open job is still needed (`FIN_POOL_JOB_MISSING`). The quantities and the amount are those of the houses that take part (largest remainder, ties to the first, the foreign and the local amount each conserved; `FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS` when their basis is all zero; a weight FIN cannot convert in a house left out is of no matter); MANUAL names the houses that take part exactly once and not the others (`FIN_MANUAL_SHARES`). The command's request hash covers `NotUsed` and leaves it out when empty, so a command recorded before hashes as it did. The API's view of a share says `NotUsed` (false for a house that took part, so a view reads as before). A job lists only the lines that used its house, and `GetConsolProfit` lists every house the lines name, a house left out of every line with pool cost zero and its own revenue and cost, so its figures still add up to the consolidation's. Two methods are added, as the line's default and as the command's `Method`: `GROSS_WEIGHT` divides by each house's gross weight in grams (planned at PLANNED, loaded at ACTUAL; unit `GRM`, now documented as chargeable or gross grams by the method; a weight that does not convert is `FIN_POOL_UNIT`) and `SHIPMENT` gives each house that takes part the same share (quantity 1, unit `SHP`; the minor units that do not divide go to the first houses in shipment order). CargoWise has seven methods: Manual, Shipment, Chargeable Units, Gross Weight, Container Count, TEU and Revenue. FIN has Manual, Shipment, Chargeable Units (CHARGEABLE, air chargeable weight or ocean W/M) and Gross Weight, and its own VOLUME, PIECES and RETAINED, which stay.
  - Stage and close. An apportionment is ACTUAL when every house that takes part has loaded packs and PLANNED otherwise: a house left out does not hold the stage back. Closing compares each line with PHY's current basis over the houses the line's latest apportionment used: its shares still name exactly the live houses, the houses used are all loaded and, for a weighed method, have their current quantities; a house not used is compared for its presence only. A pool whose lines leave out a house that was never loaded can close; a house attached or detached after the apportionment still refuses the close, and a line apportioned with nothing left out refuses it while any house is not loaded, as before.
  - Database. Migration `fin/000012_fin_apportionment_used`: a share's `used` (default true, which is right for every row written before: each house took part), with a check that a share not used has quantity 0, no unit and both amounts 0; the method checks of cost lines and of apportionment lines accept `GROSS_WEIGHT` and `SHIPMENT` beside the others (RETAINED stays on apportionment lines); the unit check accepts `SHP`; the conservation check at commit now also needs a line that is not RETAINED to have at least one share used; and the check at commit on a cancelled job counts shares used only. Three check constraints are replaced by wider ones and two functions are replaced; no table is rewritten and no row changes. The schema never tied a share's unit or quantity to its line's method, so none is added for the new methods. The share table stays immutable (the new column is part of an immutable row), written with its pool, under row-level security; the application role inserts shares already and needs no new grant.
  - Solved of V2.77's known limits: a container's cost goes to its own houses by leaving the others out, and a house left out of a sealed container bears nothing and no longer blocks the close (the cross-context test runs it with PHY). Not solved: the house is still attached in PHY, which can neither ship it nor release it; FIN does not know which houses are in which container, so the person apportioning names the houses to leave out; a filter per line as CargoWise's Apportionment Filter is not built.
  - Kept out until said otherwise. A house left out of a line stays out of it: a command that names no `NotUsed` keeps, line by line, the houses the line's latest apportionment left out (those still live; a house detached since is dropped, and a list that would leave no house taking part is refused), so re-apportioning after a cost changes does not charge the house left behind again; one command can carry different lists for different lines, and MANUAL takes the carried list. `AllHouses` takes every live house in again, another `NotUsed` replaces the list, and `AllHouses` with `NotUsed` is refused (so is `AllHouses` with RETAINED). Both are part of the command's request hash when given. This is the rule of CargoWise's Is Used, a tick that stays on the line. A house left out of a line bears nothing of it even if it is loaded later, and the pool closes so: the person apportioning decides who takes part (FIN does not know which container a house is in), and the design text that says a line is apportioned with every house loaded is no longer the rule.
  - Cancelling a job. A job whose only current shares are not used bears nothing of the pool, so its cancellation is allowed (the migration replaces the check at commit with one that looks at shares used only); a job with a share used is still refused. A house PHY still reports live needs an OPEN job for every apportionment that names it, as before, so after its job is cancelled the next apportionment is refused with `FIN_POOL_JOB_MISSING` until PHY reports the house gone.
  - The schema does not tie a share's unit or quantity to its line's method, for the new methods as for the old: FIN computes them from PHY's facts, which are not in the schema.
  - Not yet: Container Count, TEU and Revenue (they need each house's containers from PHY, and the houses' revenue per charge code); the per-unit loaded measures in `PhysicalCostBasis v1`; a default list of houses per cost line (the list is given with each apportionment); the shut-out scenario end to end (2c).
  - Tests: in the model (`pool_test.go`), `TestApportionNothingLeftOutKeepsEveryMethod`, `TestApportionLeavingOneOfThreeHousesOut`, `TestApportionBasisIsThatOfTheHousesThatTakePart`, `TestApportionNotUsedRefusals`, `TestApportionManualWithAHouseLeftOut`, `TestApportionStageIsThatOfTheHousesThatTakePart`, `TestApportionGrossWeightPlannedAndActual`, `TestApportionShipmentExtraMinorUnits`, `TestRecordCostAcceptsTheNewMethods`, `TestCloseOverTheHousesUsed` and `TestCloseWithNothingLeftOutNeedsEveryHouseLoaded`, `TestApportionKeepsTheHousesLeftOut`, `TestCarriedListDropsHousesNoLongerLive` and `TestStageOfAnApportionmentThatCarriesTheHousesLeftOut`; on PostgreSQL with stand-ins, `TestHousesLeftOutOfAnApportionmentOnPostgres` and `TestApportionmentSharesPastFINOnPostgres` and `TestHousesLeftOutStayOutOnPostgres`, `TestTheJobOfAHouseLeftOutIsCancelledOnPostgres`, `TestTwoLinesLeaveDifferentHousesOutOnPostgres` and `TestTheBasisIsThatOfTheHousesThatTakePartOnPostgres`, and the migration list of `cmd/cargo-migrate`; with every context, `TestAHouseLeftOutOfTheContainerBearsNoCostWithEveryContext` (`internal/bootstrap`).
- V2.79 makes PhysicalCostBasis v1 say which house is in which container (PHY candidate 23, the per-unit loaded measures of the contract; FIN carries them and uses none yet). No migration.
  - What the contract adds. Each house of the basis gains `Units`: one `HouseUnitLoad` (`UnitNo`, `ExecutionID`, `LoadedPacks`, `LoadedGrossWeight`, `LoadedVolume`) for every load unit whose stuffing holds live packs of the house, in unit order, measured as the house's totals are, for that stuffing alone. A house loaded only by a tender (air, co-load) has none. Where the units of measure allow the totals to be added, a house's entries sum to its totals; with mixed units the totals read zero and an entry keeps its own stuffing's measure (two lots of one house in one unit with different volume units make the entry's volume read zero too, so a zero volume cannot tell a lot with no volume from mixed units; changed in V2.84: weights and volumes in mixed units are converted to grams and cubic centimetres and added, in the total and in an entry, and only a volume not known for a line still reads zero). Each planned load unit (`PlannedLoadUnit`, in the cost basis and in the consolidation query alike) gains `ExecutionState` and `ExecutionRevision`, and the basis gains `PackingLists`, one `UnitPackingList` for every unit that has a stuffing, in unit order.
  - The stuffing's state and revision. `ExecutionState` is the state of the stuffing opened on the unit (empty while none) and `ExecutionRevision` the revision of that stuffing's own record (0 while none): it moves when the stuffing itself is written (opened, loading started, a load, an unload, a hold, its release, the seal). It does not see what changes the unit's figures from outside the stuffing: a lot measured again (also after the seal), more packs received of a lot, the consolidation reopened. It is not the version of the packing list. A unit never gets a second stuffing, so an execution id and its revision never repeat. A unit's `Status` (PLANNED, IN_USE, CLOSED, REMOVED) and the stuffing's `ExecutionState` (OPEN, LOADING, ON_HOLD, CLOSED) are two different things.
  - The packing list and its digest. A `UnitPackingList` has the unit, the execution id and state, the houses with live packs in the unit (`Lines`, in shipment order, the same figures as their `Units` entries, built from one computation) and a `Digest`: the SHA-256 of a canonical text written in the type's comment (the unit, the execution id, the state and each line's shipment, packs, weight and volume). Two bases give the same digest for a unit exactly when they give the same stuffing, state and lines for it, whatever changed them. It is computed on every read from what PHY stores; nothing is stored. It is for a reader that needs to know that what it would apportion on has changed, for instance between a preview and its confirmation.
  - It only grows. Existing fields keep their names, types and values, `Status` keeps its meaning, and PHY has no new command, rule or stored data.
  - FIN carries the facts and no rule uses them. The adapter fills `CostBasis.LoadUnits` (the units that are not removed), `CostBasis.PackingLists` (unit, execution, state and digest) and `HouseFacts.Units`; no apportionment, stage, check, close or API field reads them, so every apportionment gives what it gave.
  - What they are for. Apportionment by container count and by TEU needs each house's containers; putting a container's cost on the houses in it without naming them by hand needs to know who is in it; and FIN should not apportion on a packing list that is still changing, and should notice that a packing list changed between a preview and its confirmation.
  - Known limits found in review. Per-unit weights of one lot can add up to more than the lot when some of its packs were weighed for themselves in one unit and the others take their share of the lot's weight in another (a lot of 600 kg read 300 + 400 = 700 kg) (corrected in V2.82); a lot split 4 + 2 reads 533 + 266 = 799 of 800 kg by rounding down per stuffing (corrected in V2.82). This is how `HouseMeasure` has worked per execution since stuffings exist, the house's totals are the same sums, and it is not changed here (corrected in V2.82); a method that divides by weight per container would divide on these figures. The stuffing's revision does not move when a lot is measured again, more packs are received of a lot, or the consolidation is reopened; the digest does.
  - Not yet: the methods by container count and TEU; choosing the houses of a container automatically; FIN holding back while a container is reworked (PHY has no command to open a sealed container again yet (built in V2.85), so a unit's state only goes PLANNED, IN_USE, CLOSED today); a seal number or its history in the contract; a version of the packing list that survives a stuffing being replaced by another.
  - Tests: PHY on PostgreSQL (`phy_costbasis_units_test.go`): `TestTheCostBasisShowsEachHouseInEachUnit` (two containers, three houses, one split 4 + 2, hand calculations in its comment, stuffing ids that sort opposite to the unit numbers, the packing lists' lines equal to the houses' entries), `TestAUnitsPackingListHasADigestOfWhatItHolds` (the digest changes with a load, an unload, a hold and the seal and is recomputed from the canonical text), `TestAPackingListChangesWithoutTheStuffingRevisionMoving` (a lot measured again after the seal; more packs received of a lot), `TestMixedVolumeUnitsInOneUnitReadZero` (renamed `TestMixedVolumeUnitsInOneUnitAreAddedInCubicCentimetres` in V2.84, which changed its figure: the entry's and the house's volume were zero and are 1,500,000 CMQ) and `TestATenderedHouseHasNoUnitEntries`; FIN's adapter (`physical_test.go`): `TestTheAdapterCarriesTheLoadUnitsAndEachHousesUnitLoads` and `TestABasisWithoutUnitsOrEntriesMapsToNone`; across contexts (`ocean_pool_integration_test.go`): `TestTheCostBasisTellsWhichHouseIsInWhichContainerWithPHYAndFIN`, whose apportionment gives the shares of the existing two-container test.
- V2.80 apportions by container count and by TEU (FIN candidate 26, FB3; CargoWise's Container Count and TEU; plan stage 2b-3). Migration fin/000013.
  - What they divide by. `CONTAINER_COUNT` divides by the number of containers each house is in (unit `CNT`) and `TEU` by the tenths of a TEU of those containers (unit `DTE`: a 20-foot container is 10, a 40-foot 20), over the houses that take part. A house's containers are the distinct load units of its `Units` entries in PHY's cost basis, and a unit's tenths are those of the same unit in the basis's `LoadUnits`: the per-unit loads V2.79 carried and nothing used. The amount is divided with the same largest-remainder rule as every method (ties to the first, the foreign and the local amount each conserved). Both are accepted as a cost line's default method and as the command's `Method`.
  - Loaded and sealed, and why. A house's containers are known once it is stuffed: before that PHY has planned units, but no plan says which house goes into which container. The other methods fall back to the planned scope at PLANNED; these do not, so a line by one of them at stage PLANNED (a house that takes part has no loaded packs) is refused with `FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS`. Stage ACTUAL is not enough: it means only that every house that takes part has some packs loaded. If a house is split over two containers and only the first is stuffed and sealed, the house would be counted in one container, the pool would close on that, and the figure would be wrong once its other packs went into the second container (the case found in review, with the real PHY). So the two methods also need every load unit of the consolidation sealed (status `CLOSED` in PHY, the unit's stuffing closed), and are refused with `FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS` naming the first unit that is `PLANNED` or `IN_USE`; a unit that was planned and never opened is removed in PHY first, but a unit on which a stuffing was opened and that holds nothing can be neither sealed nor removed in this version of PHY (PHY has no command that gives up an empty stuffing), and until it has one the lines of that pool are apportioned by another method (`MANUAL` takes any division, the count or the TEU worked out by hand). This does not require a house to be wholly loaded: packs of a house left outside every sealed container count for nothing, and whether they still travel is PHY's matter. A command that is refused for one of its lines is refused as a whole, and nothing is stored: a command that names no `LineNos` takes every line, so on a pool with a line by one of these methods it is refused until the containers are sealed; name the other lines to apportion them earlier. A house left out (V2.78) does not hold the stage back, as for the other methods. At the close the quantities are computed again from PHY's current basis, as for the other weighed methods, with the same two rules: a line by either method refuses the close (`FIN_POOL_NOT_CLOSABLE`) once a house that takes part is in other containers (an entry appeared or went, or it moved to a unit of another TEU for `TEU`; a move between two units of equal TEU does not change a `TEU` quantity, and a move between units does not change a count) or a unit is planned or being loaded, and closes when nothing changed.
  - The refusals. `FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS` at PLANNED, while a load unit is not sealed, and when no house that takes part is in a container (an air consolidation, or a co-load loaded by tender: every quantity is zero). An entry without loaded packs is not a container the house is in (PHY gives none; FIN skips one). A house that takes part and is loaded but has no `Units` entry while others have gets quantity 0 and a zero share, as a house with no volume under `VOLUME`; it cannot happen in an own-container consolidation, where loading is stuffing. `FIN_POOL_UNIT` only for `TEU`, only for a unit that a house that takes part is in, when the basis's `LoadUnits` lack that unit or give it without a positive TEU: PHY's contract does not let that happen, and an inconsistent basis is not divided on silently. `CONTAINER_COUNT` does not read the tenths. Both methods are accepted as a cost line's default by `RecordConsolCost` and by `RateConsolCostPool` (its `Method` applies to every rated line); the command's `Method` on `ApportionConsolCosts` takes them as well.
  - Equal shares for houses in one container. Houses in the same containers get the same quantity, so for LCL in one container both methods give what `SHIPMENT` gives. They matter when the houses are spread unequally over containers: a house in two containers bears twice the count of a house in one, and by TEU the more of the larger containers it is in, the more it bears.
  - CargoWise's seven methods. FIN now has six of them: Manual, Shipment, Chargeable Units, Gross Weight, Container Count and TEU, besides its own `VOLUME`, `PIECES` and `RETAINED`. Revenue is next (built in V2.81).
  - Database. Migration `fin/000013_fin_container_methods`: the method checks of cost lines and of apportionment lines accept `CONTAINER_COUNT` and `TEU` beside the others (RETAINED stays on apportionment lines), and the unit check accepts `CNT` and `DTE`. Three check constraints are replaced by wider ones, keeping every value they accepted; no table is rewritten, no row changes, and there is no function, column or grant change. As for the earlier methods, the schema does not tie a share's unit or quantity to its line's method. Each `ALTER TABLE` takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on its table and checks the existing rows against the new constraint (one scan per table; the pool tables are small), as `000012` did.
  - Not yet: a PHY command that gives up an empty stuffing (built in V2.82), without which one opened by mistake keeps a pool out of these two methods; Revenue (built in V2.81); choosing the houses of a container automatically (who takes part is still what the command and the line's carried list say); a planned allocation of houses to containers, which would let these methods work before stuffing; an FCL job kind.
  - Tests: in the model (`pool_test.go`), `TestApportionByContainerCountAndTEU` (1 : 1 : 2 and 10 : 20 : 30 against a hand calculation, both currencies conserved), `TestContainerMethodsGiveShipmentShareForOneContainer`, `TestContainerMethodsNeedTheActualStage`, `TestContainerMethodsNeedEverySealedContainer`, `TestCloseRefusesANewUnitNotSealed`, `TestContainerMethodsSkipAnEntryWithoutLoadedPacks`, `TestContainerMethodsWithAHouseHalfStuffed`, `TestContainerMethodsCountDistinctUnits`, `TestContainerMethodsNeedContainers`, `TestContainerMethodsInconsistentBasis`, `TestContainerMethodsHouseWithoutAnEntry`, `TestContainerMethodsWithAHouseLeftOut`, `TestCloseByContainerCountAndTEU`, `TestExistingMethodsOnABasisWithUnits` and `TestRecordCostAcceptsTheContainerMethods`; on PostgreSQL (`container_methods_postgres_test.go`) `TestApportionmentByContainerCountAndTEUOnPostgres` and `TestContainerMethodsPastFINOnPostgres`; the migration list in `cmd/cargo-migrate`; and with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN (`ocean_pool_integration_test.go`) `TestCostsAreApportionedByContainerCountAndTEUWithPHYAndFIN` and `TestContainerMethodsNeedEverySealedContainerWithPHYAndFIN` (a house split over two containers, one still loading: refused until both are sealed; a line by SHIPMENT is accepted in between), and the adapter's `TestAClosedUnitArrivesAsTheDomainConstant`; the first of the two: a 20-foot and a 40-foot container (PHY answers 10 and 20 tenths), a line by TEU refused before the stuffing, then container count 1 : 1 : 2, TEU 10 : 20 : 30 and, with one house left out, 10 : 30, and the pool closes.
- V2.81 apportions by revenue (FIN candidate 26, FB3; CargoWise's Revenue; plan stage 2b-4). Migration fin/000014.
  - What it divides by. `REVENUE` divides a cost line in proportion to the revenue of the same charge code on each house's freight job: a line of AIR_FREIGHT is weighed by what each house's job sells under AIR_FREIGHT and by nothing else, and each line of a command is weighed by its own code. A job's revenue under a code is the sum of its charge lines' sell sides of that code, in the functional currency (minor units), over the houses that take part. The share records the revenue it used: its quantity is that revenue and its unit is `REV`. It is accepted as a cost line's default method, by `RateConsolCostPool` and as the command's `Method`. FIN reads nothing from another context for it: the jobs are FIN's own, read in the command's transaction under the locks it already takes on them.
  - The estimate rate, and what an invoice does not change. The revenue is taken at the ESTIMATE rate, the rate every estimate is converted at: it is each sell side's local amount as it was converted when that version was written, at the ESTIMATE rate in force at its business time (`side` in `job.go` converts with `RateEstimate` at the time of the command). A rate recorded later does not move it; a revision of the side, or a re-rating or re-evaluation that writes a new version, converts again and does move it, and the close then asks for a new apportionment. A debit note converts a foreign sell side at the invoice or the accounting rate, so the job's profit follows the note once a line is invoiced, but an apportionment must not move because someone issued an invoice. A sell side that is invoiced is therefore read through the version the debit note replaced, the latest version before it that is not invoiced, which has the same amount at the estimate rate; a credit note returns a side to a copy of that estimate. The two figures agree while nothing is invoiced and for a line in the functional currency. A side with no version before its invoice cannot be written by `IssueDebitNote`; if a stored job had one its own local amount would be used. A line with no sell side, or a zero one, adds nothing.
  - Zero, all zero and negative. A house with no revenue under the code gets a zero share (as a house with no volume under `VOLUME`). When no house that takes part has any, the apportionment is refused as `FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS`, the message naming the charge code. A job's revenue under a code can be negative, because a quote's discount line is a negative sell side (`ApplyBasis`, `ReviseSide`) and may share a code with the charge it discounts; a cost is never divided on a negative weight, so the apportionment is refused as `FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS` naming the house and the code. The jobs' revenue is read only when a line of the command is by REVENUE, so the other methods read what they read before.
  - Both stages. `REVENUE` does not read PHY's measures, so it is accepted at PLANNED and at ACTUAL, and the sealed rule of the container methods does not apply to it. The apportionment's stage is what it is for every method (PLANNED while a house that takes part is not loaded), and the close still wants ACTUAL: a line apportioned by REVENUE at PLANNED must be apportioned again once every house that takes part is loaded before the pool can close, as a line by any method must. The houses left out and the carried list work as for every method: the revenue is that of the houses that take part. Every house that takes part still needs its open job.
  - The close. Revenue that changes later does not re-apportion anything by itself. At the close of the pool each share of a line apportioned by REVENUE is compared with its job's revenue under the line's code now, as the other methods' quantities are compared with PHY's basis now, and the close is refused (`FIN_POOL_NOT_CLOSABLE`, naming the line) if any differs: a sell side revised, a sell line added under the code, a sell side written off or revised to zero, a house that had none now selling. The line is apportioned again and the pool closes. An invoice issued for a house's job does not refuse it, and neither does a change in the revenue of a house left out. The comparison also refuses a used house the jobs do not answer for; this is a safeguard no command reaches, because the database refuses cancelling a job that holds a current used share (the commit trigger of `fin/000012`), so the open jobs of the houses that take part are always there. The close locks the houses' jobs when a line's latest apportionment is by REVENUE, and only then (also when that line's final cost is zero or the consolidation is gone, where it compares nothing: more locks than needed, never a wrong result): after the pool and the matches and before the policy, in job id order, as `ApportionPoolCosts` does, so the two cannot wait for each other; any other pool closes with the locks and the queries it had.
  - CargoWise's seven methods. FIN now has all seven: Manual, Shipment, Chargeable Units, Gross Weight, Container Count, TEU and Revenue, besides its own `VOLUME`, `PIECES` and `RETAINED`.
  - Database. Migration `fin/000014_fin_revenue_method`: the method checks of cost lines and of apportionment lines accept `REVENUE` beside the others (RETAINED stays on apportionment lines), and the unit check accepts `REV`. Three check constraints are replaced by wider ones, keeping every value they accepted; no table is rewritten, no row changes, and there is no function, column or grant change. As for the earlier methods, the schema does not tie a share's unit or quantity to its line's method. Each `ALTER TABLE` takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on its table and checks the existing rows against the new constraint (one scan per table; the pool tables are small), as `000013` did.
  - Not yet: revenue at a rate other than the estimate rate, or in a currency other than the functional one; a revenue weight that counts only invoiced sell sides; choosing the houses of a container automatically; a PHY command that gives up an empty stuffing (built in V2.82). The close wants stage ACTUAL for every method, so a line apportioned by REVENUE at PLANNED is apportioned again once the houses are loaded, as the others are.
  - Tests: in the model, `TestEstimatedRevenueByCode` (`billing_test.go`: a side never invoiced, invoiced at the invoice and at the accounting rate, credited and returned, revised and invoiced again, written off, two lines of one code summed, a line in the functional currency, an invoiced side with nothing before it) and, in `pool_test.go`, `TestApportionByRevenue` (60,000 : 30,000 : 10,000 over 10,001, hand calculation in its comment, both currencies conserved, quantity and unit recorded), `TestApportionByRevenueEachLineByItsOwnCode`, `TestApportionByRevenueRefusals`, `TestApportionByRevenueAtBothStages`, `TestApportionByRevenueWithAHouseLeftOutAndCarried`, `TestCloseByRevenue`, `TestExistingMethodsIgnoreRevenue` and `TestRecordCostAcceptsRevenue`; on PostgreSQL (`revenue_method_postgres_test.go`) `TestApportionmentByRevenueOnPostgres` (a debit note at the invoice rate does not refuse the close; a sell side revised, a line added and a side revised to zero each do until the line is apportioned again; replay), `TestRevenueMethodPastFINOnPostgres` and `TestApportionByRevenueRacesASellRevision` (since V2.91 `TestPreviewByRevenueRacesASellRevision`); the migration list in `cmd/cargo-migrate`; and with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN (`ocean_pool_integration_test.go`) `TestCostsAreApportionedByRevenueWithPHYAndFIN` (three houses with quotes of USD 600.00, 300.00 and 100.00 apportioned 6 : 3 : 1 before any stuffing and again at ACTUAL, a code no job sells refused, one sell revised: close refused, apportioned again, closed).
- V2.82 makes a lot's measure over its containers add up and lets an empty stuffing be given up (PHY candidate 23; found by the reviews of V2.79 and V2.80). Migration phy/000006.
  - The defect. `HouseMeasure` worked out a house's loaded weight and volume for one execution alone, so a lot loaded in two containers was measured twice over. A lot of 600 kg and 6 packs with 2 packs weighed for themselves at 300 kg in the first container and the other 4 loaded in the second read 600 x 4/6 = 400 kg in the second: 700 kg of a lot of 600 kg. A lot of 800 kg and 6 packs loaded 4 and 2, all apportioned, read 533 + 266 = 799 kg, each stuffing rounding down by itself. The house's totals in the cost basis are the sums of these, so every apportionment by weight or volume read them.
  - The rule. Over all the executions that hold live packs of a lot: lines measured for themselves count as measured, as before; the lot's remaining measure is its latest measurement less what all those lines took (never below zero) and its remaining packs its packs received less theirs; the lot's apportioned live packs, over all executions together, take of the remaining measure their share of the remaining packs (all of it when they complete the lot); and that amount is divided over the executions by their apportioned live packs by the largest remainder, equal remainders going to the lower load unit number and then the lower execution id, so that the parts add up to it exactly. Weight and volume each. The 600 kg lot now reads 300 + 300 (the 300 kg left for the 4 packs that complete it) and the 800 kg lot 533 + 267 (533.33 and 266.67: the kilogram that rounding down leaves goes to the larger remainder). When all of a lot's packs are loaded and its own measurements do not exceed its measure, the containers add up to the lot's measurement exactly. A container's figure for a lot is its part of the lot's measure as the lot is loaded now, so it can change after the container is sealed: when another container loads or unloads packs of the lot, when packs of the lot are measured for themselves elsewhere, or when the lot is measured again. A lot of 6 packs at 301 kg with 5 packs in a sealed container reads 250 kg there, and 251 kg once a second container takes the sixth pack; the packing list's digest changes with it. The digest is there for a reader that keeps it (none does yet: the step that holds an apportionment back while a container is reworked will); FIN's close comparison today notices a moved figure because the house's recorded quantity differs from the one PHY's basis gives now, and asks for a new apportionment (the same as for a lot measured again after the seal). A test pins this. Lines measured for themselves are believed: when they exceed the measure nothing is left for the other packs and the sum exceeds the lot, as before. The volume rules stay per result (a volume not known for a line, or in mixed units, reads zero for that container only; changed in V2.84: volumes in mixed units are converted to cubic centimetres and added, and a line measured for itself is converted before it is taken off its lot's measure, so only a volume not known for a line reads zero).
  - What changes and what does not. With one execution holding a lot's packs the figures are exactly those of V2.81, to the gram: every test with one execution per lot is unchanged, and a pure test keeps the V2.81 function as the oracle. What changes is the per-container and per-tender weight and volume of a lot split over several executions, in the cost basis (the houses' totals, their `Units` entries, the packing lists and so their digests) and in the tender evidence of a shipment's physical facts, which now answer the same figure for the same execution and house. A shipment is live in one consolidation only and its packs leave a consolidation before it does, so the executions of the consolidation (cost basis) and the executions with lines of the shipment (physical facts) are each every execution that can hold live packs of the lot. The division is computed on every read in `AllotLots` (a pure function over the lot's lines in several executions); nothing is stored. The contract keeps its shape and its comments are corrected. The check of a stuffing against its container's limits still measures the stuffing alone.
  - The command. `CancelLoadExecution` (command id, tenant, actor, execution id, expected revision, reason of at most 500 characters, occurred at) gives up a stuffing that holds no live packs: a STUFFING that is OPEN, LOADING or ON_HOLD with nothing loaded or every loaded pack unloaded again; a stuffing with live packs, a sealed container, a tender and a stuffing already cancelled are refused (rejected: invalid execution or invalid transition, the message saying which rule), as is a missing reason or a business time before the execution's last change. It is authorised as the other execution commands are, on the execution's consolidation. The execution becomes CANCELLED, terminal: it keeps its lines, reversals and holds as history, records who cancelled it, when and why (shown by `GetLoadExecution`), and takes no further command. A hold still placed ends with it and stays recorded as placed. Carrier evidence and discrepancies follow a closed tender, so a stuffing that can be given up has none. The event is `LoadExecutionCancelled.v1`, carrying the load unit as the opening's does (the reason, free text, stays out of it). Executions are still never deleted.
  - The unit afterwards. A unit is PLANNED again: its status, and `ExecutionID`, `ExecutionState` and `ExecutionRevision` in the consolidation query and the cost basis, come from its stuffing that is not cancelled (none: empty and zero). A cancelled stuffing has no per-unit entries and no packing list. The unit takes a new stuffing (a new execution id: ids are never reused, so an execution id and its revision still never repeat) or is removed with `RemovePlannedLoadUnit`; a unit has at most one stuffing that is not cancelled at any time. A consolidation with only cancelled stuffings is cancelled or reconciled as one with none.
  - The database. Migration `phy/000006_phy_cancelled_stuffing`: the execution's state check accepts CANCELLED, and four columns (who, when in business and entered time, why) are set exactly when it is; a cancelled execution was a STUFFING; `load_execution_one_per_unit` is unique over the stuffings that are not CANCELLED; a stuffing is cancelled only from OPEN, LOADING or ON_HOLD and with no live packs (the trigger counts lines less reversals under the execution's row lock, which a load takes too); a cancelled execution changes no more and takes no line, reversal, hold, release of a hold or evidence; a unit is removed only when all its stuffings are cancelled; a consolidation is cancelled or reconciled with only closed or cancelled executions; a reopen, a new stuffing of the unit and the unit's removal are dated not before a cancellation of a stuffing of the unit. The dated-not-before-the-last-change rule of the command stays the domain's, as it is for the other execution commands and for a seal: a cancellation is dated against the execution's own last change and not against the consolidation's versions, as `Start` and the hold commands are, so one dated before a later reopen is accepted. A cancellation changes nothing of the row but the state, the four columns, `revision` and `updated_at`. Four functions of earlier migrations are replaced with every rule they had (the header names them), and the application role's column grant on `load_execution` in the workflow gains the four columns. No existing row changes and no table is rewritten.
  - FIN: words only. The message of a container method refused for a unit not sealed, and the comments of `containerQuantities` and `ApportionConsolCosts`, no longer say that a stuffing opened and left empty needs another method; the message is one sentence for the three cases of a unit not sealed: a container still being stuffed is sealed in PHY, a stuffing opened and left empty is given up in PHY (`CancelLoadExecution`), a unit never opened is removed in PHY. No FIN rule changes.
  - Not yet: tenders are out of scope (an empty tender has no unit to free and closes with its receipt even when nothing is tendered, so it has not the gap of an empty stuffing); opening a sealed container again (built in V2.85); the container limits check still measures a stuffing alone (corrected in V2.83), so for a lot split over containers it can read more than the allotted figure (a hold the allotted figure would not ask for: 2 packs weighed at 300 kg in one container leave 300 kg of a 600 kg lot for the 4 in the other, which alone reads 400 kg) or less (an overweight missed: 2 packs weighed at 100 kg and 4 apportioned in one container, 4 apportioned in the other, of 10 packs and 1,000 kg: the other alone reads 400 kg, the allotted figure is 450 kg); the comment of `HoldIfOverLimits` has the working.
  - Tests: pure (`allotment_test.go`): `TestALotsMeasureOverTwoContainersAddsUp` (the two cases above), `TestAllotmentTiesAndRemainders`, `TestAPartlyLoadedLotAllotsItsShare`, `TestLinesMeasuredForThemselvesInTwoContainers`, `TestMeasuredPacksAreBelieved`, `TestVolumeUnknownInOneContainerOnly` (its mixed-units part changed in V2.84: a line of 1 MTQ beside a lot of 600 CMQ read no volume for its container and 300 - 1 = 299 cm3 for the other; they read 1,000,000 CMQ and 0 CMQ), `TestALotWithNoMeasurementHasNothingToAllot`, `TestAllotmentOfOneExecutionIsTodaysMeasure` and `TestRandomSplitsAddUp`; and the command (`cancel_test.go`): `TestCancelAStuffingWithNoLivePacks`, `TestCancelAStuffingThatHeldPacksAndNoLongerDoes`, `TestCancelRefusals`, `TestACancelledStuffingTakesNoFurtherCommand`, `TestACancelledStuffingIsRestoredOnlyAsOne` and `TestACancelledStuffingHoldsNothing`. On PostgreSQL: `phy_lot_measure_test.go` (`TestALotsMeasureOverTwoContainersOnPostgres`: 300 + 300 and 533 + 267, unload and reload between containers, a lot measured again after the seals, the cost basis, the packing lists and the shipment's physical facts answering one figure) and `phy_cancel_stuffing_test.go` (`TestAnEmptyStuffingIsGivenUp`, the two probes of the review of V2.80 each ending with the unit removed or stuffed again; `TestGivingUpAStuffingIsRefusedOutsideItsRules`; `TestACancelledStuffingDatabaseRules`; `TestAConsolidationEndsOverCancelledStuffings`; `TestTheOrderOfTimesAroundACancellation` (a new stuffing of a unit and its removal dated not before the cancellation, by the commands and past PHY), `TestOneExecutionPerLotReadsTheFiguresOfBeforeOnPostgres` (one execution per lot: both callers answer the whole lots' figures) and the races `TestACancelRacesALoadIntoTheSameStuffing`, `TestTheDatabaseAloneKeepsALoadAndACancellationApart` and `TestACancelRacesTheOpeningOfASecondStuffing`). Across contexts (`ocean_pool_integration_test.go`): `TestASplitHousesWeightIsItsLotsWithPHYAndFIN` and `TestAnEmptyStuffingIsGivenUpAndThePoolClosesWithPHYAndFIN`; the migration list in `cmd/cargo-migrate`. Three existing assertions on the per-container figures of a split lot were changed to the new figures: `TestTheCostBasisShowsEachHouseInEachUnit`, `TestTwoOwnContainersAndASplitHouseAreCostedWithPHYAndFIN` and `TestTheCostBasisTellsWhichHouseIsInWhichContainerWithPHYAndFIN`.
- V2.83 makes the container limit check read the allotted figure (PHY candidate 23; found by the review of V2.82). No migration.
  - The two errors of before. The check (`HoldIfOverLimits`) measured a stuffing by itself while the weights PHY publishes are allotted over all the stuffings of a lot. It could read more than the allotted figure: a lot of 6 packs and 42,900 kg with 2 packs weighed at 21,450 kg in container 1 and 4 apportioned in container 2 read 42,900 x 4/6 = 28,600 kg in container 2, over the 28,230 kg of cargo of a 22G1 (a hold without need); the allotted figure is the 21,450 kg the weighed packs leave. And less: a lot of 6 packs and 50,000 kg with 1 pack weighed at 2,000 kg and 2 apportioned in container 1 and 3 apportioned in container 2 read 50,000 x 3/6 = 25,000 kg in container 2 (no hold), the allotted figure is 48,000 x 3/5 = 28,800 kg (an overweight missed). Nothing re-ran the check on a container when a load, an unload or a measurement elsewhere moved its figure.
  - The rule. The container's cargo is the sum over its live lines: lines measured for themselves as measured, apportioned lines at their part of the lot's measure as `AllotLots` divides it over every execution that holds live packs of the lot; each weight and volume is converted to grams and cubic centimetres, rounded up, added and capped, and a measure in a unit that cannot be converted is "limits not checked", as before. One function (`CheckLimits`) works the figures out for the hold and for the cost basis's `Limits`, so those two agree with each other. They do not always equal the sum of the entries the cost basis publishes for the container's houses (`HouseMeasureAllotted`): a house with a line or a lot measure without a volume beside others with one publishes no volume while the check counts the volume that is known, so the check can read more volume than the published figure and never less (changed in V2.84: a house with volumes in different units is converted line by line like the check); the check converts every line and rounds each up, while the published figure of a house whose lines share one unit is added as it is; and a house with lines in different weight units is published as the amounts added under the unit of its last line, which the check does not do (corrected in V2.84: it is converted and added in grams, as the check does; see the latent defect below). With one stuffing per lot the outcome, the hold and its text are exactly those of before: a pure test keeps V2.82's `unitCargo` as the oracle, on a table and on 3,000 generated cases.
  - When it runs. Every command that can move a lot's allotted figures is followed by the check of the stuffings of the lot that are LOADING or ON_HOLD: a load, an unload, a receipt of more packs of the lot and a measurement. A seal changes no line and a cancelled stuffing has none, so neither moves a figure (reasoned, not run). A receipt only lowers the apportioned figures by exact arithmetic, but through the rounding of the largest-remainder division one unit of the plan's measure can pass from one container to another (a lot of 7 packs weighed at 4 kg with 3, 3 and 1 packs apportioned in three containers: 2, 2 and 0 kg become 1, 1 and 1 kg once an eighth pack is received), and the unit is a cubic metre when the lot's volume is in MTQ: a container at its capacity can be pushed over by a receipt. The hold on another stuffing has the evidence, the actor and the time of the command that caused it, and its own event.
  - Who is held. The stuffing a load goes into is held whenever it is over, as before this version, and so is every stuffing after a measurement: the measurement addresses the lot of each of them, and that has been so since V2.58, so it is the one place where a hold a person released can come back without a rise of the figure. In every other case (the other stuffings after a load, every stuffing after an unload, the one unloaded from included, and the stuffings after a receipt) a stuffing is held only for a dimension the command raised into excess: it is over the maximum gross and its grams are greater than before the command, or it is over the capacity and its cubic centimetres are greater than before, or it is not checked now and could be checked before (the text of the hold names the dimension that rose). So a hold a person released does not come back because of a command on another container that left its figure where it was. A search of 2.5 million cases here, and 1.5 million of the reviewer, found no unload that raises the figure of the container it comes out of.
  - The sealed container and the `Limits` field. A sealed container takes no hold. The command that pushes its allotted figure over the limits is not refused (a measurement is a fact, and the container's weight of record is the terminal's VGM): `UnitPackingList.Limits` (`UnitLimitsCheck`: checked or the unit not converted, cargo in grams and cubic centimetres, over the maximum gross, over the capacity) says it, for every unit that has a packing list in any state, worked out on every read. It is not part of the digest, whose canonical text is untouched. Nothing in the repository reads `Limits` yet (FIN copies the identity fields and the digest of a packing list), and no event says that a sealed container went over: it is seen by whoever reads the cost basis.
  - The conflict. A load, an unload or a receipt of a lot now writes a hold on another stuffing, whose row another command may hold while it waits for the lot this command holds: a deadlock if this one waited. The other stuffings of the lot that are LOADING or ON_HOLD are locked after the lot, without waiting (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, and all must be got), because reading them unlocked would let two commands on two lots of one container each conclude that the container is not over. If one is busy the command answers `ErrConflict` ("phy: conflicts with the stored state", the message naming the busy stuffing: "load execution X-1 is busy with another command; try again") at once, even if no hold would have been placed, and writes nothing; the caller tries again. No caller in this repository retries by itself (a retry in the command path since V2.84), and two callers that load one lot into two containers in a tight loop can keep one of them waiting: the reviewer saw one container answered 328 conflicts in 8 seconds while the other loaded. A receipt of a lot conflicts whenever another command holds one of the open stuffings that hold the lot, and under a stress of loaders, unloaders and a measurement loop on those stuffings the reviewer's receipt loop got 969 conflicts and no success in ten seconds, without a deadlock or a database error (only a lot that some open stuffing holds is concerned). A conflict comes before a business rejection: the stuffings are locked before the change is applied, so that the figures of before can be read, and a load of more packs than were received answers the conflict while another stuffing of the lot is busy, and the rejection only when none is. A load into a container that holds no packs of the lot still needs the other containers that hold some. A stuffing that is CLOSED is never locked: it is never written, so it is read for the allotment and not locked.
