A receipt locks the lot as before and then these stuffings; a lot nobody has loaded into a stuffing locks nothing else. The measurement locks the stuffings of the lot before the lot, in id order, as before, and needs nothing more. The lock order: a load and an unload take the consolidation (shared), their own execution, the house's ledger and the lot, then the other open stuffings without waiting; a measurement takes the consolidations (shared), the stuffings in id order and the lot; a receipt takes the lot, then the open stuffings without waiting. So none of them waits while it holds a lot.
  - Latent defect, outside this change (corrected in V2.84). `HouseMeasure` adds the amounts of a house's lines under the unit of the last line without converting them, and PHY has no guard that a lot's measurement unit equals the unit of the house's other lots: a lot's measurement and the apportioned lines drawn from it are in the unit of the plan the lot was opened under, and nothing compares that with the house's other lots. FUL as built does not send such a plan: it refuses an obligation in another unit (`CargoScope.add`, `ful/internal/domain/plan.go:138-157`), the release of the last obligation (`ErrLastObligation`, `shipment.go:38` and `378`) and a part in other units than its obligation's (`ObligationRef.fits`, `shipment.go:129`), and LoadIntent v1 takes the unit from the plan's scope (`ful/module.go:213`). So the case is latent, and `TestLatentDefectPHYAcceptsTwoWeightUnitsInOneHousesLinesAndPublishesTheirAmountsAdded` builds it with a stand-in for FUL to document what PHY does if it is sent: 3,000 KGM and 3 TNE are published as 3,003 TNE, while the check and `Limits` read 6,000,000 g. A reader that took such a published figure would read it wrong (FIN converts the amount under its unit for the weight methods). It is not fixed here.
  - What does not change. `HouseMeasure`, `AllotLots` and the published figures; the hold's reason and text; who may release a hold, and the seal's rules (a stuffing whose OVERWEIGHT hold was released may be sealed); tenders, which have no limits. FIN reads nothing new.
  - Not yet: a retry of a conflict in the command path (built in V2.84); reading the figure of a container from `Limits` anywhere (an event or a reader for a sealed container that went over); the container's plate (SEA's VGM) against the cost basis's figure; a guard in PHY that a lot's unit equals its house's, or a conversion of a house's weights in `HouseMeasure` (corrected in V2.84).
  - Tests: pure (`limits_allotted_test.go`): `TestTheLimitCheckReadsMoreThanTheAllottedFigureNoLonger` and `TestTheLimitCheckReadsLessThanTheAllottedFigureNoLonger` (the two errors, weight and capacity, with the hand calculation), `TestTheLimitCheckOfAnUnconvertedUnit`, `TestOneStuffingPerLotReadsTheFigureOfBefore` (the oracle), `TestTheLimitCheckSaysOverForASealedContainerThatIsNotHeld` (loading, on hold and sealed), `TestAReceiptCanMoveOneUnitOfTheMeasureIntoAnotherContainer` (the example above), `TestAnUnloadNeverRaisesTheContainerItComesOutOf` (the search), `TestAContainerExactlyAtItsLimitsIsNotOver`, `TestAStuffingIsHeldWhenTheCommandRaisedItsCargo` and `TestAStuffingIsHeldOnlyForTheDimensionThatRaisedIntoExcess`. On PostgreSQL (`phy_limits_allotted_test.go`): `TestTheLimitCheckReadsTheAllottedFigureOnPostgres`, `TestALoadIntoAnotherContainerHoldsTheOneItPushesOver`, `TestAnUnloadHoldsTheOtherContainerItPushesOver`, `TestALotMeasuredAgainHoldsTheContainersByTheAllottedFigure`, `TestASealedContainerPushedOverIsNotHeldAndSaysSo`, `TestTheAllotmentCountsTheSealedContainersPacks`, the deterministic lock tests `TestALoadOrAnUnloadDoesNotWaitForAnotherStuffingsLock` and `TestTwoLoadsOfOneLotQueuedBehindItsLock` and the race `TestLoadsOfOneLotIntoTwoContainersAtOnceRace`; and (`phy_limits_raised_test.go`) `TestAReleasedHoldDoesNotComeBackFromAnotherContainersLoad`, `TestAReleasedHoldDoesNotComeBackFromAnUnload`, `TestAReceiptHoldsAContainerItPushesOver` (a volume in MTQ), `TestAReceiptDoesNotHoldAgainAContainerWhoseFigureFell`, `TestAReceiptDoesNotHoldAgainAContainerOverInWeightWhoseVolumeRoseByRounding`, `TestASealedStuffingIsReadAndNeverLocked`, `TestAPartlyBusySetOfStuffingsMakesTheLoadConflict` (three containers), `TestTheCheckReadsTheLotsOfTheContainerThatWereNotChanged` and the latent defect above. The existing limit tests (`TestStuffingHoldsOverTheContainersLimits`, `TestStuffingIsCheckedAtThePublishedMeasure`, `TestStuffingRemeasuredOverTheLimits`, `TestStuffingWithMeasuredAndApportionedLines`, `TestStuffingVolumeAndUnits`, `TestStuffingInAnUnconvertedUnit`, `TestStuffingOnPostgres`, `TestStuffingHeldWhenTheLotIsWeighedAgain` and `TestOneExecutionPerLotReadsTheFiguresOfBeforeOnPostgres`) pass unchanged. No existing assertion was changed.
- V2.84 retries a command that finds a stuffing busy and adds a house's weights in one unit (PHY candidate 23; found by the review of V2.83). No migration.
  - What is retried. `phy.ErrConflict` meant three things. (a) The caller's expected revision is stale: never retried, because the caller holds data it has not seen the change of and a retry would apply its command to a state it did not see. (b) A stuffing is busy with another command (`LockWithoutWaiting`) and (c) the stuffings or executions loading a lot changed between their reading and their lock ("changed; try again", a measurement and a FAILED screening result): these two come of a race, the transaction has rolled back and written nothing, and they now carry an error of their own, `phy.ErrRetryable`. It is an `ErrConflict` for everyone who asks (`errors.Is`), says the same words and is answered as the API's conflict kind, so no caller and no mapping changes.
  - Where, how often and for how long. In the command path's transaction wrapper (`Deps.write`, now `Deps.retrying` around one attempt): the whole transaction function is run again, so the command id's receipt is looked up again and every aggregate is loaded again on every attempt. `Deps.Attempts` is the number of attempts in all (default 6; 1 is no retry; a negative number is read as 1) and `Deps.Backoff` the first wait (default 5 ms, also for a value of 0 or less), doubled for each next wait with up to its own length added at random; no wait is longer than a second before the random part, so the doubling cannot overflow. The five default waits add up to between 155 ms (5 + 10 + 20 + 40 + 80) and just under 310 ms (twice that): arithmetic, reasoned, not a measurement. The module takes `WithConflictRetry(attempts, backoff)`; `New` refuses an `attempts` below 1 (0 included, so that 0 cannot silently mean six) and a negative `backoff`, naming the option. The wait observes the context: a cancelled or expired context ends it with the context's own error, not the conflict. Nothing is held while it waits: the transaction of the attempt has ended and rolled back, and its connection is back in the pool (`Tx.WithinTransaction` returns after the rollback; the rows are free, run: `TestACommandBetweenAttemptsHoldsNothing`; the connection is reasoned from bento's `transaction.go`). A measurement and a screening result read the stuffings or executions they will lock again with every attempt, since the set of the attempt before is the one that changed.
  - What reaches the caller. The conflict of the last attempt, as before, when the stuffing stayed busy (or the set kept changing) through all of them, with nothing written; a stale revision at once, a plain conflict; any other error as before. A refused command now takes 0.3 to 0.4 s, not about 20 ms, before it answers. Starvation of a caller under a sustained stream of commands on the same stuffings is bounded in attempts but not excluded, and "about a third of a second" bounds the waits only, not the command: an attempt can itself wait for the lot's lock.
  - Conflicts that are not retried, and why (reasoned from the code, not run). A stale revision (`ports.go`, `lots.go`): never. "The houses changed while FUL was asked" (`CloseConsolidation`, `ReconcileConsolidation`): a race, but the houses and FUL's answers were read before the transaction, so a retry would have to ask FUL again outside the wrapper; the caller tries again. The reference data edition that changed while a route was resolved: outside any transaction. The conflicts of the database (a ledger attached first by another command, the one-live-entry index, the frozen-houses trigger, the one-stuffing-per-unit index, a deferred check at commit, a save at a stale revision): they come from the intent the command read before its transaction or from a commit, and a retry would have to read it again; left as they are.
  - What a retry cannot do twice. A retried attempt works on aggregates it loaded itself in a transaction that rolled back; the state a handler keeps outside the transaction function (the command, the hash, the time it is recorded, FUL's intent of a receipt) is only read, and what a failed attempt did to an aggregate in memory is gone with the aggregate (run: `TestARetryNeverWritesTwice`, `TestAReplayAcknowledgesNothing`, and on PostgreSQL `TestAMeasurementIsRetriedWhenTheStuffingsChanged` and `TestAFailedScreeningResultIsRetriedWhenTheExecutionsChanged`: one version or screening, the lot's revision up by one, one receipt). The retry assumes the command runs in a transaction of its own; no caller in this repository passes one (reasoned by reading, not for code outside it).
  - What was measured, and no more. Two measurements of the V2.83 mixed stress (a load and an unload loop on each of two containers of one lot and a measurement loop, 8 seconds, callers that do not retry on a conflict), mine and the independent reviewer's, on one machine each. Busy answers per run fell: the reviewer's runs from about 250 to about 13 for loads and for unloads (mine, two runs together, from 1,321 conflicts to 49). Successful commands did not differ beyond the run-to-run spread (the reviewer's sums of successful commands were 499 and 444 before and 504 and 604 after; mine 1,067 before and 1,350 after in one pair of runs, 1,164 in another before, so counts move). With callers that go again at once on a conflict, 4 loads succeeded and 613 answered busy in 8 seconds before, and 116 succeeded and 17 answered busy after (reviewer). In the reviewer's receipt loop (10 s, without a measurement loop) 1 receipt succeeded and 737 answered busy before; after, 38 succeeded and 24 of 62 attempts were still answered busy. The two-loads loop released together needed one attempt for every load before and after in both our runs (80 of 80 in mine, apart from one earlier pair of before-runs in which 2 of 80 needed two; 70 of 70 in the reviewer's, after only): it did not reproduce the starvation here. No deadlock and no livelock in any run.
  - The weights. `houseMeasure` added a house's line weights as amounts under the unit of the last line, so 3,000 KGM and 3 TNE were published as 3,003 TNE. A `WeightSum` adds them: quantities in one unit are added as they were and keep the unit, quantities in different units that PHY converts (GRM, KGM, TNE and, as the limits check does, LBR) are each converted to grams, rounded up, and the sum is published in GRM: 3,000 KGM and 3 TNE are 6,000,000 GRM, whatever the order of the lines. It is used wherever PHY adds the weights of one house: in a stuffing's measure of a house (`houseMeasure`, so the per-unit entry, the packing-list line and so its digest, and the shipment's physical facts) and in the cost basis's total of a house over its stuffings, which read zero where the stuffings' units differed. A quantity PHY cannot read makes the sum a quantity in the smallest unit it cannot convert, with the amounts in that unit added and at least 1: a unit it does not know, an amount without a unit and a negative amount (never produced) are all that; FIN's `Grams` refuses a weight in a unit other than GRM, KGM and TNE, so the basis is refused, where a zero would have been read as a weight and grams that left the quantity out as a wrong one. For a reader: a house whose lines are all in LBR is published in LBR, which FIN refuses; a house with LBR among other units is published in GRM, which FIN reads (PHY converts LBR, FIN does not). The container's limits check and `consolidation.go`'s sum of the planned weights already converted and are unchanged.
  - Volumes in different units are converted too (the owner's decision of 2026-11-19, after the review). When the plan declares no volume, which the real COM and FUL chain does, nothing makes a line's own volume agree with its lot's measure: the reviewer ran a line of 1 MTQ on a lot measured at 600 CMQ through the real commands. V2.83 subtracted 1 from 600 in the allotment, read 1,000,599 cm3 in the limits and published no volume for the house. Weight cannot be reached so: a line's own weight must be in the plan's unit and a measurement in the plan's (run through the real chain by the reviewer), so the weight case stays latent and the tests make it with a stand-in for FUL. Now: (1) the allotment converts before it subtracts (`taken`, `lotPart`): when every line that took anything is in the unit of the lot's measure the amounts are subtracted as before, so with one unit nothing moves; when a line is in another unit everything is converted to grams or cubic centimetres (rounded up) and the figure is in that base unit, because converting back to the lot's unit would round a figure the base unit holds exactly; and when a unit cannot be converted the amounts are taken off as before and the figure carries the unit it was in, so that the house's sum, or the check, says so. (2) The sums of volumes (`VolumeSum`: a house's total over its stuffings and in a stuffing) add one unit as it is and different units in cubic centimetres (CMQ), published in CMQ; a unit PHY cannot convert makes the sum a quantity in that unit, which FIN's `CubicCm` (CMQ, DMQ, MTQ only: it refuses LTR and FTQ as well, which PHY converts) refuses and which the limits check reports as "not checked". (3) A volume that is not known for a line (no volume measured, an apportioned line of a lot measured without one) still makes the house's volume not known, zero: a zero volume reads as a house with none, which a house with none is, whereas a zero weight would be read as a weight, so a weight is never zero for want of a figure. The reviewer's case now reads 1,000,000 CMQ in the entry, the house's total, the packing-list line and the physical facts, and 1,000,000 cm3 in `Limits` (run on the real chain: the line's 1,000,000 cm3 is more than the lot's 600 cm3, so nothing is left for the other four packs); with a lot of 2,000,000 CMQ the house reads 2,000,000 CMQ.
  - What is left, as the independent review measured it on the real chain (real COM, FUL and PHY and the real reference data, a plan that declares no volume, the containers sealed and FUL's `RecordVerifiedLoadEvidence` recorded, run on this tree and on V2.83; these are the review's findings, not a measurement of this change, and nothing is said of versions before V2.83). (1) FUL's measured volume of an ocean plan (`OceanOperationPlan.MeasuredVolume`, from FUL's adapter `total.add`, which adds the tenders' volumes unconverted and reads two units as not known) was zero in V2.83 in every case the review built with such a plan; it is a figure now when the containers publish one unit (a line of 1 MTQ on a lot of 600 CMQ: zero in V2.83, 1,000,000 CMQ now; a lot of 2 MTQ with a line of 500,001 CMQ: 2,000,000 CMQ), and it is still none when two containers of one house publish different units (a lot of 2,000,000 CMQ with the line of 1 MTQ in one container and the other four packs in the other: the entries read 1 MTQ and 1,000,000 CMQ, the cost basis 2,000,000 CMQ, and FUL, and SEA's document facts that convert FUL's figure, read none). The review found no case where this version turns a figure of V2.83 into a blank, and found no reader of FUL's measured volume in FIN, RATE or DOC (only the SEA and AIR adapters); it did not follow SEA's volume beyond its document facts. (2) A per-unit entry keeps its own container's unit: with a second container holding a line in LTR the entry reads LTR while the house's total is in CMQ, and FIN's `CubicCm` refuses LTR and FTQ, as it did in V2.83 for a single such unit; what FIN does with the entries' volume was not read. (3) The stored apportioned line's measure can disagree with the allotment when units differ (a lot of 600 CMQ: 600 CMQ stored, 0 allotted; a lot of 2,000,000 CMQ: 2,000,000 stored, 1,000,000 allotted; a lot of 2 MTQ with a line of 500,001 CMQ: 2 MTQ stored, 1,499,999 CMQ allotted), as it did in V2.83 (`LotStore.Loaded` keeps only the lines in the lot's volume unit); the review found as its only readers PHY's own line view (`LiveVolume`), the share a reversal takes and the next stored line, and found no conservation check, refusal, document or published figure that reads it. Whether to leave it is the owner's decision.
  - The latent defect is closed, not made reachable. FUL as built does not send PHY a plan in another weight unit: run against the real FUL and COM, an obligation in TNE added to a plan in KGM is refused ("unit mismatch"), so is a part in KGM of an obligation in TNE, and so is the release of the last obligation; the other ways a plan could change unit (an amendment, a replanned part) carry no weight of their own or go through the same part rules (reasoned from `plan.go` and `shipment.go`, not run).
  - What does not change. With one unit per house every published figure, every packing-list digest and every existing test is unchanged except the three listed below (a generated search against the previous function, for weight and for volume). The lock order, the hold rules, FIN and the contract's shape (`Quantity`).
  - Not yet: a retry of the conflicts the database raises and of "the houses changed while FUL was asked"; a guard in PHY that a lot's unit equals its house's; FUL's adapter converting as PHY does; entries in a unit every reader converts; storing the converted figure in the apportioned line.
  - Tests: pure: `TestWeightsInDifferentUnitsAreAddedInGrams`, `TestAWeightInAUnitPHYCannotConvertIsNeverReadAsAWeight`, `TestAHouseMeasureConvertsWeightsOfDifferentUnits`, `TestAHouseInOneUnitIsMeasuredAsBefore` (3,000 random one-unit houses against `houseMeasureOfV281`), `TestVolumesInDifferentUnitsAreAddedInCubicCentimetres`, `TestAQuantityPHYCannotReadIsNotDroppedFromASum`, `TestAHouseMeasureConvertsVolumesOfDifferentUnits` (the reviewer's case by hand), `TestAnAllotmentConvertsBeforeItSubtracts`, `TestAVolumeInAnUnconvertedUnitIsNeverAVolume`, `TestAVolumeNotKnownStaysNotKnownBesideAKnownOne`, `TestAHouseInOneVolumeUnitIsMeasuredAsBefore`, `TestARetryableConflictIsAConflict`, `TestARetryableConflictIsAnsweredAsAConflict`, `TestWithConflictRetryReachesTheCommandPath`, `TestWithConflictRetryRefusesWhatItCannotMean`, and with a stand-in for the transaction (the sleep and the random part injected, no timing) `TestARetryNeverWritesTwice`, `TestAReplayAcknowledgesNothing`, `TestOnlyARaceIsRetried`, `TestTheWaitBetweenAttemptsEndsWithTheContext`, `TestTheRetryWaitsLongerEachTime`, `TestTheDefaultWaitsAndTheirJitter` and `TestAGivenBackoffIsTheFirstWaitAndNoWaitPassesASecond`, `TestTheProductionJitterIsBetweenTheBaseAndTwiceIt` (the random source that runs in production, 2,000 waits, no timing) and `TestTheLimitCheckReadsAnAllottedPartInTheUnitItIsGivenIn` (a lot in MTQ with a line in CMQ, a lot in TNE with a line in KGM). On PostgreSQL, with a row held on a second connection (`phy_retry_test.go`): `TestABusyStuffingIsRetriedUntilItIsFree`, `TestAStuffingBusyForLongerThanAllAttemptsIsAConflict` (the attempts counted, not timed), `TestAStaleRevisionIsNeverRetried`, `TestACancelledOrExpiredContextEndsTheWait`, `TestACommandBetweenAttemptsHoldsNothing`, `TestAMeasurementIsRetriedWhenTheStuffingsChanged`, `TestAMeasurementWhoseStuffingsKeepChangingIsAConflict`, `TestAMeasurementWithAStaleRevisionIsNotRetried`, `TestAFailedScreeningResultIsRetriedWhenTheExecutionsChanged`, `TestAFailedScreeningResultWhoseExecutionsKeepChangingIsAConflict` and `TestTwoLoadsOfOneLotRetryTheConflictThemselves`; and (`phy_limits_raised_test.go`, `phy_weight_units_test.go`) `TestAHouseWithLinesInTwoWeightUnitsIsPublishedInGrams` (the V2.83 latent-defect test, renamed: 6,000,000 GRM in the entry, the total, the packing-list line and the physical facts, `Limits` at 6,000,000 g, and FIN's conversion of it), `TestAHouseInTwoContainersInTwoWeightUnitsHasATotalInGrams`, `TestAHouseInTwoContainersHasATotalVolumeInCubicCentimetres` and `TestVolumesInDifferentUnitsAreConvertedThroughTheRealCommands` (PHY's real commands on PostgreSQL; only FUL is a stand-in, which declares a plan with no volume as the real chain does). Existing assertions changed: the latent-defect test (3,003 TNE before, 6,000,000 GRM now); three tests of V2.83 that assert a busy stuffing is answered at once, `TestALoadOrAnUnloadDoesNotWaitForAnotherStuffingsLock`, `TestAPartlyBusySetOfStuffingsMakesTheLoadConflict` and `TestTwoLoadsOfOneLotQueuedBehindItsLock`, which now run with `WithConflictRetry(1, 0)` and assert what they did; and the three that assert a volume in mixed units reads zero, on purpose: `TestVolumeUnknownInOneContainerOnly` (the mixed container read no volume and the other 300 - 1 = 299 cm3 of a lot of 600 CMQ with a line of 1 MTQ; they read 1,000,000 CMQ and 0 CMQ), the V2.82 oracle's case "mixed volume units" in `TestAllotmentOfOneExecutionIsTodaysMeasure` (a line of 2 MTQ on a lot of 5,000 CMQ: no volume before, 2,000,000 CMQ now; the case moved out of the oracle's table, whose function cannot describe it; `TestAHouseMeasureConvertsVolumesOfDifferentUnits` covers the same kind) and `TestMixedVolumeUnitsInOneUnitReadZero` (renamed, above).
- V2.85 opens a sealed container for rework: a whole house is unloaded and the container is sealed again (PHY candidate 23; the first of seven steps of "a house leaves a sealed container"). Migration phy/000007.
  - The plan and this step. The owner's plan (2026-11-18 to 2026-11-20) has seven steps; this is the first, in PHY only: before the consolidation departs, a sealed container is opened for rework, one whole house is unloaded from it (several houses, one command each) and the container is sealed again with a new seal. The later steps are, as the brief named them: the unloaded house leaves the closed consolidation (PHY); FUL, SEA and DOC follow the new seal and contents (one step each); FIN holds apportionments back during the rework (since V2.91: frozen) and re-attributes the container's costs. FUL, SEA, DOC and FIN are not changed here. The decisions that bind it: only whole houses (partial unloading is designed separately); the rework is opened on a request with a reason and the unloading is confirmed by the warehouse with evidence, with no second person's approval here (FIN's later confirmation has it), but who asked for the rework and when is recorded and published, because FIN will require that its confirmer is another person; the business date of the unloading is the date the warehouse confirmed it (the command's `OccurredAt`); the new seal is recorded and the broken one is kept; and the unloading opens the house's closed lot again (the owner's decision of 2026-11-20; before it a closed lot never changed).
  - The state and its commands. A new execution state `REWORK`, for a STUFFING only, on the same execution: SEA keeps the execution id of the stuffing on its container visit and cannot change it, so a rework is never a new execution on the unit. `OpenStuffingForRework` (command id, tenant, actor, execution id, expected revision, reason of at most 500 characters, occurred at; the receipt's item is the rework's number) takes a STUFFING in state CLOSED of a CLOSED consolidation (a RECONCILED or CANCELLED one is refused, and an OPEN one cannot hold a sealed container), with no carrier evidence recorded on it (the cargo has then left the forwarder's hands) and no loading discrepancy open, dated not before the execution's last change. The state becomes REWORK, the seal moves into the rework's record, the execution carries no close facts while it is in REWORK, the actor is recorded as the one who asked, and `LoadExecutionReworkOpened.v1` is published with the unit as its item (the reason, free text, stays out of the event). `UnloadHouseForRework` (command id, tenant, actor, execution id, expected revision, shipment id, evidence, occurred at; the receipt's item is the number of lines reversed) needs the execution in REWORK and the house to have live packs in it and in no other execution of the consolidation (a house in two containers is partial unloading, refused with the message saying so); it reverses every live line of the house completely, each with the reason UNSTUFFED and the command's evidence, in one command, and opens again a line's lot that is CLOSED first. The house stays attached to the consolidation with nothing loaded (it leaves since V2.86): until the next step takes it out the consolidation cannot reconcile (its loaded packs are fewer than its plan) and FIN reads the house at the stage PLANNED, with no unit entry. Sealing again is `CloseLoadExecution` with a SEAL evidence from REWORK as from LOADING: the container still holds cargo ("nothing is stuffed in the container" otherwise), the consolidation is CLOSED and the time not before its latest change, and the seal's reference differs from every seal this execution carried before (a broken seal is not put back, and "the same seal" is told as SEA tells it, by the ASCII letters and digits of the reference in capitals, so `seal-1`, `SEAL 1` and `SEAL1` are `SEAL-1`; a reference with a blank at either end is refused on every seal PHY records, the first one included); the rework ends, sealed again by whom and when with the new seal's reference, and `LoadExecutionClosed.v1` is published as for a first seal. Giving up is `CancelLoadExecution` from REWORK once no live packs are left (the V2.82 rules otherwise): the rework ends given up, the stuffing is CANCELLED and its unit is planned again. In REWORK nothing else is accepted, and each refusal says so in its message: no load, no start, no hold placed or released, no carrier evidence, no discrepancy, and no ordinary `RecordCargoUnloaded` (unloading in a rework is by whole house only). A sealed (CLOSED) stuffing still takes no reversal, no line and no hold: it is opened for rework first, and the message of the refusal of an ordinary unload of a sealed container now says so (its wording was "a sealed container is unstuffed once it can be sealed again"; no test asserted it).
  - The rework's record, which is also the history of seals. One row per rework of an execution, numbered 1, 2, ...: who asked and when (business and entered time), the reason, the seal that was broken (the whole close evidence, who had sealed and when) and how it ended: sealed again (who, when, the new seal's reference) or given up (who, when). The domain keeps the same in `LoadExecutionState.Reworks`, restored by `RestoreLoadExecution` with the checks its other parts have (numbering, who asked and why, the seal it broke, the ending of all but the last and of the last exactly when the state is not REWORK, the new seal being the seal the next rework broke, no seal carried twice, a CLOSED execution after a rework only when it was sealed again with that seal, a CANCELLED one only when it was given up). Rows are never deleted, the opening never changes and the ending is written once. Two rounds on one execution give two rows and the chain of seals (SEAL-1 broken, SEAL-2 put on, broken, SEAL-3).
  - What PHY publishes. `PlannedLoadUnit.ExecutionState` reads `REWORK` and the unit's `Status` reads IN_USE while its stuffing is in REWORK: it is not sealed, and the derivation gives IN_USE for any state but CLOSED (run). The packing list of a unit in REWORK lists what is in it now, state REWORK; its digest follows from the unchanged canonical text of state and lines, so it moves when the state does, but it is not a witness of a rework: a rework with nothing unloaded and a reseal leaves the state and the lines as they were, and the digest the same (run); a reader that must know of a rework reads `Reworks` (run: the tests recompute the digest from the text, and the existing digest tests pass unchanged). `GetLoadExecution`, `TenderEvidence` (the shipment's physical facts) and `UnitPackingList.Reworks` carry the reworks (number, who asked, when, reason, the broken seal with who sealed it and when, and how it ended); the packing list's `Reworks` is where FIN reads who asked, outside the digest. `CargoLot.Reopens` carries a lot's reopenings. A house unloaded in a rework has zero loaded packs and no unit entry, as the cost basis gives for a house without loads. New events: `LoadExecutionReworkOpened.v1` (item: the unit) and `CargoLotReopened.v1` (the lot's event, naming the execution in its payload; item: the reopening's number). The limit check does not hold a container in REWORK (it only loses cargo) and its packing list's `Limits` is worked out as for any other. The commands of another stuffing that read or lock the stuffings of a lot (a load, an unload, a measurement) lock the ones that are LOADING or ON_HOLD, which a stuffing in REWORK is not, and read it for the allotment as they read a sealed one (run: with the container in rework locked on a second connection, an unload, a load and a measurement on another container answer at once). For a lot shared with another container, which the whole-house rule excludes for the house that is unloaded but not for the other houses of the container, the allotment divides the lot's measure over both containers as before and unloading another house of the container moves none of its figures (run: 310 kg over 4 and 2 packs, 207 and 103, before and after).
  - The consolidation while a container is in rework. It cannot be reconciled or cancelled: its rule refuses while an execution is neither CLOSED nor CANCELLED, which counts REWORK as open (run, in the domain through the application's count and in the database). It cannot be reopened either: a container in REWORK is neither sealed nor tendered, so the refusal is new, in the domain (`Stuffed.Reworking`), in the application's count of executions and in the database (`consolidation_reopen_during_rework`; run, with tests that fail without each). A reopen of a consolidation with a sealed container still answers the tendered-cargo rule first, as before. A new stuffing on another unit, loads into other containers and the other commands of a CLOSED consolidation go on as today. A stuffing in REWORK counts as the unit's stuffing that is not cancelled: the unit takes no second stuffing and is not removed (run, in the commands and the database).
  - The lot opened again. `CargoLot.Close` set the state CLOSED, `ClosedBy`, `ClosedAt` and, when PHY entered it, `ClosedEnteredAt`. Unloading a house opens each of its CLOSED lots again, back to the state it had before it was closed (RECEIVED once its receipt was completed, RECEIVING otherwise), clears those three facts and records on the lot a reopening: its number, the execution and rework that caused it, the first reversal of the lot's packs in the command, the closing that was undone (who and when) and who reopened it and when. Only `UnloadHouseForRework` reopens a lot: `reopenForRework` is not exported, and no other command or method calls it (reasoned from the code, and in the database run by raw writes). The command refuses a lot's reopening while the shipment has another lot in force at the lot's facility (one lot in force per shipment and facility; the database's unique index is the second line when a lot is received meanwhile). A lot opened again stays open: closing it again needs its packs loaded or accounted for, which a house with nothing loaded does not have, until the house leaves.
  - The database. Migration `phy/000007_phy_container_rework`: the state check accepts REWORK and a CHECK keeps REWORK to a started stuffing (no close facts outside CLOSED is the existing `load_execution_closed`); `cargo_phy.stuffing_rework` (the rework's record, tenant isolation, `SELECT, INSERT` and an `UPDATE` of its five ending columns for the application role, never deleted, the opening never changed, the ending once, at most one rework open per execution, a rework numbered after the last, taking over exactly the close facts the execution has) and `cargo_phy.cargo_lot_reopen`; `check_load_execution_change` lets a sealed STUFFING go to REWORK only (its consolidation CLOSED, no carrier evidence, the close facts cleared in the same change and the rework inserted in the same transaction), REWORK go to CLOSED (the rules of a first seal, cargo in it, a seal unlike every broken one) or to CANCELLED (no live packs) and nowhere else, and everything a CLOSED row is refused stays refused; `check_execution_addition` takes a reversal in REWORK only of a whole line for the reason UNSTUFFED and no line, hold, carrier evidence or discrepancy, and a reversal records the rework it was written in (`reversal_line.rework_no`, set by a trigger from the execution's state at the insert and never changed), and a constraint trigger at commit leaves nothing of the house loaded in the consolidation after such a reversal, whatever the state of the execution is by then, so a reseal in the same transaction does not excuse a part-unloaded house (a whole house, with the house's ledger locked as a load locks it, so a load of the house elsewhere and the unloading wait for each other); at commit an execution and its reworks agree (a REWORK execution has its last rework open, a CLOSED one after a rework was sealed again with that seal by that person at that time, a CANCELLED one after one was given up); `check_cargo_lot_change` lets a CLOSED lot go back to its state only with every other column unchanged and a `cargo_lot_reopen` row of the same transaction naming the closing it undoes, and the row is checked when it is inserted (the lot CLOSED with those facts, the stuffing in that rework) and at commit (a reversal of a line of that lot in that execution for the reason UNSTUFFED written in the same transaction, `xmin` being the transaction's, and the lot no longer CLOSED); `check_reopen_after_stuffing` refuses a reopen with a container in REWORK. `check_sealed_reversal`, `check_stuffing_line`, `check_consolidation_version` and the one-stuffing-per-unit index need no change and were checked (their header lines). What only the domain keeps: that a rework is asked for a reason, that the dates of a rework, an unloading and a seal are not before the execution's own lines, reversals and holds (the database checks them against the seal that was broken and the consolidation's versions; run: a raw seal dated before the unloadings it follows is accepted), that the lots of a house are read and locked before the command writes, and which house is the one the warehouse confirmed. Seals are compared by `cargo_phy.seal_key` in the database and `sealKey` in the domain, one rule: the letters and digits in capitals that are ASCII, independent of the database's locale. SEA's `sealKey` (`sea/internal/domain/visit.go`, line 358) keeps every Unicode letter and digit in capitals; ours is exactly the ASCII part of that key (upper-case first, then the ASCII test, so U+0131 and U+017F count as I and S), so two references SEA calls equal always have the same key here (a combining mark such as U+0345 is dropped by both) and ours is stricter for references that differ only in non-ASCII letters; the domain refuses first, so a command ends in no database error. A Unicode white space at either end of a seal's reference (what `strings.TrimSpace` trims: the ASCII ones, U+0085, U+00A0, U+1680, U+2000 to U+200A, U+2028, U+2029, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000) is refused when a stuffing is sealed, by the domain and by the database (rows sealed before are not looked at). "The same transaction" is told by `xmin = pg_current_xact_id()`, the top-level id: a row written inside a savepoint is refused though it is of the same transaction; the test is never permissive and the commands use no savepoints. Header: the objects replaced and the files of their latest definitions, the locks (ALTER TABLE takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on `load_execution` for its own duration and its CHECK constraints scan it once; the new tables are empty; no table is rewritten) and that no existing row changes. The application role's grants are in the workflow's list and in `tools/localpg/localpg.sh grants`'s source.
  - What the other contexts read, with today's code. FUL reads PHY's CLOSED executions only. Run (`TestAContainerIsReworkedWithEveryContextAndFINApportionsAsTodayAssert`, since V2.91 `TestAContainerIsReworkedWithEveryContextAndFINFreezesThePool`, real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN): before the rework FUL takes the sealed container into each house's OceanOperationPlan v1 (execution X-1, unit 1, the seal, the house's lines); while the container is in REWORK, whichever house is asked about (H01, still in it, and H03, out of it), FUL takes the evidence it has, the packs as received, with no stuffing and no refusal, so the stuffing it had recorded is gone from the plan until the container is sealed again; after the reseal H01 is tendered again in the same execution with the new seal, and H03, with no packs in a sealed container, reads as received with no stuffing, though it is still attached to the consolidation. SEA and DOC, as the independent review of V2.85 ran them on the real chain (real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY, BOOK, AIR, SEA, FIN and DOC over the real database; three houses in one 22G1, the third unloaded, the container sealed again with another seal; the developer did not run these, the review's findings are quoted): PHY's rework and unloading reach FUL, SEA and DOC only when their own commands read again; nothing tells them (no event of PHY is consumed). With the container stuffed in SEA, its VGM asserted, the facts FINAL and three house bills ISSUED, the opening and the unloading move nothing there: FUL's plans still say TENDERED with the old seal for every house, SEA's visit stays LOADED with the old seal and the FINAL OceanDocFacts stay FINAL. Once FUL takes the evidence again house by house it reports RECEIVED with no stuffing for every house asked during the rework (so FUL holds, house by house, a mix of the old sealed state and no stuffing until each is asked); SEA's `RefreshHouseBillFacts` is then accepted for every house and makes the FINAL ocean doc facts SUPERSEDED, silently; after the reseal the refresh of the two houses in the container is accepted and the unloaded house's is refused ("FUL reports the plan version, the measures and the containers SEA froze"); then `FinalizeOceanDocFacts` is refused ("... is not stuffed in a container") and DOC's `OpenDocumentAmendment` is refused (DOC_SOURCE_NOT_FINAL, the latest facts version is not FINAL; the review tried the bill of the house that left and expects the same for every issued bill of the movement, which it did not run), and the issued bills keep the old seal and the packs that left. The movement stays so until the house leaves the consolidation (the next step) and SEA follows the new seal (a later step). With a container assigned in SEA and its seal recorded but not stuffed, SEA's `RecordStuffingDone` during a rework is ACCEPTED against SEA's stale frozen facts (SEA reads its houses' frozen facts, not PHY): the visit becomes STUFFED with the execution and the seal PHY broke, and it cannot take a difference reason later (invalid state transition), so it keeps the broken seal. When FUL took the evidence and SEA refreshed every house first, `RecordStuffingDone` is refused during the rework with `ErrNotStuffed` ("refresh the houses when FUL has newer evidence") and after the reseal and the refresh with `ErrStuffingDiffers` (PHY's seal is none of the container's seals), which a difference reason lets through. DOC reads FUL and SEA and has no read of PHY. FIN (run, same test; since V2.91: frozen, a preview, a submission and the close are refused while the container is in rework): its container methods (CONTAINER_COUNT, TEU) refuse a container in REWORK at once, as any container not sealed ("load unit 1 is IN_USE"); CHARGEABLE and GROSS_WEIGHT read the loaded facts, which have not moved, and give the shares of before; with H03 unloaded and not named out FIN reads the stage PLANNED (a house that takes part is not loaded) and divides on the plan, and the container methods are refused for the stage; with H03 named out (`NotUsed`) the stage is ACTUAL on the two houses in the container, before and after the reseal, and the container methods work again after it; the shares are asserted in the test (W/M 10 : 15 : 5 gives 3,334, 5,000 and 1,667 of CNY 100.01; with H03 out 4,000 and 6,001). To list for the later steps: FUL drops the stuffing of every house of a container in rework and falls back to the received packs, without telling that a rework is why; FIN does not know that a container is in rework and apportions what PHY reads loaded (since V2.91: frozen; the step that holds an apportionment back, with a confirmer other than `UnitPackingList.Reworks[].AskedBy`), and a house unloaded in a rework must be named out by hand until the container's costs are attributed again; the action names `OpenStuffingForRework` and `UnloadHouseForRework` are new for the identity policy to grant, as every PHY action is. For the later steps (found by the review, not changed here): SEA must not accept a stuffing done against an execution that is not CLOSED; SEA must follow PHY's new seal; FUL must say that a container is in rework instead of falling back to the received packs; DOC must be able to amend again once SEA finalises again.
  - Not yet: the six later steps by name (the unloaded house leaves the closed consolidation in PHY; FUL follows; SEA follows; DOC follows; FIN holds an apportionment back while a container is in rework (since V2.91: frozen) and needs another person to confirm it; FIN re-attributes the container's costs); partial unloading of a house (a pack count less than the house's, or a house in two containers); loading into a container in rework (a house that is added to a container after its seal broke); a lot opened again closed again while its house is attached with nothing loaded; a reseal of a container that holds no cargo (give it up); and the rework of a container with carrier evidence, whose cargo has left the forwarder's hands.

- V2.86 lets a house unloaded in a rework leave the closed consolidation (PHY candidate 23; step 2 of 7 of "a house leaves a sealed container"). Migration phy/000008.
  - The rule. `DetachHouse` and `TransferHouse`, the existing commands, now also take a house out of a CLOSED consolidation, with the operator's reason, when it has no live packs in any execution of the consolidation and was unloaded by a rework of it: at least one reversal of its lines in an execution of the consolidation carries a rework number (`reversal_line.rework_no`, V2.85). A house that was simply never loaded has no such reversal and stays refused (a shut-out before stuffing is decided while the consolidation is open; both changed in V2.87 for sea consolidations: such a house leaves); every other house of a CLOSED consolidation stays frozen (changed in V2.87 for sea consolidations: a house with no live packs leaves); RECONCILED and CANCELLED consolidations refuse; an OPEN consolidation works as before. The reason is one of the existing list (`SHIPMENT_CANCELLED` stays PHY's own, `OTHER` needs a text). A transfer needs a target that is OPEN and admits the house, as today. Whether the container the house was unloaded from is still in REWORK or sealed again does not matter (both run). "No live load after the unloading" means no live packs now, as the owner decided on 2026-11-20: a house unloaded in a rework, loaded into another container of the consolidation and unloaded from there for an ordinary reason leaves, naming the rework (run). No new command was needed: the commands read the rework inside their transaction (`ExecutionStore.HouseRework`, under the house's ledger lock) and call `DetachAfterRework` and `TransferAfterRework` of the domain, which `Detach` and `Transfer` share their rules with; a CLOSED consolidation with no rework gets the same refusal as before (run; changed in V2.87 for sea consolidations). The domain also refuses an entry dated before the house was unloaded; it compares with that rework\'s own unloading time only (the latest business time of the house\'s reversals in it), not with a later ordinary reversal in another container.
  - The rework link. The entry that ends records the rework that unloaded the house: `membership_entry.end_rework_execution_id` and `end_rework_no`, both set together and only on an entry that ended as DETACHED or TRANSFERRED in a CLOSED consolidation, NULL on every other entry (an entry that ends in an OPEN consolidation names none). They are read as `MembershipEntry.ReworkExecutionID` and `ReworkNo` of ConsolMembership v1 and `Entry.ReworkExecutionID` and `ReworkNo` in the domain. Columns on the entry were the lightest way: the entry is the record FIN will read, the database can check it against the house's own reversal when the entry ends, and nothing has to be derived on read. Of several reworks that unloaded the house the domain records the latest by the time PHY entered the reversals (two containers, two rounds: run; one container cannot unload one house twice, because a container in rework takes no load, reasoned from `check_execution_addition`); the database accepts any rework that unloaded the house in that consolidation (run: a raw write that names the earlier one is accepted). The event, `HouseDetached.v1` or `HouseTransferred.v1`, is unchanged and carries the reason as before. The payload has `execution_id` and `item_no`, which could hold the rework, but they mean the load execution and the item of its events, and a house event that used them for a rework would change what a reader may assume of them: the link is left to the read side.
  - The database. Migration `phy/000008_phy_house_leaves_after_rework`: the two columns and `membership_entry_rework` (both or neither; only on a detach or a transfer), and `check_membership_entry` of `000001`, replaced with every rule it had (the entry is only ended, once; its consolidation is read FOR SHARE). It now locks the house's ledger first (a load of the house takes it first, and the commands hold it already) and then the consolidation, and lets a live entry of a CLOSED consolidation end as DETACHED or TRANSFERRED only naming a rework (a reversal of that house in that consolidation, in that execution, with that number: `membership_entry_rework_link`) and with no live packs of the house in the consolidation (`membership_entry_rework_loaded`; the deferred `membership_entry_loaded` of `000002` still refuses the same at commit, under its own name); an entry that ends in an OPEN consolidation names no rework (`membership_entry_rework_link`); `membership_entry_frozen` stays for every other end and every other state. A reversal only names a rework of its own execution (`set_reversal_rework`, read from the code), so the pair has no foreign key. What only the domain keeps: that the reason is an operator's, the target's mode and that it is OPEN and admits the house (the new entry's deferred check refuses a target that is not OPEN, run), that the named rework is the latest, that the end is not dated before the unloading (run: the database accepts it), and that the house was unloaded whole (V2.85, at the unloading). It takes the locks in the commands' order (the consolidation FOR SHARE, then the house's ledger; every command, in `application/ports.go`, locks consolidations, executions, ledgers, lots in that order, which I read in the code and the independent review confirmed). A writer outside the commands locks the entry's row by its UPDATE before the trigger runs, while a command holds the ledger and waits for that row, so a trigger that waited for the ledger deadlocked with it (the review: 3 to 4 runs in 25 of a raw end racing a detach and a reconcile); the ledger is asked for without waiting and such a writer is refused at once with `lock_not_available` (the same probe: 0 deadlocks in 50 runs). A new trigger `membership_entry_insert` refuses an entry inserted already ended or naming a rework (`membership_entry_inserted_live`): nothing writes an ended entry (the store inserts new entries live, a house command ends an existing one and starts the next live, no test or earlier migration inserts an ended one; read from the code), and the end rules fire on UPDATE. The application role's column grant on `membership_entry` in the workflow gains the two columns. Header: the object replaced and the file of its definition, the locks (ALTER TABLE takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on `membership_entry` for its own duration, its CHECK scans it once, no table is rewritten, no row changes).
  - What follows (run, through the real commands). The consolidation reconciles once the others are loaded as planned and every container is sealed: it is refused while the container is in rework and succeeds after the reseal, with H01 and H02 (the house that left no longer counts). The cost basis no longer lists the house; its share in FIN's pools is FIN's later step. Taking every house out empties the consolidation: the container, empty, is given up (`CancelLoadExecution`) and the consolidation, with no sealed container, reopens (`TestEveryHouseLeavingEmptiesTheClosedConsolidationAndItReopens`, run): it is OPEN again before departure with a rework in its history, which the rule that froze its houses made impossible; whether that is wanted is for the owner. The lot of the house, opened again by V2.85, stays RECEIVED: after a detach it is attached nowhere; after a transfer into an OPEN own-container consolidation the target is closed, its container stuffed from that lot and sealed (H03 at 5 W/M in the cost basis of the target). FUL's own command `RecordConsolDetachment` records the leaving from PHY's membership, and after a transfer `RecordConsolAttachment` records the target and ends the old attachment in one command.

FUL (run, `TestAHouseUnloadedInAReworkLeavesTheConsolidationWithEveryContext`): it still records the house in the consolidation until `RecordConsolDetachment` is run (the plan names C-1, entry 1); afterwards the plan names no consolidation, no entry, the status stays PLANNED, no stuffing, the revision moves from 4 to 5; after a transfer `RecordConsolAttachment` names C-2, entry 2. - V2.87 lets a house shut out before stuffing leave the closed consolidation (step 2b of "a house leaves a sealed container"). Migration phy/000009.
  - The rule. `DetachHouse` and `TransferHouse`, the existing commands, now take a house out of a CLOSED consolidation of a sea kind (`SEA_LCL_OWN` or `SEA_LCL_COLOAD`) when it has no live packs in any execution of the consolidation, with the operator's reason, whether or not it was ever loaded: a house shut out before stuffing (attached, never loaded or never tendered), or taken out by ordinary reversals only, leaves with no rework recorded; a house unloaded by a rework of the consolidation leaves as since V2.86, the entry naming the latest such rework and the leaving not dated before that rework's unloading. A house with live packs stays frozen; a transfer's target is OPEN and admits the house; `SHIPMENT_CANCELLED` stays PHY's own; RECONCILED and CANCELLED consolidations refuse; an OPEN consolidation behaves as before. The domain keeps its two entry points (`Detach` and `DetachAfterRework`, `Transfer` and `TransferAfterRework`): the application already chooses between them by whether it found a rework, and `leaving`, the one function that decides, now lets a CLOSED sea consolidation go with no rework, so the change is one condition and no signature moves; one path with an optional rework would have changed the two commands and every call in the domain tests for no gain. A house unloaded by a rework of another consolidation, or of another house, has no rework of this one: `ExecutionStore.HouseRework` reads the reversals of this house's lines in this consolidation, so it finds none and the house leaves with no link if it has no live packs (run: `TestAHouseUnloadedByTheReworkOfAnotherConsolidationLeavesWithNoLink`, a house unloaded in C-S's rework, moved to C-T, loaded and unloaded there, leaves C-T recording none; and `TestAHouseNeverLoadedLeavesAClosedConsolidationAndOnlyTheUnloadedOneNamesARework`, H03 never loaded leaving while H01's rework exists). A leaving with no rework is not dated before the house's latest reversal, of any kind, in the consolidation (`ExecutionStore.HouseLastReversal`, read inside the command's transaction under the house's ledger lock, as `HouseRework` is; nothing to compare for a house never loaded; the equal time is allowed; run, domain and commands; the database does not check it, as it does not for the rework path). The domain has two more entry points for it, `DetachAfterReversals` and `TransferAfterReversals`, which the commands call when no rework was found; `Detach` and `Transfer` keep their signatures. `AIR_CONSOL` is not opened: a house of a CLOSED air consolidation stays frozen as today, because AIR's documents were not analysed for it (run, in the domain and on PostgreSQL, command and raw write); an air consolidation is never stuffed, so it has no rework (reasoned from `ErrKindNotTendered`).
  - The database. Migration `phy/000009_phy_shut_out_house_leaves`, no new column and no grant: `check_membership_entry` of `000008` replaced with every rule it had (the entry is only ended, once; the consolidation FOR SHARE, then the house's ledger FOR NO KEY UPDATE NOWAIT, then the reads, as in V2.86 and with its header's account of why; `membership_entry_frozen`; an end in an OPEN consolidation names no rework), changing only the CLOSED case. An end as DETACHED or TRANSFERRED needs, as before, no live packs of the house in the consolidation (`membership_entry_rework_loaded`; the deferred `membership_entry_loaded` of `000002` still refuses the same at commit); then, if a reversal of the house's lines in the consolidation carries a rework number, the end names one such rework (`membership_entry_rework_link`, as before, and now also for an end that names none of a house a rework unloaded); if none does, the end names no rework and the consolidation is of a sea kind (`membership_entry_closed_sea_only`, new; an end that names a rework no reversal of the house carries is `membership_entry_rework_link`). Locks: `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` locks no table and rewrites nothing; the previous definition is in `000008`. What only the domain keeps, run as raw writes the database accepts (rolled back): the successor of a TRANSFERRED end (a raw TRANSFERRED end with no new entry is accepted), that the end is not dated before the unloading, nor, with no rework, before the house's latest reversal, nor after the departure, that the named rework is the latest, that the reason is an operator's and the target's mode and state, and that the house was unloaded whole; and that the house was shut out at all: with no live packs the database cannot tell a house never loaded from one taken out by ordinary reversals, and does not need to.
  - What follows (run, through the real commands). The consolidation reconciles once the remaining houses are loaded as planned and every container is sealed: it is refused while H04 is attached and never loaded (`SH-H04 plans 6 packs, 0 loaded`) and succeeds after H04 left, with H01 and H02 (10 and 15 W/M). After a transfer into an OPEN own-container consolidation the house is loaded there from its lot and sealed (H04 at 5 W/M in the cost basis of the target), and both consolidations reconcile; a co-load consolidation with a house never tendered reconciles after it left; so does one whose house was tendered and taken back by an ordinary reversal before the tender closed (run, `TestAHouseTakenBackFromTheTenderBeforeItClosedLeavesTheCoLoadConsolidation`). Carrier evidence that the house was offloaded raises a loading discrepancy on the closed tender; it changes nothing for the leaving (the house has no live packs, run) but the consolidation does not reconcile (`1 discrepancies open`) until the discrepancy is resolved (run, `TestAHouseOffloadedByTheCarrierLeavesTheCoLoadConsolidationOnceItsDiscrepancyIsResolved`). The lot of a house that was received stays RECEIVED until it is loaded (run, after a transfer). FUL's `RecordConsolDetachment` records the leaving, and after a transfer `RecordConsolAttachment` records the target and ends the old attachment in one command.
  - What FUL, SEA, DOC and FIN read after the house left, with today's code. FUL (run, `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingLeavesTheConsolidationWithEveryContext`): it records the house in the consolidation until `RecordConsolDetachment` is run (the plan names C-1, entry 1); afterwards the plan names no consolidation and no entry and the status stays PLANNED; after a transfer `RecordConsolAttachment` names C-2, entry 2. FIN (run, the same test and `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingIsTransferredWithEveryContext`): line 1 (OCEAN_FREIGHT by CHARGEABLE, CNY 100.01) was apportioned at the stage PLANNED on the three houses, 3,334, 3,334 and 3,333, and the house's job is charged 3,333; after the house left, lines 2 and 3 apportion at the stage ACTUAL on the two houses that are attached (5,001 and 5,000; 5,099 and 4,902), the PLANNED share of the house on line 1 stands and its job is still charged 3,333 (since V2.93: pending finance's attribution); the pool cannot close (`FIN_POOL_NOT_CLOSABLE`: line 1 is not apportioned on the loaded basis it has now) until line 1 is apportioned again, on H01 and H02, after which it closes and the job of the house carries nothing from the pool. After a transfer, with a pool P-2 opened on the target and one line of CNY 100.01 apportioned there, the job is charged 13,334, 3,333 from the source pool and 10,001 from the target, in both pools (since V2.93: pending finance's attribution); it falls to 10,001 once line 1 of the source pool is apportioned again and the pool closed. FIN's later steps (PR 5 and 6) take the house's share out of the source pool and into the target's. SEA (run, `TestSEAFinalizesAfterAShutOutHouseLeftOnceItsAssociationIsEnded`): its association of the house stands after the house left; SEA cannot refresh the house's facts (`PLANNED SEA LCL in "" of LE`: FUL no longer reports it in the consolidation) nor finalize the movement (`STALE_HOUSE`, naming the house) until `EndHouseBillAssociation`, after which the movement finalizes with the two houses; PR 3 makes SEA follow the leaving. DOC (reasoned, not run): it reads SEA's OceanDocFacts v1, which after the finalization lists the two houses, and AIR's facts, COM, ORG and FIN's credit status; it imports no PHY (checked by grep of the DOC and SEA packages).
  - Not yet: air consolidations (a house of a CLOSED `AIR_CONSOL` with no rework stays frozen: AIR's documents were not analysed for it); FIN's steps (PR 5 and 6: the house's pool share and the double cost after a transfer (since V2.93: pending finance's attribution)); SEA's step (PR 3: the end of the house's association when it leaves); the rework link in the event; the database's own refusal of a raw end dated before the unloading, of a TRANSFERRED end with no successor, or naming an earlier rework.
  - Tests: pure (`shut_out_house_leaves_test.go`, domain): `TestAHouseWithNoReworkLeavesAClosedSeaConsolidation` (both sea kinds, each reason, OTHER without a text, SHIPMENT_CANCELLED, nobody, the entry's record with no link, the event, restore, a second time), `TestAHouseOfAClosedAirConsolidationWithNoReworkStaysFrozen`, `TestAHouseWithNoReworkIsTransferredFromAClosedSeaConsolidation` (the same consolidation, a CLOSED, RECONCILED, air and non-admitting target, the reasons, the history), `TestTheEntryRecordsAReworkOnlyWhenTheCallerFoundOne` (with and without a link, RECONCILED, CANCELLED, OPEN), `TestALeavingWithNoReworkIsNotDatedBeforeTheHousesLatestReversal` (both kinds, detach and transfer, the equal time, never loaded, air, OPEN). PostgreSQL, real commands (`phy_shut_out_house_leaves_test.go`, H01, H02 and H04 of 10, 15 and 5 W/M): `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingLeavesTheClosedConsolidation`, `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingIsTransferredStuffedAndSealed`, `TestAHouseOfAnAirConsolidationWithNoReworkStaysFrozen`, `TestTheDatabaseRulesOfAHouseShutOut` (every rule of the new definition as raw writes), `TestAHouseOfACoLoadConsolidationNeverTenderedLeaves`, `TestAHouseUnloadedByTheReworkOfAnotherConsolidationLeavesWithNoLink`, `TestAHouseWithNoReworkOfItsOwnIsNotRefusedForAnotherHousesReworkInAnotherExecution` (line numbers repeat across executions), `TestALeavingWithNoReworkIsNotDatedBeforeTheHousesLatestReversal` (commands, the equal time), `TestAHouseTakenBackFromTheTenderBeforeItClosedLeavesTheCoLoadConsolidation`, `TestAHouseOffloadedByTheCarrierLeavesTheCoLoadConsolidationOnceItsDiscrepancyIsResolved`, `TestALeavingWithNoReworkIsNotDatedBeforeTheLatestReversalInASecondExecution` (the latest reversal is in a second container); with a lock held on a second connection, each order: `TestADetachOfAHouseWithNoLoadAndALoadOfTheSameHouseWaitForEachOther`. Cross-context (`house_shut_out_integration_test.go`, real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN, BOOK and SEA in the last): `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingLeavesTheConsolidationWithEveryContext`, `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingIsTransferredWithEveryContext`, `TestSEAFinalizesAfterAShutOutHouseLeftOnceItsAssociationIsEnded`. Three tests of V2.86 changed where they pinned that a house never loaded stays (`TestAHouseTakenOutByOrdinaryReversalsLeavesWithNoLinkWhileAnotherHousesReworkExists`, `TestAHouseNeverLoadedLeavesAClosedConsolidationAndOnlyTheUnloadedOneNamesARework`, and the detach from C-T in `TestAHouseUnloadedInAReworkIsTransferredStuffedAgainAndSealed`, where the command refuses again once the house has live packs in C-T); the first two were renamed to say what they now prove, and the migration list test names the new file.
- V2.88 lets SEA follow a container's rework: PHY's new seal and a new VGM (step 3 of 7 of "a house leaves a sealed container") Migration sea/000010 (deployment: SEA writes the two new columns in every visit UPDATE, and the application role's UPDATE grant on them is in the grants step, `.github/workflows/org-pg16-contract.yml`, not in the migration, as for every earlier column, so apply the migration and then the grants before SEA runs).
  - FUL's new query. `GetOceanEvidenceCurrency` (OceanEvidenceCurrency v1, relationship 10, FUL to SEA; a new api interface `OceanEvidenceQueries` and a new type, beside `OceanOperationPlanQueries`, which keeps its shape; `OceanOperationQueries` joins the two, and SEA's `New` and the composition root's `SEA` take the joined one) answers, for a shipment, whether `RecordVerifiedLoadEvidence` would record a new evidence version now (`NewVersionDue`), with the revision, status and evidence version FUL held. It reads as the command reads: the shipment first, without a lock, for the consolidation FUL records it attached to; PHY's evidence for that consolidation, outside any transaction; the shipment again, a conflict if its attachment changed meanwhile; then `Shipment.WouldRecordEvidence`, which is the comparison `RecordEvidence` makes (the digest, the execution status and the packs final for planning), shared between the two in `evidenceChange`. It writes nothing and takes no lock (`Shipments.Read` is the unlocked read; run: FUL's revision and evidence version are the same before and after each question). What it answers: a cancelled shipment, not due, with the status CANCELLED and PHY not asked (the command refuses it with SHIPMENT_CANCELLED and records nothing; run, real PHY); a shipment with no evidence recorded yet and nothing in PHY, due (the command records an empty first version, and none is due once it has; run for a sea and for an air shipment, real PHY); an air shipment, by the same comparison, since FUL reads PHY for air with the screenings in the evidence, as the command does (run against the real PHY; that the answer depends on the air flag is run in a unit test whose PHY returns other evidence for air); a shipment PHY cannot answer, the refusal the command gives (reasoned from the adapter, not run). SEA reads it as `Plan.EvidenceBehind`, outside its transaction, next to the plan; it is not part of what SEA freezes.
  - The seal followed. When SEA writes a house's entry (`AssociateHouseBill`, `RefreshHouseBillFacts`) that carries a stuffing of execution X, each live visit of the movement that SEA took as stuffed with X (STUFFED, VGM_SUBMITTED, GATED_IN or LOADED) is offered it (`Visit.FollowSeal`): when its seal, by `sealKey`, is none of the visit's seals and either PHY's sealed-at is after the visit's `StuffedAt`, or the visit has no difference reason, or the container is resealed (below), SEA records it in the same transaction as a SHIPPER seal applied at PHY's sealed-at, with the source "followed from PHY's stuffing X, sealed ..., as FUL relays it (PHY's text \"...\")", by the command's actor, at its business time, through `RecordSeal`, so it is numbered, stored and evented as any seal. The second condition is for a reseal PHY records after SEA's stuffing but dated before it (the independent review's case, run): without a difference reason `StuffingDone` required PHY's seal of that time to be one of the visit's seals, and a visit's seals are never removed, so a PHY seal the visit does not hold is a seal PHY applied since; a container is resealed when PHY's seal now differs from what SEA took when it took the container as stuffed: SEA keeps, with the stuffing, PHY's seal as FUL relayed it and its sealed-at (`stuffed_phy_seal`, `stuffed_phy_sealed_at`, migration sea/000010: set together, only at the step ASSIGNED to STUFFED, from the stuffing `StuffingDone` takes, checked at commit against a live house's stuffing, never changed afterwards), and "resealed" is a difference from it by seal key or by sealed-at, nothing else: not business times, not the entries, not the difference reason (run: PHY resealing before SEA's stuffing-done is no reseal since the stuffing, with a reason or without, and a reason stated against SEA's own seal is kept and that seal published; the review's two cases through the real contexts). For a visit stuffed before this version, which has neither column, the earlier rule stands as a known limit: PHY's sealed-at after `StuffedAt`, or no difference reason, or the movement's history of entries (SEA keeps every entry of a house, the ended ones included, with their stuffings) showing the stuffing carried another seal key or sealed-at, whatever the business times (run through the real contexts for a container taken at hour 30 with a difference reason and PHY's rework recorded afterwards with times 22 to 25: the new seal is followed and published); that rule misfires when PHY resealed before SEA's stuffing-done (a false warning, or a reason dropped). Known limit K3: that false positive remains for every visit stuffed before V2.88. Once resealed, the reason no longer exempts F2 (it was stated against a seal PHY no longer has), the seal is followed, F3 and the determination time apply, and the warning below is added. PHY keeps free text: it is recorded upper-cased and trimmed when that fits SEA's seal form (capital letters A-Z and digits, single spaces, hyphens or slashes inside, 35 characters at most), else with each run of other characters made one space when that fits and has the same `sealKey` (`seal#9 new` is followed as `SEAL 9 NEW`; run), else the seal is not recorded and the command is not refused: the house's entry is written all the same and the seal becomes a pending item (below) that says what to do (run). A seal the seal rules refuse for its time (the command's business time is before PHY's sealed-at or before the visit's last change) is treated the same way: the refresh is written, no seal is recorded, and the item says to record PHY's seal at a business time not before PHY sealed it (the time) and not before the container's last change (the time) (run). A later refresh of the same house records the seal by itself once its business time allows: the house's facts are then as SEA froze them, which a refresh refused before (nothing to refresh); it now follows the seals of the entry the house has and is refused only when nothing was followed (run). When a seal is recorded this way on a FINAL movement the movement returns to OPEN, as `RecordContainerSeal` does, and that needs a business time not before the movement's last change (reasoned from `Supersede`; not run). The second house carrying the same seal finds it held and records nothing (run: the visit's revision does not move). A visit still ASSIGNED is not followed (`RecordStuffingDone` compares as before), nor is a RELEASED one. The locks are taken in the order `ChangeVisit` takes them: the command id, then the movement's live visits in the order they were assigned, then the movement; a container assigned while the visits were being taken makes the command a conflict, to be tried again (run: the movement's lock is held on a second connection, the assignment queues first and the refresh after it; the refresh is a conflict with nothing written and passes when tried again). Deterministic tests hold the movement's lock on a second connection and queue a refresh that follows a seal with `RecordContainerSeal` and `AssertVgm` of the same visit, in three orders (run, and a probe that a refresh waiting for the visit does not hold the movement). The seal event's payload: the contract's shape would allow it (an optional `reason` exists), but `SealRecorded` carries none today and a reason would change what its consumers read from it, so it is left to the seal's source text, which the visit's read model shows.
  - The principle (the owner's design of 2026-11-20): hard stops only at external commitments (`FinalizeOceanDocFacts`) and at physical rules; everything else is accepted and recorded, and a refusal names its way out. Information is never blocked: a refresh or an association is not refused because a downstream rule cannot take what PHY reported. Propagation stays manual (FUL and SEA are read by hand); the commitment point checks freshness (F0); and the pending list says what to re-read or record.
  - The pending items. `Movement.PendingItems(plans, visits)` is the one function, and `Finalize` refuses while an item is blocking, naming the first with a count of the others (the stuffing-done path uses its house items). SEA's api has `GetPendingItems` (`api.PendingQueries`, read-only; it reads FUL's plans and evidence currency as `FinalizeOceanDocFacts` does, outside any transaction; not offered over HTTP). Each item has a kind, a scope (HOUSE or CONTAINER), the shipment or container number, a message that says who does what, and whether it blocks. Blocking kinds: `FUL_BEHIND_PHY` (FUL: record the evidence; refresh the house if SEA then reports it stale), `HOUSE_PLAN_NOT_READ`, `HOUSE_STALE` (SEA: refresh the house), `HOUSE_NOT_REPORTED` (FUL no longer reports the house in the consolidation: refresh it, or end its association), `CONTAINER_NO_SEAL`, `HOUSES_DISAGREE_ON_SEAL` (F1: refresh the houses), `PHY_SEAL_NOT_ON_CONTAINER` (F2, with the reason: refresh a house that carries it, or record it by hand in SEA's form with the same letters and digits at a business time not before PHY sealed it and the container's last change, or PHY has to seal the container again when no form of the text has the same letters and digits), `LATER_SHIPPER_SEAL` (F2), `GATED_IN_BEFORE_RESEAL` (F3; its way out is the next step, the container's return from the terminal (since V2.89: GATE_OUT_TERMINAL)), `NO_VGM_BY_CUTOFF`, `VGM_BEFORE_RESEAL` (F4: assert a new one), `HOUSE_NOT_STUFFED`, `HOUSE_NAMES_UNKNOWN_CONTAINER`, `HOUSE_PACKS_NOT_STUFFED`. Two warnings never block (the design's known risk, that business times are trusted), and both are given only when the times are out of order, that is when a resealed container's reseal is dated not after `StuffedAt` (told by what SEA took, or for a visit stuffed before this version by the history or by the seals the container held): `RESEAL_DATED_BEFORE_STUFFING`, and `VGM_ENTERED_BEFORE_SEAL_KNOWN`, when the container's latest VGM was entered (the system clock, `EnteredAt`) before SEA entered the seal with P's key, so check that its mass was determined after the reseal. In the ordinary order (the reseal after `StuffedAt`) neither is given: a correct new VGM keyed before SEA read the reseal is no cause for a warning, and the determination time already blocks an old weighing. Run: every kind in the domain tests, the pending list at each stage of the owner's day through the real contexts (before the rework none; FUL behind for the three houses; stale once FUL read it; and so on to none after the new VGM), the two warnings through the real contexts, and `Finalize` refusing on each blocking kind and passing with only warnings.
  - Finalize's and stuffing-done's rules. Own-container movements only. F0, FUL is current: for every live house FUL's evidence is PHY's current one (`Plan.EvidenceBehind` false), else "FUL has not recorded PHY's latest facts of ...; record them in FUL, and refresh the house if SEA then reports it STALE_HOUSE"; the module reads it before SEA's transaction, with the plans, and it is asked before the facts are compared, so that FUL behind says so and not STALE_HOUSE; the message says to record the facts in FUL and to refresh the house if SEA then reports it STALE_HOUSE (a change of PHY's evidence that is not a change of the house's facts needs no refresh). With P the seal and sealed-at of PHY's stuffing X as the live houses carry it: F1, the houses agree (the same seal by key and the same sealed-at), else "refresh the houses"; F2, P's key is one of the visit's seals and every seal after the latest with that key is a CUSTOMS or CARRIER seal, with today's behaviour kept when the visit has a difference reason and the container is not resealed (resealed is: P's sealed-at is after `StuffedAt`, or the visit has no difference reason and none of its seals with P's key was recorded at a business time not after `StuffedAt`); F3, when the container is resealed and has a GATE_IN_TERMINAL or LOADED_ON_BOARD event of its own that occurred before P's sealed-at, or a carrier LOADED report that names its number or names no container (a report naming another container is that container's; since V2.89 they only warn when the movement has two or more live containers) that occurred before it, it gated in or was loaded before PHY sealed it again and its return from the terminal is not recorded yet (since V2.89: GATE_OUT_TERMINAL), the message saying which (gate-in, loading, the carrier's report); a time equal to P's sealed-at is not before it (run for each; the review's two-container case, one loaded and the other resealed, now finalizes); F4, the latest VGM was submitted not before P's sealed-at, in addition to `vgmReady`, and for a resealed container its mass was determined not before it (the message says to assert one determined after it; run: an old weighing submitted again is refused). The order is no seal, F1, F2, F3, `vgmReady`, F4, so that the gated-in container is told F3 and not that its VGM is old. Without a reseal F4 always holds (reasoned from the code: an assertion needs STUFFED, which `StuffingDone` takes not before PHY's sealed-at, and a stored visit with an assertion before `StuffedAt` is refused on restore; every existing finalizing test passes unchanged, run). A container no live house names has no P and none of F1 to F4 apply. `RecordStuffingDone` now reads FUL's plans and evidence currency before the transaction and `Movement.CheckHouses` (F0 and the STALE_HOUSE checks `Finalize` makes, now shared) runs in it, so that a stuffing is not taken during a rework that FUL or SEA has not read; its other rules stay as they were.
  - What is still refused, and why. A refresh and an association are never refused for what PHY reported (they write what they can and the rest is a pending item); stuffing-done and finalize check that FUL and SEA have read PHY, by design, because they are commitments. Only commitments and physical rules: `FinalizeOceanDocFacts` on a blocking item, `RecordStuffingDone` while FUL or SEA has not read PHY (this version's check), a VGM on a gated-in or loaded container. A new VGM is required after PHY seals a container again: F4. A container that gated in or loaded before PHY sealed it again is refused with F3's message, because SEA has no way back from GATED_IN or LOADED yet and a VGM is still refused on both; recording the container's return from the terminal is the next step (3b) (since V2.89: GATE_OUT_TERMINAL). A later SHIPPER seal that is not PHY's is refused at finalize, also PHY's broken seal recorded again after the new one, because the seal SEA publishes must be PHY's latest unless customs or the carrier sealed it since. A later CUSTOMS or CARRIER seal is accepted and published.
  - What DOC reads after it (run, `TestAReworkAfterSEAFinalizedAndDOCIssuedWithEveryContext`, real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY, BOOK, AIR, SEA, FIN and DOC). With the commands in their real order F3 refuses this case: DOC issues only from FINAL facts that state an on-board date, which comes from a loading, and a container loaded before PHY sealed it again is refused at finalize, so after a DOC issue a rework is not followed by a version n+1 (run in the other cases: finalize refused with F3's message (since V2.89 a returned container is not)). The test dates the container's VGM, gate-in and loading (business times 26 to 28) after PHY's reseal (25), and runs the commands in the real order otherwise, only to show what DOC does with a version n+1; it is not a chronology the rules allow, and it still runs as described after the batch-2 changes (run). With nobody re-reading, PHY's unloading, reseal and detachment move nothing in SEA or DOC: the FINAL version 1 stays current with the three houses and the old seal, and the two issued house bills (H01 and H03, released EXPRESS) stay as issued. FUL taking the evidence and SEA ending H03's association reopens the movement (version 1 is SUPERSEDED) and DOC's `OpenDocumentAmendment` is refused (DOC_SOURCE_NOT_FINAL). Refreshing H01 records the new seal, refreshing H02 records nothing, and the next finalize is version 2 with the new seal, H01 and H02 (12 packs, 612 kg), the VGM v1. DOC then still holds both bills as issued from version 1, with the old seal; an amendment of H01's bill is accepted and its draft is from version 2, with the new seal and H01's 6 packs and 312 kg; an amendment of H03's bill is refused (DOC_MASTER_MISMATCH). DOC is not changed; what its amendment says after a house left is PR 4's.
