  - Not yet: the container's return from the terminal, so that a gated-in or loaded container can take a new VGM (PR 3b) (since V2.89: GATE_OUT_TERMINAL, for a gated-in container only); DOC's amendment of what the issued bills say, and of a bill whose house left (PR 4); a STUFFED container that no house names any more (it publishes as before: no P to compare); FUL and SEA still read PHY only when they are made to, by hand; FUL and SEA are still read by hand (the pending list says what to read); DOC's per-container on-board date and the drafts of a house that left are PR 4's; FIN's follow-up is PRs 5 and 6; FUL still does not say that a container is in rework. Known limits (reasoned from the code, the first also run): nothing marks a VGM as unconfirmed (the warnings are only shown in the pending list); a seal with a letter outside A-Z can only be resolved by PHY sealing again, which a gated-in or loaded container cannot do until the next step (PR 3b) (since V2.89: GATE_OUT_TERMINAL); a refresh that only follows a seal on a FINAL movement supersedes it as `RecordContainerSeal` does, but no real command sequence reaches it (a followable seal blocks finalize, and any change of a house reopens the movement first), so that branch is reasoned, not run; SEA relies on the business times it is given, so a VGM whose mass is stated as determined after PHY's reseal is taken as weighed after it, and a visit with a difference reason whose PHY seal is dated before `StuffedAt` is not counted as resealed (the one case a reseal can then go unseen).
  - Tests: FUL, pure (`evidence_current_test.go`, domain): `TestWouldRecordEvidenceIsTheComparisonOfRecordEvidence`; (`evidence_currency_test.go`, application): `TestGetEvidenceCurrencyAsksLikeTheCommandAndWritesNothing`, `TestGetEvidenceCurrencyOfACancelledShipmentIsNotDueAndAsksNoOne`. SEA, pure (`rework_test.go`, domain): `TestFinalizeWithoutAResealIsAsBefore`, `TestFollowSeal`, `TestASealTheRulesCannotTakeIsNotRecordedAndIsPending`, `TestFinalizeAfterAReseal`, `TestFinalizeLaterSealsAfterPHYsSeal`, `TestFinalizeTheDifferenceReasonBeforeAndAfterAReseal`, `TestFinalizeTheHousesAgreeOnPHYsSeal`, `TestFinalizeAContainerGatedInBeforeTheReseal`, `TestHousesAreCheckedAgainstFULsEvidence`, (`pending_test.go`) `TestAMovementReadyToFinalizeHasNoPendingItems`, `TestEveryBlockingPendingItemIsListedAndRefusesFinalize`, `TestWarningsDoNotBlockFinalize`, `TestACoLoadHasOnlyHouseItems`, `TestAResealTheBusinessTimesCannotShowIsTakenFromTheEntryHistory`, `TestAResealWithTheSameSealedAtAndAnotherKeyIsTakenFromTheEntryHistory`; (`stuffed_seal_test.go`) `TestStuffingDoneKeepsWhatSEATook`, `TestAResealBeforeTheStuffingIsNoResealSinceTheStuffing`, `TestAResealAfterTheStuffingIsAnyChangeFromWhatSEATook`, `TestAVisitStuffedBeforeV288KeepsTheLegacyRule`, (`stuffed_seal_test.go`) `TestStuffingDoneKeepsWhatSEATook`, `TestAResealBeforeTheStuffingIsNoResealSinceTheStuffing`, `TestAResealAfterTheStuffingIsAnyChangeFromWhatSEATook`, `TestAVisitStuffedBeforeV288KeepsTheLegacyRule`; (`pending_test.go`) `TestAResealWithTheSameSealedAtAndAnotherKeyIsTakenFromTheEntryHistory`; (`pending_test.go`, package sea) `TestPublishedPendingKindsAreTheModels`; `TestFinalizeAContainerNoHouseNamesIsAsBefore`; (`rework_batch2_test.go`) `TestAHouseInTwoContainersBothResealedFollowsBothSeals`, `TestF3IsPerContainer`, `TestF3CarrierReportsAndBoundaries`, `TestF2AndFollowCompareSealsByKey`, `TestF1ComparesTheSealKeys` (F1 is not reachable through the real contexts: `CheckHouses` demands each house's facts equal FUL's first, and two houses of one container carry different seals only when FUL's plans differ in a way it does not already refuse; reasoned, not proven impossible), `TestFollowSealTextForms`, `TestABackdatedResealIsFollowedAndCounted`, `TestTheVGMIsDeterminedAfterTheReseal`. FUL (`evidence_currency_test.go`): `TestGetEvidenceCurrencyIsAConflictWhenTheAttachmentChangesWhilePHYIsAsked`, `TestGetEvidenceCurrencyReadsAnAirShipmentAsAir` (the air flag changes the evidence the fake PHY returns, as the adapter's does, so the answer depends on it). PostgreSQL (`sea_rework_test.go`): `TestSEAFollowsPHYsNewSealOnPostgres`, `TestAssociatingAHouseFollowsPHYsNewSealOnPostgres`, `TestASealTheRulesCannotTakeIsWrittenAndPendingOnPostgres`, `TestWhatSEATookAtStuffingOnPostgres` (the raw writes past SEA for each rule of the migration, and the visit stuffed before this version), `TestAnAssignedVisitIsNotFollowedOnPostgres`, `TestFinalizeFollowsPHYsRework`, `TestFinalizeAContainerGatedInBeforeTheResealOnPostgres`, `TestStuffingDoneChecksFULAndSEAHaveReadPHYOnPostgres`, `TestARefreshThatFollowsASealTakesTheVisitBeforeTheMovement` (three orders), `TestTheRefreshWaitsForTheVisitNotForTheMovement`, `TestAContainerAssignedWhileTheVisitsAreTakenMakesTheRefreshAConflict`. Cross-context (`internal/bootstrap`): `TestFULSaysWhetherItsEvidenceIsPHYsCurrentOneWithPHY`, and `TestSEAFollowsAContainersReworkWithEveryContext`, `TestAReworkBeforeSEATookTheStuffingWithEveryContext`, `TestAContainerGatedInBeforeTheReworkIsStoppedWithEveryContext`, `TestAResealRecordedAfterSEAsStuffingButDatedBeforeItIsFollowedWithEveryContext`, `TestTheVGMIsDeterminedAfterTheResealWithEveryContext`, `TestADifferenceReasonContainerResealedWithBackdatedTimesWithEveryContext`, `TestAResealBeforeTheStuffingIsNoResealSinceWithEveryContext`, `TestAReleasedVisitIsNotPendingOnPostgres` (PostgreSQL), `TestPHYsSealTextSEAsFormDoesNotTakeWithEveryContext` (it also asserts finalize's STALE_HOUSE "FUL no longer reports" for the house that left, no longer asserted by the existing house-leaves test), `TestPHYsSealTextThatNeedsTheSealRecordedByHandWithEveryContext`, `TestPHYsSealTextWithALetterOutsideAZStopsTheMovementWithEveryContext`, and `TestAReworkAfterSEAFinalizedAndDOCIssuedWithEveryContext` (the owner's box A, H01, H02 and H03 of 10, 15 and 5 W/M in one 22G1). Two existing tests changed, `TestSEAFinalizesAfterAHouseLeftOnceItsAssociationIsEnded` and `TestSEAFinalizesAfterAShutOutHouseLeftOnceItsAssociationIsEnded`; each now asserts that the stuffing is refused with STALE_HOUSE while the house that left is still associated, and ends the association before taking it.
- V2.89 records a container taken back out of the terminal (step 3b of "a house leaves a sealed container"). Migration sea/000011.
  - The event and the status. `RecordContainerEvent` takes a new kind, `GATE_OUT_TERMINAL` (`api.EventGateOut`): the full container taken back out of the terminal to the forwarder's warehouse, with a location, the time it happened, a source and a reference as any event (the same source and reference again is a replay: no new revision and no new event, and the same reference with other content is refused). It is accepted on a GATED_IN visit only and moves it back to VGM_SUBMITTED; it is refused on a LOADED visit (the message says that a container loaded on board is handled outside the system), on VGM_SUBMITTED, STUFFED and ASSIGNED (a container is taken back out of the terminal only while it is gated in) and on a RELEASED one. The VGM stands: `AssertVGM`, `RecordVgmResponse` and `GATE_IN_TERMINAL` work as on any VGM_SUBMITTED visit, and a second round (in, out, in) is allowed. The model's check of a stored visit (`validate`) says the same as the database: GATED_IN or LOADED exactly when the latest gate-in comes after the latest gate-out, LOADED exactly when a loading is recorded, no gate-out after a loading, and each gate-out follows a gate-in with no gate-out between them. A return is not dated before the latest gate-in it follows (a time equal to it is accepted); the model refuses it, the database does not. On a FINAL movement the return is recorded and the movement stays FINAL, with its published facts as they were (they do not state a gate status: run through SEA's commands, `TestAContainerIsTakenBackOutOfTheTerminalOnPostgres`, and through the real contexts, `TestAReturnOnAFinalMovementKeepsItFinalWithEveryContext`); what follows a rework reopens it as V2.88 does (run: the end of H03's association, in the real contexts, and a new VGM after the return, through SEA's commands; a new seal reopens it by the rule of V2.88, not run again here). The event in the outbox is the existing `ContainerEventRecorded.v1` with the kind as its reason, in the shape and under the id rule of a gate-in; the published contract needs no change (the reason is free text there, no list of kinds is published), the kind is listed in `api` and in the model, and the register of the archived design documents that lists the kinds was not changed.
  - F3 with returns, and the warning. `GATED_IN_BEFORE_RESEAL` is raised when the container was at the terminal at PHY's sealed-at: its latest gate-in that occurred before that time (a time equal to PHY's is not before it) has no later gate-out at all (by the event's number, whatever the gate-out's date), or when it was loaded before that time, by its own loading or by the carrier's loading reports for it as in V2.88. A container that was at the terminal at the reseal and has been loaded since is told the same, since its LOADED status would not allow the return or a new VGM. A carrier's loading report that names no container makes no container loaded before the reseal and blocks none: it cannot show which container was loaded when a movement's containers gate in and load at different times. F3 decides from the container's own gate-in, gate-out and loading events and from the reports that name this container. Such a report dated before PHY's reseal of a resealed container gives the warning `CARRIER_LOADING_BEFORE_RESEAL` (it does not block): check whether this container was the one loaded; if it was, it is handled outside the system. A container that never gated in is therefore not blocked by it, and one at the terminal is told to record its return. With exactly one live container (released visits are not live) such a report can only be about that container, and it counts as naming it: it blocks as a named report does, as the releasable conflict of the next bullet, and gives no warning. The live containers are counted as they stand when the items are read, so a second container assigned later makes the report a warning from then on (the report no longer shows which container was loaded), and a released second visit leaves it blocking. A container that SEA's own events show loaded before the reseal is told first: "was loaded before PHY sealed it again, by its loading, a container loaded on board is not supported in SEA: it is handled outside the system". Since batch 6 a carrier's loading report is not told that way: the container's own events come first, and when they show neither a loading nor a stay at the terminal at the reseal, the report conflicts with SEA's records and the item is the conflict of the next bullet. A container at the terminal is told: "was at the terminal when PHY sealed it again, it gated in (the time) and its return is not recorded; record it (RecordContainerEvent GATE_OUT_TERMINAL, dated before PHY opened the rework), then assert a new VGM (AssertVgm) and gate it in again". A container gated in and returned before the reseal, or gated in only after it, is not asked; after the return the old VGM is F4's (`VGM_BEFORE_RESEAL`) until a new one is asserted. The order of the checks is as before (no seal, F1, F2, F3, `vgmReady`, F4). The new warning `RETURN_DATED_AFTER_RESEAL` (it does not block) is given when the container is resealed, its latest gate-in before the reseal is followed by a gate-out, and no gate-out after that gate-in is dated before the reseal (the return is dated at or after it): the times say it was resealed at the terminal, so check them. The container counts as returned all the same, so the operator who recorded the return with a late time is not left without a way out (events cannot be corrected): finalize passes with the warning once a new VGM is asserted. SEA judges the return only by the times it is given.
  - A conflict with the carrier's report, released with a reason (the owner's decision of 2026-11-21). When SEA's own records and a carrier's loading report contradict each other at a reseal, Finalize blocks and says so, and the operator, having checked, may release it by stating a reason, recorded with who and when (the same kind of rule as the difference reason at stuffing-done). The item is `GATED_IN_BEFORE_RESEAL` raised only because of a carrier LOADED report (one that names the container, or one that names none on a movement with exactly one live container, released visits not counted) dated before PHY's sealed-at, while the container's own events show neither a loading before then nor a stay at the terminal then. An item raised by SEA's own events (its own loading, or a gate-in with no return) cannot be released this way: the order is the container's own events first. Its message says what conflicts (the report's time and how it names the container, PHY's reseal time, and, truthfully for both cases, either that SEA has no gate-in or loading of it before then or that SEA has it gated in and taken back out before then and no loading of it) and the ways out: release it with a reason once checked (`AcceptCarrierLoadingConflict`), or, if the container was in fact loaded, it is handled outside the system. The new command `AcceptCarrierLoadingConflict` (`api.AcceptCarrierLoadingConflict`, action `AcceptCarrierLoadingConflict`, not offered over HTTP) takes the visit, its expected revision, a reason (required, 500 characters at most), the actor and the business time, and is accepted while the movement is OPEN (a FINAL or cancelled movement is refused, as for any visit change without an event) and the visit has such an item. It records which carrier report (its number on the movement) and which PHY seal (as the houses carry it, and when it was sealed) it releases: kept, immutable, numbered from 1 per visit, in the visit's read model (`Releases`, latest first). The release covers that report against that seal and nothing else: PHY sealing the container again, or another report, raises a new item with its own release; releasing the same report and seal twice is refused. A second container assigned later makes a nameless report a warning from then on, and the release records the report's number, so a release made earlier stays on the visit; while a second container is live, a recorded release shows on the visit (`Releases`), not in the pending list, because the report only warns then. After a release the item is the non-blocking warning `CARRIER_LOADING_CONFLICT_RELEASED` naming who released it, when and why, and Finalize passes if nothing else blocks; the release itself changes nothing of the movement or of the published facts. The event is `CarrierLoadingConflictReleased.v1` (subject the visit; its payload names the release number, `release_no`, and carries no reason, no seal and no names); the receipt names the release (`ReleaseNo`). The command takes the command id, the visit, then the movement, so a release racing a finalize of the same movement is ordered by the movement's lock: whichever commits first, the other follows the rules, and a FINAL movement never holds an unreleased blocking item (run, deterministic, both orders). Migration sea/000011 also holds `cargo_sea.carrier_conflict_release` (immutable, numbered without gaps per visit, row level security; written with a step of its visit's revision, on a visit that is stuffed or later, of a movement that is OPEN at commit, of a loading the carrier reported on the visit's movement; one release at most of a report against a seal and its sealed-at (a unique key, refused by name, whatever the number); the receipt table gains `result_release_no`); the application role's grants are the schema-wide SELECT and INSERT of the grants step, which covers the new table, so the workflow file did not change. Run past SEA as the application role: each rule refuses by name. What only the domain keeps: that the report is dated before PHY's seal, that the seal is PHY's, that the container's own events leave the conflict open, that the count of live containers allows a nameless report to count, and that the business time is not before the visit's last change (the database accepts a release of a loading the movement's carrier reported, whatever else). Run through the real contexts: the reviewer's K1 case, one container never gated in and a carrier report naming no container (`TestAConflictWithTheCarriersReportIsReleasedWithEveryContext`).
  - The database. Migration sea/000011: the container event kind check gains `GATE_OUT_TERMINAL`; `check_visit_change` (000010) also allows GATED_IN to VGM_SUBMITTED, only the status changing, by one revision, as for the other moves, and still no other backward move; `visit_inconsistency` (000006; none of 000007 to 000010 replaced it) says that a visit is GATED_IN or LOADED exactly when its latest gate-in (by event number) comes after its latest gate-out, LOADED exactly when a loading is recorded, and no gate-out comes after a loading, every other rule unchanged. `check_container_event_at_commit` (000008) is not replaced: it reads the visit's status under its lock and asks `visit_inconsistency`, so it applies the new rules to the new kind as it is. No column is added, so the grants step does not change; the kind check is dropped and added again (ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on `container_event` for that statement, one scan, no rewrite). Run past SEA as the application role (`TestTheDatabaseRulesOfTheReturnOnPostgres`): each rule refuses by name, a refused write leaves the visit as it was, and the return as SEA writes it is accepted. A gate-out follows a gate-in of the same visit with no gate-out between them (by event number), refused by name, and the model's check of a stored visit says the same; no row written before the migration has the kind (the kind check of 000004 refused it), so the rule meets no existing row (run by the independent reviewer on a populated database: 71 visits, every status, digests unchanged). What only the domain keeps: that a return is not dated before the gate-in it follows (the database accepts it; the model refuses it in `RecordContainerEvent`, asking for the right time) and that a command's business time is not before the visit's last change. A return and a loading of the same visit racing: through SEA's commands one takes the visit first and the other is a conflict, then refused by the rules with the new revision; past SEA the second write waits for the visit's row and is refused by the trigger once the first commits, in both orders (run, deterministic, a lock held on a second connection, the waiters counted by `pg_blocking_pids`).
  - What DOC and FIN read after it (reasoned from the code, and run for SEA's facts). DOC reads SEA only through `GetOceanDocFacts` (the version, its hash, whether it is FINAL, the containers' number, size-type and seal, the houses' breakdown, the on-board date); none of it states a visit's status or a gate event, so a return changes none of it and a movement with no return publishes what it did. The on-board date comes from a loading, and a loaded container is not supported, so a container taken back never carries one from before the rework. FIN does not read SEA at all (it imports nothing of it); the cost of the pull-back is FIN's later step.
  - Not yet: a container already loaded on board (handled outside the system: SEA has no way back from LOADED, the database refuses it, and F3 says so); the cost of the pull-back (FIN, a later step); DOC's amendment of what the issued bills say, and of a bill whose house left (PR 4). The return is not checked against FUL or PHY: nothing tells SEA that PHY or the terminal agrees.
  - Tests: domain (`container_returns_test.go`): `TestTheReturnIsAcceptedOnAGatedInVisitOnly`, `TestAReturnedContainerIsAsAnyVGMSubmittedVisitAndGatesInAgain`, `TestRestoreRefusesAVisitThatDisagreesWithItsGateEvents`, `TestFinalizeAfterAReturnFromTheTerminal`, `TestAContainerLoadedBeforeTheResealIsHandledOutsideTheSystem`, `TestAContainerGatedInBeforeAndLoadedAfterTheResealIsHandledOutsideTheSystem`, `TestACarrierReportNamingNoContainerOnlyWarns` (two live containers), `TestACarrierReportNamingNoContainerWithOneLiveContainerNamesIt`, `TestAReturnIsNotDatedBeforeItsGateIn`, `TestAGateInOnALoadedVisitLeavesItLoaded`; domain (`conflict_test.go`): `TestACarrierLoadingConflictIsReleasedWithAReason`, `TestACarrierLoadingConflictIsReleasedOnlyWhenItIsTheCarriersAlone`, `TestALaterResealOrReportRaisesANewConflict`, `TestRestoreRefusesAVisitWithInconsistentReleases`; the module (`container_returns_test.go`): `TestAReturnIsPublishedAsTheContainerEventWithTheNewKind`, `TestTheReleaseOfAConflictIsPublishedWithItsNumber`. PostgreSQL (`sea_container_returns_test.go`): `TestAContainerIsTakenBackOutOfTheTerminalOnPostgres`, `TestF3WithReturnsOnPostgres`, `TestTheDatabaseRulesOfTheReturnOnPostgres`, `TestAReturnRacingALoadingOfTheSameVisitOnPostgres`, `TestAReturnRacingALoadingPastSEAOnPostgres`, `TestALoadingAfterTheResealAndAnEarlyReturnOnPostgres`, `TestACarrierReportNamingNoContainerOnlyWarnsOnPostgres` (one container, a second assigned later, a released second visit), `TestAConflictWithTheCarriersReportIsReleasedWithAReasonOnPostgres`, `TestALaterResealNeedsItsOwnReleaseOnPostgres`, `TestTheDatabaseRulesOfTheReleaseOnPostgres`, `TestAReleaseRacingAFinalizeOfTheSameMovementOnPostgres`, `TestANamelessReportIsReleasedOnlyWithOneLiveContainerOnPostgres`, `TestTheReleaseRulesOfFactReceiptAndTenantOnPostgres`. Cross-context (`internal/bootstrap`, `container_returns_integration_test.go`): `TestAContainerGatedInBeforeTheReworkIsTakenBackWithEveryContext` (the owner's box A, the pending list at each stage), `TestAContainerLoadedBeforeTheReworkStaysRefusedWithEveryContext`, `TestAReturnOnAFinalMovementKeepsItFinalWithEveryContext`, `TestACarrierReportNamingNoContainerWithOneContainerWithEveryContext` (one container, then a second assigned later), `TestAConflictWithTheCarriersReportIsReleasedWithEveryContext`. The migration list test (`cmd/cargo-migrate`) names 000011. Existing tests changed: `TestTheModelEmitsThePublishedEventTypes` (it enumerates the published event types: `CarrierLoadingConflictReleased` joins the list and the test emits it), and those that pin `GATED_IN_BEFORE_RESEAL`'s message, whose way out changed (`TestEveryBlockingPendingItemIsListedAndRefusesFinalize`, `TestF3IsPerContainer`, `TestF3CarrierReportsAndBoundaries` (a report naming no container now only warns), `TestFinalizeAContainerGatedInBeforeTheReseal`, `TestFinalizeAContainerGatedInBeforeTheResealOnPostgres`, `TestAContainerGatedInBeforeTheReworkIsStoppedWithEveryContext`, `TestAResealRecordedAfterSEAsStuffingButDatedBeforeItIsFollowedWithEveryContext`).
- V2.90 dates a house bill by its own containers' loading (step 4 of "a house leaves a sealed container").
  - SEA's per-container date. Each `DocContainer` of OceanDocFacts v1 states `on_board_at`, the container's own earliest loading: its own LOADED_ON_BOARD event, or the carrier's earliest LOADED_ON_BOARD report that names its number, or a report that names no container when the movement has exactly one live container (the reading of V2.89's F3, `carrierLoadingFacts`); none when it is not loaded (a report that names no container with two or more live containers is the movement's date and no container's). The movement's `on_board_at` is as it was, the earliest of any loading, because other readers use it (the carrier's document). The facts also state `container_dates: true` (`omitempty`): the contract is OceanDocFacts v1 with two fields added, a compatible addition (a reader that does not know them reads what it read; the api struct and the domain struct say so in their comments). Versions published before V2.90 are stored and stay as they were (their digest is over the stored bytes): they read with `ContainerDates` false and no container date, and a reader must not take that as "not loaded"; DOC does not (below). A loading that changes what the latest facts state for that container (none, or a later date) now also returns a FINAL movement to OPEN, by the container's own event (`Movement.ContainerLoadedOnBoard`, called by the visit command) and by a carrier report that names the container (`RecordCarrierFact`; a report that names none with exactly one live container is the movement's own earliest loading then, and reopens by it), even when the movement's own date does not change, provided a house of the published facts has packs in that container (a loading of a container no house names changes no house's date and leaves the movement FINAL; run, `TestALoadingOfAContainerNoHouseNamesLeavesTheMovementFinal` and the detach case with the real contexts): without it the movement stays FINAL after the first container loads and DOC would never see the second. The database's own rule (sea/000008) refuses a loading written to a FINAL movement only when it changes the movement's date, so it lets the wider reopening through and needs no migration. Known limit: the wider reopening is held by the Go code only. A direct write, or a Go path that forgets `ContainerLoadedOnBoard`, leaves a FINAL movement with stale container dates and the database refuses nothing; widening the trigger would need a migration. For facts published before V2.90 the reopening is read by the movement's date only, as it was (run: `TestFactsOfEarlierVersionsAreReadByTheMovementsDate`). A container that has no loading when SEA finalizes again is not an error.
  - DOC's house date and the issue rule. A house's on-board date is the latest date among the containers its breakdown names (`OceanFacts.HouseOnBoard`), none when any of them has none; the house bill's content carries it instead of the movement's (`OceanHouseContent`). A draft without it is allowed as before. Issue and issue of an amendment are refused with DOC_INCOMPLETE while a container carrying the house is not loaded, naming the container, "wait for its loading to be recorded and SEA to finalize again, then refresh the draft" (`houseNotLoaded`, in the issue gate after the source check, so facts that are not the draft's are refused for that first). Facts published before V2.90 (`ContainerDates` false) are read by the movement's date, as before: facts that state no on-board date at all (a co-load with no carrier report, old facts with none) are refused with the way out named ("record the loading in SEA, by the container's event or the carrier's report, and finalize the movement again") and are a blocking HOUSE_NOT_ALL_LOADED item that says the same (run, `TestFactsThatStateNoLoadingAtAllAreRefusedWithTheWayOut`). The other choice for old facts, a refusal with a refresh as its way out, is not a dead end either (SEA's next finalization states container dates), but it would block every bill of every movement that is FINAL at the deployment until someone finalizes it again, and a fall-back leaves no dead end at all; the cost is that such a bill is dated as it was. A house whose breakdown names no container (a co-load movement has no container of its own) is also read by the movement's date, which there is the carrier's report. A movement whose containers all load together states one date for every container, and every house's date is the movement's (run, `TestWhenTheContainersLoadTogetherTheHousesDateIsTheMovementsWithEveryContext`, real contexts).
  - The bill of a house that left. A house transferred to another movement: its draft or approved bill is refreshed onto the new movement with the existing revise path, `RefreshDocumentDraft` (or `OpenDocumentAmendment` for an issued bill) with `MovementID`, which the code allowed for an HBL and a house sea waybill and tested for a HAWB only; for a draft it is run now for an HBL through the real contexts, and for an issued HBL the amendment with `MovementID` is run on PostgreSQL in the stand-in world (`TestAHouseBillMovedToAnotherPortIsIssuedAtItsPlaceOnPostgres`, opened, approved and issued on the new movement) but not through the real contexts, where an issued bill's house cannot leave by a PHY transfer since V2.89 (a loaded container is not reworked and issue needs every container loaded), so only bills issued under facts published before V2.90 could reach it: the same number moves with the shipment in SEA (`FindHouseBill` names the shipment and the new movement), the draft becomes a draft of OM-2 and C-2 (the row follows), is approved and issued there, dated by the loading of its container in OM-2; a refresh that does not name the movement is refused with DOC_MASTER_MISMATCH, and an issue from the old facts with DOC_SOURCE_STALE. Nothing stopped it. The issue place is the new movement's port of loading (`OceanHouseContent`; with an authority for that place, run in a stand-in world: DOC_ISSUANCE_BLOCKED without one). Through the real contexts the port is the same: SEA associates a house only into a movement of the route of its FUL plan (run: a movement booked from another port refused the association), so a house cannot reach another port without a new plan, and the claim "the new port" is run only with the stand-in. A house only detached: its draft or approved bill is cancelled with `CancelTransportDocument` (run). The number stays with the shipment in SEA (`FindHouseBill` names the shipment and no movement; run); DOC binds a number only when a bill is issued (reasoned from the store's `issued_number`, not run for this case), so a bill never issued binds nothing, and an issued one stays bound to its shipment as before (a cancelled bill does not free it).
  - The abandon fix. `AbandonAmendment` put the document row back on the valid version's movement and consolidation when the abandoned draft had moved it (it did not; reasoned in V2.88's review, run now: `TestAbandoningAnAmendmentThatMovedTheBillOnPostgres`). The restore check (`validate`) required the row to name the latest version's movement, which after the fix is a discarded draft's, so it now accepts the row naming the latest version's or the latest version that is not discarded: a row written before the fix (naming the discarded draft's) still restores (`TestAbandoningAnAmendmentRestoresTheMovement`). `CancelTransportDocument` of a document whose amendment draft had moved it still leaves the row on the draft's movement (not changed: the row of a cancelled document is only read by its own id).
  - The pending items. `GetPendingItemsOfMovement` and `GetPendingItemsOfDocument` (`api.PendingQueries`, read-only; each item has a kind, the document, shipment and number, a message that says who does what, and whether it blocks issue) list for each not cancelled and not surrendered HBL or house sea waybill: HOUSE_NOT_ALL_LOADED (blocking: a container carrying the house has no loading; SEA's operator records it and finalizes again, then the bill is refreshed), SOURCE_SUPERSEDED (a draft, an approved bill or an amendment's draft was built on facts that are not SEA's latest FINAL version: refresh it), ISSUED_CONTENT_CHANGED (an issued bill: what SEA's latest FINAL facts now say of the house, the on-board date, the containers, the seal, the packs, weights or volume, the goods description, the package type, the movement or consolidation, or the voyage, ports and master bill, differs from what the bill states, compared as `OceanHouseContent` would build it from SEA's fields, `OceanFacts.HouseChanges`; the message names what changed and says the bill needs an amendment; parties and the entered fields are not compared, nor three SEA fields that cannot differ for a document DOC holds: the HBL number and the issuer's legal entity, which a document never changes, and the order ids, whose parties COM keeps locked for the bill while it is issued), HOUSE_LEFT_MOVEMENT (the house is not in SEA's latest facts of its movement: refresh onto the new movement or cancel), SOURCE_NOT_FINAL (SEA's movement is open: wait). A house that is not in the facts has only the third (for an issued bill the message says it stays valid and that an amendment naming the new movement moves it, or it is cancelled); with the facts not FINAL only the fourth; otherwise a draft, approved or amending document has the first and the second as they apply, and an issued bill has neither but ISSUED_CONTENT_CHANGED when its house changed: SEA finalizing again with nothing changed for the house leaves no item (run through the real contexts, including a carrier report naming CV-2 earlier than the loading that dated H03's bill after it was issued). No item of an issued bill blocks (it has no issue waiting); every item of a draft, approved or amending document does. A movement SEA has published no facts of (unknown, or never finalized) has no bill built on any and answers an empty list; an empty tenant or movement is refused. The query reads SEA's latest facts of the movement first, outside any transaction, as the commands do, and then each document in its own snapshot. It finds the documents of a movement by the existing index on the consolidation (`transport_document_by_consolidation`, the consolidation being the one the facts name) with the movement as a filter (`DocumentStore.ListOfMovement`; it lists the not cancelled bills, a surrendered one is dropped later by the domain): no scan of the tenant, no migration and nothing new in DOC's tables. A document found through the movement its row names: a bill whose house left is found through its old movement until it is refreshed onto the new one (run: the new movement lists nothing until then).
  - Another known limit. A carrier report that names no container counts for a container only while the movement has exactly one live container (V2.89's reading): after a second container is assigned and SEA finalizes again, the first container's date from such a report is gone, and a house that could be issued needs its container's own loading recorded (a container event, or a report naming it) before it can be issued again. The way out exists and the pending item names the container.
  - What FIN reads. FIN reads DOC only through `GetHouseDocumentSnapshot` (reasoned from `doc/api/snapshot.go` and `module.go`, not run for FIN): the valid issued version's number, master number, consolidation, origin, destination, pieces and weights, and the time of issue; none states the on-board date, so a house dated by its own containers changes nothing FIN reads. A bill moved by an amendment reads as it did for a HAWB: its consolidation is the valid version's.
  - Not yet: FIN's own step (the cost of the house that left, PR 5 and 6); automatic propagation (nobody tells DOC that SEA reopened or finalized or that a house left: the pending items are read by hand, as SEA's are); a STUFFED container that no house names any more is still published and finalized as before (run: after H03 left CV-2, SEA finalized OM-1 with CV-2 in it and no house); the stand-in only for a movement of another port; DOC's cancellation restores no row.
  - Tests: SEA, pure (`container_on_board_test.go`, domain): `TestEachContainerStatesItsOwnLoading`, `TestALoadingOfAnotherContainerReopensTheMovement`, `TestALoadingEarlierThanTheContainersPublishedDateReopensTheMovement`, `TestAContainersDateIsTheEarliestOfItsOwnLoadings`, `TestALoadingOfAContainerNoHouseNamesLeavesTheMovementFinal`, `TestACarrierLoadingReopensByTheContainerItIsAbout`, `TestFactsOfEarlierVersionsAreReadByTheMovementsDate`; SEA on PostgreSQL (`sea_container_on_board_test.go`): `TestEachContainerStatesItsOwnLoadingOnPostgres`, `TestACarrierReportGivesAContainerItsDateOnPostgres`. DOC, pure (`house_on_board_test.go`, domain): `TestAHousesOnBoardDateIsTheLatestOfItsContainers`, `TestAHouseBillIsIssuedOnlyWhenEveryContainerIsLoaded`, `TestPendingItemsOfAHouseBill`, `TestAbandoningAnAmendmentRestoresTheMovement`, `TestFactsThatStateNoLoadingAtAllAreRefusedWithTheWayOut` (it pins the words of the way out), `TestAHouseNotLoadedNamesItsContainersOnce`, `TestARowNamingAnEarlierDiscardedDraftsMovementDoesNotRestore`; on PostgreSQL (`house_on_board_test.go`): `TestAHouseBillIsDatedByItsOwnContainersOnPostgres`, `TestAbandoningAnAmendmentThatMovedTheBillOnPostgres`, `TestAHouseBillMovedToAnotherPortIsIssuedAtItsPlaceOnPostgres`, `TestTheStoreListsTheHouseBillsOfAMovementOnPostgres`. Cross-context (`internal/bootstrap/house_on_board_integration_test.go`, real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY, BOOK, SEA, FIN and DOC): `TestAHouseBillIsDatedByItsOwnContainersWithEveryContext`, `TestAHouseThatMovedToAnotherMovementTakesItsBillWithEveryContext`, `TestAHouseThatWasOnlyDetachedHasItsBillCancelledWithEveryContext`, `TestWhenTheContainersLoadTogetherTheHousesDateIsTheMovementsWithEveryContext`. No existing test was changed.
- V2.91 freezes a pool while a container is reworked and previews every apportionment before it is submitted (step 5 of "a house leaves a sealed container"). Migration fin/000015.
  - What FIN reads. FIN's PHY adapter maps each packing list's `Reworks` (the rework's number, `AskedBy`, how it ended, `SEALED` or `GIVEN_UP`, empty while it is open, and when), which it did not map before, and FIN's cost basis tells the units whose stuffing is in `REWORK` now (`CostBasis.UnitsInRework`: the load unit's or the packing list's execution state) and every rework with its end. Nothing else of PHY changes: PHY, FUL, SEA and DOC are not changed.
  - Preview and submit. `ApportionConsolCosts` is removed from FIN's api, from the module and from the actions (`ActionApportionConsolCosts`; FIN has no HTTP route, checked by search, so there is none to remove). Every apportionment is now `PreviewConsolCostApportionment`, which takes the inputs `ApportionConsolCosts` had (lines, method, manual shares, houses left out, all houses, reason) and computes the apportionment by the same code, then stores it with its number (the receipt's `Version`), who and when, and a fingerprint of what it was computed on; it writes no share, no job cost and no current apportionment. Then `SubmitConsolCostApportionment` (pool, preview number, expected revision, actor, time) reads PHY's basis and the pool again, computes the fingerprint again and refuses `FIN_PREVIEW_STALE` when it differs, naming each fact that changed (up to five, then "and N more") and the way out, preview again; otherwise it writes the previewed apportionment as `ApportionConsolCosts` wrote one, by the person who submits and at the time it is submitted, linked to its preview (`PoolApportionment.PreviewNo`; `PoolPreview.SubmittedAs`). A preview is submitted once (`FIN_PREVIEW_SUBMITTED`) and not at a time before it was made. The fingerprint is a text of one fact to a line, a key and a value, sorted, with its SHA-256: the basis revision; each load unit with its type, TEU, status and stuffing and its state; each packing list with its stuffing, state and digest, and each rework of it with its end and when; each live house with its job and its plan and loaded figures (and its loads in each unit), which are wider than the digests (a house loaded by a tender is in no packing list); each line the preview divided, and only those, with its version, its cost and the number of the apportionment in force for it (0 when none), so that another apportionment of the line submitted after the preview makes it stale (a preview that carries the houses left out of the latest apportionment no longer silently undoes a newer one), while a revision or a new line of another line does not; and, for a line by REVENUE, the revenue of each house that takes part, read under the jobs' locks. Who asked for a rework is not in it. A preview moves the pool's revision as every change of the pool does, so an apportionment moves it twice; a cost revision moves it too, so a submission asked at the revision before the revision is refused as a conflict, and one asked at the revision after it as stale (both run); a cost that changes under one version, such as an invoice matched final below the accrual, makes the previews of the line stale too (run). A preview has no event of its own: it changes nothing another context reads, and the submission keeps `ConsolCostsApportioned.v1` with the apportionment's number as its version, whose payload names no person (run). Every figure FIN computes is unchanged, and a pool with no rework previews and submits what `ApportionConsolCosts` wrote (run on the owner's box A, the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN: CNY 3,000.00 on W/M 10 : 15 : 5 gives 1,000.00, 1,500.00 and 500.00, and the pool closes as before).
  - The confirmer. The confirmation is per line. For each rework of the consolidation's containers that has ended, sealed again or given up, and for each line of the preview, the person who submits must not be the rework's `AskedBy` unless an earlier submitted apportionment divided that line from a preview whose fingerprint read the rework as ended: `FIN_CONFIRMER_ASKED_REWORK` names the person, the rework and the lines, and says that another finance user submits. Another person's apportionment of one line does not confirm the others (run, the review's case), a preview that was not submitted carries nothing, and a line recorded after the rework ended is carried by nothing, so its first apportionment is submitted by someone other than the asker too. An apportionment made before V2.91 has no preview and carries no rework: after an old ended rework the next submission is by someone other than its asker (run on the data of V2.90 by the review). The rule reads PHY's basis when the submission is made; the people are not in the fingerprint. What FIN cannot see (run, `TestAReworkGivenUpLeavesNothingForFINToReadWithEveryContext` (renamed in V2.92: `TestTheAskerOfAReworkGivenUpIsRefusedAndAnotherFinanceUserAcceptedWithEveryContext`)): PHY's basis drops a cancelled stuffing together with its packing list, so when a stuffing is given up all its reworks are gone from what FIN reads, the earlier ones that ended sealed as well as the rework that ended in the give-up, and the rule cannot apply to any of them (since V2.92 it does); the rule holds for a rework that ends `GIVEN_UP` in the domain, where a basis lists one (a stand-in), and with the real contexts it has nothing to apply to (since V2.92 it does). The preview made before is stale (its packing list is gone), the pool is not in work, and the asker's submission is accepted (since V2.92: refused). This is for PR 6: PHY keeps a given-up stuffing's reworks in the basis. When two people each asked for an ended rework of these lines that is not yet carried, neither can submit and a third person is needed; the refusal says so (another finance user, who did not ask for an ended rework of these lines that is not yet carried, submits). A preview of several lines is refused whole when one of its lines is not carried, naming that line; the asker can preview the carried lines apart, or another person submits.
  - The freeze. While PHY's basis shows any unit of the consolidation in `REWORK`, the whole pool is frozen: a preview, a submission and the close are refused `FIN_POOL_IN_WORK`, naming the containers (`load unit 1 (stuffing X-1)`) and the way out, wait for PHY to seal the container again or to give its stuffing up. FIN reads PHY before its transaction and cannot lock PHY, so a rework opened after that read is not seen: the command takes effect as of its read of PHY, as if it had been made just before the rework (run, with a stand-in PHY and the pool's lock held: the submission commits, the pool is then in work with it as its last submitted apportionment, the rework ended later is not carried by it, and a close in the same window is reopened with `ReopenConsolCostPool`). Recording, revising and releasing a pool's costs and matching invoices go on as before (run). `GetConsolCostPool` and `GetConsolProfit` say `InWork` and name the containers (`WorkUnits`), reading PHY's basis outside FIN's transactions as the commands do (their first read of PHY: they fail when PHY does not answer); they show the apportionments as they were, the last submitted one being what the houses bear, and the previews apart in `Previews`. A preview made before a rework opened is refused in work when it is submitted, and after the reseal its submission is refused as stale (the packing list's state and digest, and the rework's end, are not what it read; run); it can be left, as no command removes a preview and a later one supersedes it. The way out of every refusal is a command: PHY's reseal or give-up lifts the freeze, a new preview clears a stale one, and another finance user's submission clears the confirmer's.
  - The database. Migration `fin/000015_fin_apportionment_preview`: a table `pool_apportionment_preview` (pool, number, the figures as JSON, the fingerprint, its SHA-256, by, at, entered at; immutable, written with its pool while it is OPEN, numbered without gaps, the hash checked against the text), and on `pool_apportionment` a nullable `preview_no` with a foreign key on the same pool, a unique constraint (at most one apportionment per preview) and a deferred trigger that checks the apportionment against its preview (basis revision, stage, not made before it, and the same lines and shares); the pool's consistency function is replaced by fin/000006's with the previews' numbering. The application role gets it from the workflow's existing grant on every table; the table is never updated, so nothing more is granted. What only the domain keeps, because PHY's facts are not in the schema: the comparison of the fingerprint with PHY's basis and the pool, the confirmer rule, the freeze, and that an apportionment made after the migration comes from a preview at all (rows of before have none).
  - Not yet: the three bearers of a former share of a house that left a container and the double charge after a transfer (PR 6 (since V2.93: pending finance's attribution)); that FIN reads a rework given up (see the confirmer) (since V2.92: done).
  - Tests: pure (`preview_test.go`, domain): `TestAPreviewChangesNothingButTheListOfPreviews`, `TestASubmissionWritesThePreviewedApportionment`, `TestAPreviewIsSubmittedOnce`, `TestEachPartOfTheFingerprintChangesIt`, `TestTheCostLinesArePartOfTheFingerprint`, `TestASubmissionIsRefusedWhenWhatThePreviewReadHasChanged` (each kind of change), `TestAPoolIsFrozenWhileAContainerIsReworked` (preview, submission and close; costs go on; the reseal), `TestTheConfirmerIsPerLine`, `TestAPreviewIsStaleOnceAnotherApportionmentOfItsLineWasSubmitted`, `TestTheConfirmerOfTheApportionmentAfterAReworkIsNotWhoAskedForIt` (not carried, carried, two reworks, given up, an apportionment with no preview), `TestAPreviewAndASubmissionAreChangesOfTheOpenPool`, `TestAPoolWithPreviewsIsRestored`; the existing domain tests apportion through a helper that previews and submits. The adapter (`TestTheAdapterCarriesTheReworksOfEachPackingList`). On PostgreSQL (`preview_postgres_test.go`, through FIN's commands): `TestAPreviewIsSubmittedOnPostgres` (figures, replay of both commands, the outbox), `TestAStaleSubmissionIsRefusedOnPostgres` and `TestAStaleRevenueIsRefusedOnPostgres`, `TestAFrozenPoolOnPostgres`, `TestTheConfirmerIsPerLineOnPostgres`, `TestAnOlderPreviewDoesNotUndoANewerApportionment`, `TestAReworkOpenedInTheWindowIsNotSeenByACommandThatReadPHYBefore`, `TestASubmissionWaitsForTheJobsLock`, `TestThePreviewTableHasForcedRowSecurity`, `TestAPoolWithoutReworkIsApportionedAsBefore`, `TestAPoolApportionedByPreviewCloses`, `TestPreviewsPastFINOnPostgres` and `TestAPreviewIntoAClosedPoolPastFINOnPostgres` (a raw write for each rule of the database), and the deterministic concurrency tests `TestASubmissionRacesACostRevision` and `TestTwoSubmissionsOfOnePreviewRace` (a lock held on a second connection, the commands queued behind it in each order, counted as blocked only when `pg_blocking_pids` leads to the holder). Across contexts (`preview_freeze_integration_test.go`, the owner's box A with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN): `TestAPoolWithNoReworkIsApportionedAsBeforeWithEveryContext`, `TestAPoolFrozenForAReworkIsConfirmedByAnotherPerson` (before the rework, the rework opened, H03 unloaded, the reseal, a new preview by ops-1, its submission by the asker refused and by another finance user accepted: 1,200.00, 1,800.00 and 0) and `TestAReworkGivenUpLeavesNothingForFINToReadWithEveryContext` (renamed in V2.92: `TestTheAskerOfAReworkGivenUpIsRefusedAndAnotherFinanceUserAcceptedWithEveryContext`); and `TestAContainerIsReworkedWithEveryContextAndFINFreezesThePool`. Existing tests that called `ApportionConsolCosts` call a helper that previews and submits, and assert the revisions that follow; `TestApportionByRevenueRacesASellRevision` is now `TestPreviewByRevenueRacesASellRevision`: the apportionment is a preview and a submission, and a revision between the two is refused as stale, naming the house; otherwise the outbox order and the close after the apportionment that came first are asserted as before. `TestApportionRacesJobCancel` now races the submission of a preview with the cancellation of a house's job: a cancellation first leaves the preview stale (`FIN_PREVIEW_STALE`, naming the house), a submission first leaves the cancellation refused at commit.
- V2.92 lets FIN read which houses left a consolidation and the reworks of a given-up stuffing (step 6a of "a house leaves a sealed container"). No migration.
  - PHY, the former houses. `PhysicalCostBasis.FormerHouses` lists every house whose latest membership entry in the consolidation ended by a detach, a transfer or the shipment's cancellation, one per house, in shipment order. The latest entry of the house in the consolidation decides, whatever came before: a house attached again after a departure is live and not listed; a house that left, came back and then had its shipment cancelled is listed as cancelled, with that entry's reason (`SHIPMENT_CANCELLED`), person and times, not with the departure the re-attach undid (run). A refresh is no departure: the house stays with the next entry, so a refreshed house is live and a refresh before a detach or a cancellation does not hide it (run). A house whose shipment was cancelled is a former house, because who bears its former share needs finance's decision as a detached house's does. Each entry carries the shipment, how it ended (`DETACHED`, `TRANSFERRED` or `SHIPMENT_CANCELLED`) and for a transfer the consolidation it went to (the next entry of its ledger), the reason and its text, who ended it, when (business time) and when PHY recorded it, and the house's rework: the stuffing, the rework's number, the load unit and who asked for it, read from the stuffing's reworks, which a stuffing given up still has, and `ReworkFrom`, which says where PHY found it. `ENTRY`: the entry that ended names it, which the database records only for an entry ended in a CLOSED consolidation. `REVERSAL`: the entry names none (it ended while the consolidation was OPEN, or by the cancellation) but a reversal line of the house in one of the consolidation's stuffings carries a rework number, and the house names the latest rework that unloaded it, found as `HouseRework` finds it for the leaving checks but over the reversals PHY recorded since the house's latest entry began (`HouseReworkSince`), so that a house unloaded by a rework, detached, attached again and detached again with nothing loaded in between names no rework for its second departure (`NONE`, run), and one attached again, loaded and unloaded by a second rework names the second (run); a house unloaded by a rework, loaded again in another stuffing and then cancelled names that rework still (run). `NONE`: no rework unloaded the house (shut out before stuffing, or never loaded). The statements are in `ConsolidationStore.FormerHouses` and `ExecutionStore.HouseRework`, in the transaction of the rest of the basis.
  - PHY, the stuffings given up. `PhysicalCostBasis.GivenUpStuffings` lists every stuffing of the consolidation in state `CANCELLED` that has at least one rework, in unit order: the unit, the stuffing and its reworks as `UnitPackingList.Reworks` gives them (the earlier ones ended `SEALED`, the last `GIVEN_UP`). A cancelled stuffing still has no packing list and a stuffing given up that was never reworked is not listed (run). Nothing else of the basis changes: the `Revision`, the houses, the units and the packing lists with their digests are the same as in V2.91; digests and revisions were compared with V2.91 on the same scenarios.
  - PHY, the read. The cost basis is read in one snapshot (`TenantTransactor{Snapshot: true}`: `REPEATABLE READ`, `READ ONLY`, set before the first statement), which it was not in V2.91: it was read committed, so each statement saw what had been committed when it ran, though the comment said "one snapshot". The live houses, the houses that left, the executions, the reversal lines and the lots are one state, so a house detached while the basis is read is in the live houses or in the former houses, never in neither or in both. Only `GetPhysicalCostBasis` takes the option; the other reads of PHY (`Reads.Consolidation`, `Membership`, `Evidence` and the rest) are unchanged, and every other context reaches the basis through that one query. `TestTheCostBasisIsReadInOneSnapshot` (PHY integration, `phy_basis_snapshot_test.go`) shows it: a restrictive row-level-security policy, for the test's tenant only, makes the live-houses statement wait for an advisory lock that a second connection holds, the test waits until `pg_blocking_pids` shows the reader blocked by that connection, a detach commits, the lock is released, and the answer still has both houses live and none former (read committed gives the detached house in both lists). It creates and drops the policy and a function on `cargo_phy.membership_entry_part` as the migrator role, which owns the table, each statement with a 300 ms `lock_timeout` retried for up to ten seconds, so that it never queues another package's sessions behind it while `go test ./...` runs the packages in parallel (the test skips without `IDP_CARGO_MIGRATOR_DSN` outside CI).
  - FIN. The adapter maps both lists (`CostBasis.FormerHouses`, `CostBasis.GivenUpStuffings`). The fingerprint of a preview holds, for each former house, a fact with its end, the consolidation it went to, when it ended and its rework (the stuffing and the number), and, for each stuffing given up, each of its reworks with its end and when, under the key a rework has in a packing list (so a rework that a preview read as ended is still read as that after its stuffing is given up): a preview made before a house left or a stuffing was given up is stale and says which fact (run). The reason, who ended the entry, when PHY recorded it, where PHY found the rework (`ReworkFrom`), and the load unit and the asker of the house's rework are carried and are not in the fingerprint. The confirmer rule also applies to the reworks of the stuffings given up: an ended rework there that the person who submits asked for, and that no earlier submitted apportionment of the line carried, refuses the submission, named as the others are, with "given-up stuffing" for the stuffing. Carried as before. Nothing else in FIN reads the former houses: only the fingerprint does (by search), and no rule, figure or query uses them yet.
  - Not yet: the finance confirmation, line by line and by someone other than the person who asked for the unloading, of who bears a departed house's former share of the original consolidation's costs (the remaining cargo, the house's own re-routing loss or the consolidation's exception cost), step 6b; the double charge after a transfer (since V2.93: pending finance's attribution).
  - Tests: PHY, through the real commands on PostgreSQL (`phy_former_houses_test.go`): `TestAHouseDetachedFromTheClosedConsolidationAfterAReworkIsAFormerHouse` (in rework and sealed again), `TestAHouseTransferredAfterAReworkNamesTheConsolidationItWentTo`, `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingIsAFormerHouseWithNoRework`, `TestFormerHousesOfAnOpenConsolidationInShipmentOrder`, `TestAHouseWhoseLatestEntryEndedByRefreshOrCancellationIsListedByTheLatestEnd` (refresh only; refresh then cancel; cancel with no earlier end; detach, attach again, cancel), `TestAHouseThatLeftAndCameBackIsLiveAndAHouseThatLeftTwiceIsListedOnce`, `TestAStuffingGivenUpAfterTwoReworksKeepsBothAndItsHousesNameThem`, `TestAStuffingGivenUpWithNoReworkIsNotListed`, `TestStuffingsGivenUpAreListedInUnitOrder` (two reworked stuffings: each house names its own stuffing, unit and asker), `TestTwoStuffingsGivenUpOnOneUnitAreListedByStuffingId`, `TestAHouseUnloadedByAReworkAndDetachedAfterTheConsolidationReopenedNamesTheReworkFromItsReversals`, `TestAHouseUnloadedByAReworkLoadedAgainAndCancelledNamesTheReworkThatUnloadedIt` `TestTheReworkOfAFormerHouseIsBoundedToItsLatestEntry`, `TestAHouseAttachedAgainLoadedAndUnloadedByASecondReworkNamesTheSecond` and `TestTheCostBasisIsReadInOneSnapshot`. FIN: `TestTheAdapterCarriesTheFormerHousesAndTheGivenUpStuffings`, and in the domain (`former_houses_test.go`) `TestTheFormerHousesAndTheGivenUpStuffingsArePartOfTheFingerprint`, `TestAPreviewIsStaleOnceAHouseLeftOrAStuffingWasGivenUp`, `TestTheConfirmerOfARealGivenUpStuffingIsNeitherAskerButAThirdPerson`, `TestAReworkCarriedWhileItsStuffingHadAPackingListIsCarriedAfterItIsGivenUp` and `TestAnOpenReworkOfAStuffingGivenUpNamesNobody`. Across contexts (`former_houses_integration_test.go`, the owner's box A with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN): `TestTheReworksOfAStuffingGivenUpAreConfirmedByAThirdPersonWithEveryContext` (the V2.91 review's case: rework 1 asked by ask-1 ends sealed, rework 2 asked by ask-2 gives the stuffing up; ask-1 and ask-2 are refused and a third person accepted) and `TestAHouseTransferredAfterAReworkIsAFormerHouseOfItsConsolidationWithEveryContext` (H03 unloaded in a rework asked by ops-1, sealed again, transferred to C-2 by ops-9: C-1 lists it with the rework, ops-1, C-2 and the time, and a preview made before is stale). One existing test changed: `TestTheAskerOfAReworkGivenUpIsRefusedAndAnotherFinanceUserAcceptedWithEveryContext` (V2.91) asserted that the person who asked for a rework that was given up is not refused, which is what this step changes; it is renamed in this step and now asserts that PHY's basis tells of the stuffing and that the asker is refused and another finance user accepted.
- V2.93 lets finance attribute a departed house's former share of the original consolidation's costs (step 6b of "a house leaves a sealed container"). Migration fin/000016.
  - The former share. A house that PHY's basis lists among the former houses of a consolidation (V2.92: detached, transferred or shipment cancelled) and that holds a used share of an amount above zero on the apportionment in force of a cost line has a former share on that line. It is pending finance's confirmation until an apportionment of the line attributes it: it is nobody's cost. A share of a house left out of the line, a share of nothing and the share of a house that is live again are none. `FormerShares` of the pool (domain) lists them from PHY's basis; a transfer makes the share pending in the source pool and the house is apportioned in the target pool as any live house is.
  - The attribution. `PreviewConsolCostApportionment` takes `Attributions` (`AttributionStatement`: line, shipment, `Loss`, `Exception`, `Party`, `PartyReference`, `Voucher`, `Reason`), in the line's currency. For each former house the loss is a share its own job bears, the exception cost stays on the consolidation with the party responsible (`CARRIER`, `WAREHOUSE`, `OWN_OPERATIONS`, or `PENDING` while the cause is not established; empty when the exception cost is zero) and a free reference, and the voucher is required, also when both are zero (all of the line to the remaining cargo). What remains of the line's cost (the cost less every loss and exception cost) is divided over the live houses by the line's method as before (MANUAL: the manual shares sum to it). The line's local cost is divided over the remainder and each loss and exception cost by the same largest remainder `split` the shares use, in proportion to the amounts in the line's currency, so both currencies conserve. A loss or an exception cost below 0, losses and exception costs above the line's cost, a party that does not match the exception cost, a house PHY does not list as having left, one with no former share and no attribution in force on the line, a house stated twice and a line the preview does not divide are refused (`FIN_INVALID`, naming the line and the house). Each attribution records the house's departure (`FormerHouse.EndedAt`) as its business date and the open job of the house and the pool's legal entity, which bears the loss (`FIN_POOL_JOB_MISSING` when a loss has none). The attributions are inputs and are not in the fingerprint: what makes a preview stale is, as before, the former houses and the apportionment in force of each line. The worked example is the owner's box A: freight CNY 3,000.00 apportioned 1,000.00, 1,500.00 and 500.00; H03 leaves; a loss of 100.00 and an exception cost of 200.00 (party pending) divide the remaining 2,700.00 over H01 and H02 as 1,080.00 and 1,620.00; a loss of 0 and an exception cost of 0 give 1,200.00 and 1,800.00.
  - Missing attribution. A preview of a line that holds a former share of a house with no attribution stated and none in force is refused `FIN_ATTRIBUTION_MISSING`, naming the line, the house, the former share (the loss it would bear by default, for reference) and the way out: state who bears it, with a voucher. A RETAINED line is previewed under the same rule and takes attributions too (the remainder is what the consolidation retains).
  - Carried forward and corrected. An attribution in force on a line is carried when the line is apportioned again and none is stated for the house: the same loss, exception cost, party, reference, voucher and reason, marked `Carried`. The remainder absorbs a change of the line's cost; when the carried amounts no longer fit the cost the preview is refused naming the line and the way out, state them again. A statement that differs from the one in force is a correction and needs a reason (`FIN_INVALID`); one equal to it (the same amounts, party, reference and voucher) is taken as the one in force and carried. The earlier apportionments are kept as they were. A carried attribution keeps its amounts: a later change of the line's cost goes to the remaining cargo, and finance corrects the attribution with a reason and a voucher if it should not (the confirmer is then checked as for any change). An attribution in force of a house PHY lists as live again is not carried (a decision, tested in the domain with a stand-in basis and not run with the real contexts): the house takes its share of the line with the others.
  - The confirmer. A submission whose preview carries a new or changed attribution of a house H is refused `FIN_ATTRIBUTION_CONFIRMER`, naming the house, the person and why, when the submitter is the person who asked for the rework that unloaded H (`FormerHouse.ReworkAskedBy`, however PHY found the rework) or who ended H's place in the consolidation (`EndedBy`: who detached or transferred it); the way out is that another finance user submits. Both are read from PHY's basis when the submission is made. The confirmer is the person who submits, as in V2.91; who made the preview is recorded on the preview (`PoolPreview.By`) and not on the attribution, so the asker of the rework may state the figures and another finance user confirms them by submitting. An attribution carried unchanged needs no check, and V2.91's per-line rule for the apportionment after a rework stays as it was (the new rule is applied first, so that the refusal of a person who is both names the attribution).
  - To be allocated. When no house of the consolidation is live but some left, the remainder of a line is not refused: it is recorded on the line as an amount to be allocated (in both currencies) and the line has no share. With no house live and none that left the preview is refused as before (`FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS`). A line that costs nothing, with no house live and nothing attributed on it, is refused at the preview (`FIN_INVALID`, naming the line): it needs no apportionment and does not hold back the close, so preview the other lines. A house shut out before stuffing is attributed in the same way (the owner's design, question 3). Known limit: a line apportioned before V2.93 that already left a departed house out has no former share and no attribution in force, so there is nothing to attribute and a statement for it is refused; no pending item lists it (6c).
  - The close. `CloseConsolCostPool` is refused (`FIN_POOL_NOT_CLOSABLE`) while a line holds a former share not attributed, naming the line and the house and the way out (attribute it), or an amount to be allocated, naming the way out (add cargo and apportion the line again, attribute it as a loss or an exception cost of a house that left, or cancel the consolidation and retain it). Both are checked before the case "every house is gone or the consolidation is cancelled", which until V2.93 let a former share stand. An exception cost whose party is `PENDING` does not stop the close (the owner, 2026-11-22).
  - Jobs. A loss is borne by the house's FIN job of the pool's legal entity, which must be OPEN (the preview and the submission lock it, in job id order, with the live houses' jobs; only the jobs of former houses that hold a former share or an attribution in force on the pool are added, so a pool that no house left locks what it did). A job cannot be cancelled while it bears a loss in force, one under the latest apportionment that covers the line: the cancellation is refused at commit (`pool_loss_job_open`) and the message names the way out, finance changes the attribution first (a correction with the loss changed). A former share pending already blocked the cancellation (fin/000012); its message now names the same way out, finance attributes it. Since the review FIN also refuses the cancellation itself, before it writes, with `FIN_JOB_BEARS_POOL_COST` naming the job and every share and loss it bears, each with its pool, its line and its way out (with the pool closed, reopen it first with `ReopenConsolCostPool`, then apportion the line again moving the loss); the database's rules stay as the backstop and say the same. A submission with a loss for a job that is not open any more is refused `FIN_POOL_JOB_MISSING` (the database refuses it too, `pool_attribution_job_open`).
  - The views. `GetJobProfit` does not count a former share pending in `PoolCost` or `Profit`: it lists it apart (`FormerShares`, `FormerSharesPending`: pool, line, amount), and counts a loss in force (`ReroutingLosses` with the pool, the line, the voucher and the house's departure; `ReroutingLoss` is their sum, part of `PoolCost`). To tell a former share it reads PHY's basis of each pool the job has a share in, outside FIN's transactions, as the pool queries do since V2.91; when PHY does not answer the query fails (what the job bears cannot be told), and a job with no share asks PHY nothing (with no PHY wired a job that holds a pool share is answered `ErrMissingDependency`: a defensive check, tested on the application's function with a nil port; the module itself refuses to start without PHY). `GetConsolCostPool` gives, per line apportioned (`Attributions`), the losses (house, job), the exception costs by party with `PENDING` apart, the amount to be allocated and the former shares pending; `GetConsolProfit` gives the same per line (`Lines`) and in total (`Losses`, `Exceptions`, `ExceptionsPending`, `ToAllocated`, `FormerSharesPending`), keeps the former shares out of the houses' costs and puts a loss into its house's, and conserves: `Pooled` = `Apportioned` + `Retained` + `Losses` + `Exceptions` + `ToAllocated` + `FormerSharesPending` + `Unapportioned`. `ApportionedLine` (apportionments and previews) carries `Attributions`, `ToAllocate` and `ToAllocateLocal`.
  - The database. Migration `fin/000016_fin_former_share_attribution`: a table `pool_apportionment_attribution` (a row for each house of an apportionment line: tenant, pool, apportionment, line, shipment, job, loss and its local amount, exception cost and its local amount, party, reference, voucher, reason, the departure, whether carried; immutable; row security enabled and forced; statement trigger for READ COMMITTED); the amount to be allocated as two columns of `pool_apportionment_line` (`to_allocate_minor`, `to_allocate_local_minor`, default 0, checked; columns because a line has one amount or none and it is part of the line's own figures, read by the close and the preview check beside its cost). Replaced, each with every rule of its latest earlier definition: `pool_line_shares_check` (fin/000012; shares + losses + exception costs + to be allocated are the line's cost in both currencies, a line has a used share or no share at all and then an amount to be allocated or an attribution, never a share beside an amount to be allocated, a RETAINED line has no share and nothing to be allocated and its attributions do not exceed its cost), `check_job_pool_shares_at_commit` (fin/000012; and a cancelled job bears no loss in force), `consol_cost_pool_closure_check` (fin/000007; and the latest apportionment of each line leaves nothing to be allocated) and `pool_apportionment_preview_check` (fin/000015; and the attributions and the amount to be allocated are the preview's). A new commit trigger refuses an attribution not written with its pool, into a closed pool, that breaks its line's conservation, or whose job is not the shipment's job of the pool's legal entity, OPEN when it has a loss (`pool_attribution_job_open`). No row changes and nothing is dropped; the ALTER TABLE takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on `pool_apportionment_line` and checks its rows against the new constraints, and does not rewrite it (reasoned, not measured). The workflow's grant of SELECT and INSERT on every table of `cargo_fin` covers the table and the columns (checked: nothing is updated). What only the domain keeps, because PHY's facts are not in the schema: that a shipment is a house PHY lists as having left and held a former share or an attribution in force on the line, the default loss shown, that an attribution a preview did not state is the one in force carried, the confirmer, that a former share was attributed when the pool closes, and that a to-be-allocated amount exists only while no house is live.
  - What does not change. A pool no house left is previewed, submitted and closed as in V2.92: no attribution, nothing to be allocated, no former share, every figure as before (run on box A with the real contexts, `TestAPoolWithNoDepartureHasNoAttributionWithEveryContext`, besides V2.91's `TestAPoolWithNoReworkIsApportionedAsBeforeWithEveryContext`, which is not changed). PHY, FUL, SEA and DOC are not changed.
  - Not yet: FIN's pending list (former shares pending, amounts to be allocated, exception costs whose party is pending, a closed pool with a house that left) and the presentation of each house's original and new amount and margin change before the confirmation (6c); cost lines recorded for a rework itself with their default attribution (question 9); the prompt for a closed pool (question 10); a correction that names the party of a pending exception cost reopens a closed pool through `ReopenConsolCostPool`, as any change of a closed pool does.
  - Tests: pure (`attribution_test.go`, domain): `TestAFormerShareIsAUsedShareOfAHouseThatLeft`, `TestAPreviewOfALineHoldingAFormerShareNeedsItsAttribution`, `TestAnAttributionTakesItsAmountsOutBeforeTheRemainderIsDivided` (the owner's two outcomes), `TestTheLocalAmountsOfAnAttributionConserveByLargestRemainder` (USD 3,000.01: 1,881,995 + 87,938 + 167,094 minor units of CNY), `TestAttributionAmountsAreChecked`, `TestAnAttributionInForceIsCarriedForward`, `TestACorrectionNeedsItsReasonAndAnEqualStatementIsCarried`, `TestTheConfirmerOfAnAttributionIsNeitherTheAskerNorWhoEndedTheHouse`, `TestWhoDetachedAHouseDoesNotConfirmItsAttribution`, `TestALossIsBorneByTheHousesOpenJob`, `TestASubmissionWithALossNeedsTheHousesOpenJob`, `TestWithNoLiveHouseTheRemainderIsToBeAllocated` (also RETAINED with attributions and the close), `TestTheCloseNeedsEveryFormerShareAttributed`, `TestAnAttributionLapsesWhenTheHouseIsAttachedAgain`, `TestManualSharesSumToWhatRemainsAfterTheAttributions`, `TestAnApportionmentWithAttributionsIsRestored`, `TestAPoolWithNoFormerHouseIsAsBefore`, `TestAnAttributionNeedsAShareOrAnAttributionInForce`, `TestAZeroLineWithNoLiveHouseIsRefusedAtThePreview`, `TestAStatementIsTheOneInForceOnlyWhenEveryFigureIsEqual`; the job's cost (`jobprofit_test.go`, application): `TestAFormerSharePendingIsNotInTheJobsCostAndALossIs`. On PostgreSQL through FIN's commands (`former_share_postgres_test.go`): `TestAFormerShareIsAttributedAndReplayedOnPostgres` (both commands replayed, the confirmers, the views), `TestAnAttributionIsCarriedAndCorrectedOnPostgres`, `TestAJobBearingALossIsNotCancelledOnPostgres`, `TestARetainedLineTakesItsAttributionsOnPostgres`, `TestAJobBearingPoolCostIsRefusedBeforeItIsWrittenOnPostgres`, `TestFormerBasesWithNoPHYAreRefusedOnlyForAJobWithAShare`, `TestTheRefusalOfACancelNamesEveryHolding` (application), `TestAttributionsPastFINOnPostgres`, `TestTheAttributionTableHasForcedRowSecurity`, `TestAnApportionmentIsWhatItsPreviewComputedOnPostgres`, `TestAClosedPoolWithAnAmountToBeAllocatedPastFINOnPostgres` (a raw write for each rule of the database), and the deterministic concurrency tests `TestASubmissionWithALossRacesTheCancelOfThatJob` (a lock held on a second connection on the job, the two commands queued behind it in each order, counted only when `pg_blocking_pids` leads to the holder) and `TestACancelledJobAndALossTakeTurnsAtCommitOnPostgres` (the same at the database's commit, in each direction). Across contexts (`former_share_integration_test.go`, the owner's box A with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN): `TestAFormerShareIsPendingUntilFinanceAttributesItWithEveryContext` (H03 unloaded in a rework asked by ops-1, resealed, transferred to C-2 by ops-9; C-2's pool apportions H03 normally; before confirmation H03's job excludes C-1's 500.00 and lists it, C-1's close is refused naming H03; a preview without attribution is refused naming 500.00; with the loss 100.00 and the exception cost 200.00 pending under CLAIM-1 ops-1 and ops-9 are refused and fin-1 accepted: 1,080.00 and 1,620.00, the loss in H03's profit, the exception cost pending in C-1, and C-1 closes), `TestAFormerShareAllToTheRemainingCargoWithEveryContext` (1,200.00 and 1,800.00), `TestWhenEveryHouseLeavesTheRemainderIsToBeAllocatedWithEveryContext` (the remainder to be allocated, the close refused with the way out, a correction that attributes the rest) and `TestAPoolWithNoDepartureHasNoAttributionWithEveryContext`. Existing tests changed, because they pinned what a departed house's share did until V2.93 or the database's raw error on cancelling a job that bears a share (`TestApportionRacesJobCancel`, `TestConsolCostPoolOnPostgres` and `TestTheJobOfAHouseLeftOutIsCancelledOnPostgres` now see `FIN_JOB_BEARS_POOL_COST`; the stand-in ORG of FIN's integration tests also verifies a second legal entity): `TestAHouseUnloadedInAReworkIsTransferredWithEveryContext`, `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingLeavesTheConsolidationWithEveryContext` and `TestAHouseShutOutBeforeStuffingIsTransferredWithEveryContext` (the job's cost excludes the pending share and lists it, the close and the new apportionment of the line are refused until finance attributes it); the comment of `TestAHouseUnloadedInAReworkLeavesTheConsolidationWithEveryContext` and the list of migrations of `cmd/cargo-migrate`.
- V2.94 lists a cost pool's pending items and shows what a preview would change before it is submitted (step 6c of "a house leaves a sealed container"). No migration.
  - The pending items. `GetConsolCostPoolPendingItems` (pool) is a read-only query that reads the pool in one snapshot and PHY's basis outside it, as the pool's other queries do since V2.91, and lists in a fixed order (by kind, then line, then house) each item with its kind, the line, the house or the container where it applies, the amount where there is one (in the line's currency and in the functional currency), what to do next and who acts, `FINANCE` or `OPERATIONS`, in the words of SEA's and DOC's lists. `IN_WORK`: a container of the consolidation is in rework (V2.91), so nothing is apportioned until PHY seals it again or gives its stuffing up; operations. `FORMER_SHARE_PENDING`: a former share nobody attributed (V2.93) with its amount, which is the loss the house bears by default; finance attributes it by previewing the line again with the house's loss and exception cost, a voucher and a reason, and the message names who cannot submit it, the person who asked for the rework that unloaded the house and the person who ended its place in the consolidation. `TO_BE_ALLOCATED`: an amount to be allocated on a line; finance adds cargo and apportions again, attributes it as a loss or an exception cost, or cancels the consolidation and retains it. `EXCEPTION_CAUSE_PENDING`: an exception cost in force whose party is `PENDING`; finance names the party by a correction with a reason and a voucher once it is established, and the message says that it does not hold back the close. `REOPEN_TO_ATTRIBUTE`: the pool is `CLOSED` and a line holds a former share nobody attributed; finance reopens the pool with a reason (`ReopenConsolCostPool`) and then attributes it, and nothing reopens it by itself (design question 10). `LINE_NOT_APPORTIONED` and `LINE_NOT_ON_CURRENT_BASIS`: what the close would refuse for a line today (not apportioned or apportioned at another cost; not on the loaded basis); finance previews and submits again. `COST_NOT_FINAL`: the line's accrual is not matched final or released; finance matches the creditor invoice as final or releases the accrual. `INVOICE_HELD`: an invoice matched to the line is disputed or awaits a variance approval; finance settles the dispute or approves the variance. A pool with nothing pending lists nothing (an empty list, not an error), and the list, apart from `EXCEPTION_CAUSE_PENDING` which does not hold the close back, is empty exactly when the pool can be closed (tested for every state of the unit tests, and through FIN's commands with a disputed invoice). Every amount in a message is written with its currency and thousands separators (`CNY 1,500.00`); while a container is in rework the way out of an item that asks for a preview says it is done once the container is sealed again or given up.
  - How the list shares the close's checks. The close's accrual test is `accrualRefusal` (domain, `pool.go`: the line is not matched final or released, or an invoice matched to it is held) and its per-line test is `lineRefusal`; neither is inline in `Close` any more. `lineRefusal` (domain, `pool.go`), which answers the first of "not apportioned or apportioned at another cost", "an amount to be allocated", "a former share nobody attributed" and "not on the current loaded basis" with the close's own error, text unchanged, and the kind of what it found. `Close` returns those errors as it did; `PendingItems` calls the same functions for each line and writes an item from the kind, taking the words after "FIN_POOL_NOT_CLOSABLE:" from the same error, so a rule changed in the close changes the list. A line whose accrual is open is listed for it and, when it also fails the line test, for that too.
