The words of `LINE_NOT_APPORTIONED` for a cost that moved are written by the list in currency units, and the close's own error is as it was. Decisions: a former share of an open pool is `FORMER_SHARE_PENDING` and that of a closed pool `REOPEN_TO_ATTRIBUTE` and not both, because the second says the same with the reopening first; while a container is in rework and in a closed pool the two line kinds are not listed (nothing is previewed before the container is sealed or the pool reopened), the first reason being tested with the real contexts (a house unloaded and sealed again but still attached with nothing loaded is `LINE_NOT_ON_CURRENT_BASIS`) and the second reasoned; a line apportioned at a cost that has since changed is `LINE_NOT_APPORTIONED` with the close's words ("was apportioned at ...; its final cost is ..."); a line by REVENUE is compared against the houses' revenue read without locks, as the close reads it under them. When PHY does not answer the query fails and lists nothing (run: the stand-in's answer is gone): an empty list would say nothing is pending when it cannot be told.
  - The preview's effect. `GetConsolCostPreviewEffect` (pool, preview number) tells what submitting a stored preview would change against the apportionment in force of each of its lines, for the owner's rule of 2026-11-18, item 6. Per line: the cost now and in the preview and the parts (shares, losses, exception costs, amount to be allocated and, for a retained line, what is retained) with a flag that they sum to the preview's cost in both currencies, a figure shown and not a refusal; and whether the line's cost is no longer the one the preview divided. Per house and line (live or former): what it bears now (`SHARE`, `FORMER_SHARE_PENDING`, `LOSS` or `NONE`), what the preview gives it (`SHARE`, `LOSS` or `NONE`), the difference, and the change to the house's job profit if the preview is submitted in the functional currency: a share or a loss lowers the profit by its amount and a former share pending is not in the profit (V2.93), so a loss of 100.00 for a house with 500.00 pending lowers it by 100.00 and attributing it nothing leaves it as it is; the total per house over the lines. The exception costs by line and party before and after, the amount to be allocated of each line before (`ToAllocateNow`, in force) and after (`ToAllocate`, in the preview), and the reasons: for each former house, PHY's end, its reason, who ended it, when and where to, and the preview's attribution (party, reference, voucher, reason, whether it only carries one in force); the method of each line (and the reason of a manual one). Whether the preview is still current is the submission's own test, the fingerprint computed again from PHY's basis, the pool and the houses' open jobs (read without the locks a submission takes), answered as a flag with what changed (up to five facts, then "and N more") and not as a refusal; a preview already submitted is not current, has no changes and says which apportionment took it; a flag says that PHY reads a container in rework, which a submission refuses, and another (`PoolClosed`, with `WayOut`: reopen the pool with a reason, then submit the preview if it is still current) that the pool is closed, which a submission refuses too: the preview is not current then. The other refusals of a submission (the confirmer, a loss's job not open) are not told. It changes nothing and needs no particular person. An unknown pool or preview is not found; when PHY does not answer it fails.
  - What does not change. No command: every figure, receipt, event and refusal is as in V2.93 (`Close` and the preview's refusals were not changed in behaviour: `inWork` and `Close` share two small functions with the new code, `unitName` and `housesInOrder`, with the same output). The pool's other queries, PHY, FUL, SEA and DOC are not changed. No existing test was changed.
  - Not yet (6d, next): the cost lines recorded for a rework itself with their default attribution (design question 9), and the small PHY fix of the error a cancelled wait answers.
  - Tests: pure (`pending_test.go`, domain): `TestPendingItemsListAContainerInRework`, `TestPendingItemsKeepTheLinesAsideWhileAContainerIsInRework`, `TestPendingItemsListAFormerShare` (500.00 naming ops-1 and ops-9; no rework named when there was none), `TestPendingItemsListAnAmountToBeAllocated` (2,700.00), `TestPendingItemsListAnExceptionCostWhoseCauseIsPending` (also closed, and an established party), `TestPendingItemsSayThatAClosedPoolIsReopenedToAttribute`, `TestPendingItemsListTheLinesTheCloseWouldRefuse` (not apportioned, apportioned at another cost, a house unloaded, a house measured again, a line that costs nothing), `TestPendingItemsDoNotListLinesOfAClosedPool` and `TestPendingItemsAreInAFixedOrder` (open, in rework, lines, closed); (`effect_test.go`): `TestTheEffectOfALossAndAnExceptionCost` (the owner's figures on a line of CNY 3,000.00: H01 1,000.00 to 1,080.00 and profit -80.00, H02 1,500.00 to 1,620.00 and -120.00, H03 500.00 pending to a loss of 100.00 and -100.00, exception cost 200.00 pending, the line 3,000.00 on both sides), `TestTheEffectOfAttributingAFormerShareNothingOrItsWholeAmount` (1,200.00 and 1,800.00, profit -200.00 and -300.00, H03 unchanged; a loss of all 500.00), `TestTheEffectOfACorrection`, `TestTheEffectOfAnAmountToBeAllocated` (2,700.00; a retained line), `TestTheEffectShowsWhetherThePartsSumToTheLine`, `TestTheEffectSaysWhetherThePreviewIsStaleOrSubmitted` and `TestTheEffectGivesTheReasons` and `TestTheEffectTotalsEachHousesProfitChangeOverTheLines` and `TestTheEffectOfAForeignCurrencyLineIsToldInBothCurrencies` (USD 3,000.01 at 7.1234: a loss of USD 123.45 is CNY 879.38 in the profit). On PostgreSQL through FIN's commands (`pending_effect_postgres_test.go`): `TestAPoolsPendingItemsAndAPreviewsEffectOnPostgres` (nothing pending, in rework, a former share of CNY 230.77, the effect of a loss of 50.00 and an exception cost of 200.00 pending with each figure, stale and current again, unknown pool and preview, submitted, only the exception cost left, the close, PHY silent) and `TestAClosedPoolAndTheLinesTheCloseRefusesAreListedOnPostgres` (closed before the house left, reopened, every house gone and CNY 750.00 to be allocated, a line not apportioned, a line not on the basis, in rework) and `TestALineByRevenueIsListedAndAPreviewByRevenueIsStaleOnPostgres` (a line by REVENUE listed once a sell side is revised, a preview by revenue stale with the revenue named) and `TestAPreviewIsStaleOnceAHousesJobIsCancelledOnPostgres` and `TestTheEffectTakesThePoolsLegalEntitysJobOnPostgres`. Across contexts (`internal/bootstrap/pending_effect_integration_test.go`, the owner's box A with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN): `TestAPoolsPendingItemsAndAPreviewsEffectWithEveryContext` (during the rework `IN_WORK`; sealed again with H03 attached and nothing loaded, the line is not on the loaded basis; after the transfer `FORMER_SHARE_PENDING` of 500.00 naming ops-1 and ops-9; the effect of a loss of 100.00 and an exception cost of 200.00 pending; after the submission `EXCEPTION_CAUSE_PENDING` only and the pool closes) and `TestAPoolClosedBeforeTheHouseLeftIsToBeReopenedWithEveryContext` (`REOPEN_TO_ATTRIBUTE`). V2.94 batch 3 (after the independent review): `TestPendingItemsListACostNotFinalAndAnInvoiceHeld`, `TestThePendingListIsEmptyExactlyWhenThePoolCloses`, `TestPendingItemsOfAForeignCurrencyLineCarryTheirLocalAmounts`, `TestPendingItemsSayThatTheWayOutWaitsForTheContainer`, `TestPendingItemsReadTheRetainedAndTheZeroLinesAsTheCloseDoes`, `TestAnAmountToBeAllocatedInTheFunctionalCurrencyOnlyIsListedAndRefusedByTheClose` (and in the line's currency only), `TestPendingItemsListTheAccrualKindsAfterTheLineKinds`, `TestPendingItemsListACostThatMovedInOnlyOneCurrency`, `TestTheEffectOfAPreviewOfAClosedPoolSaysSoAndNamesTheWayOut`, `TestTheEffectGivesTheAmountToBeAllocatedInForceAndInThePreview`, `TestACostChangedInOnlyOneCurrencyIsFlaggedInTheEffect` and `TestTheEffectHasNoRowForAHouseNeitherApportionmentUsesAndKeepsAJobForOneLeftOut` (domain), and on PostgreSQL `TestThePendingListIsEmptyExactlyWhenTheCloseIsAcceptedOnPostgres` (accrual not matched, invoice disputed and resolved, effect of a preview of a closed pool) and `TestAForeignCurrencyFormerShareIsListedInBothCurrenciesOnPostgres`. A mutation script (`mutate.py`, kept outside the tree) removes each rule in turn and lists the test that fails.
- V2.95 answers in one query what became of a reworked container, and runs box A from start to finish through every context (step 7 of "a house leaves a sealed container"). No migration. The one change outside `internal/bootstrap` is a read-only field of PHY's cost basis.
  - PHY's field. `HouseCostBasis.UnloadedBy` (`HouseUnloadedBy`: the stuffing, the rework number, the unit and who asked) is, for each live house, the rework that last unloaded it from a container of the consolidation since its latest entry began; the zero value when none did. PHY reads it in the cost basis's snapshot with the reversal lines V2.92 uses for a former house whose entry names no rework (`HouseReworkSince`, called for each live house with the time its entry was recorded). It says nothing of where the house is loaded now (a house unloaded and loaded again in another container has it and has units). It is part of no digest and does not change the basis's revision or any other figure of the basis: the packing lists' digests are still the digests of their text, the basis's revision is still the consolidation's (both asserted in the new PHY test), and every existing PHY and FIN test passes unchanged. FIN reads the basis field by field and ignores the new one: FIN is not changed.
  - The query. `ReworkedContainerReport` (`internal/bootstrap/reworked_container_report.go`; the tenant, the consolidation and the load unit) is read-only and composes the contexts' query sides where the contexts are composed, so no context depends on another for it (`tools/dependency-check` is clean). It answers five things. The reworks of the unit's stuffing from PHY's cost basis: the stuffings given up first and the one in force last, and for each rework its number, who asked, when and why, the seal broken, how and when it ended and the seal put on. Where each house of the container is now: PHY's membership (the consolidation now, or in none), how it left (detached, transferred to which consolidation, shipment cancelled, with who and when) or the rework that unloaded it while it stays attached, what PHY has loaded of it in the consolidation it is in now (the unit, the stuffing and its state, or nothing, or another container), SEA's movement (state, whether the house is still associated) and the container visit of that unit (status; on board or not, with the date of the latest loaded-on-board event), and DOC's transport documents of the house (kind, number, state, the movement, and the on-board date of an issued bill). Who bears each cost, from FIN: the consolidation's pool, line by line, which is not the container's (cost lines are not per container, the owner's design, question 5), so the lines, the totals and the attributions of every house of the pool are given, each loss, exception cost and former share pending tagged with its house and the unit it left from or was unloaded from, and the totals labelled as the pool's: the cost in both currencies, the shares of the houses still in the consolidation, each former house's loss with its voucher and departure, the exception costs by party with their reference (`PENDING` is its own party value), the amount to be allocated, what is retained, the former shares not attributed yet, what no apportionment covers, and FIN's totals with whether they conserve (they do only when FIN's totals conserve, every line's parts add up to its cost in both currencies and the lines add up to each of FIN's totals). What there is to recover, derived from FIN's attributions and decided by nothing in the report, and only what the houses this report lists give rise to: an exception cost of the carrier or the warehouse is to be recovered from that party (its reference), a pending one needs the responsibility to be established, one of the own operations is not recoverable (listed so that the total is complete), a loss borne by a house may be charged to that house's customer if the commercial terms allow (the system does not decide: the owner's rule of 2026-11-18) and a former share pending is not decided yet; the answer says that claims are not built. Last, FIN's pending items of the pool, as FIN lists them: the pool's, all containers' of the consolidation, and labelled so (`PendingScope`).
  - When a context does not answer. PHY's cost basis of the consolidation is the anchor: a consolidation PHY does not know, and a load unit that PHY's basis does not list and that has no stuffing (in force or given up), fail the report (`ErrReworkedContainerUnknown`, naming which; a unit planned and never stuffed is a unit with an empty report) and a PHY that is not wired or fails fails it too (`ErrReworkPHYMissing`), since nobody can then tell which houses the container held. SEA, DOC and FIN that are not wired or fail make only their own part say so (`Unanswered`, with the context's words) and the rest is given: SEA is found through the movements DOC's documents of a house name (SEA has no query by shipment or consolidation), so with DOC unanswered SEA says that and a house with no document says that no movement was found (`NoDocument`). A PHY membership or another consolidation's basis that fails makes the house's consolidation, or what it has loaded, unanswered. A consolidation with no cost pool says so. The reads are not one snapshot: each is made on its own, outside any transaction, as FIN's pool queries read PHY's basis.
  - Decisions. The houses listed are those loaded in the unit now, the live houses whose latest unloading (`UnloadedBy`) was a rework of this unit's stuffing and the former houses PHY lists whose rework is a rework of this unit's stuffing (the unit and the execution both match, a stuffing given up included, whatever way PHY found the rework), each once, so in a consolidation with two containers each report lists its own houses. FUL is not read: the consolidation comes from PHY's membership and the unit from PHY's basis. A house loaded in two units has no visit named. The recoveries are ordered by kind (from a party, to be established, not recoverable, may be charged to the customer, not decided), and within a kind by line.
  - The acceptance. One test with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY, BOOK, SEA, DOC and FIN runs box A in eight steps, every step a command of a context: C-1 with container 1 sealed S1, H01, H02 and H03 of 10, 15 and 5 W/M and the freight CNY 3,000.00 previewed and submitted as 1,000.00, 1,500.00 and 500.00; SEA's movement finalized with the VGM and DOC's drafts of the three bills; ops-1's rework, during which FIN's preview, submission and close are refused naming the container (`IN_WORK`) and SEA's pending items say that FUL is behind PHY for each of the three houses (absent before the rework: the signal the rework causes; a finalization of the FINAL movement is refused too, for its state, which is not caused by the rework, and the refusal "FUL has not recorded PHY's latest facts" comes once ending H03's association reopens the movement); H03 unloaded, the container sealed again with S2 and a new VGM, and the report read then, with H03 listed as unloaded by rework 1 and attached to C-1 with nothing loaded; ops-9's transfer of H03 to C-2 and FUL's recording of it, with C-2's own pool; SEA refreshed with S2 and finalized with two houses, C-2 finalized, the containers loaded 25 hours apart (hours 22 and 47, so that the two bills' on-board dates differ by day in UTC+8, whatever the time of day the test runs; the rework's business times are early for that), H01 and H02 issued dated by container 1's loading, H03's draft moved to C-2 and issued dated by container 2's; FIN on C-1 (H03's 500.00 pending and out of its profit, `FORMER_SHARE_PENDING` naming ops-1 and ops-9, the effect of a loss of 100.00 and an exception cost of 200.00 of the carrier under CLAIM-1, refused for ops-1 and ops-9 and accepted for fin-1 as 1,080.00, 1,620.00 and H03's loss, the creditor invoice matched final, the pool closed); and the report: 1,080.00 + 1,620.00 + a loss of 100.00 + an exception cost of 200.00 (the carrier) = 3,000.00, CNY 200.00 to recover from the carrier, CNY 100.00 that may be charged to H03's customer, and FIN's pending list empty.
  - Not yet, and known limits: a house is not listed when it left with no rework (shut out before stuffing, or never loaded), when it left from another unit's stuffing, or when no rework unloaded it and it is not loaded in the unit now; SEA has no query by shipment or consolidation, so a house with no bill shows no movement or visit (`NoDocument`); claims (the report lists what there is to recover and opens nothing); the rework's own cost lines with their default attribution (design question 9); the configuration of the rules (`DESIGN_规则配置.md`, not in the repository).
  - Tests: PHY on PostgreSQL through its real commands (`phy_unloaded_by_test.go`): `TestAHouseUnloadedInAReworkAndStillAttachedSaysWhichReworkUnloadedIt` (in rework and sealed again; never unloaded houses say nothing; the basis's revision and the digests), `TestAHouseUnloadedAndLoadedAgainInAnotherContainerHasBoth`, `TestAHouseUnloadedInTwoReworksSaysTheLatest` and `TestAHouseAttachedAgainAfterItWasUnloadedAndLeftSaysNothing` (the reversals of an earlier stay do not count). Pure, with stand-ins for the contexts (`reworked_container_report_test.go`): `TestTheReportListsTheReworksOfTheStuffing`, `TestTheReportSaysWhereEachHouseIsNow` (still in the container, unloaded and attached, loaded again in the unit or in another, detached with no new consolidation, transferred, shipment cancelled, a visit not yet loaded, a visit of no unit, a house in two units, a house with nothing loaded, an entry of no packs), `TestTheReportSaysWhoBearsEachCost` (a shared line with a loss and an exception cost, a former share pending, an exception cost pending, a retained line, a line no apportionment covers, a line revised after its apportionment, a USD line conserving in both currencies with a loss and an exception cost, totals that do not conserve, lines that do not add up to the totals, a closed pool, no pool), `TestTheReportSaysWhatThereIsToRecover` (each party, a loss with no exception cost, a former share pending of a house the report lists and of one it does not, an unknown party, nothing to recover), `TestTheReportRecoversOnlyWhatItsOwnHousesGiveRiseTo`, `TestLinesAgreeWithTheirPartsAndWithEachOfFINsTotals` (a line whose parts do not add up to its cost, and lines that do not add up to each of FIN's totals, one at a time), `TestARecoveryOfAForeignCurrencyLineCarriesItsLocalAmount`, `TestTheReportListsOnlyTheHousesOfItsOwnContainer`, `TestTheReportListsFINsPendingItems` and `TestTheReportSaysWhatItsContextsDoNotAnswer` (SEA, DOC and FIN not wired or failing at each read, PHY's membership and another basis failing, PHY failing or not wired). With the real contexts (`reworked_container_integration_test.go`): `TestBoxAIsRunFromStartToFinishWithEveryContextAndTheReportAnswers`, `TestAHouseOnlyDetachedIsInNoConsolidationAndItsShareIsPendingWithPHYAndFIN` (also an exception cost pending), `TestAHouseWhoseShipmentWasCancelledIsListedWithPHYAndFIN`, `TestAContainerGivenUpIsReportedWithItsReworksWithPHYAndFIN` (every house unloaded and the stuffing given up before anyone is detached: the three houses are listed), `TestTheReportOfAnUnknownContainerFailsWithPHYAndFIN`, `TestEachContainerReportListsOnlyItsOwnHousesWithPHYAndFIN` (two containers each reworked, a house leaving from each and a house shut out, a freight line, an attribution of a loss and an exception cost of the carrier for the house of container 2, which container 1 does not recover and container 2 does) and `TestALineRevisedAfterItsApportionmentHasItsRestUnapportionedWithPHYAndFIN`. A mutation script (`mutate.py`, kept outside the tree) removes each rule of the report in turn and runs the bootstrap package, and for PHY's field the PHY integration package: the report lists, for each, the test that fails.
- V2.96 adds the settings mechanism and FIN's settings, stored and changed with approval, not yet read by FIN's rules (step C1a of the configuration of the rules). Migration fin/000017.
  - The mechanism. A shared package, `internal/foundation/settings` (pure Go, no SQL, importing nothing of Cargo), holds what every context's configurable rules have in common (the design's sections 2, 3 and 6). A `Catalogue` is data in code: each item has a key (`fin.F1`), titles in Chinese and English, its values from strict to loose (each an identifier with titles and, for a value that needs one, a `Param`: an amount in the functional currency of a legal entity, in minor units, required when the value is chosen, no preset, and `LooserWhenHigher`), the default value (the standard profile's), the kinds of scope it may be set at, whether it is an internal control and an explanation; `Catalogue.Check` finds the catalogue's own mistakes and `Catalogue.Table` renders it as a Markdown table for the manual. The scope chain is a `Hierarchy` the context supplies, from a scope to its chain, the most specific first and the company last; the value in force for a legal entity is its own, else the company's, else the default, and `Setting` says which and which change set set it. Stored rows name a scope by kind and ID only, never by its place in the chain, so a branch or a transport mode is added by adding a kind to the context's hierarchy (and, in FIN, to the database's list of kinds) with no stored row changed (`TestALevelCanBeAddedWithoutChangingStoredRows` reads the same entries through a chain with a branch and a mode). A `ChangeSet` is one or more entries (scope, key, value, parameter) proposed together with a reason by an actor at the system's clock, never a business time, with an effective time not before that and not more than 366 days after it (zero: at once). It is a loosening when an entry moves its item looser than the value in force at its scope at the effective time (a higher parameter on the same value too), or sets a legal entity's value looser than the company's, the floor, the company's entries of the same change set counted; a loosening waits for approval by a different actor (actors are compared as written, so "Ann" is not "ann": an actor ID is an identifier that identity keeps case-sensitive), who may instead refuse it with a reason (a cancellation of kind refused), and one that only tightens or keeps is approved by its proposer when it is proposed (`Auto`). An approval makes the next version, numbered per tenant and context from 1 without a gap, and takes effect at the later of the effective time and the approval, so that no approval is retroactive. Change sets take effect in the order of their versions: one that would take effect before an approved change set with a lower version that is still to come is refused (`SETTINGS_ORDER`, the way out: a time at or after it, or cancel that one first), so the version in force never goes back in time and names one configuration. History is written forwards: a time before the latest recorded for the tenant is refused (`SETTINGS_CLOCK`, try again). A change set not in force (proposed, or approved with a time to come) is cancelled with a reason, by a holder of `ConfigureSettings` or of `ApproveSettings` (so that one configurer cannot leave a far-off approved change set in the way of every later change for up to 366 days: the way out the order rule names, to cancel it first, is open to an approver, and the cancellation records the role the canceller acted under, `CONFIGURER` or `APPROVER`, and says so in the history); one in force is not (a rollback is a new change set). `ConfigAt` answers the settings as of a time and the version in force. Before a change set is accepted, approved or cancelled the whole configuration is checked for every scope it touches (for a company entry, every scope with entries of its own) and for each later change set that takes effect from the time of the command (a rule added to a catalogue later does not refuse every command because of an older change set): each value known, each parameter present exactly where its value asks for one, and the context's rules across items; and no change set approved by its proposer alone may come to loosen because another was cancelled before it (`SETTINGS_WOULD_LOOSEN_UNAPPROVED`, naming the change set to cancel first). An exception: a value of a scope below the company that was approved looser than the company's stays in force when the company tightens, as the design's "to be looser, treat it as a loosening" allows; it is never silent: `Setting.Exception` marks it with the change set that set it, `Ledger.Exceptions` lists them, and the proposal that tightens the company answers the legal entities that stay looser (the receipt's `Notice`, kept with the receipt so a replay says the same). Every refusal wraps `ErrRefused` and one kind, names the item or the change set and says the way out.
  - FIN's catalogue (`fin/internal/application/settings_catalogue.go`: the dependency check keeps the domain to the standard library and the kernel, so the catalogue, the chain and the events are in the application layer). The items of the design's section 5 for the apportionment, F1, F2, F3, F4, F6, F7, F8 and F9, each set at the company and at a legal entity, each an internal control, the standard value as the default: F1 NOT_ALLOWED, NOT_ABOVE_THRESHOLD (with the threshold, for a legal entity only), UNRESTRICTED (default the first); F2 SUBMITTER_AND_PREVIEWER, SUBMITTER_ONLY (default); F3 EVERY_ATTRIBUTION (default), ONLY_WHEN_AMOUNT_MOVES; F4 ALL_PREVIEWED (default), ONE_STEP_WITHOUT_OPENED_OR_LEFT; F6 SEPARATE (default), INCLUDED_MARKED; F7 BLOCKS, DOES_NOT_BLOCK (default); F8 REJECT_WITH_DEFAULT (default), DEFAULT_TO_REMAINING; F9 RECONFIRM, KEEP_RATIO, KEEP_AMOUNT (default). The rule across items is F8 DEFAULT_TO_REMAINING only with F3 ONLY_WHEN_AMOUNT_MOVES. The chain is a legal entity, then the company. F5, S1 and the finance policy (E1 to E4) are not in it.
  - Storage (`fin/000017_fin_settings`). Four tables, immutable, row security enabled and forced, written at READ COMMITTED: `settings_change` (the proposal, with whether it loosens), `settings_change_entry`, `settings_approval` (at most one for a change set; it carries the proposal's own columns through a composite foreign key so that the rules about the pair of rows are checks) and `settings_cancellation` (at most one, of kind CANCELLED or REFUSED); `FinSettings` among the result types of `command_receipt`, which gains `result_notice`. The database holds the value order once, in the functions `settings_rank`, `settings_default_rank` and `settings_looser_when_higher` (a migration replaces them when a value is added; `TestFinSettingsDatabaseOrderIsTheCatalogues` keeps them and the code in step), and recomputes whether a change set loosens (`settings_loosening`: an entry looser than the value in force at its scope at the effective time, or than the company's value for a legal entity, or a higher threshold): an automatic approval of a change set it finds loosening is refused (`settings_approval_loosening`) and the stored flag must be its answer (`settings_change_loosening_flag`, at commit), so that a raw write by the application role cannot loosen alone. The database also refuses: an approver who is the proposer (the proposer's own approval is the automatic one, written with the change set, at the proposal); an approval before the proposal, or not taking effect at the later of its time and the effective time; an effective time before the proposal or more than 366 days after it; an approval after a cancellation, a cancellation of a change set in force or before it was proposed, a refusal of a change set approved, the three taking turns under the tenant's advisory lock that every insert takes (the application role may not update these rows, so it cannot lock them as FIN's other checks do); a second approval or cancellation; a version twice or skipped; an entry's scope (the company has no ID), item and value and parameter (only F1's threshold has one, 1 to 10^15 minor units, for a legal entity); a change set without entries, with a gap in their numbers or whose entry comes in a later transaction. The recorded times are tied to the database's clock: each time written (proposal, approval, cancellation) is within one minute of `clock_timestamp()` at the write (not the transaction's start, because a command may wait for the lock after its transaction began; one minute so that FIN's host may differ from the database by what a time service leaves, and a row cannot be back-dated by more), and not before the latest time recorded for the tenant, so history is written forwards. The tests run on one machine, where FIN's clock and the database's are the same. A deployment requirement follows: the clocks of FIN's application servers and of the database are kept synchronised (for example by NTP) to well within a minute. When they are not, every settings command is refused with `SETTINGS_CLOCK_SKEW` (the database's `settings_time_window` answered as a refusal that names the cause: the clocks differ by more than a minute; the way out: synchronise them and try the command again), and nothing else of FIN is affected; reads are not. What only the domain keeps: that the configuration is valid and F8's rule holds for each scope and each later change set, that nothing approved alone comes to loosen when an earlier change set is cancelled, that change sets take effect in the order of their versions, that a legal entity has a finance policy and that an amount is in its functional currency, that the approver holds `ApproveSettings` and is another person (the database compares two strings), and that the item may be set at the scope. A known limit: a role that can insert into these tables can write any approver's name; the controls above hold against it for loosening and for the order of times, not for who the second person is. A raw writer with the application role's rights who also writes another approver's name is not stopped by the database (it cannot tell who an actor is), nor is a raw cancellation written in between two other rows; the domain keeps these, and so does the audit of the approvers' names. No row changes; the ALTER TABLE of `command_receipt` takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock for its brief check of the existing rows (reasoned, not measured).
  - Commands and permissions. `ProposeFinSettingsChange`, `ApproveFinSettingsChange`, `CancelFinSettingsChange` and `RefuseFinSettingsChange` (idempotent by command id with receipts like FIN's other commands, one event each: `FinSettingsChangeProposed`, `FinSettingsChangeApproved` with the version, `FinSettingsChangeCancelled`, `FinSettingsChangeRefused`; a proposal approved on the spot has the first two; identifiers only) take the tenant's settings lock first, then look for their own receipt (two identical commands together: the second waits and answers the first's receipt), read the whole ledger, read FIN's clock only then (so commands are stamped in the order they commit) and check and write in one transaction. A legal entity named must have a finance policy and an F1 threshold is given in its functional currency; an effective time more than 366 days away (or beyond the year 9000) is refused with the way out, a nearer time. Proposing needs the action `ConfigureSettings`, approving and refusing `ApproveSettings`, cancelling either (a holder of both cancels as a configurer); both are granted by name only in the platform's permissions (`internal/bootstrap/api.go`, `AuthorizeFinAction`, as SEA's VGM signatory): `fin:*` and `*` grant neither. That the approver is not the proposer is refused by name (`SETTINGS_SAME_ACTOR`, way out: another approver).
  - Queries. `GetFinSettings` (scope, as of a time, now when zero: each item's value, threshold in the legal entity's currency, the scope and change set it comes from or the default, whether it is an exception, and the version in force), `ListFinSettingsChanges` (the history in the order proposed: proposer, approver, reason, times, whether it loosens, status PROPOSED, APPROVED, IN_FORCE, CANCELLED or REFUSED, version) and `GetFinSettingsCatalogue` (and the table). Like FIN's other queries they do not authorize.
  - Not yet: FIN's rules read none of this (C1b, which also records the version in each decision and in a preview's fingerprint); the finance policy brought under the mechanism (C1c); SEA's items and S1 (C2); presets, onboarding, the exceptions report (which will list the approved exceptions above, and a person who holds both permissions), the design's rule for a customer with one administrator (a loosening confirmed in writing by that administrator and put on the exceptions report: today such a customer cannot loosen, because the approver must be another person), the prompt for a new item at an upgrade, the previous version as a stored field, export and import, the impact preview and pairwise and property tests (C3).
  - Tests: pure, the mechanism (`settings_test.go`): `TestCatalogueCheck`, `TestCatalogueTable`, `TestResolutionThroughTheChain`, `TestALevelCanBeAddedWithoutChangingStoredRows`, `TestLoosening` (each kind of move), `TestAHigherParameterLoosens`, `TestTheFloor`, `TestApproval`, `TestCancellation` (before and after the effective time), `TestVersionsAndAsOf`, `TestValidation` (each kind), `TestValidationOfTheWholeConfiguration`, `TestAnAutoApprovedChangeSetStaysTightening`, `TestALooseningIsMeasuredAtTheEffectiveTime`, `TestAParameterLoosensOnlyWhereTheCatalogueSays`, `TestChangeSetsInForceTogetherAreOrderedByVersion`, `TestAProposalIsNotChangedByItsCaller`, `TestTimeIsRecordedForwards`, `TestChangeSetsTakeEffectInTheOrderOfTheirVersions`, `TestAnApprovedExceptionIsMarkedAndStaysWhenTheCompanyTightens`, `TestBoundariesOfAProposal` (reason, 64 entries, identifier, actor, 366 days, parameter), `TestActorsAreComparedExactly`, `TestAnApproverRefusesAProposal`, `TestEveryRefusalSaysTheWayOut`, `TestANewRuleDoesNotRefuseOldHistory` and `TestAnApprovalChecksTheConfigurationAgain`; FIN's catalogue (`settings_catalogue_test.go`, application): `TestFinSettingsCatalogue` (ordering and defaults), `TestFinHierarchy`, `TestF8NeedsF3`, `TestSettingsEvents`; permissions (`permissions_fin_test.go`, bootstrap): `TestFinSettingsPermissionsAreGrantedByNameOnly`. On PostgreSQL through FIN's commands (`settings_postgres_test.go`, `settings_review_postgres_test.go`): `TestFinSettingsProposeApproveCancelOnPostgres` (propose, approve, cancel, history, as-of), `TestFinSettingsReplaysAndEventsOnPostgres`, `TestFinSettingsDatabaseRulesRefuseRawWrites` (each database rule by its name), `TestFinSettingsDatabaseKnowsEveryValueOfTheCatalogue`, `TestFinSettingsDatabaseOrderIsTheCatalogues`, `TestFinSettingsDatabaseRecomputesWhetherAChangeSetLoosens` (the reviewer's single-actor loosening, the floor, the company's entry of the same change set, a threshold raised and lowered, the flag both ways), `TestFinSettingsDatabaseAgreesWithTheLedger` (random sequences of the real commands), `TestFinSettingsDatabaseMeasuresAgainstTheValuesInForce` (the value in force at the effective time: the latest by time and version, none cancelled, none still to come), `TestFinSettingsDatabaseTiesTheTimesToItsOwnClock` (the reviewer's back-dated and stale-clock cases), `TestFinSettingsDatabaseLengthAndPatternChecks`, `TestFinSettingsTheOwnerCannotChangeOrDeleteARow` (as the migrator), `TestFinSettingsAnApprovedExceptionIsShownAndAnnounced`, `TestFinSettingsChangeSetsTakeEffectInTheOrderOfTheirVersions`, `TestFinSettingsAnEffectiveTimeFarAheadIsRefused`, `TestFinSettingsAnApproverRefusesAProposal`, `TestFinSettingsAnApproverCancelsAFarOffChangeSetThatStandsInTheWay`, `TestFinSettingsAClockOffByMoreThanAMinuteIsRefusedWithItsWayOut`, `TestFinSettingsTheTimeWindowIsOneMinuteWide`, `TestFinSettingsDatabaseRemainingBoundaries`, `TestFinSettingsRefusalsSayTheWayOutThroughTheCommands`, and the deterministic concurrency tests (a lock held on a second connection, the waiting sessions counted by `pg_blocking_pids` from the test's own holder): `TestFinSettingsProposalsWaitForTheSettingsLock`, `TestFinSettingsCommandsAreStampedAfterTheyHoldTheLock`, `TestFinSettingsTwoIdenticalCommandsTogetherAnswerOneReceipt`, `TestFinSettingsTwoApprovalsOfOneChangeSet`, `TestFinSettingsApprovalRacesCancellation` (each order) and `TestFinSettingsDatabaseOrdersAnApprovalAndACancellation` (the database's own lock). A mutation script (`mutate.py`, kept outside the tree) removes each rule in turn and lists the test that fails.
- V2.97 lets FIN's rules read the settings F1, F2, F3, F6 and F7, and records the settings version on every preview, apportionment and closure (step C1b-1 of the configuration of the rules). Migration fin/000018.
  - Reading. A command or query that a setting steers reads the settings in force for the pool's legal entity (the scope chain of V2.96: the legal entity's own value, else the company's, else the default) at FIN's own clock, never a business time, inside its own transaction, with the version in force (`Deps.RuleSettingsOf`, which hands the domain a `RuleSettings`: the version, F1 with its threshold in the functional currency, F2, F3, F6 and F7). `PreviewPoolApportionment`, `SubmitPoolApportionment` and `ClosePool` read it in their transaction; `GetJobProfit`, `GetConsolProfit`, `GetConsolCostPoolPendingItems` and `GetConsolCostPreviewEffect` read it in their read-only snapshot. The domain does not know the ledger. The zero `RuleSettings` is the standard profile, so with no settings set FIN behaves as in V2.96 and every earlier test passes unchanged (`Close`, `PendingItems` and the unexported `fingerprint` and `reworksAskedBy` are kept as the standard profile's; the commands call `CloseUnder`, `PendingItemsUnder` and the versions that take the settings). A decision that FIN records (a preview, a submission, a close) takes the tenant's settings advisory lock in shared mode inside its transaction (`Deps.DecisionSettings`), reads FIN's clock after that lock, reads the ledger as of that time and stamps its records with it, so that the version it records is the one the ledger says was in force at its recorded system time: a settings command takes the same lock exclusively and stamps its time after it, so it waits for a decision that holds the lock and is stamped after it, and a decision that waited for it reads its version. The lock order has no cycle: a pool command holds its pool, its houses' jobs and its policy before it takes the settings lock, and a settings command takes the settings lock first and no pool, job or policy lock (it reads the policy without one). The settings store reads the entries of the change sets it read only, so a reader meets no entry of a change set it has not read. A query takes no lock: it reads the pool or job and the settings in one snapshot (`GetConsolProfit` too) and reports the version of that snapshot.
  - F1, segregation of duties, governs both rules that keep a person from confirming their own case: V2.91's (the asker of a rework does not submit the first apportionment of a line after it) and V2.93's (the asker of the rework that unloaded a house and whoever ended its place in the consolidation do not confirm its attribution). `NOT_ALLOWED` is as before. `NOT_ABOVE_THRESHOLD` applies the rules only when the amount at risk is strictly above the threshold, which is in the legal entity's functional currency (an amount equal to it is not above it): for V2.93's rule the local amount of the former share that the attribution answers, each attribution on its own (for a correction of an attribution in force, the share the house held when it left, the one the latest apportionment that gave it a used share recorded); for V2.91's rule the local cost of the line, because that submission redistributes the whole line over the houses. A submission with one part above and one below is refused when the submitter is barred, naming the part above (the house, the line, the amount and the threshold) and the way out (another finance user submits). `UNRESTRICTED` applies neither rule. F1's explanation in the catalogue now says it governs both; its values do not change.
  - F2, who counts as the confirmer. `SUBMITTER_ONLY` is as before. `SUBMITTER_AND_PREVIEWER` checks the person who made the preview (the preview records who) against the same bars, for both rules, and the refusal names "the previewer" and the way out (preview again as another finance user). One person who previews and submits is checked once.
  - F3, voucher. `EVERY_ATTRIBUTION` is as before. `ONLY_WHEN_AMOUNT_MOVES`: an attribution with a loss of 0 and an exception cost of 0 needs no voucher (it gives the whole former share to the cargo that remains), and a correction still states its reason; an attribution that moves an amount needs a voucher, a correction (one that replaces a different attribution in force, even back to nothing) always needs its reason and its voucher under both values, because it changes who bears an amount that was recorded with a voucher, and a voucher given is always checked (at most 500 characters).
  - F6, a pending former share in the house's profit. `SEPARATE` is as before. `INCLUDED_MARKED`: `GetJobProfit` counts the pending former shares in `PoolCost` and `Profit` and marks them (`PendingInProfit` is the amount, each entry of `FormerShares` has `Pending`), and `GetConsolProfit` counts each house's pending former share in its `PoolCost` and `Profit` and marks it (`HouseProfit.PendingInProfit`); the consolidation's totals and `FormerSharesPending` are unchanged, and the costs stay conserved. Every profit view says its basis (`ProfitBasis`: `SEPARATE` or `INCLUDED_MARKED`) and the settings version it was read under, so that figures under the two bases are not mixed up.
  - F7, a pending cause and the close. `DOES_NOT_BLOCK` is as before. `BLOCKS`: the close is refused while an exception cost of party `PENDING` is in force on a line, naming the line, every house with a pending cause on it and the way out (establish the party and correct the attribution: preview the line again stating the party as a correction, with its reason and a voucher, and submit it). The pending item `EXCEPTION_CAUSE_PENDING` says which: that it holds the close back (F7 BLOCKS), or that it does not (F7 DOES_NOT_BLOCK, and that under BLOCKS it would). The pending list's rule, empty exactly when the pool can be closed, stays true under both values, with `EXCEPTION_CAUSE_PENDING` counted as blocking under `BLOCKS` and, as in V2.94, not under `DOES_NOT_BLOCK`.
  - The version and the fingerprint. A preview records the settings version it was made under, a submitted apportionment the version in force at its submission, a pool closure the version in force at the close (`SettingsVersion` in the pool view; 0 when no change set was in force, and on a row written before). The preview's fingerprint holds the version as one more fact, `settings` with `version N`, left out when the version is 0 so that the text is what V2.96 wrote and a preview made before V2.97 stays current while no setting is set. A preview made under one version and submitted under another is refused as stale (`FIN_PREVIEW_STALE`), saying what changed ("the settings changed from version N to M", first of the changes) and the way out (preview again and submit the new preview); `GetConsolCostPreviewEffect` says the preview is not current in the same words. The version is the tenant's, not the legal entity's: a change set for another legal entity makes the previews of this one stale too, which costs a new preview and keeps a preview and its submission on one version.
  - The database (`fin/000018_fin_settings_version`). A column `settings_version` (at least 0, NULL for a row written before) on `pool_apportionment_preview`, `pool_apportionment` and `consol_cost_pool_closure`, added without a default. `pool_apportionment_preview_check` is replaced with every rule of its latest definition (fin/000016) and now an apportionment's version equal to its preview's (an apportionment of a preview that has a version records the same one, and none is refused; a preview with no version, written before the migration, is not compared). The voucher rule of `pool_apportionment_attribution` is replaced: the column check becomes a table check of the same name, `pool_apportionment_attribution_voucher_check`, which allows the empty voucher (the empty string, not a blank one) only when the loss and the exception cost are both 0, and keeps the others (not NULL, at most 500 characters once trimmed). A closure's version is kept by the trigger every closure has (fin/000007), and the application role may update only the four columns of the reopening. What only the domain keeps: which setting is in force for a legal entity at a time, the thresholds and who is barred by them (F1 and F2), that a voucher is needed only when F3 asks for it (the database cannot know the setting, and lets the empty voucher through whatever F3 says when the amounts are 0), what a profit view counts (F6), whether a pending cause holds back the close (F7), and that a correction always has a voucher (the database cannot tell a correction), and that the version recorded is the one in force at the decision (the database holds only the apportionment's equal to its preview's, and holds an apportionment of a preview that has a version to the same one, a NULL included, and compares nothing only for a preview with no version, which is a row written before the migration). The ALTER TABLEs take an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock briefly and do not rewrite a table (reasoned, not measured); the new voucher check is validated against the existing rows, which all satisfy it (reasoned from the old check). No row changes.
  - Not yet: F4, F8 and F9, which nothing reads (C1b-2); the impact of a change before it takes effect (C1b-2); the finance policy under the mechanism (C1c); SEA's items and S1 (C2); presets, onboarding, the exceptions report and the rest of the design's section 7 (C3).
  - Tests: pure, the domain (`rules_test.go`): `TestF1NotAllowedBarsTheAskerAndTheEnder`, `TestF1UnrestrictedLiftsBothRules`, `TestF1ThresholdBoundaryOfTheAttribution` (49,999, 50,000 and 50,001 against CNY 500.00, and 0), `TestF1ThresholdReadsEachAttributionOnItsOwn`, `TestF1ThresholdReadsTheFormerShareOfACorrection`, `TestF1ThresholdOfTheReworkRule` (the boundary and two lines), `TestF2ChecksThePreviewerAsTheSubmitter` (both rules, with the threshold and UNRESTRICTED), `TestF3VoucherOnlyWhenTheAmountMoves`, `TestF7ABlockingPendingCauseHoldsBackTheClose` (both values, the list, the version of the closure), `TestThePreviewsFingerprintHoldsTheSettingsVersion` and `TestTheZeroRuleSettingsAreTheStandardProfile`; the application (`rule_settings_test.go`): `TestRuleSettingsOfReadsTheScopeChainAtTheClockWithTheVersion` (the legal entity's value and the company's, a change set that takes effect later, version 0, no store, no clock) and `TestTheRuleSettingsNamesAreTheCataloguesValues`. On PostgreSQL through FIN's commands, with the settings changed through FIN's settings commands (propose by one person, approve by another where it loosens) (`rules_settings_postgres_test.go`): `TestF1AndF2OnTheAttributionOnPostgres` (each value, the boundary, both bars, the previewer, the versions recorded), `TestF1ThresholdNamesThePartAboveOnPostgres`, `TestF1AndF2OnTheReworkRuleOnPostgres`, `TestF3VoucherOnlyWhenAmountMovesOnPostgres` (a correction back to nothing needs its voucher), `TestASubmissionWaitsForASettingsChangeAndReadsItsVersionOnPostgres`, `TestASettingsChangeWaitsForADecisionAndIsStampedAfterItOnPostgres` and `TestPreviewsWhileSettingsChangeFailWithNoInternalErrorOnPostgres` (`rules_concurrency_postgres_test.go`, with `pg_blocking_pids` and a clock stopped inside the lock), `TestF6PendingFormerShareInTheProfitOnPostgres`, `TestF7APendingCauseAndTheCloseOnPostgres`, `TestAPreviewMadeBeforeASettingsChangeIsStaleOnPostgres` and `TestAChangeTakingEffectInTheFutureDoesNotApplyBeforeOnPostgres` (a module whose clock is after the effective time and one before it, then a cancellation); the database (`rules_database_postgres_test.go`): `TestTheVoucherRuleOfTheDatabaseOnPostgres`, `TestAnApportionmentRecordsItsPreviewsSettingsVersionOnPostgres` and `TestAClosuresSettingsVersionIsRecordedOnceOnPostgres`. Across contexts, box A with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY and FIN (`internal/bootstrap/rules_settings_integration_test.go`): `TestUnrestrictedLetsTheAskerAndTheTransfererSubmitWithEveryContext`, `TestTheThresholdIsReadOnTheFormerShareWithEveryContext` (H03's former share is CNY 500.00: with a threshold of CNY 300.00 ops-1 and ops-9 are refused, naming the attribution; with CNY 600.00 neither is barred from the attribution and ops-9 confirms it, while ops-1 is still stopped by V2.91's rule, the line costing CNY 3,000.00 and the rework not being carried; with CNY 3,000.00 ops-1 confirms) and `TestBlocksRefusesTheCloseWhileTheCauseIsPendingWithEveryContext`. A mutation script (`mutate.py`, kept outside the tree) removes each rule of this step in turn and lists the test that fails.
- V2.98 lets FIN's rules read the settings F4, F8 and F9 (step C1b-2 of the configuration of the rules): an apportionment in one step, a default attribution to the cargo that remains, and a confirmed attribution kept by its proportion or confirmed again when its line's cost changes. Migration fin/000019.
  - Reading. `Deps.RuleSettingsOf` and `Deps.DecisionSettings` hand the domain F4, F8 and F9 with the others (`RuleSettings.F4`, `F8`, `F9`; an empty value is the item's default), under the same lock, clock and scope chain as in V2.97. With none of the three set FIN behaves as in V2.97. The catalogue's value names are the domain's constants (`TestTheRuleSettingsNamesAreTheCataloguesValues`, `TestRuleSettingsOfReadsF4F8AndF9`).
  - F4, preview before an apportionment. `ALL_PREVIEWED` is as before, and the new command `ApportionConsolCostInOneStep` is refused with `FIN_ONE_STEP_REFUSED`, naming F4 and the way out (preview the lines and submit the preview, or ask a configurer to set F4). `ONE_STEP_WITHOUT_OPENED_OR_LEFT`: the command takes a preview's input without attributions (lines, method, manual shares, houses left out, all houses, reason) and the pool's expected revision, and in one transaction does what a preview and a submission do together: PHY's basis first, then the houses' jobs in job id order, the policy, the revenue of a line by REVENUE, the settings in force (`DecisionSettings`) and the same checks, and it writes an apportionment that names no preview (`preview_no` is NULL), with the settings version in force and its submitter. Nothing is stored as a preview, the revision moves once, and the receipt's `Version` is the apportionment's number. It carries the attributions in force as a submission would, F9 included, and states none (the command has no field for one, and the domain refuses any it is given with `FIN_INVALID`). It emits `ConsolCostsApportioned.v1` as a submission does, whose payload names the pool, the legal entity and the apportionment's number and no preview and no person (the event has no preview number: read in `domain.Event`, and the payload of the stored event is checked by `TestOneStepApportionmentEndToEndOnPostgres`). The action is authorized as FIN's other pool actions (`fin:*`, `*` or its own name; not granted by name only; there is no other list of FIN's actions in the platform to add it to: `AuthorizeFinAction` takes any action name; `TestTheOneStepActionIsAuthorizedLikeThePoolActions`).
  - What refuses a one step, each with its way out. While the pool holds a former share pending finance's confirmation on any line (the refusal names the houses and lines). While a line of the pool that is apportioned has not been apportioned, from a preview, since a rework that PHY reads as ended (V2.91's rule: the first apportionment after a rework is always previewed; the refusal names the rework and the lines). A line first apportioned after a rework ended may be apportioned in one step, and the rework's asker is still refused. A line not apportioned yet is not one of these: its first apportionment after a rework is kept from the person who asked for the rework by V2.91's own check, which the one step makes as a submission does (`FIN_CONFIRMER_ASKED_REWORK`, under F1 as in V2.97), so that a person who asked for a rework cannot make a first apportionment in one step that a submission would refuse them. While a container is in rework (`FIN_POOL_IN_WORK`, as any apportionment). While a line holds a carried attribution that F9 RECONFIRM asks to be stated again, that KEEP_RATIO cannot scale (a cost not above 0), or that no longer fits the line's cost: the refusal is `FIN_ONE_STEP_REFUSED` and also the error of the attribution (`FIN_ATTRIBUTION_RECONFIRM` or `FIN_INVALID`), and says to preview the line stating it again. A carried loss needs its house's job open, as in a submission (`FIN_POOL_JOB_MISSING`). A house that left before a line was first apportioned holds no share on it and does not stop a one step; one that left after does, until it is attributed (reasoned from `formerSharesOf`, which reads the used shares of the apportionment in force; not run).
  - F8, when no attribution is stated. `REJECT_WITH_DEFAULT` is as before. `DEFAULT_TO_REMAINING` (the catalogue's rule across items already requires F3 `ONLY_WHEN_AMOUNT_MOVES` with it, and the domain also refuses to default when the voucher would be needed): in a preview, a former house that holds a pending former share on a line, is not stated and has no attribution in force gets an attribution by default: loss 0, exception cost 0, no party, no voucher, marked `ByDefault`, its departure as its date, and its job as any attribution's. Its whole former share goes to the cargo that remains (to be allocated when no house is live), as any attribution of nothing. The preview shows it, the fingerprint covers it (the settings version is a fact of it, and the former houses and the line's apportionment in force already were), and the submission records it with the mark. It is a confirmation like any other: the bars of F1 and F2 apply to it as to a stated attribution, measured by the former share (the one in force at the line, `formerShareLocal`), so that it is no way past segregation of duties. It never replaces an attribution in force. A statement of nothing is a statement, not a default. A later apportionment carries it, still marked. The pending item of a former share says, under this value, what the default will do, and the preview's effect says that the attribution is a default and what it does (`PreviewReason.ByDefault`, `Says`).
  - F9, a confirmed attribution when its line's cost changes. A line's cost has changed when its amount in its currency, or its local amount, differs from what it was at the apportionment that stated the attribution (the latest apportionment of the line that holds the house's attribution not carried, found through the apportionments that carried it; if a link is missing, which no command writes, the apportionment in force is read as the one that stated it). `KEEP_AMOUNT` is as before (carried as it is; the refusal when the carried amounts exceed the new cost stays). `KEEP_RATIO`: a carried attribution's loss and exception cost are each the amount stated times the line's cost now over its cost then, in the line's currency, to the nearest minor unit (a half away from zero), always from the stating apportionment and never from an earlier rescale; the rest of the line is what remains, divided as before, and the local amounts are split by the existing largest remainder, so a line is conserved in both currencies. A line that did not change carries the amounts stated (so a line back at its cost gives them again). A cost then or now not above 0 is refused, naming the way out (state the attribution again). The row is recorded as carried and marked `ByRatio`, and the mark belongs to the apportionment that rescaled: a row carried by amount after F9 changed from `KEEP_RATIO` to `KEEP_AMOUNT` keeps the amounts in force and is not marked, and the preview effect says which amounts are stated, which are rescaled and which are carried. A rescale is the configured arithmetic applied to a confirmed attribution and nobody chooses its amount, so, as for an amount carried under `KEEP_AMOUNT`, the bars of F1 and F2 do not apply to it (the lever on it is the revision of the line's cost, a command of its own). Because each amount is rounded on its own, carried amounts can exceed a line that fell by a minor unit; that is refused as before, with the way out of stating them again. `RECONFIRM`: a preview of a changed line that does not state that house's attribution again is refused (`FIN_ATTRIBUTION_RECONFIRM`), naming the house, the line, the cost then and now in both currencies, and the way out. A restatement equal to the attribution in force (amounts, party, reference, voucher) is then recorded as stated, not carried, with no reason (it is not a correction), and the next change is measured from it; it is a confirmation, so F1 and F2 bar who they bar; a different statement is a correction as before. On a line that did not change, an equal statement is the attribution in force carried, whatever F9 says; under `KEEP_RATIO` on a changed line it is carried by ratio, like no statement. One step is refused on a line RECONFIRM asks to be stated again.
  - The database (`fin/000019_fin_attribution_defaults`). Two columns on `pool_apportionment_attribution`, `by_default` and `by_ratio` (boolean, not NULL, default false: false for every row written before, added without rewriting the table). `pool_attribution_by_default`: a row marked by default has loss 0, exception cost 0, no party and the empty voucher. `pool_attribution_by_ratio`: a row marked by ratio is carried. `check_attribution_at_commit` is replaced with every rule of fin/000016 and one more, checked last: an attribution of an apportionment that names no preview must be carried (`pool_attribution_stated_by_preview`), so that a one-step apportionment states none; the database checks only that such a row is carried, so a raw write cannot state an attribution without calling it carried, and that a carried row equals the attribution in force (or is the right proportion of it) is kept by the domain alone. `pool_apportionment_preview_check` is replaced with every rule of its latest definition (fin/000018) and now compares the two marks of each attribution with the preview's figures (a preview written before the migration has none, read as false). What only the domain keeps: F4, F8 and F9 themselves (the database cannot tell that a preview-less apportionment was allowed by F4, that a default was made where F8 says and none was in force, or that F9 was what it was), that a one step was not made while a former share was pending or a rework uncarried, and whether the amounts of a row carried by ratio are the right proportion (it knows only that the row is carried). The loss and exception cost clauses of the default's check are implied by its voucher clause with fin/000018's voucher rule (an amount needs a voucher), and so is its party clause; they are kept so that the check says what a default is. The existing raw writes of two tests insert an attribution into an apportionment that names no preview; they are carried now (see Tests).
  - Not yet: the impact of a settings change before it takes effect (C1b-3); the finance policy under the mechanism (C1c); SEA's items and S1 (C2); presets, onboarding, the exceptions report and the rest of the design's section 7 (C3).
  - Tests: pure, the domain (`one_step_defaults_test.go`): `TestF8DefaultsAnUnstatedFormerShareToTheCargoThatRemains`, `TestF8NeverReplacesAnAttributionInForce`, `TestF8AStatementIsNotADefault`, `TestF8WithNoHouseLiveLeavesTheFormerShareToBeAllocated`, `TestF8TheBarsOfF1AndF2ApplyToTheDefault` (NOT_ALLOWED, UNRESTRICTED and the threshold at its edge, F2), `TestAPreviewOfTheDefaultIsStaleWhenTheSettingsChange`, `TestF8ThePendingItemAndThePreviewEffectSayWhatTheDefaultDoes`, `TestF9WhenTheCostOfALineChanges` (each value, a line raised and lowered, unchanged), `TestF9ConservesALineInAForeignCurrency`, `TestF9RoundsAtAHalfAwayFromZero`, `TestF9EachRescaleStartsFromTheStatingApportionment` (a loss of 5 on 3,000.00 is 6 on 3,300.00 and 6 on 3,600.00, not 7, and 5 again at 3,000.00), `TestF9KeepRatioNeedsACostAboveZero`, `TestF9ReconfirmTheEqualRestatementIsRecordedAsStated`, `TestAnEqualStatementOutsideReconfirmIsCarried`, `TestF9ALocalChangeIsAChange`, `TestF9TheStatingApportionmentIsFoundThroughTheCarriedOnes`, `TestF4OneStepWritesTheApportionmentOfAPreviewAndNoPreview`, `TestF4OneStepIsRefusedUnlessF4AllowsIt`, `TestF4OneStepRefusalsEachOnItsOwn`, `TestF4OneStepCarriesTheAttributionsUnderF9`, `TestF4OneStepNeedsTheJobOfACarriedLoss`, `TestF4OneStepKeepsTheRulesOfAnyChangeOfThePool`, `TestF9ARescaleIsConfirmedByNobodyAndTheBarredMaySubmitIt`, `TestF9TheRatioMarkBelongsToTheApportionmentThatRescaled`, `TestF8ACarriedDefaultSaysItWasMadeByDefault`; the application's `TestRuleSettingsOfReadsF4F8AndF9`. On PostgreSQL through FIN's commands, the settings changed through its settings commands (`rules_one_step_postgres_test.go`): `TestOneStepApportionmentEndToEndOnPostgres` (refused under the default, then the receipt, the row with no preview, the event, the replay, the idempotency conflict, a stale revision, and the pool closing on it), `TestOneStepApportionmentIsRefusedOnPostgres` (a former share, a container in rework, a rework not carried and the asker), `TestOneStepApportionmentCarriesTheAttributionsUnderF9OnPostgres`, `TestF9EndToEndOnPostgres` (each value, raised and lowered, the equal restatement and its confirmer, measured from the restatement), `TestF8DefaultEndToEndOnPostgres` (the default, the pending item, the effect, F1 and F2, the profit views, carried), `TestAPreviewMadeBeforeAChangeOfF4F8OrF9IsStaleOnPostgres`, `TestOneStepApportionmentPassesItsWholeInputOnPostgres`, `TestOneStepApportionmentIsStampedWithTheTimeOfItsSettingsOnPostgres`, `TestOneStepApportionmentAsksForItsOwnActionOnPostgres`, `TestAStatedAttributionAfterAOneStepApportionmentOnPostgres`, `TestOneStepAndAPreviewRaceForOnePoolOnPostgres` (the pool's lock held, the two commands queued behind it in each order, released together); and the database by raw writes (`rules_attribution_marks_postgres_test.go`): `TestTheMarksOfAnAttributionOnPostgres`, `TestThePreviewCheckComparesTheMarksOnPostgres`. Across contexts on box A (`internal/bootstrap/one_step_defaults_integration_test.go`): `TestADefaultAttributionGoesToTheCargoThatRemainsWithEveryContext` (fin-2 previews and submits without stating H03: H01 and H02 bear 1,200.00 and 1,800.00; ops-1 submitting the same preview is refused under F1 NOT_ALLOWED), `TestAConfirmedAttributionWhenTheCostChangesWithEveryContext` (H03's loss 200.00 and exception cost 300.00 and the freight revised to 3,600.00: KEEP_AMOUNT keeps them, KEEP_RATIO gives 240.00 and 360.00, RECONFIRM refuses and accepts the equal restatement), `TestASecondLineIsApportionedInOneStepOnceTheFormerShareIsAttributedWithEveryContext`, and `TestTheOneStepActionIsAuthorizedLikeThePoolActions`. Existing tests changed: the raw attribution inserts of `rawAttribution` (`former_share_postgres_test.go`) and `rawVoucher` (`rules_database_postgres_test.go`) write `carried` true, because their apportionments name no preview (fin/000019); and `TestRuleSettingsOfReadsTheScopeChainAtTheClockWithTheVersion` expects F4, F8 and F9 at their defaults in the settings it reads.

- V2.99 lets FIN serve EII (another IDP project): a read-only profit API, a cost review request for a loss-making job, and a test tenant. Migration fin/000020.
  - The query and the profit version. `ListJobProfits` (legal entity, `LossOnly`, a page of 1 to 500 after a job id) answers, for each job of the legal entity that is not cancelled, in job id order: the job, shipment and customer, the state, the functional currency, revenue, cost (the job's cost and what it bears of pools: revenue less cost is the profit), profit, the profit basis (F6), the settings version and the profit version; with `AsOf` on FIN's clock and the next cursor. FIN's rows of a page are read in one read-only snapshot; PHY's basis of each consolidation the page's jobs have a share in is read just after it, outside FIN's transaction (T3), once for each consolidation, so a page is not one snapshot across FIN and PHY. A loss-only page looks at no more than 2,000 jobs (`scanWindow`), so a page may hold fewer than the limit and still have a next page. `GetJobProfit` gains `ProfitVersion`, and now takes the actor (`ActorID`), checked for `fin:ReadProfit` when it is named (an in-process caller that names none reads as before). The profit version is the SHA-256 in hex of a text of one fact to a line, sorted: the job's revision, the revision of each pool the job has a share in, the settings version, and the figures (revenue, invoiced, cost, matched and accrued, pool cost, profit, what is pending and the re-routing loss, the basis, each charge code's figures). Equal figures from the same sources give the same version and any change gives another; clients compare it for equality only (the contract says so). `GetJobProfit` now computes through the same function (`application.ProfitOf`) as the request and the page, so the three cannot differ. Measured on the test tenant (30 jobs, 3 with shares in 2 consolidations, one machine): a page of all 30 takes 32 to 45 ms (median 33 ms) over HTTP against 4 ms for one job's profit; how much is PHY was not separated, and a page with many consolidations was not measured. Nothing was optimized.
  - The request and its resolution. `RequestCostReview` (command id, job, the profit version the requester saw, a reason of 1 to 500 characters) first asks FIN's authorizer for `RequestCostReview` (before the receipt is looked for, so that a replay by a person who may not is refused), then reads PHY's bases of the job's pools (T3), then in one transaction locks the job's row (the lock an apportionment and a job change take), takes the settings lock in shared mode and reads the settings under it (`DecisionSettings`), reads the job's shares, losses and pools' revisions and computes the profit, and refuses, each with its way out: the job is not OPEN (`FIN_REVIEW_JOB_NOT_OPEN`), its profit is not below 0 (`FIN_REVIEW_NOT_A_LOSS`), the version differs (`FIN_REVIEW_STALE`, read it again), an unresolved request exists (`FIN_REVIEW_OPEN`, naming it), in that order; a job that gained a share in a pool after the bases were read is stale too. It writes the request under the tenant's numbering lock as `CRR-<n>` without a gap (a refusal takes no number), with the job, the requester (the token's actor), the reason, the figures, the basis, the settings version, the profit version and FIN's clock, and the event `CostReviewRequested.v1`; a replay answers the same receipt (subject `CRR-n`, revision 1, version n). `ResolveCostReview` (command id, request, outcome `COST_CORRECTED` or `NO_CHANGE`, a note of 1 to 500 characters) locks the same job row, so that a request and the resolution of the job's earlier one take turns, and is refused for the requester (`FIN_REVIEW_SAME_ACTOR`) and for a request resolved already (`FIN_REVIEW_RESOLVED`); it writes the resolution and `CostReviewResolved.v1` (receipt revision 2). Neither changes an amount: a test reads the job's profit version, revision and profit before and after. `GetCostReviewRequest` and `ListCostReviewRequests` (by job, by `OPEN` or `RESOLVED`, paged after a request id) read them. Permissions, through FIN's authorizer: `fin:ReadProfit` (the profit queries and the request queries), `fin:RequestCostReview`, `fin:ResolveCostReview`; `fin:*` grants all three and none is by name only, because none changes an amount or a control, and the rule that matters, that the resolver is not the requester, is FIN's and the database's, whatever a token says.
  - The HTTP routes and the contract. FIN's first routes, under `/v1/fin/`: `GET legal-entities/{legalEntityId}/job-profits`, `GET jobs/{jobId}/profit`, `POST cost-review-requests`, `GET cost-review-requests/{requestId}`, `GET jobs/{jobId}/cost-review-requests`, `POST cost-review-requests/{requestId}/resolution`, in `internal/contexts/fin/internal/adapters/http`, registered by `Module.RegisterHTTP` and wired in `Platform.API` (DOC's snapshots stay unbound there: no route issues a debit note). They follow RATE's and SEA's conventions: problems with a business code, strict snake_case bodies with no unknown field (a body that names a tenant or a person is refused as an unknown field), a body of at most 1 MiB, `Idempotency-Key` as the command id, tenant and actor only from the token, 201 with `Location` for a request and 200 for a resolution, each with `ETag`. They differ in one thing: a body states no `occurred_at`; these commands take FIN's own clock, like the settings commands, and the command's hash covers the actor and the body but no time. The contract for EII, with every route, field, status, refusal code and an example of each, how a command id is replayed, the profit version and a plain statement that the tokens are development tokens until the IDP identity platform is connected, is `docs/api/fin-eii.md`.
  - The test tenant. `tools/localpg/localpg.sh` has a fourth instance, `demo` (port 25435). `cmd/cargo-demo` (a thin program over `bootstrap.SeedDemoTenant` in `internal/bootstrap/demo_seed.go`: the dependency check lets entry points reach the contexts only through `internal/bootstrap`) seeds the tenant `eii-test` through the contexts' commands (ORG's roles, RATE's quotes, COM's orders, FUL's shipments, PHY's consolidations, stuffing, rework and transfer, FIN's policy, jobs, lines, pools and apportionments) with every id, amount and time fixed (the times are hours from `-at`), so that a second run sends the same commands and the contexts answer their receipts: a run on the seeded database left the counts of FIN's and PHY's command receipts, of the Outbox, of jobs and of side versions as they were, and printed the same digest of the figures. It seeds one legal entity in CNY, three customers, 30 jobs (19 at a profit, 8 at a loss and 3 at exactly zero), two sea consolidations C-1 and C-2 with a cost pool each, apportioned, and box A: C-1 with H01, H02 and H03, the rework ops-1 asked for, H03 transferred to C-2 by ops-9 and its former share attributed (so JOB-H03 is a loss that bears a re-routing loss and a share of C-2's pool). `tools/demo/eii-test.sh` brings `demo` up, migrates and grants it, seeds, writes a token key if there is none, mints the tokens of `alice` and `fin-reviewer` with `cargo-api -mint-token` and starts `cargo-api -dev-tokens` on 127.0.0.1:8095; the key, the tokens, the seed's start time, the binaries and the server's pid and log are under `/home/tops/idp-cargo-tools/eii-test/`, not in the repository, and the script prints only the paths.
  - The database (`fin/000020_fin_cost_review`). Two tables, immutable and with row security forced, written at READ COMMITTED: `cost_review_request` (the figures, the profit version, the requester; `request_id` is `CRR-<no>`; revenue less cost is the profit and the profit is below 0) and `cost_review_resolution` (the outcome, a note, the resolver; one for a request; the resolution carries the requester and the request's time under a foreign key, so that the resolver is not the requester and the resolution is not before the request). A request takes the tenant's numbering lock and must be the next number, and at commit the tenant's numbers have no gap, the request names its job's shipment, legal entity and currency, and its job has no other request unresolved (checked with the job's row locked). The result types of `command_receipt` gain `CostReviewRequest` and `CostReviewResolution`. What only the domain keeps: that the figures are the job's profit as FIN computes it (the database cannot: it needs PHY's basis and the settings), that the version the requester saw is the current one, that the job was OPEN, and that the actors hold the permissions.
  - After the independent review. A query parameter a route does not define, a malformed `after`, `limit` or `loss_only`, and a control character (a NUL byte, an escape; a line break and a tab only in a reason or a note) in a body field or a path value are `400 MALFORMED_REQUEST` naming the parameter or the field. `ProfitVersion` of a job that bears only a re-routing loss of a pool covers that pool. A resolution stamped before its request (servers' clocks apart) is `FIN_REVIEW_CLOCK_BEHIND`, with the way out (synchronise the clocks, try again). `WithScanWindow` sets the loss-only scan window for a test (2,000 in production). The demo seed writes only tenants that start with `eii-` or `demo-`. `GetJobProfit` with no actor reads without authorization, as FIN's other queries do (no HTTP path reaches it). Tests: `TestARequestForAJobThatBearsPoolCostRecordsItsCostOnPostgres`, `TestTheCommitCheckOfTheNumbersStandsAloneOnPostgres`, `TestTheCommitOfARequestWaitsForTheJobsRowOnPostgres`, `TestAPoolTheJobBearsOnlyALossOfIsInItsProfitVersionOnPostgres`, `TestAResolutionStampedBeforeItsRequestIsRefusedWithTheWayOutOnPostgres`, `TestALossOnlyPageOfAFullScanWindowGivesItsCursorOnPostgres`, `TestTheDemoSeedRefusesATenantThatIsNotATestTenant`, and more cases in `TestTheDatabaseKeepsTheCostReviewRulesOnPostgres` and `TestHTTPFinProfitAndCostReviewOnPostgres`.
  - Not yet: connecting the IDP identity platform (tokens are development tokens, 24 hours at most: the verifier refuses a longer life, so the 30 days the brief asked for are 24 hours and the script mints again when it runs); ORG's parties and legal entity (ORG has no command that makes them: `cargo-demo` writes them with a statement of its own, as the cross-context tests do, until the identity platform is connected); an OpenAPI contract; a page of profits that is one snapshot across FIN and PHY; an optimization of the PHY reads (not needed on the test tenant, not measured beyond it); the authorization of the other FIN queries; a reopening of a resolved request (a new review is a new request).
  - Tests: pure, the domain (`costreview_test.go`): `TestACostReviewRequestRecordsTheFiguresItSaw`, `TestACostReviewRequestRefusals` (a cancelled job, zero and positive profit, a stale version, an open request, an empty and a too long reason), `TestACostReviewIsResolvedOnceByAnotherPerson`; the application (`jobprofit_test.go`): `TestTheProfitVersionIsEqualForEqualSourcesAndFiguresAndChangesWithAny`. On PG16 (`cost_review_postgres_test.go`): `TestJobProfitsArePagedAndCarryTheProfitVersionOnPostgres`, `TestTheProfitVersionChangesWithItsSourcesOnPostgres`, `TestAJobAtZeroIsNotALossOnPostgres`, `TestJobProfitsAreInJobIdOrderAndTheirCostIncludesThePoolOnPostgres`, `TestACostReviewIsRequestedAndResolvedOnPostgres`, `TestTheCostReviewAndTheProfitNeedTheirPermissionsOnPostgres`; the deterministic waits, with the job's lock held on a second connection and `pg_blocking_pids` (`TestARequestWaitsForAJobChangeAndIsRefusedAsStaleOnPostgres`: a request queued behind a revision of a cost is refused as stale; `TestTwoIdenticalRequestsAtOnceAnswerOneReceiptOnPostgres`; `TestTwoDifferentRequestsAtOnceOneWinsAndTheOtherIsRefusedNamingItOnPostgres`; `TestTwoResolutionsAtOnceOneWinsOnPostgres`), the settings lock (`TestARequestWaitsForTheSettingsLockAndReadsTheSettingsVersionOnPostgres`) and the numbering lock (`TestARequestWaitsForTheNumberingLockOnPostgres`), `TestRequestsOfDifferentJobsAtOnceAreNumberedWithoutAGapOnPostgres`, and the rules past FIN on the application role (`TestTheDatabaseKeepsTheCostReviewRulesOnPostgres`). Through the API (`api_fin_integration_test.go`): `TestHTTPFinProfitAndCostReviewOnPostgres`: tokens and permissions, the losses and the profit with its version, the request and its replay, the key with another body, a tenant, an actor or a requester in the body, a stale version, an open request, a job that is not a loss, the resolution by another person, by the requester and twice, the read back, tenant isolation, and the review changing no profit version. The end-to-end check over HTTP against the running API with the two tokens of the script was run and its output kept (`/home/tops/idp-cargo-tools/v299/e2e-output.txt`).
- V2.100 shows the impact of a settings change before it takes effect, puts the cost variance threshold (E1) under approval, lists E1 to E4 in the catalogue, and makes a cancelled wait in PHY answer the cancellation (batch 1 of the step); SEA's settings and S1 follow. Migration fin/000021.
  - The impact. `GetFinSettingsChangeImpact` takes the `ChangeID` of a change set that is proposed or approved and not in force yet, or `Entries` as a proposal would state them (checked as a proposal is, nothing is proposed; both or neither is refused with the way out). The change is put into the ledger as if approved and in force now, each rule the settings steer is asked under the settings in force and under those the change would make, through the function a command asks it by, and what answers differently is told. It changes nothing and takes no lock: FIN's rows are read in one read-only snapshot; PHY's basis of each open pool of a legal entity that changes is read just before it, and that of the consolidations the jobs have a share in just after (T3), so a pool opened between the two is listed as `NotAssessed`. The answer holds, for each legal entity whose resolved settings differ, the items that change from what to what (with the thresholds in the functional currency); the unsubmitted previews of the tenant's open pools, all of which become stale, because the settings version is the tenant's; F7 the pools whose close would become refused or allowed, with the refusal; F6 the jobs whose profit would change, now and after, with the basis of each; F8 the lines whose pending former shares would be defaulted or would stop being; F9 the lines whose carried attributions would be kept, rescaled (with the amounts now and after) or have to be stated again, once the line's cost has changed; F4 the pools that would become able or unable to be apportioned in one step; F1, F2 and F3 the unsubmitted previews holding a new or changed attribution (or a line a rework that ended is not carried for) whose submission would be checked differently, with the rule and how it is checked now and after, and no person named as the submitter. Every kind of impact but the stale previews is the changed legal entities' own: a change for one legal entity reports only that legal entity's pools, jobs and previews' checks. The stale previews are the tenant's unsubmitted previews of open pools whichever legal entity the change is for, because the settings version is the tenant's and every change set makes a version (a change for LE2 makes a preview of LE's pool stale).
