The domain answers through `CloseRefusal`, `OneStepRefusal`, `LineOutcomes` and `PreviewChecks`, which `CloseUnder`, `ApportionInOneStep`, the preview's `attribute` and the submission's checks also ask by (`closeRefusal` and `oneStepRefusal` were taken out of `CloseUnder` and `ApportionInOneStep`, unchanged). It needs ReadProfit, ConfigureSettings or ApproveSettings, any one of them, since whoever proposes or approves a change has to see what it does before deciding; the job profits (F6) are given only to a holder of ReadProfit, and a holder of only ConfigureSettings or ApproveSettings is told how many jobs' profit would change (`JobsChanged`), without amounts. Not told: who will submit (F1 and F2 are told as a rule, not as a person); a pool that is closed (it takes no apportionment until reopened).
  - E1, the cost variance threshold, under approval. A version is stamped with FIN's clock, read under the policy's lock, never with the command's business time (`OccurredAt` stays on the command as its business time, as on every record, and the existing rules on it are kept: a version is not set before the latest one's or before an invoice matched). It is looser than the version in force at that clock when its amount or its rate is higher; the first version is always looser. A version that is not looser is in force at once and recorded as approved by its setter, automatic (as an automatic settings approval is); a looser one is proposed and in force only from the time of its approval, by another person who holds `ApproveSettings` (named only). `ApproveCostVarianceThreshold` and `RefuseCostVarianceThreshold` (with a reason) name the version that waits and the policy's revision, are idempotent by command id with receipts, and have the events `CostVarianceThresholdApproved` and `CostVarianceThresholdRefused` (a version approved at once has `CostVarianceThresholdSet` and `CostVarianceThresholdApproved`). The setter neither approves nor refuses (`FIN_THRESHOLD_APPROVAL`: have another approver do it); while a version waits another set is refused (`FIN_THRESHOLD_PROPOSED`: wait, or have an approver refuse it). The final match reads the threshold in force at FIN's clock after it holds the policy `FOR SHARE`, the lock an approval takes exclusively, so that an approval at the same moment is either in force for the match or not, records that version (`ThresholdVersion`, as before) and stamps `match_entered_at` with that clock; a match decided before an approval keeps its threshold. `GetFinancePolicy` shows each version with who set it and when (`ProposedAt`, FIN's clock), whether it is looser, its status, who decided it and when, and from when it is in force, and which version is in force and which waits.
  - The database (`fin/000021_fin_threshold_approval`, SHA-256 `12757e1c11c1132f78a8799084587120119ac1112d28c1f3fda04f35f323b6d8`). Two columns on `cost_variance_threshold` (`proposed_at`, `looser`, NULL for a version written before) and a table `cost_variance_threshold_decision` (an approval or a refusal, one for a version, immutable, row security forced, written at READ COMMITTED). The decision is a row, not columns of the version, because the application role has no UPDATE on the version (the grants are the workflow's); the brief said columns, and this is the one difference. Each version written before gets a decision row, approved by its setter at its `set_at`. What the database refuses: a version with no proposal time or a looser flag that is not its own answer; a version not looser without its automatic approval; a second version while an earlier has no decision; a decision of a version written before; a looser version approved by its setter, and a refusal by the setter, and an automatic approval of a looser version or by another person; a decision before the proposal; times not within a minute of the database's clock or before the latest recorded for the legal entity (as in fin/000017); a decision not written with its policy; and an invoice that names another threshold than the latest one approved and in force at its `match_entered_at`; and a match time (`match_entered_at`) that is not within a minute of the database's clock or is earlier than the latest in-force time of an approved version of the legal entity's policy written since the migration (a match holds the policy `FOR SHARE` and a decision takes it exclusively, so a match that read its clock after its lock is never earlier than a decision that committed before it; reasoned and tested with a raw write). Only the domain keeps that the approver holds `ApproveSettings` (the database compares two strings), that the time is the clock read under the policy's lock, that the approval names the version that waits, and that a match read its clock after its lock (the database cannot tell the time FIN read from another within the minute that lies after the latest approval).
  - The catalogue. `GetFinSettingsCatalogue` also lists E1 to E4 (`Reference`), in the design's order: E1 the cost variance threshold, E2 the tax treatment of charge codes, E3 the exchange rates, E4 the invoice number series; name, what each governs, the commands that change it and whether a change needs approval (E1's loosening; E2 to E4 none, since rates, tax treatments and series are facts with their own history); the table the mechanism generates (`Catalogue.Table`) includes them.
  - PHY's cancelled wait. When an attempt fails and the context has ended, `retrying` answers the context's error (`context.Canceled` or `context.DeadlineExceeded`), not the driver's network error that a cancellation reaching the driver while it writes gives; `TestACancelledOrExpiredContextEndsTheWait` now cancels only after the first attempt's transaction has ended, and `TestAFailedAttemptAnswersTheContextsErrorWhenItEnded` covers the case deterministically.
  - Existing tests changed: `TestVarianceThreshold` (domain) gains the approval of version 1 by another person; `creditorWorld` and `TestReviewFindingsOnPostgres` gain the approval of the threshold they set (ctrl-1); the migration list of `cmd/cargo-migrate`.
  - Tests: pure, the domain (`threshold_approval_test.go`): `TestTheFirstThresholdIsLooserAndWaitsForAnotherPerson`, `TestAThresholdIsLooserByAmountOrByRateOnly`, `TestASecondThresholdWaitsForTheFirstToBeDecided`, `TestAMatchReadsTheThresholdInForceAtItsSystemTime`; on PostgreSQL (`threshold_approval_postgres_test.go`, `settings_impact_postgres_test.go`): `TestAThresholdIsLooserOrNotOnPostgres`, `TestAThresholdApprovalAsksForApproveSettingsOnPostgres`, `TestAMatchBeforeAndAfterAThresholdApprovalOnPostgres`, `TestAMatchRacesAThresholdApprovalOnPostgres` (the policy's lock held by the test, the two commands queued behind it in each order, `pg_blocking_pids`), `TestAThresholdPastFINOnPostgres`, `TestTheThresholdDecisionTableHasForcedRowSecurity`, and for the impact `TestTheImpactListsTheItemsAndTheStalePreviewsOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactOfF7OnTheCloseOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactOfF6OnTheJobProfitOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactOfF8OnTheDefaultOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactOfF9OnTheCarriedAttributionsOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactOfF4OnTheOneStepOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactOnTheCheckOfAPreviewOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactChangesNothingOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactOfAChangeSetOnPostgres`, `TestTheImpactIsToldToThreePermissionsOnPostgres`, `TestTheCatalogueListsTheFinancePolicyOnPostgres`; PHY: `TestAFailedAttemptAnswersTheContextsErrorWhenItEnded`.
- V2.100 (batch 2) gives SEA settings of its own and reads S1 at the release of a carrier conflict: under SUPERVISOR_ONLY only a supervisor may release it. Migration sea/000012.
  - The settings. SEA has its own catalogue `sea` (`sea/internal/application/settings_catalogue.go`) over the shared ledger (`internal/foundation/settings`), with one item, S1 "a carrier report that contradicts SEA's records" (承运人报告与系统记录矛盾时), an internal control set at the company and at a legal entity. Its values, strict to loose: `SUPERVISOR_ONLY` (blocked; only a supervisor may release it) and `WITH_REASON` (blocked; released with a reason), the standard profile's value and today's behaviour, so that with no settings set SEA behaves as in V2.99. The ledger's rules are FIN's (V2.96): a change set takes effect at SEA's clock, at most 366 days ahead, in the order of its version; a loosening (SUPERVISOR_ONLY to WITH_REASON, or a legal entity looser than the company) waits for another person; a proposal that only tightens or keeps is approved by its proposer; a configurer or an approver cancels a change set not in force and an approver refuses one waiting; a legal entity looser than the company is marked as an approved exception, and the receipt of a proposal that leaves one says so. The commands are `ProposeSeaSettingsChange`, `ApproveSeaSettingsChange`, `RefuseSeaSettingsChange` and `CancelSeaSettingsChange` (idempotent, each with its receipt and its event `SeaSettingsChange{Proposed,Approved,Refused,Cancelled}.v1`, whose payload names the change set and, for an approval, the version, and nothing else); the queries are `GetSeaSettings` (a scope at a time), `ListSeaSettingsChanges` and `GetSeaSettingsCatalogue`. A legal entity an entry names must be one SEA knows (it has an HBL series or a movement; the refusal names `RegisterHblSeries`). The permissions `sea:ConfigureSettings` (propose, and cancel), `sea:ApproveSettings` (approve, refuse, and cancel) and `sea:SuperviseCarrierConflicts` are granted by name only (`bootstrap.permissions.AuthorizeSeaAction`): `sea:*` and `*` grant none of them. The ledger names FIN's commands in a few ways out (`CancelFinSettingsChange`); SEA says its own refusals in SEA's words (`seaWords` in `settings.go`), because this batch changes only SEA and the shared package is not SEA's.
  - The read. `AcceptCarrierLoadingConflict` reads S1 for the movement's legal entity inside its transaction, last, after the command id, the visit and the movement are locked: it takes the tenant's settings lock in shared mode, reads SEA's clock after that lock, and reads the ledger as of that time (`Deps.DecisionSettings`, FIN's `DecisionSettings`). The release is entered at that time (`carrier_conflict_release.entered_at`), so that the version it records is the version the ledger says was in force at the time recorded: a settings command stamps its time under the exclusive lock, so the release is stamped before it when it was granted the lock first and after it when it waited behind it. It records the settings version and the value of S1. Under `WITH_REASON` nothing more is asked than before. Under `SUPERVISOR_ONLY` the release also needs `sea:SuperviseCarrierConflicts`, asked of the authorizer in the transaction; without it the release is refused (`ErrUnauthorized`, `domain.ErrNotSupervisor`) with the way out, that a holder of `SuperviseCarrierConflicts` (and `AcceptCarrierLoadingConflict`) releases it, and nothing is written. The pending item of an open conflict says who may release it under the value in force (`GetPendingItems` reads S1 and `Movement.ReadUnder` passes it on), and says both when it is read without the settings, as the final declaration reads it; every wording keeps the V2.89 sentence. A release made before a change of S1 keeps its validity: a decision made is not reopened, its conflict stays released and its command id still answers its receipt. A change set that takes effect in the future does not apply before. The final declaration (`FinalizeOceanDocFacts`) is held back by an unreleased conflict under both values and reads no settings, so it records no version: this was read in the code (nothing in its path touches `Deps.Settings`) and run (`TestS1TheFinalDeclarationReadsNoSettings` finalizes while the test holds the settings lock, which a command that took the lock would wait for).
  - The impact. `GetSeaSettingsChangeImpact` takes a `ChangeID` of a change set not yet in force (proposed and waiting, or approved with a time to come) or `Entries` (checked as a proposal is, nothing written), one of the two, and answers, in one snapshot and writing nothing, when the change would take effect (its effective time, or now for one waiting for approval) and, for each legal entity SEA knows whose S1 would then differ from what it would be without the change set, from what to what, with the unreleased conflicts of the legal entity's open movements (`Movement.OpenConflicts`, the conflicts a release would clear): the movement, the visit, the container, the report, and who could release it before (`ANY_OPERATOR` or `SUPERVISOR`) and after. A change set in force, cancelled or refused has no impact to come and the refusal says so and names the way out.
  - The database (`sea/000012_sea_settings`). `cargo_sea.settings_change`, `settings_change_entry`, `settings_approval` and `settings_cancellation` (immutable, row security forced, written at READ COMMITTED), with fin/000017's rules over SEA's one item: the database recomputes whether a change set loosens from the order of the values (`settings_rank`, `settings_default_rank`) and holds the stored flag to its answer, refuses an automatic approval of a loosening and an approval by the proposer, numbers the approvals from 1 without a gap, keeps recorded times within a minute of its clock and not before the latest recorded for the tenant, records the canceller's role and lets only an approver refuse, and takes the tenant's lock on every insert. `command_receipt` gains the result type `SeaSettings`, `result_settings_version` and `result_notice`; `carrier_conflict_release` gains `settings_version` and `s1_value` (both or neither, so a release of V2.99 has neither). What only the domain keeps: that the version and the value a release records are the ones in force when it is entered, that a release under SUPERVISOR_ONLY was made by a supervisor, that a legal entity is one SEA knows, that change sets take effect in the order of their versions, that nothing approved alone comes to loosen, and that the approver holds the permission. SHA-256 `d9d9e8430cdb292c3d6a77e76ab2d4bab1660d9d797ae59833cbdbedf3a116ae`.
  - What FIN's settings code could share later. The store (`fin/.../settings_store.go` and SEA's copy differ in the schema, the lock key and the parameter), the commands' skeleton (propose, approve, cancel, refuse; the notice of exceptions) and the decision read are the same code over another schema; the shared ledger's ways out name FIN's commands. A shared settings store parameterised by the schema and the lock key, and ways out that name the context's own commands, would remove both copies. Not done here: this batch changes only SEA, and `internal/contexts/fin/` is batch 1's.
  - Not yet: the settings are not on SEA's HTTP routes (FIN's are not either); the exception report and the bulk export of a configuration (the last step of the design).
  - Tests: pure, the domain (`settings_rules_test.go`): `TestSupervisorOnlyNeedsASupervisorToReleaseAConflict`, `TestWithReasonNeedsNoSupervisorToReleaseAConflict`, `TestAReleaseWithoutTheSettingsIsAsItWas`, `TestAReleaseKeepsTheTimeTheSettingsWereReadAt`, `TestRestoreRefusesAReleaseOfAnUnknownS1OrANegativeVersion`, `TestTheConflictItemSaysWhoMayReleaseIt`, `TestOpenConflictsAreThoseAReleaseWouldClear`; the application (`settings_catalogue_test.go`): `TestSeaSettingsCatalogue`, `TestSeaHierarchy`, `TestSeaSettingsEvents`, `TestSeaLedgerRefusesWhatTheCatalogueDoesNotHave`, and `settings_decision_test.go`: `TestADecisionReadsTheSettingsUnderTheLockAndAtTheTimeAfterIt`; the module (`settings_test.go`): `TestTheSettingsNamesAreThePublishedOnes`, `TestWhoReleasesAConflictUnderEachValueOfS1`; the platform's permissions (`permissions_sea_test.go`): `TestSeaSettingsPermissionsAreGrantedByNameOnly`. On PG16, the mechanism through SEA's commands (`sea_settings_test.go`): `TestSeaSettingsProposeApproveRefuseCancelOnPostgres` (a loosening approved by another person, refusals and cancellations with their roles, the history, the settings as of a time), `TestSeaSettingsReplaysAndEventsOnPostgres`, `TestSeaSettingsTwoIdenticalCommandsAtOnce`, `TestSeaSettingsTwoApprovalsOfOneChangeSet`, `TestSeaSettingsApprovalRacesCancellation`, `TestSeaSettingsProposalsWaitForTheSettingsLock`, `TestSeaSettingsLockIsTheTenants`, each wait shown by `pg_blocking_pids` from the test's own lock holder; the database's rules past SEA on the application role (`sea_settings_rules_test.go`): `TestSeaSettingsDatabaseRulesRefuseRawWrites`, `TestSeaSettingsDatabaseKnowsEveryValueOfTheCatalogue`, `TestSeaSettingsDatabaseOrdersAnApprovalAndACancellation`, `TestSeaSettingsDatabaseRulesOfTheReleaseAndTheReceipt`; S1 (`sea_s1_test.go`): `TestS1EachValueOnTheRelease`, `TestS1WithReasonIsTheBehaviourOfV299`, `TestS1IsReadForTheLegalEntityOfTheMovement`, `TestS1AChangeInTheFutureDoesNotApplyBefore`, `TestS1AReleaseBeforeAChangeKeepsItsValidity`, `TestS1TheReleaseWaitsForASettingsChangeHoldingTheLock` (a release queued behind a settings change reads the new version, and one granted the lock first reads the old one and the change waits for it), `TestS1TheFinalDeclarationReadsNoSettings`, `TestS1AReleaseWithNothingToReleaseIsRefusedAsBefore`, `TestSeaSettingsChangeImpactOnPostgres`, `TestSeaSettingsImpactIsAgainstWhatWillBeInForceThen`, `TestSeaSettingsADatabaseProposalWaitsForTheSettingsLock`. Across the contexts (`internal/bootstrap/sea_settings_integration_test.go`, the real contexts and the platform's permissions, the conflict of the V2.89 K1 test): `TestAConflictIsReleasedUnderTheStandardValueOfS1`, `TestAConflictIsReleasedUnderSupervisorOnlyByASupervisor`, `TestALooseningOfS1IsApprovedByAnotherPersonAndTheOperatorReleases`, `TestSeaSettingsRefuseALegalEntityOfNoMovementAndNoSeries`. `tools/localpg/localpg.sh` has a fifth instance, `dev2` (port 25436), a second developer's integration runs.
- V2.100 (batch 3) corrects what the independent review found, on the joined branch. Migrations fin/000021 and sea/000012 are changed, not merged yet.
  - The database bounds a match's time (R1): see the database paragraph of the first V2.100 bullet. `TestARawMatchCannotChooseItsOwnTimeOnPostgres` (the review's write, dating a match between versions 2 and 3 and naming version 2, is refused; a time ahead of and behind the database's clock too).
  - E2 is the tax treatment and E3 the exchange rates (R2), as the design has them; E4 the series.
  - An approval's in-force time is read under the policy's lock (R3): `TestAnApprovalIsInForceFromTheClockReadUnderTheLockOnPostgres` (a match granted the lock first, the approval queued behind it for 300 ms; the approval is in force from a time after the release of the lock, not from the time it was asked).
  - A decision is never timed before the latest record it depends on (R4). FIN's `DecisionSettings` and SEA's take the later of the clock read after the shared lock and the latest time the tenant's ledger records (`Ledger.Latest`: a proposal, an approval or a cancellation), read the settings at that time and stamp the records with it; a query still reads at its own clock. A final match is read at the later of the clock after the policy's lock and the latest in-force time of an approved version (`matchTime`). `TestADecisionOnALaggingClockReadsTheLatestCommittedChange` (FIN and SEA), `TestAMatchIsNotReadBeforeTheLatestApproval`, `TestAThresholdOnAClockBehindARecordOnPostgres`. One existing test changed: SEA's `TestADecisionReadsTheSettingsUnderTheLockAndAtTheTimeAfterIt` asked for the standard value on a clock four hours behind recorded change sets, which is what R4 reverses; it asks on an empty ledger now.
  - A proposal's notice always fits the receipt (R5): the legal entities that stay looser are listed as long as 3900 characters allow, then "and N more legal entities; see GetFinSettings" (GetSeaSettings in SEA). `TestTheNoticeOfAnExceptionListFitsTheReceipt` (FIN and SEA, 40 legal entities) and `TestTheNoticeOfAProposalFitsTheReceiptOnPostgres` (SEA, through the receipt). FIN's is tested without the database, since the test world verifies two legal entities only.
  - Kept as built, and said (R7). While a looser threshold version waits, its setter cannot withdraw it and every other set, a lowering too, is refused (`FIN_THRESHOLD_PROPOSED`): the way out is an approver other than the setter refusing it. E1 is set with the permission `fin:SetCostVarianceThreshold`, which `fin:*` grants, not `fin:ConfigureSettings`; only the approval and the refusal need the named `fin:ApproveSettings`. Actor ids are compared as given: that the setter and the approver are two people relies on the identity platform giving canonical ids ("CTRL-1" and "ctrl-1" are two). PHY's fix is tested with a simulated driver error, and the integration test avoids the race instead of proving the fix.
  - F6's profit figures in the impact need ReadProfit (R8): `TestTheImpactGivesProfitFiguresOnlyToAHolderOfReadProfitOnPostgres`.
  - Test gaps closed (R10): the clock-behind refusal says the cause and the way out ("synchronise the clocks of FIN's servers"); `TestARawFirstVersionStoredAsNotLooserIsRefusedOnPostgres`; `TestAPoolOpenedBetweenPHYAndTheSnapshotIsNotAssessedOnPostgres` (a hook in the test's own stand-in for PHY opens a pool after PHY's answer, no production hook); `TestTheImpactOfAChangeOfF1sThresholdAloneOnPostgres`.
- V2.100 (batch 4) closes the second review's findings.
  - A threshold set and an approval are stamped at the later of FIN's clock after the policy's lock and the system time of the legal entity's latest match (`recordTime`, `CreditorStore.LatestMatchEntered`), so that a lagging server does not put a version in force before a match that has committed: `TestALoweringOnALaggingServerIsNotDatedBeforeAMatchOnPostgres`, `TestAnApprovalOnALaggingServerIsNotDatedBeforeAMatchOnPostgres`. A match is read at the later of the clock and the latest approval, as in batch 3.
  - A time that the database refuses (`cost_variance_time_window`, `cost_variance_time_monotone`, `cost_variance_decision_after_proposal`, `creditor_match_time_window`, `creditor_match_time_monotone`) reaches the caller as `SETTINGS_CLOCK_SKEW`, a refusal that gives the cause and the way out ("synchronise the clocks of FIN's servers and the database's"), as the settings' own window already did (`postgres.ClockSkew`, in `classify`): `TestAServerFiveMinutesOffIsToldToSynchroniseItsClockOnPostgres` (an approval, a setting and a match five minutes ahead; an approval and a setting five minutes behind; a match five minutes behind is read at the latest approval and made). SEA's settings commands already answer the window as a refusal (`clockSkew` in its settings store); SEA's clock cannot be moved in a test, so that is not tested here.
  - `LatestApprovalAt` counts only approved versions written since V2.100: `TestTheLatestApprovalCountsOnlyApprovedVersionsOfThisKind`.
  - The SHA-256 of the two migrations are those in the first and the second V2.100 bullets above (`fin/000021`: `12757e1c11c1132f78a8799084587120119ac1112d28c1f3fda04f35f323b6d8`; `sea/000012`: `d9d9e8430cdb292c3d6a77e76ab2d4bab1660d9d797ae59833cbdbedf3a116ae`).
- V2.101 adds FIN's MCP server behind the IDP TELOS gateway: three read-only tools, the caller known only from the gateway's signed assertion. No migration.
  - The server. `cmd/cargo-mcp` (a thin program over `bootstrap.Platform.MCP`; `-config` names a JSON file, `IDP_CARGO_POSTGRES_DSN` the database, `-addr` defaults to 127.0.0.1:8600) serves `POST /mcp` only, with the official Go SDK `github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk` v1.7.0 in its stateless Streamable HTTP form (the only one that speaks 2026-07-28), JSON responses, a body of 1 MiB at most. The platform's guide for upstream servers (`idp-mcp/docs/guides/upstream-mcp-server.md`, with the answers of its section 9) is what it follows; `docs/api/fin-mcp.md` is what the platform's operators register it with.
  - The caller. `internal/transport/mcp` holds the verifier of the gateway's assertion (ES256, `typ` `idp-caller+jwt`, a compact JWS of 8,192 bytes at most) as the guide's section 3.3 says, in a middleware over the raw JSON-RPC body before the SDK reads it: the key by `kid` from one or more PKIX P-256 public keys, `aud` equal to the registered endpoint, the times (30 seconds of skew, a life of 5 minutes at most), `sub` and `jti`, the digest `req` of the method and the params without `_meta` (idp-mcp's canonical JSON), and the id seen once. The issuer is checked too (`idp-mcp-gateway:<gateway id>`), after the other rules and before the id is recorded. `tools/list` and `server/discover` need no assertion and every other method does; a refusal is `403` with one fixed body, its reason only in the log; a batch, a body in which an object repeats a key at any depth (the digest covers one value of each key, and the SDK could take the other) or that spells `method` or `params` in another case (so that the middleware and the SDK could read two methods), anything but POST and a body over 1 MiB are refused before. There is no switch that turns the check off, the server does not start without a valid public key, and it logs the `kid` of each key it holds. The plain `io.idp.telos/caller` is never read. The caller goes to the tool on the request's context, which the SDK carries into the tool (a test shows it, as the guide asked) with the body the middleware gave back (a test shows that too).
  - The tools (`internal/contexts/fin/internal/adapters/mcp`, `Module.RegisterMCP`). `list_loss_jobs` (a legal entity; `limit` 1 to 50, default 20; a cursor) reads FIN's `ListJobProfits` with `LossOnly`, continuing while `next_after` is not empty, at most 5 FIN pages a call (`truncated` when that stopped it), at most 200 rows along a chain of cursors (`row_cap_reached`, and no further cursor); `get_job_profit` (a job) reads `GetJobProfit`; `list_job_cost_reviews` (a job, an optional `state`, `limit`, a cursor) reads `ListCostReviewRequests`, with the same 200 rows. Closed input schemas with every field typed and bounded, no tenant or person field in an input or a result (the requester and the resolver of a request are not told), `readOnlyHint` true and `openWorldHint` false, an `outputSchema`, a short text and `structuredContent`, amounts as the integer `minor` and `currency` FIN gave, `profit_version` as it is, FIN's `as_of` and the profit basis. The results are written with their integers as they are: the SDK would validate a result through a float64 and round an integer above 2^53, so the tools are declared with their schemas and write their own structured content, and a test checks the two against each other. A unit test pins the digest of the catalogue (idp-mcp's canonical digest of the `tools` of `tools/list`): the gateway blocks a target whose catalogue drifted from the approved one, so a change of a declaration fails the test as a reminder.
  - The cursor is opaque: FIN's next position, the rows delivered, the legal entity (or the job and the state) and an expiry of 15 minutes, with an HMAC (key of 32 bytes or more, from a file) over them, the tool, the CargoPai tenant and the subject. Another member's cursor, an expired, a changed one and one for another request are refused with the way out, to start again without a cursor.
  - The principal and the policy. The configuration maps each gateway tenant to the CargoPai tenant and each role to FIN permissions (only `fin:ReadProfit`; anything else stops the server from starting) and to the legal entities it sees (a list, or `*`). The tool puts an `identity.Principal` (the CargoPai tenant, the assertion's `sub` as the actor, the union of the roles' permissions) on the context and calls FIN in process; FIN authorizes as it always does, so FIN sees the member, never a service account. Default deny: a tenant or a role the configuration does not name, and a legal entity not allowed, are `you are not allowed to do this in FIN`; a job of a legal entity the member may not see is `no such job or request`, the same as a job that is not there, and the job's legal entity is read before anything else about it. FIN gained one small query for that, `GetJobLegalEntity` (authorized by `ReadProfit` like the other reads); no existing behaviour changed.
  - Errors, limits and audit. Every error is a fixed sentence (not found, not allowed, invalid input, `FIN is not available now; try again later` for a timeout, a cancelled call, a connection or a commit not confirmed, `the FIN tool failed to complete the request` with a reference id that is also in the log with the detail); no FIN text, statement, stack or address reaches a result, and the SDK's own wording of a refused argument goes to the log. The guard decides a refusal (no caller, too many calls, a policy that names nothing) and the tool returns it, so that the SDK writes every result with the result type of the protocol, and a caller who is refused reads the refusal whatever its arguments were. A call has 20 seconds, FIN's queries included. Each member may make 60 calls a minute and 4 at once (configurable, in memory), over which the answer is `too many requests; try again in a minute`. One structured log line a call: the tool, the outcome, the time and a short hash of the caller (the first 4 bytes of the SHA-256 of the gateway tenant and the subject); no argument and no amount.
  - The dependency. `github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk v1.7.0`, the version the guide checked, and what it needs to build: `github.com/google/jsonschema-go v0.4.3` (a direct requirement of Cargo's, because FIN's adapter derives the results' schemas with it, at the version the SDK needs), `github.com/segmentio/encoding v0.5.4`, `github.com/segmentio/asm v1.1.3`, `github.com/yosida95/uritemplate/v3 v3.0.2`, `golang.org/x/oauth2 v0.35.0`, `golang.org/x/time v0.15.0` and `golang.org/x/sys v0.41.0` (`go.mod`, written by `go mod tidy`); `go.sum` also holds the hashes of `github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5 v5.3.1` and `golang.org/x/tools v0.48.0`, which the SDK's module names and no package of this build imports. Nothing already in `go.mod` changed its version.
  - Running it. `tools/demo/fin-mcp.sh` makes a local gateway key pair and a cursor key under `/home/tops/idp-cargo-tools/fin-mcp/` (mode 600; for local testing only), writes a configuration that maps the gateway tenant `eii-test` to the CargoPai tenant `eii-test` and the role `finance-analyst` to `fin:ReadProfit` for the legal entity `LE`, and starts `cargo-mcp` on `127.0.0.1:8600/mcp` against the `demo` database, which it only reads; `tools/demo/fin-mcp.sh check` (`tools/demo/fin-mcp-check`) calls it over HTTP with assertions signed by the local key: the catalogue without an assertion, each tool, and the refused calls. `FIN_MCP_INSTANCE=dev2` names another localpg instance to read in place of `demo` (seeded with `cmd/cargo-demo`). `internal/transport/mcp/mcptest` signs assertions for tests and for that check.
  - Tests. The platform's vectors (`internal/transport/mcp/testdata/caller-assertion-v1.json`, a byte for byte copy of `idp-contracts/vectors/caller-assertion/v1.json`): every case through the real middleware with the keys, audience and time of the file and the gateway id `g1`, a fresh verifier for each case (`TestEveryCaseOfTheCallerAssertionVectorsComesOutAsTheFileSays`; every accepted case is issued by `idp-mcp-gateway:g1`, and a verifier of another gateway id refuses them all: `TestAnotherGatewayIdRefusesEveryAcceptedVector`); the pinned single vector of idp-sandbox for the digest and its invariance to the spelling. The guide's negative tests of 7.3 and the issuer against the real handler (`TestRefusedCallsAnswer403AndRunNothing`, `TestABodyThatCouldBeReadTwoWaysIsRefused` and `TestSplitRequestTakesOneCallAndNothingElse`, `TestTheVerifierNeedsAValidP256PublicKey`), the SDK's two behaviours (`TestTheCallerOnTheRequestContextReachesTheToolAndTheBodyGivenBackIsRead`). The tools with a fake FIN: the pinned catalogue, annotations and closed schemas, no tenant or person field, descriptions and titles of 1,024 bytes at most without a control or invisible character, each input refused (missing, extra including `tenant_id` and `actor_id`, a wrong type, out of range, a control character), every kind of error and an unknown one, default deny and the legal entity scope, the principal FIN sees, paging (an empty page with a `next_after`, the 5-page stop, the 200-row cap, cursors of another member, expired, changed, for another request), amounts above 2^53, the rate and concurrency limits, the audit line, a panic, the timeout, and that every result, a refusal too, has the result type of the protocol. On PG16 (`TestMCPFinToolsOnPostgres`): each tool end to end with the real FIN and assertions signed by a key pair the test makes, tenant A not seen by tenant B, a legal entity not allowed, a hidden job told as a missing one, and that FIN's authorizer was asked about the member of the assertion and no one else; and `GetJobLegalEntity` for a job, an unknown job, another tenant's job and each way to be refused.
  - After the independent review. The body is read a second time with the SDK's own JSON library (`github.com/segmentio/encoding/json`) after the assertion is verified, and refused (400) when the method, the tool's name or its arguments differ from what was verified; the public-key file is read strictly (nothing but blanks outside the blocks, a truncated block refused); the catalogue is pinned in the gateway's own form too (`release.Catalog.Digest`, to compare with `snapshotDigest`); the audit line says `hidden` for a job of a legal entity the caller may not see (the answer stays the same bytes as for a missing one) and names the cause of a refused subject; an audience with credentials, a fragment, a query or an empty host is refused at start; `docs/api/fin-mcp.md` says what the listen address must be.
  - Not yet: a write tool (a cost review request stays in FIN's HTTP API until the platform decides how EII asks for it); registration with idp-mcp (the stack was not run: see `docs/api/fin-mcp.md` for what to register); the result sensitivity `confidential` (idp-mcp D54 and idp-host D64 are not implemented, and the owner confirms it); limits and the table of assertion ids shared between copies of the server.
- V2.102 (batch A) adds the profiles, onboarding, a single administrator, the exception report, export and import of the settings, and the catalogue document. Migrations org/000012, fin/000022, sea/000013.
  - Profiles (`internal/bootstrap/profiles.go`). STANDARD, STRICT and LEAN are data, each listing the value of every item of FIN (F1, F2, F3, F4, F6, F7, F8, F9) and SEA (S1): STANDARD is every default; STRICT is F2 `SUBMITTER_AND_PREVIEWER`, F7 `BLOCKS`, F9 `RECONFIRM`, S1 `SUPERVISOR_ONLY`; LEAN is F1 `UNRESTRICTED`, F3 `ONLY_WHEN_AMOUNT_MOVES`, F4 `ONE_STEP_WITHOUT_OPENED_OR_LEFT`, F8 `DEFAULT_TO_REMAINING`. They live in the bootstrap, which sees both catalogues. `SettingsProfiles()` and `SettingsProfile(id)` answer them (copies). `fin.CheckSettingsEntries` and `sea.CheckSettingsEntries` run a module's whole-configuration validation on entries without writing; a test checks each profile passes both, and lists exactly the catalogues' items, so an item added to a catalogue fails it until the profiles say what they make of it.
  - Onboarding. ORG holds the record (`api/onboarding.go`: `ProposeOnboarding`, `ConfirmOnboarding`, `RecordOnboardingModule`, `GetOnboarding`, `ListOnboardings`); the bootstrap applies it (`internal/bootstrap/onboarding.go`, `Onboarding.ProposeOnboarding`, `ConfirmOnboarding` and, since batch F, `WithdrawOnboarding`). The consultant needs `org:ProposeOnboarding` and the owner `org:ConfirmOnboarding`, both by name only, which the bootstrap checks before it calls ORG: ORG's own `ProposeOnboarding` and `ConfirmOnboarding` run only under the platform's mark (batch E), so the only way in is the bootstrap, which checks the plan and the consultant; because the modules' own commands are called in their own identity (batch F), the consultant must also hold `fin:ConfigureSettings` and `sea:ConfigureSettings` and the owner `fin:ApproveSettings` and `sea:ApproveSettings` (by name), which the bootstrap checks before anything is recorded. A proposal is checked with FIN's and SEA's own rules when it is made. The confirmer is not the proposer and acknowledges the risk statement exactly as proposed; the text is kept as acknowledged. When the consultant proposes, the bootstrap proposes, for FIN and then SEA, the entries the proposal stored (the profile merged with the overrides, resolved when the consultant proposed and not read again at the confirmation: batch D) with the module's own propose command and the consultant's own context, held for the onboarding (batch F: not in force, and not approved even when it only tightens). When the owner confirms, it declares a single administrator when the onboarding says so (the platform's own act, made as the owner) and approves each held change set with the module's own approve command and the owner's own context, who is not the consultant, so the usual rule that the approver is not the proposer holds. No principal is made to act in a person's name any more. The change set is `onb-<onboarding>-fin` or `-sea` and each step's command id is derived from it, so a retry of the same `ProposeOnboarding` or `ConfirmOnboarding` completes a module that failed and makes no second change set. A module that fails does not stop the other: the answer lists the failures and the error is `ErrOnboardingIncomplete` when sending the same confirmation again can cure it and `ErrOnboardingBlocked`, with the way out (a new onboarding under another id), when it cannot (batch D); a build that is not the proposal's is the first kind, and is refused before the confirmation is recorded (batch E). ORG records each module's change set and state (PROPOSED when it is held, APPROVED with the settings version it made: batch F). STRICT with fewer than three declared finance staff gives a notice in the receipt of the proposal and of the confirmation and is not refused.
  - A single administrator. The declaration is a table in each module's schema (`single_administrator`), written by `DeclareFinSingleAdministrator` and `DeclareSeaSingleAdministrator`, not an argument of the approval: the database must know it, and a trigger in the same schema can read a table but not an argument. The command needs the permission `DeclareSingleAdministrator`, by name only, and the bootstrap's authorizer grants it only to a context that carries the platform's mark, which only the principal the onboarding builds has: a token that carries the permission's name, alone or with every other name, declares nothing (batch E). `ApproveFinSettingsChange` and `ApproveSeaSettingsChange` take a `RiskAcknowledgement` (1 to 2000 characters); the proposer of a loosening approves their own only when they are the tenant's single administrator in force (the one its latest declaration names) and the acknowledgement is given; another approver gives none. A tenant has one administrator in force: a later onboarding that names another replaces the earlier one from its declaration's time, and the earlier administrator may no longer approve their own loosening; a later onboarding that declares none changes nothing; a declaration cannot be withdrawn (not done). The order of a tenant's declarations is total because no two have the same time (`single_administrator_time_once`, a unique key on the tenant and `declared_at`; the command refuses a declaration not later than the latest and the approval trigger reads the latest at or before the approval), and the ledger refuses more than one administrator. The acknowledgement is stored in `settings_approval.risk_acknowledgement`, with the onboarding of the declaration it relies on in `basis_onboarding_id` (batch F), and listed with the change set and in the report, and the report lists every declaration, so a replacement is visible. The module takes the onboarding on its caller's word, as its schema cannot read ORG's: the permission by name together with the platform's mark is the guard.
  - The exception report. `SettingsTools.GetExceptionReport(ctx, tenant, actor, from, to)` composes `GetFinExceptionReport` and `GetSeaExceptionReport`, each for a holder of that module's `ApproveSettings` (by name) and read-only: the legal-entity exceptions looser than the company, the items in force now looser than STANDARD with who proposed and who approved, the self-approved loosenings with their acknowledgements and the declarations, and who proposed or approved a change set in the period. The actors who both changed settings and submitted an apportionment or confirmed an attribution (an apportionment that states an attribution of its own, not one carried forward) are computed from the records (`pool_apportionment.made_by` by its entry time) for a holder of FIN's part; SEA's changers count when the caller may see SEA's part too.
  - Export and import. `ExportSettings` answers the configuration in force per module as JSON (format `cargo-settings/1`), with each catalogue's version (`Catalogue.Version`, a hash of the items, values, defaults, scopes and controls); `DiffSettingsImport` answers the entries that differ, as entries a proposal takes, the items unknown here with the way out, and a note when a catalogue's version differs. It proposes nothing.
  - The catalogue document. `go run ./cmd/cargo-settings-doc` writes `docs/settings/catalogue.md`; `TestTheCommittedCatalogueDocumentIsWhatTheProgramWrites` fails when it differs.
  - The database. `org/000012_org_onboarding.sql` (SHA-256 `479a06ea380618c2675ec4d787ad030603ccbeb63ac124dafc1b441f2fdb673c`), `fin/000022_fin_single_administrator.sql` (`4bf98ac27619763fdbcd6a343ca112a0be8f312540dc495f3500e4c999211af0`) and `sea/000013_sea_single_administrator.sql` (`5d24d0d65530061d5a4026387e53d086ab83c68db3a1dfafdef6c987ac2d11f6`).
  - Existing tests changed: the migration list of `cmd/cargo-migrate`.

- V2.102 (batch B) adds FIN's after-the-fact review list, the LEAN profile's weekly compensation. Migration fin/000023.
  - The list. `GetAfterTheFactReview` (legal entity, an ISO week `YYYY-Www`, a page of 1 to 500 lines and the `NextAfter` of the page before; it needs `ReadProfit`) lists, read-only and in one snapshot, every attribution confirmed in the week that is listed for a reason. The week is the half-open interval from Monday 00:00:00 UTC to the next Monday, by the apportionments' system time (`entered_at`, FIN's clock) to the microsecond; week 53 exists only in a year that has one. A line gives the pool and its consolidation, the line, the house and its job, the apportionment and its preview, the system time, the loss and the exception cost (with who is responsible) in the line's currency and in the functional currency, the voucher, who submitted and who previewed, and the reasons, in the order `NO_SECOND_PERSON`, `NO_VOUCHER`, `MADE_BY_DEFAULT`; a line may have several. Lines are in the order pool, apportionment, line, house, and the cursor is the last line of the page, so a page neither repeats nor skips a line.
  - What the records tell, decided here. `NO_SECOND_PERSON` is that the submitter of the apportionment and the person who made its preview are one person; FIN's own rows say so, under F2 `SUBMITTER_ONLY` and `SUBMITTER_AND_PREVIEWER` alike, because in both nobody else looked at the preview. Not told: that the submitter is the person who asked for the rework that unloaded the house, or who ended its place, allowed under F1 `UNRESTRICTED` or a threshold. FIN reads that from PHY at the command and does not record it with the attribution, so the line names the submitter and the previewer and does not say whether the submitter is the asker or the ender; a submitter who is the asker but whose preview was made by another person is not listed for that. `NO_VOUCHER` is the empty voucher (F3 allows it when no amount moves, and an attribution made by F8 has none), `MADE_BY_DEFAULT` the mark of F8. An attribution made by default always has no voucher too (fin/000019 allows the mark only with the empty voucher). Carried rows are not listed again (they were confirmed, and listed, in the week of the apportionment that stated them, whatever F9 did to their amounts). An apportionment made in one step names no preview and, by fin/000019, states no attribution (its rows are all carried), so none of its rows is listed.
  - The review. `MarkAfterTheFactReviewed` (legal entity, week, a conclusion and a note: batch F) needs the permission `fin:ReviewAfterTheFact`, granted by name only (`fin:*` and `*` grant `ReadProfit`, so the list, and not this; the reviewer also needs `ReadProfit`, which `MarkAfterTheFactReviewed` checks too since batch D, so that they can read what they sign). It takes the week's lock first, reads the whole list in one statement under it, and writes the mark with the list's size and its SHA-256 digest (every field of every line, in order). The reviewer is neither the submitter nor the previewer of any line of the list (`FIN_AFTER_THE_FACT_REVIEWER`, naming the line and the way out: another person reviews it); a week is reviewed again when its list differs from the latest review's, in size or digest, and is refused only when it is the list already reviewed (`FIN_AFTER_THE_FACT_REVIEWED`, naming who and when, and that there is nothing new to review; batch E) and only once it has ended, from the next Monday at 00:00 UTC by FIN's clock (`FIN_AFTER_THE_FACT_WEEK_NOT_ENDED`, whose way out is to review it from that Monday; a week that has not begun is `FIN_AFTER_THE_FACT_WEEK_NOT_BEGUN`; batch D); a week with nothing listed is reviewed with a list of 0. It is idempotent as FIN's commands are (a receipt whose subject is `<legal entity>/<week>` and whose version is the list's size; the event `AfterTheFactReviewed.v1`, subject the same). The query's answer for the week says whether it is reviewed and, for the latest review, its number, by whom, when, the conclusion, the note, the size and the digest then, and the size and digest now with whether they match. The whole list is read again only when the week is reviewed; the digest still tells a list that changed after its review.
  - The database (`fin/000023_fin_after_the_fact_review`, SHA-256 `27eca7d88d585a5ab1ea96aca0bd5955cb5b121b598acaef0cd65f0428896cd4`). One table, `after_the_fact_review`: one row for each review of a legal entity (with a finance policy) and a week (the primary key has the review's number, from 1 without a gap; batch E), immutable, row security enabled and forced, written at READ COMMITTED; the week is checked as `YYYY-Www` with 01 to 53 and as the week `week_start` (a Monday at 00:00 UTC) belongs to, so week 53 of a year with 52 is refused; a review is not made before its week has ended (`reviewed_at >= week_start + 7 days` in UTC, whatever the session's time zone, batch D); a note of 1 to 2000 characters, a reviewer of 1 to 128, a list size of at least 0 and a digest in lower-case hexadecimal. The result types of `command_receipt` gain `AfterTheFactReview`. What only the domain keeps: that the reviewer is neither the submitter nor the previewer of a line of the week's list, and what the list is (so that the size and the digest are the list's at the time): the list is a query over other tables, and a check at commit that read it would lock every apportionment of the week. A raw write of a review by the submitter of a line, with another size and digest, is accepted (tested as such).
  - Tests: pure, the domain (`afterthefact_test.go`): `TestAWeekIsAnISOWeekFromMondayToMonday`, `TestAnAttributionIsListedForEachOfItsReasons`, `TestTheDigestOfAListTellsAnyChange`, `TestAReviewIsMadeByAnotherPersonOnceAfterTheWeekBegan`. On PG16 through FIN's commands (`after_the_fact_postgres_test.go`, every attribution on a clock stopped in a week three to four weeks ahead): `TestTheAfterTheFactListOnPostgres` (each reason, a line with two and with three, a line with a second person and a voucher left out, a carried row left out, the amounts of a USD line in both currencies, the people, another legal entity's attribution left out, paging by one, three and four, the refusals), `TestTheWeekOfTheListHasItsBoundsOnPostgres` (a microsecond before the Monday, the Monday, a microsecond before the next Monday, the next Monday), `TestAWeekIsReviewedOnceByAnotherPersonAndReplayedOnPostgres` (the submitter and the previewer barred, the replay, a command id reused, once, a week not begun, an empty week), `TestTheDigestTellsAListThatChangedAfterItsReviewOnPostgres`, `TestTwoReviewsOfOneWeekAtOnceTakeTurnsOnPostgres` (the week's lock held on a second connection, the reviews started one after another and counted only when `pg_blocking_pids` leads to the holder; then two copies of one command), `TestTheDatabaseKeepsTheReviewRulesOnPostgres` (a raw write for each rule, the row security, and what the database does not keep), `TestTheListNeedsReadProfitAndTheReviewItsOwnPermissionOnPostgres`. The permission by name only (`permissions_after_the_fact_test.go`): `TestFinReviewAfterTheFactIsGrantedByNameOnly`. Across contexts (`after_the_fact_integration_test.go`, box A under LEAN-like settings proposed by cfg-1 and approved by cfg-2: F1 `UNRESTRICTED`, F3 `ONLY_WHEN_AMOUNT_MOVES`, F8 `DEFAULT_TO_REMAINING`): `TestTheDefaultOfOneUnrestrictedPersonIsOnTheWeeksReviewListWithEveryContext` (ops-1 previews and submits H03's default; the list shows it with the three reasons; ops-1 is refused as reviewer and head-1 marks the week). Existing tests changed: only the list of migrations of `cmd/cargo-migrate`.
  - Not done: an HTTP route for the list and the review, and a rule that the reviewer holds an authority over the person who made a line (the permission is the only one).

- V2.102 (batch C) adds tests only: the covering array of 12 rows over all the items of FIN and SEA (every pair of values appears), box A under each of the three profiles, and the property tests (random legal configurations through a whole flow, checking conservation of the amounts, the version trail, a stale preview refused and the refusal convention). The refusal checker (`settingstest.Check`) accepts a message that has a kind (a code such as `FIN_ONE_STEP_REFUSED` or a prefix such as `fin:`) and, after the first clause, a listed verb that opens a clause or "another/a <person> <verb>s". It is a heuristic by the form of the sentence: it cannot tell a listed verb used as a noun ("fin: the pool is closed, cost cannot be moved") from a way out, and does not judge whether the way out is right; the tests that name a way out do that.

- V2.102 (batch D) corrects what the independent review found. Migrations org/000012, fin/000022, sea/000013 and fin/000023 are changed (none of them is merged); no migration is added.
  - The proposal stores what will be applied (F5). `ProposeOnboarding` resolves the profile and the overrides into each module's entries and keeps them in the record (`org/000012`, the column `plan`: for FIN and SEA the module's catalogue version and 1 to 64 entries), shown in the receipt's onboarding and by `GetOnboarding`. `ConfirmOnboarding` applies exactly those entries and no longer reads the profile from code; a module whose catalogue version differs at the confirmation is refused before the confirmation is recorded, and the way out is to run the build the plan was made with or to propose a new onboarding under another id (batch E). `Catalogue.Version` covers the items, values, defaults, scopes and controls; it does not cover the cross-item rules, which the modules check when they are proposed to (a plan is checked with them when proposed and again when applied).
  - The owner declares the single administrator and names them (F3, F7). `ConfirmOnboarding` carries `AdministratorID`: it is named if and only if the proposal says the customer declares one, and it is not the consultant; ORG's confirmation keeps it and the database checks it agrees with the proposal. FIN's and SEA's `single_administrator` rows record the administrator (not the consultant), and the proposer of a loosening approves their own only if they are the administrator in force (batch E: the one the latest declaration names), with an acknowledgement, in the ledger and in the approval trigger of `fin/000022` and `sea/000013`.
  - The trail says the platform acts on the consultant's proposal (F2). Superseded by batch F: the platform no longer proposes in the consultant's name at the owner's confirmation with a permission it gives itself. The consultant's own proposal calls the module's own command in their own identity and the owner's confirmation approves in theirs, so there is no limit of a permission checked only once; each change set's reason names the onboarding, the consultant and the time of their proposal, and the confirmation's receipt says by whom and when each change set was proposed and approved.
  - Only the platform records a module's progress, and it trusts the module (F1). `RecordOnboardingModule` needs `org:RecordOnboardingModule`, by name only (`org:*` and `*` do not grant it) and held only by the principal the bootstrap builds, which carries a mark no token can carry; the database keeps that the recorded actor is the confirmer. `approveModule` and `holdModule` (`applyModule` before batch F) decide whether a module is done from the module's own change set (found by its derived id), and write ORG's record when it is missing, so a crash between the approval and ORG's record is completed by a retry without a second change set.
  - A failure a retry cannot cure says so (F9): a derived change set id or command id taken by another change, or a change set refused or cancelled (batch E: a catalogue of another version is no longer in the list, because the build the plan was made with cures it). The error is `ErrOnboardingBlocked` and the way out is a new onboarding under another id; a cause a retry can cure keeps `ErrOnboardingIncomplete` and "send the same ConfirmOnboarding again".
  - The after-the-fact review (F4, F6): a week is reviewed only from the next Monday at 00:00 UTC (`FIN_AFTER_THE_FACT_WEEK_NOT_ENDED`, and the database's `after_the_fact_review_week_ended`), and `MarkAfterTheFactReviewed` also needs `ReadProfit`.
  - Export and import (F12): the diff compares scope by scope, so a legal entity's own entry equal to the company's value stays that legal entity's entry unless the target has the same entry there; export and diff answer each module the caller may see (`ConfigureSettings` or `ApproveSettings`, by name, in that module), say which modules were left out and why, and refuse a caller who may see neither.
  - Words (F10, F11): "is batch B" is gone from the LEAN profile and the catalogue document (regenerated), and the refusal checker is said to be a heuristic (see batch C).
  - Not changed: the acknowledgement stays 1 to 2000 characters, kept and listed in the exception report (F8); permissions on ORG's onboarding queries wait for ORG's HTTP routes (F13); a legal entity unknown on the target is refused at the proposal, with its way out, not in the diff.
  - The database. `org/000012` (SHA-256 `479a06ea380618c2675ec4d787ad030603ccbeb63ac124dafc1b441f2fdb673c`), `fin/000022` (`4bf98ac27619763fdbcd6a343ca112a0be8f312540dc495f3500e4c999211af0`), `sea/000013` (`5d24d0d65530061d5a4026387e53d086ab83c68db3a1dfafdef6c987ac2d11f6`) and `fin/000023` (`27eca7d88d585a5ab1ea96aca0bd5955cb5b121b598acaef0cd65f0428896cd4`).
  - Existing tests changed: V2.102's own tests only, where a decision changes what they assert (the declaration names an administrator, a proposal carries its plan, a week is reviewed after it ended, the reviewer reads the list, an export by a FIN-only caller answers FIN). No test of V2.101 or earlier changed.

- V2.102 (batch E) corrects what the second review found. Migrations org/000012, fin/000022, sea/000013 and fin/000023 are changed (none of them is merged); no migration is added.
  - Only the platform declares a single administrator (R2-F1). `fin:DeclareSingleAdministrator` and `sea:DeclareSingleAdministrator` are granted by name only and, in the bootstrap's authorizer, only to a context that carries the platform's mark, which only the principal the onboarding builds has (as `org:RecordOnboardingModule`, batch D): a token that carries the permission strings, alone or with every other name, declares nothing.
  - One single administrator at a time (R2-F2). A tenant has at most one in force, the one named by its latest declaration; a later onboarding that names another replaces the earlier one from its declaration's time, and the earlier administrator may no longer approve their own loosening; an onboarding that makes no declaration changes nothing; a declaration still cannot be withdrawn. In the ledger (`settings.AdministratorInForce`, and `ApproveAs` refuses more than one) and in the approval triggers of `fin/000022` and `sea/000013` (the approver must be the administrator of the latest declaration at or before the approval). The order is total because a tenant's declarations have distinct times: `single_administrator_time_once` (unique on the tenant and `declared_at`), and the command refuses a declaration not later than the latest, saying to send the same command again.
  - ORG's onboarding commands are the platform's (R2-F3). ORG's own `ProposeOnboarding` and `ConfirmOnboarding` run only under the platform's mark, so the only way in is `bootstrap.Onboarding`, which checks the plan and the consultant; `org:ProposeOnboarding` and `org:ConfirmOnboarding` stay the people's, and the bootstrap checks them, by name, before it calls ORG.
  - The owner confirms the plan (R2-F4). The proposal's receipt carries the plan and its SHA-256 (`orgapi.PlanSHA256`: the plan as JSON, the modules in the order of their names); `ConfirmOnboarding` carries `PlanSHA256`, which must equal the stored plan's as the acknowledged text must equal the risk statement; ORG's confirmation row keeps it (`org/000012`: `plan_sha256` on the proposal and the confirmation, in the same foreign key). A mismatch is refused: read the onboarding again (`GetOnboarding`) and confirm what it shows.
  - The build is checked before anything is recorded (R2-F5). `ConfirmOnboarding` checks both modules' catalogue versions, and that this build's own rules accept the plan's entries, before ORG records the confirmation; on a difference nothing is recorded (`ErrOnboardingBuild`), and the way out names both causes: run the build the plan was made with, or propose a new onboarding under another id. After the confirmation the same difference is not permanent either (the build the plan was made with cures it). A replay of the same `ProposeOnboarding` (same command id, same command) after a deploy answers the stored receipt: the request's hash covers what the caller sent, not the plan the build resolved.
  - Bad actor ids are refused up front (R2-F6). `ProposeOnboarding` and `ConfirmOnboarding` refuse an actor id, or an administrator id, that is blank, padded or longer than 128 bytes before anything is called (`ErrInvalidActor`, code `ONBOARDING_INVALID_ACTOR`, with the way out); ORG's domain and database refuse the same. Each error a module can answer to a step of a confirmation is classed in `asPermanent`: a command id taken by another command, a change set cancelled or refused, and an approver who is the proposer or an acknowledgement where none is due are permanent (a new onboarding under another id); a module that refuses the plan's own entries is not permanent and names both ways out; a cross-item rule against the tenant's other change sets, a missing legal entity's policy, a clock behind, an approval already made, a missing permission, a conflict and a database that is down are curable. A change set cancelled between the listing and the approval (the branch of `asPermanent` for `ErrCancelled`) is reachable only by that race and has no test.
  - The diff tells a partial file (R2-F7). `DiffSettingsImport` of a file whose `leftOut` names a module says, for that module, in the answer's `LeftOut`, that the file does not carry it and why, and that the diff answers nothing about that module's configuration.
  - A week whose list changed is reviewed again (R2-F10). A command stamped inside a week can commit after the week's review, so its line is listed and was never reviewed. A week is reviewed again when its list differs from the latest review's (size or digest); it is refused only when it is the list already reviewed (`FIN_AFTER_THE_FACT_REVIEWED`: nothing new to review). Each review keeps its own row, number (from 1 without a gap), size and digest (`fin/000023`: `review_no` in the key, and the trigger `after_the_fact_review_order`); the bar on the reviewer applies to every line of the list; the query answers the latest review and whether it matches.
  - Tests the review found missing (R2-F8, R2-F9): each of the three conditions of `applyModule`'s "this is the onboarding's change set" (proposer, reason, entries) on its own, the reason guard at exactly 500 and 501 characters (batch F: the guard cannot fire any more, so that test became `TestTheReasonOfAHeldChangeSetFitsTheLongestIds`), ORG's plan checks in the domain, and raw writes for an administrator of 129 characters, a SEA plan of 65 entries and a SEA plan version of the wrong type.
  - The database. `org/000012` (SHA-256 `479a06ea380618c2675ec4d787ad030603ccbeb63ac124dafc1b441f2fdb673c`), `fin/000022` (`4bf98ac27619763fdbcd6a343ca112a0be8f312540dc495f3500e4c999211af0`), `sea/000013` (`5d24d0d65530061d5a4026387e53d086ab83c68db3a1dfafdef6c987ac2d11f6`) and `fin/000023` (`27eca7d88d585a5ab1ea96aca0bd5955cb5b121b598acaef0cd65f0428896cd4`).
  - Existing tests changed: V2.102's own tests only, where a decision changes what they assert (a declaration, ORG's commands and the confirmation need the platform's mark or the plan's digest; the same proposal with another plan under the same command id is a replay; the catalogue refusal comes before the confirmation; a tenant has one administrator in force; a week is reviewed again when its list changed). No test of V2.101 or earlier changed.

- V2.102 (batch F) meets the owner's four acceptance criteria (design section 9, item 10; 2026-11-26). Migrations org/000012, fin/000022, sea/000013 and fin/000023 are changed (none of them is merged); no migration is added.
  - Criterion 1, identity: every submission and confirmation is made in the identity of the person actually signed in. `ProposeOnboarding` records the proposal in ORG and then calls FIN's and SEA's own propose commands with the consultant's own context (their principal and permissions, not one the platform builds); `ConfirmOnboarding` calls each module's own approve command with the owner's own context; `WithdrawOnboarding` calls each module's own cancel command with the withdrawing person's. The principals the platform used to build to act in the consultant's or the owner's name are gone. The platform's mark stays only for what is the platform's own: ORG's onboarding commands, `RecordOnboardingModule` and the declaration, and for holding and approving a change set for an onboarding (the context is still the person's own: their principal and permissions stay, and the authorizer asks for their `ConfigureSettings`, or `ApproveSettings`, by name besides the mark; without it anyone who holds `ConfigureSettings` could freeze a tenant's settings with a made-up onboarding, or approve a held change set outside the confirmation). The audit records are the modules' own: each change set names its proposer and its time, its approver and the time, and the settings version the approval made.
    - A change set is held for an onboarding (`OnboardingID` on `ProposeFinSettingsChange` and `ProposeSeaSettingsChange`): it is not in force, and not approved even when it only tightens (`settings.ChangeSet.Hold`; the ledger gives no automatic approval); its approver is never its proposer (`SETTINGS_HELD_FOR_ONBOARDING`, not even a single administrator); the change ids stay derived from the onboarding (`onb-<onboarding>-fin` and `-sea`). A retry of the same `ProposeOnboarding` completes a module that did not propose and makes nothing twice, and a failure no retry can cure says so, as in batch D (`ErrOnboardingBlocked`). The confirmation checks, before it records anything, that each module holds the change set the plan stored (found by its derived id, proposed by the consultant, with the onboarding's reason, held for it, with exactly the stored entries, not refused or cancelled: `ErrOnboardingHeld`) as well as the plan's SHA-256 and the build (batch E).
    - `WithdrawOnboarding` (new; ORG `onboarding_withdrawal`, state `WITHDRAWN`) withdraws an onboarding that is not confirmed, by the consultant who proposed it (`org:ProposeOnboarding`) or the customer's owner (`org:ConfirmOnboarding`), with a reason of 1 to 2000 characters; ORG records who, when and why, and the record is immutable; a withdrawn onboarding is never confirmed, and a confirmed one is not withdrawn (both under the onboarding's lock, and in the database). The held change sets are cancelled with the modules' own cancel commands in the withdrawing person's own identity, so no new permission is needed (`ConfigureSettings` or `ApproveSettings` by name, which the bootstrap checks before anything is recorded); the same command again completes a module that did not cancel.
  - No other change while an onboarding waits. While a tenant has a change set held for an onboarding that is neither approved nor cancelled, every other change set of the tenant is refused in FIN and in SEA: a proposal, the approval of an older one, and another onboarding's hold (`SETTINGS_ONBOARDING_WAITING`, naming the onboarding and the way out: confirm or withdraw it first; a cancellation, the way out, is not refused). The domain checks it (`Ledger.Waiting`) and the database checks it under the tenant's settings lock (`settings_hold`, `settings_approval_onboarding_waiting`, and at commit `settings_onboarding_waiting`; `settings_hold_other_onboarding`). The bootstrap also refuses a second onboarding before it records one. FIN and SEA know only their own change sets, so a held change set waits until it is approved or cancelled; whether the onboarding is confirmed or withdrawn is ORG's record.
  - Criterion 2, the onboarding record: ORG's module row keeps the settings version each module put in force when its change set was approved (`onboarding_module.settings_version`, an APPROVED row; the PROPOSED row, recorded as the proposer, holds the change id from the proposal). `GetOnboarding` answers each module's change id, state and version with who proposed and when, who confirmed and when, the plan and its SHA-256, or who withdrew, when and why.
  - Criterion 3, loosening: in the normal course another person who holds `ApproveSettings` approves it; under the single administrator's exception each self-approval records, beside its acknowledgement, the declaration it relies on, the onboarding of the tenant's latest declaration at or before the approval (`settings_approval.basis_onboarding_id`, present exactly when the acknowledgement is; the approval triggers of `fin/000022` and `sea/000013` refuse a basis that is not that declaration: `settings_approval_basis`). The exception report's self-approved line answers the basis with its administrator and its time (`BasisOnboardingID`, `BasisAdministrator`, `BasisDeclaredAt`).
  - Criterion 4, after-the-fact review: the exceptions enter a review list, with the change, the reviewer and the conclusion, and what still waits.
    - Self-approvals are reviewed one by one: `ReviewFinSelfApprovedSettingsChange` and `ReviewSeaSelfApprovedSettingsChange` (the brief's `ReviewSelfApprovedSettingsChange`, named as the modules name their commands) on one self-approved change set need `fin:ReviewSettingsExceptions` or `sea:ReviewSettingsExceptions`, granted by name only; the reviewer is never the person who approved it; the conclusion is `ACCEPTED` or `NOT_ACCEPTED` with a note (required for `NOT_ACCEPTED`, 1 to 2000 characters when given); one review for a change set, immutable, replayed by its command id. A `NOT_ACCEPTED` changes no setting: undoing the change is a normal change set (`ProposeFinSettingsChange` or `ProposeSeaSettingsChange`). The table `settings_self_approval_review` (in `fin/000022` and `sea/000013`, row security enabled and forced, only inserted) checks that the reviewer is not the approver (a foreign key repeats the approval's approver), that the change set was self-approved, the conclusion, the note and one review for a change set.
    - The week's review takes a conclusion: `MarkAfterTheFactReviewed` carries `Conclusion` (`ACCEPTED` or `NOT_ACCEPTED`, with the note as above), and each review records the lines it covers, that is the list at that time (`after_the_fact_review_line`, a child table of the review in `fin/000023`; the database checks that the lines are written with their review, are of its legal entity, stated by an apportionment of its week and listed for a reason, and that a review has as many as its size says, at commit). `GetAfterTheFactReview` answers, for each line, the latest review that covers it (number, reviewer, conclusion, time) or that none does.
    - What still waits for review: `ListAfterTheFactPending` (FIN; a legal entity and a range of at most 53 weeks; `fin:ReviewAfterTheFact` by name and `ReadProfit`) answers every listed line of an ended week that no review covers and the ended weeks with no review (a week that has not ended is left out); `ListFinSelfApprovalReviews` and `ListSeaSelfApprovalReviews` (a period; the review permission) answer the self-approved change sets with no review and the reviewed ones with their reviewer and conclusion; `SettingsTools.GetReviewQueue` puts them together for a tenant, each module's part to that module's holders (`LeftOut` names the parts not told and why; a caller who holds none is refused), and is read-only.
  - The platform's mark and the new permissions: `fin:ReviewSettingsExceptions` and `sea:ReviewSettingsExceptions` are granted by name only (`fin:*`, `sea:*` and `*` do not grant them); `ProposeHeldSettings` and `ApproveHeldSettings` need the platform's mark besides the person's own `ConfigureSettings` or `ApproveSettings` by name.
  - Rewritten from batch A and D: the platform no longer proposes in the consultant's name or approves in the owner's by a principal it makes; each module's reason no longer names the owner and the confirmation's time (the approval is the module's own record: who, when and as which version); the reason guard of batch E can no longer fire (the reason is at most about 350 characters: an onboarding id of 100, a consultant of 128 bytes, a time and a fixed text), so its test became `TestTheReasonOfAHeldChangeSetFitsTheLongestIds`.
  - The database. `org/000012` (SHA-256 `479a06ea380618c2675ec4d787ad030603ccbeb63ac124dafc1b441f2fdb673c`), `fin/000022` (`4bf98ac27619763fdbcd6a343ca112a0be8f312540dc495f3500e4c999211af0`), `sea/000013` (`5d24d0d65530061d5a4026387e53d086ab83c68db3a1dfafdef6c987ac2d11f6`) and `fin/000023` (`27eca7d88d585a5ab1ea96aca0bd5955cb5b121b598acaef0cd65f0428896cd4`). New objects: ORG `onboarding_withdrawal` and its triggers (`onboarding_withdrawal_confirmed`, `onboarding_confirmation_withdrawn`, `onboarding_withdrawal_after_proposal`), `onboarding_module.settings_version` and the PROPOSED row's rules (`onboarding_module_proposed_by_proposer`, `_after_proposal`, `_withdrawn`, `_version`, `_confirmed_at`); FIN and SEA `settings_hold`, `settings_self_approval_review`, `settings_waiting_onboarding`, the column `settings_approval.basis_onboarding_id`, the unique key `settings_approval_for_review`, and the triggers above; FIN `after_the_fact_review.conclusion`, a nullable `note`, and `after_the_fact_review_line`. What only the domain keeps: that the onboarding a change set is held for exists and whom it was proposed by (ORG's record; the schemas are separate), that a held change set is approved only by the onboarding in the owner's name (the platform's mark), that the reviewer holds the review permission, and that the review's list is the week's (the digest).
  - Existing tests changed: V2.102's own tests only, where a decision changes what they assert (a proposal now holds one change set in each module and a refusal of a confirmation leaves them held; STRICT's change set is approved by the owner, not at once; the proposal, not the confirmation, fails for a module that does not know a legal entity; the trail, the record and the retry tests; ORG's module rows are the proposer's and the confirmer's; the review commands carry a conclusion; raw writes of an approval carry their basis and of a review its conclusion and lines). No test of V2.101 or earlier changed.
- V2.103 adds stage 2c: the sea line from order to profit with both boxes of the owner's scheme, as tests only. No migration.
  - The figures (the owner's scheme of 2026-11-18, all CNY). Box A: H01, H02 and H03 of 10, 15 and 5 W/M, ocean freight 3,000 (1,000, 1,500, 500). Box B: H04 and H05 of 20 and 10 W/M, ocean freight 2,400 (1,600, 800). The document fee of 300, by SHIPMENT: 60 for each of the five houses. Cost 5,700 and gross profit 3,500 before H03 leaves. H03 leaves box A after it was sealed (a rework asked by ops-1, the container sealed again, H03 transferred to C-2 by ops-9): finance attributes its former shares in one preview, all of box A's freight to the cargo that remains (H01 1,200, H02 1,800) and H03's 60 of the document fee as its own loss; H03 bears C-2's own freight of 850 and a rework handling charge of 180 (two lines of P-2, apportioned to H03 alone); box B's storage fee of 300 is a line of P-1 by CHARGEABLE with box A's houses named out (H04 200, H05 100). Cost 7,030 and gross profit 2,170 after; the revenue is 9,200 in both (H01 1,800, H02 2,500, H03 900, H04 2,600, H05 1,400: each house's ocean freight and a document fee of 100, as the test chose them). Cost by house after: 1,260, 1,860, 1,090, 1,860 and 960.
  - What the scenario runs, with the real ORG, RATE, COM, FUL, PHY, BOOK, SEA, DOC and FIN on PostgreSQL and every setting at its default: the orders and houses, the consolidation C-1 with its two containers, the booking and SEA's movement, the stuffing, the verified gross mass, gate-in and loading, the finalizing (version 1 before the rework, version 2 after it, and OM-2 for H03), the five house bills, the five debit notes (each on its own house's bill), the pool's cost lines and their apportionment, H03 leaving the sealed box, and each house's profit before and after.
  - The consolidation's profit. FIN answers C-1's economics through its pool (the pooled cost, what is apportioned, the losses, what is pending, and the houses it lists with their profit); before H03 leaves, its five houses sum to 3,500 and the test asserts it. After H03 leaves, the economics read H03 through P-1 alone, so the whole profit, 2,170, is the sum of the five jobs' profits, which the test asserts; FIN has no query for a consolidation whose house sails in another.
  - Not covered: the roll of box B. The owner's scheme rolls box B, as a whole container, to a later voyage. That is not built: moving one container to another voyage needs a design of its own (PLAN, stage 2). Here box B sails on the same voyage and in the same movement as box A, and its storage fee is a cost line of P-1 on box B's houses.
  - The tests. `TestTheSeaLineRunsFromTheOrderToTheProfitWithBothBoxesOfTheOwnersScheme` in `internal/bootstrap/sea_line_end_to_end_integration_test.go`, one test in seven steps, with the figures and the states asserted at each. No production code and no existing test changed.
- V2.104 divides a cost line of one container over the houses loaded in it (PLAN stage 2, "按箱自动圈定"; the owner's acceptance criteria of 2026-11-26 in `验收标准_V2.104_按箱自动圈定.md`). Migration fin/000024.
  - The line. `RecordConsolCost.LoadUnitNo` names one container of a sea LCL consolidation in the forwarder's own containers (1 to 999; 0, the default, is the whole consolidation, as before). The container is carried by the line's version (`PoolCostVersion.LoadUnitNo`), so moving a line to another container, or back to the whole consolidation, is a revision with a reason (`ReviseConsolCost.MoveToLoadUnit` with `LoadUnitNo`), and makes earlier previews stale. `PoolCostLine.LoadUnitNo` is the container the line belongs to now.
  - Who takes part. Only the houses with packs loaded in the line's container, each by what it has there (its packs, weight and volume in that container, as PHY's `HouseUnitLoad` tells), by the line's method. A house split over two containers takes part in each container's line with what it has in it. Finance may leave houses of the container out (`NotUsed`, carried to the next apportionment as before; a house named that is not in the container changes nothing on that line); no house outside it takes part. Each share says whether its house was in the container (`PoolShare.InUnit`; nil on a line of the whole consolidation). Finance's list carries to the next apportionment only while the line stays where it is: a line moved to another container, or to or from the whole consolidation, starts again with every house of where it belongs now. Under `REVENUE`, a house in a container's line is weighed by its whole revenue under the line's charge code, so a house split over two containers counts its whole revenue in each.
  - When. Once the container is sealed, as the warehouse's actuals decide: before, the line is refused with `FIN_POOL_NO_BASIS` and the way out (seal the container, or apportion the other lines). A co-load or an air consolidation has no container of its own and is refused, naming the whole consolidation as the way out. `CONTAINER_COUNT` and `TEU`, which within one container give every house the same share, are refused on such a line, naming `SHIPMENT`, `CHARGEABLE`, `GROSS_WEIGHT`, `VOLUME` and `PIECES`.
  - A house that changes container within the consolidation (a rework). By default its share follows its cargo at the next apportionment: the old container's line is divided over what remains in it, and the house takes part in its new container's line; the shares show it in the container under one apportionment and out of it under the next, and its share of the old container's line records where it is now (`PoolShare.LeftTo.ToUnits`, the containers it is loaded in, empty when none; the apportionment's `By` and `At` say who divided the line and when). A container left with no house leaves its line's cost to be allocated, and the pending item names the ways out (attribute it to a house that left, or move the line). Finance may instead attribute its former share of the old container's line, as for a house that left the consolidation: its own loss, or the consolidation's exception cost with the party responsible, with a voucher; carried while the house stays out of the container, dropped when it is back. A house that left the consolidation is attributed as before (finance's confirmation is still needed). The close compares a container's line with what is in that container now, and with the container the line belongs to now.
  - The database (`fin/000024_fin_container_lines`, SHA-256 `fef1cc45c01e1d0423aed6985dd6cf988db85256d6646eba37eeba2562c6e1e7`).
