Three nullable columns without a default (no rewrite): `pool_cost_version.load_unit_no` (1 to 999), `pool_apportionment_share.in_unit` (a share used is in the container) and `pool_apportionment_share.left_to_units` (the record of a move: only on a share not used and out of the container, a one-dimensional list of containers 1 to 999, or empty). Triggers: a version names a container only in a sea LCL pool and not on a line by `CONTAINER_COUNT` or `TEU` (`pool_cost_version_unit_sea`, `pool_cost_version_unit_method`); an apportionment line of a container is not divided by them (`pool_apportionment_line_unit_method`); a share says whether its house is in the container exactly when its line belongs to one (`pool_share_in_unit_of_line`). Only the domain keeps that the container exists and is sealed, and that the quantities and `in_unit` are what PHY answered. The preview's figures carry `in_unit`; the preview check of fin/000019 compares the other figures as before.
  - Tests. Domain (`container_lines_test.go`): the division, the refusals, leaving out, a line moved, a house that changed container by default and attributed. PostgreSQL (`container_lines_postgres_test.go`): the commands and queries end to end, the move, the attribution, the pending item, and a raw write for each database rule. With every context (`container_lines_integration_test.go`): the owner's two boxes with box A's and box B's freight each in its container and the document fee for the whole consolidation, apportioned at once with no house named out, give stage 2c's figures (1,000 / 1,500 / 500; 1,600 / 800; 60 each; cost 5,700, profit 3,500). Existing tests changed: the migration list of `cmd/cargo-migrate`.
  - After the independent review (one round; `out/review-v2104/09_independent_review.md`): a line moved no longer carries finance's list of houses left out to where it belongs now (high), and a house that changed container is recorded with where it is now (medium).
  - Not done: an HTTP route; RATE's rated lines stay lines of the whole consolidation. The person who asked for a rework is barred from confirming the apportionment as for any rework (`FIN_CONFIRMER_ASKED_REWORK`), but the attribution of a house that changed container does not name the rework or weigh F1's threshold per attribution, as a house that left the consolidation does: FIN's basis does not tell who unloaded a house still in the consolidation. A rework in any container holds back the preview of every line, as before.
- V2.105 rolls a whole sealed container to a later voyage (PLAN stage 2, "整箱甩柜"; the owner's decisions and acceptance criteria of 2026-11-26 in `验收标准_V2.105_整箱改到另一航次.md`). Migration sea/000014.
  - Two movements. A consolidation sails in as many movements as the carrier gives it sailings: each movement takes one capacity line of its booking (`OpenOceanMovement.BookingLine`, 1 by default; `OceanMovement.BookingLine`), and a newer capacity version keeps it on its line. One movement in force per booking line replaces one per consolidation. Both of the owner's cases: the carrier gives the rolled container a new booking allocated to the same consolidation (a new movement on it), or keeps the booking and confirms a new version with a line per sailing (the movement in force takes its line 1, a new movement line 2). BOOK is unchanged: a version with two lines on two voyages is confirmed as before.
  - The roll. `RollContainerToMovement` (visit, expected revision, the movement it goes to and a reason) moves a container visit that is stuffed, has its VGM or is gated in, and is not loaded, to another movement of its consolidation in the forwarder's own containers, in force. It keeps its number, seals, VGM and events, and the houses loaded in it go with it: their entries end in the old movement and start in the new one as `ROLLED_WITH_CONTAINER`, with their HBL numbers and facts. Each movement goes back to OPEN and is finalized on its own, with its own master bill. A house loaded in this container and in another is refused, with the way out (unload it from one of them first); so are a loaded container and one the carrier reported loaded (a report naming its number, or naming none while it is the movement's only live container, dates it on board in the facts; the carrier discharges it first), the movement it is in, a movement of another consolidation or a co-load, and a movement that already has a container on the same load unit. The visit lists its rolls (`ContainerVisit.Rolls`: the movements, the reason, who and when) and publishes `ContainerRolledToMovement.v1`.
  - DOC. A house bill not issued follows its container by an ordinary refresh (the owner's decision: it moves to the new voyage by itself): when the draft's movement no longer carries the house, DOC asks SEA where the HBL number lives now (`FindHouseBill`) and, when it is the same shipment in another movement of the same consolidation, builds the draft on that movement's facts: its vessel, voyage, master bill and on-board date. Before SEA finalizes the new movement, the refresh is refused naming it. A house transferred to another consolidation is not followed; the command names the movement, as before. An issued bill does not occur in this version: DOC issues a house bill only when its container is on board in SEA's facts, and a container SEA dates on board, by its own loading or by the carrier's report, is not rolled. When it does (a container discharged after loading, not built), the amendment follows in the same way (the review opened one by a probe). DOC's message for a house no longer in the facts names SEA's movement and the way out (it said AIR's facts).
  - FIN is unchanged: the pool stays the consolidation's, each box's freight is a line of its container, and the rolled box's new costs (the storage fee) are lines of its container (V2.104).
  - The database (`sea/000014_sea_container_roll`, SHA-256 `5aca3fbfbef8646e87c7052dded5854ea7af2d611d6afcdf3c0129bad9ceb1ba`). `ocean_movement.booking_line` (1 to 20, default 1) and `booking_link.capacity_line`; the unique index `ocean_movement_one_per_booking_line` (tenant, booking, line, while not cancelled) replaces `ocean_movement_one_open`; a link takes its movement's line (`booking_link_movement_line`). A house entry may start and end `ROLLED_WITH_CONTAINER`, and starts without a predecessor only when associated or rolled. The table `container_visit_roll` (row level security as the others): never changed, each roll the visit's next, from the movement it is in, to another (`container_visit_roll_next`, `container_visit_roll_elsewhere`). A visit's movement changes only with its roll written in the same transaction, while stuffed, VGM submitted or gated in, to an own-container movement of the same consolidation that is not cancelled (`check_visit_change`, replaced from sea/000011). The application role may update `container_visit.movement_id` (the workflow's grants).
  - Tests. Domain (`roll_test.go`): the roll with its houses, the states and refusals, restoring a rolled visit, a movement on its booking line. PostgreSQL (`sea_container_roll_test.go`): both booking cases, the refusals, a house in two containers, and a raw write for each database rule. With every context (`container_roll_integration_test.go`): the owner's box B rolled to EVER GLORY on a new booking, each movement with its master bill and facts, H01 to H03's bills issued on OM-1, H04's following by a plain refresh and H05's named to OM-2, both issued there, the storage fee of 300.00 on container 2 (H04 200.00, H05 100.00) and the profits; and the same booking split over two lines. Existing tests changed: the migration list of `cmd/cargo-migrate`, and the domain test that refused a booking of two lines (two lines are carried since this version; the case is now a booking of none).
  - After the independent review (one round; `out/review-v2105/09_independent_review.md`): a container the carrier reported loaded is refused (medium; its houses' bills could be issued on the old movement). Left as found (low): the database accepts a roll row written without the visit's movement change, after which the old movement does not read; it does not tie a roll to its houses or check the target's legal entity (the domain does); a movement's line is its position in the booking's lines, so a newer version that reorders the lines moves the movement to the other sailing, and one with fewer lines than the movement's is refused, which leaves the movement on its version.
  - Not done: a container found rolled after it was loaded (discharged back), part of a container rolled, the carrier's electronic roll message, a claim against the carrier, an HTTP route.
- V2.106 starts stage 3, the HTTP routes of the other contexts (the owner's decisions and acceptance criteria of 2026-11-26 in `验收标准_阶段3_HTTP路由.md`), with ORG. No migration.
  - The route table (`internal/foundation/httpapi`: `Routes`, `Op`, `Command`, `Query`). A route binds the body to the context's own api command, or the path and query string to its api query, in snake_case JSON (a field's json tag, else its Go name in snake_case: `PartyID` is `party_id`, `OrderIDs` `order_ids`, `SHA256` `sha256`). The server sets `CommandID` (Idempotency-Key), `TenantID` and `ActorID` (the token's), `ExpectedRevision` (If-Match) and the path's fields; a body or query string that sends one of them, an unknown key, a key not in snake_case or twice, a control character other than a line break or a tab, or the wrong type is refused (400 `MALFORMED_REQUEST`, naming the field); `occurred_at` is required on a command that has it. A list is answered as `{"items": [...]}`; a result's `revision` (a command's `new_revision`) is its ETag. The context authorizes its commands, before it replays one; a query is "<context>:Read" unless it names its own permission or the context checks it. The table is behind a small interface for the caller (`httpapi.Caller`), which the transport implements with the verified principal, so foundation still imports no other foundation package.
  - The OpenAPI 3.1 document is made from the table (`go run ./cmd/cargo-openapi > contracts/openapi/cargo.json`), and a test holds the published document to the routes. RATE's, SEA's and FIN's hand-written routes are in the table (`Op.Legacy`); their bodies and responses are described in their adapters and `docs/api` until stage 3 converts them.
  - A test lists each routed context's public methods (the api interfaces its module implements) and requires exactly one route for each, and none for those that stay in process. ORG's in process: `ResolvePartyRole` and `RecordOnboardingModule`.
  - ORG's master data (the owner's to-do of 2026-11-25): `RegisterParty` (DRAFT), `ActivateParty` (with the evidence of who it is), `CorrectPartyLegalName`, `SuspendParty`, `ArchiveParty`, `RegisterLegalEntity` (DRAFT) and `VerifyLegalEntity`, each one revision with its Outbox event and receipt; `ListParties` (by status, role and name, a page at a time) and `ListLegalEntities`. The demo's seed wrote them by SQL until now. The domain had the transitions; the database grants were already there.
  - ORG's routes: 35, under `/v1/parties`, `/v1/legal-entities`, `/v1/contact-transfers` and `/v1/onboardings`. The platform's onboarding commands (`ProposeOnboarding`, `ConfirmOnboarding`, `WithdrawOnboarding`) are routes of the bootstrap, which checks the person's permissions by name before it calls a module; its own refusals have their codes (`ONBOARDING_INCOMPLETE` 409, send the same command again; `ONBOARDING_BLOCKED`, `ONBOARDING_BUILD_DIFFERS`, `ONBOARDING_HELD_CHANGE_SET`, `ONBOARDING_INVALID_ACTOR` 422).
  - Personal data by name only: "org:ReadPartyContacts", "org:ErasePartyContact" and "org:ReadContactAccessLog" are granted by name, as SEA's VGM signatory; "org:*" and "*" no longer include them. The onboardings read by a person name the reader, who needs "org:ReadOnboarding" (the review of V2.102, F13); the platform's own reads in process name none.
  - The job roles (the owner's decision): `bootstrap.Roles`, the default permissions of ops, warehouse, docs, finance, finance-manager, privacy, admin and consultant; `cargo-api -mint-token -role docs` mints a token with them. Each version of stage 3 adds its context's.
  - Tests: the binding, the refusals, the parameters, the named permission and the document (`internal/foundation/httpapi`); the published document, the coverage and the roles (`internal/bootstrap/api_routes_test.go`); ORG through HTTP alone with the roles' tokens on PostgreSQL (`api_org_integration_test.go`): the legal entity and the parties' lifecycle, a role refused before the legal entity is verified, the address book, the contacts read with the permission by name (the administrator's `org:*` refused) and the log of the reads, an erasure, the lists and their pages, replays, stale revisions and another tenant. Existing tests changed: the transport test's registrar (the table), and none of the contexts'.

A new read for people, `GetCustomerOrder`: the order's lines as they stand, every obligation version, the reservations, the transport and the status, without contacts. The three obligation snapshots stay FUL's reads in process.
  - FUL: 11 routes under `/v1/shipments`: a shipment created from a reserved obligation, its obligations and parts, its plan amended and confirmed, cancelled, PHY's attachment and detachment and verified load evidence recorded (each after PHY's step, T6), and the shipment read (`GetShipmentPlanSnapshotV2`). The operation plans, evidence currency, load intent, cost driver and the first snapshot stay the other contexts' reads.
  - An order's contacts by name only: "com:ReadOrderContacts", "com:ReadRestrictedOrderContacts", "com:EraseOrderContacts" and "com:ReadContactAccessLog", as ORG's; "com:*" and "*" no longer include them.
  - The roles gain COM's and FUL's permissions: the operator orders and plans; the documentation staff reads the order's contacts and locks the parties; the privacy officer reads the log and erases.
  - Tests: the coverage test now holds COM and FUL; COM and FUL through HTTP with the roles' tokens on PostgreSQL (`api_com_ful_integration_test.go`): an order recorded, read by the finance staff, allocated, its shipper set (with its office and contact through ORG's routes), its contacts read only by name (the administrator's `com:Read` refused) and logged; the obligation reserved, a shipment created, planned, confirmed at a stale revision refused and then confirmed, read by the warehouse; another tenant.

A house's membership revision is in the body (`expected_membership_revision`), as PHY's commands take it. FUL's reads of a membership and of the verified load evidence, and FIN's cost basis, stay in process.

It was planned as four versions and is one, as all of it only adds routes; with it every public method of the ten contexts has a route, or is listed as staying in process with the reason.

AIR is served for the first time, with its commands authorized by the principal's permissions ("air:<Action>").

  - Tests for BOOK and AIR: the coverage test holds them; BOOK and AIR through HTTP alone with the roles' tokens on PostgreSQL (`api_book_air_integration_test.go`): the airline's codes and role through ORG's routes, the air consolidation through PHY's, a booking drafted, sent, awaiting, confirmed at a stale revision refused and then confirmed with its MAWB, found by the MAWB and listed for the consolidation, the cut-offs due; an air movement opened, a HAWB series registered, the movement found by its MAWB and cancelled; the refusals of a role without the permission and of another tenant.

A PDF is answered as base64 in JSON. FIN's snapshot of a house's bill stays in process.

The API binds DOC's snapshots into FIN, so that a debit note can name its house bill (its route is V2.110's).

    - the same with H03 leaving box A: the rework asked by the operator, H03 unloaded whole, the box sealed again and its VGM again, H03 transferred to C-2 with a box, a booking, a movement and a pool of its own, SEA's houses ended and refreshed, H03's former shares attributed (the rework's asker refused as submitter): each house's cost as stage 2c's (H01 1,260.00, H02 1,860.00, H03 1,090.00, H04 1,860.00, H05 960.00), cost 7,030.00 and profit 2,170.00;

RATE's, SEA's and FIN's hand-written routes appear with their path and method but without their fields, which their adapters and `docs/api` describe; FIN's six are EII's contract and stay as they are.
  - A place to try it: `tools/demo/trial.sh` brings up the local instance `trial` (port 25438), migrates it, mints a token for each job role, starts `cargo-api` on 127.0.0.1:8097 and registers and verifies the legal entity LE. Tokens and the key stay under `/home/tops/idp-cargo-tools/trial`.
- V2.110 starts stage 4 (exceptions during execution; `验收标准_阶段4_执行中的异常.md`): a shipment follows its booking, and a departure outside the customer's window waits for the customer's answer (the owner's decision of 2026-11-26). Migration `ful/000010`.
  - FUL links a shipment's main carriage to BOOK's confirmed capacity of the consolidation it is attached to (`LinkCarrierCapacity`): the booking, its revision, capacity version and line, the carrier's reference and the legs with their estimated departures and arrivals. It links again after the carrier's change (schedule change, rollover, reduction, split) or when the cargo moves to another booking, a container rolled to another movement for one; every link is kept, the same carriage again changes nothing. FUL reads BOOK for the first time (`ful -> book`, allowed since FUL V0.4).
  - A link whose first departure lies outside the plan's departure window waits for the customer's answer (`RecordCustomerDateAnswer`): ACCEPTED or REFUSED, with its evidence. An accepted departure widens the window the customer agreed to, which FUL publishes as `AgreedWindow` in AirOperationPlan v1 and OceanOperationPlan v1; an answer counts while the plan's window is the one it answered, so amending the window asks again. `GetShipmentExecution` shows the plan next to its links, answers and departed packs; `ListDateChangesAwaitingCustomer` is the work list, of a consolidation or the tenant.
  - SEA and AIR hold a house to the agreed window: a change of it alone is a refresh (under the same HBL or HAWB), and a house not yet refreshed is stale. Their published facts say of each house whether its first leg or flight departs outside it (`window_drift`, left out when false, so earlier versions keep their content). AIR no longer refuses to finalize a movement with a drifting house; DOC refuses to issue that house's bill instead (`DOC_DATE_CHANGE_UNANSWERED`), sea or air, while the other houses and the master are issued as before. The owner's decision: packing, consolidation and booking carry on; only the house's bill waits.
  - Database rules, each with a direct write in `capacity_postgres_test.go`: links, legs and answers are immutable; links and answers are numbered one after another, of a shipment that is not cancelled; a link is to the consolidation the shipment is attached to now, and has its legs, written with it; an answer is to the latest link and not dated before it.
  - V2.105's two roll tests record the customer's acceptance of EVER GLORY before H04's and H05's bills are issued there: until then DOC refuses them.

Bento testkit contracts:

- `RunRepositoryContract` runs `PartyRepository` against the `cargo_org` tables with tenant-bound transactions. Cargo binds one tenant per transaction, so the contract's "other scope" is a second party of the same tenant; cross-tenant isolation is covered by the RLS tests.
- `RunOutboxContract` and `RunInboxContract` run Bento's PostgreSQL adapters in per-scenario schemas that the migrator role provisions with the workflow's grants and the application role exercises. Outbox envelopes come from `ORGEnvelope`.

Defects found by real runs and fixed:

1. `go.sum` held only the two framework lines, so every Bento build failed with `missing go.sum entry`. `go.mod` / `go.sum` are now the complete graph from Go 1.26.5 `go mod tidy`.
2. `cargo-migrate` did not create the Bento schema, so the first framework migration failed with `schema "bento" does not exist`. Bento migrations never run `CREATE SCHEMA`; that is the consumer's first migration step (bento-go `docs/design/postgres-adapter-contract.md`).
3. `PartyStore.Save` hashed nanosecond validity times into `role_id`, while `timestamptz` keeps microseconds. After a reload the ID changed and the re-insert of an existing role violated `party_role_approved_no_overlap` (23P01). The hash and the insert now use microsecond-truncated UTC times.
4. `PartyStore.Load` read the party row and its roles in two statements. Under READ COMMITTED each statement takes a new snapshot, so a command committing in between produced a torn aggregate (old revision, new role) and a losing handler failed with `ErrRoleOverlap` instead of a revision conflict. `Load` now locks the root row with `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`.
5. RATE's anticorruption layer wrapped ORG's error chain with `%w`. For a party that ORG does not know, the chain carried Bento's `repository.ErrNotFound`, so RATE answered `ErrNotFound` ("no such quote") instead of `ErrRejected`. The COM slice test showed the same problem with an unknown quote, and the V2.11 code reproduces it. Anticorruption layers now keep the upstream answer as text only, so upstream error kinds never become the caller's.

NOT executed:

- Required approving review; signed build provenance or attestations.
- OIDC / IAM, KMS and privacy controls, `ErrCommitUncertain` recovery, Inbox consumers in Cargo, Cargo→COM business UAT.
- A production event transport: the HTTP webhook is a replaceable adapter, not a broker decision.

## Run against PostgreSQL 16

1. Install Go 1.26.5 and resolve the immutable framework candidate; do not use Git `main` or a local `replace`.
2. Set `IDP_CARGO_MIGRATOR_DSN` to a dedicated migration role in PG16.
3. `go run ./cmd/cargo-migrate -manifest migrations/manifest/contexts.txt` to inspect; separately authorize `-apply`.
4. Create a `cargo_app` user with no owner/superuser/BYPASSRLS/DDL rights; grant only required DML (SELECT only on `cargo_ref`; for `cargo_org` party identifiers: SELECT, INSERT on `party_identifier` and UPDATE of `retired_by`, `retired_at`, `retire_reason` and `retired_revision` only; for `cargo_org` address books: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE on `party_address_book`, `party_address` and `party_contact`; SELECT, INSERT on `party_address_book_change`, `party_address_version`, `party_address_default`, `party_contact_version` and `party_contact_default`; UPDATE of `name`, `phone`, `mobile`, `email` and `erased` only on `party_contact_version`; SELECT, INSERT on `party_contact_consent` and `party_contact_access`; for `cargo_rate`: SELECT, INSERT on `quote` and UPDATE of its status, approval (`approved_by`, `approved_at`, `approval_reason_ref`, `self_approved`), acceptance (`accepted_by`, `accepted_at`, `accepted_recorded_at`, `acceptance_evidence_kind`, `acceptance_evidence_ref`, `accepted_customer_revision`), `expired_at`, `revision` and `updated_at` only (an upgraded database revokes the table-wide UPDATE that V2.11 to V2.48 granted); SELECT, INSERT on `quote_agreement_check`; SELECT, INSERT on `quote_line`, `quote_exchange_rate` and `command_receipt`; SELECT on `charge_code_starter`; SELECT, INSERT on `charge_code_version`, `rate_card`, `rate_card_version`, `rate_snapshot`, `rate_snapshot_candidate` and `rate_snapshot_line`; UPDATE of `revision` and `updated_at` only on `rate_card` and of the status, preparation, publication, approval and withdrawal columns only on `rate_card_version`; SELECT, INSERT, DELETE on `rate_entry`, `rate_line` and `rate_break`; for `cargo_com`: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE on `customer_order`; SELECT, INSERT on `order_line`, `order_line_amendment`, `order_policy`, `fulfillment_obligation`, `obligation_reservation`, `order_transport`, `order_party`, `order_party_contact`, `order_contact_retention`, `order_contact_erasure`, `order_contact_access` and `command_receipt`; UPDATE of `released_by` and `released_at` only on `obligation_reservation`; UPDATE of `name`, `phone`, `mobile`, `email` and `erased` only on `order_party_contact`; for `cargo_ful`: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE on `shipment`; SELECT, INSERT on `shipment_obligation`, `shipment_plan_version`, `shipment_plan_confirmation` and `command_receipt`; UPDATE of `released_by` and `released_at` only on `shipment_obligation`).
5. Set `CI=true IDP_CARGO_POSTGRES_DSN=...`. The Bento contract tests also need `IDP_CARGO_MIGRATOR_DSN` to provision their throwaway schemas.
6. `go test -tags integration -count=1 -v ./...`
7. `go test -tags integration -race -count=1 ./...`
8. Collect signed, retained CI proof.
9. Relay events: `IDP_CARGO_POSTGRES_DSN=... go run ./cmd/cargo-worker -sink https://...`. A claimed batch is sent one delivery at a time under one lease, so `-lease` must exceed `-batch` × `-sink-timeout` (defaults 10 × 5s under 1m); the worker refuses to start otherwise. `-max-attempts` (50) stays five times `-max-failures` (10), as Bento recommends, so the claim limit only ends publishers that crash before settling.
10. Package rules: `go run ./tools/dependency-check`. After the Context Map changes or a context is added, regenerate the allowlist with `-generate <Context Map JSON>` from the master archive.

### The HTTP API

`cmd/cargo-api` serves `/v1`. Until the IDP identity platform is connected, a bearer token is a development token: HS256-signed with `IDP_CARGO_API_TOKEN_KEY` (base64 of 32 bytes or more), naming its issuer and audience (`-token-issuer`, `-token-audience`), the tenant, the actor and the permissions (`<context>:<Action>`, `<context>:*` or `*`; queries need `<context>:Read`), valid for a day at most. Whoever holds the key can sign any tenant and permission, so the server starts only with `-dev-tokens`, never in production. Tenant and actor always come from the token, never from a body. Use `-addr 127.0.0.1:0` locally: the server takes a free port and logs it.

```sh
export IDP_CARGO_API_TOKEN_KEY=$(head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64)
go run ./cmd/cargo-api -mint-token -tenant t1 -actor pricing-1 -permissions rate:Read,rate:MaintainChargeCodes
IDP_CARGO_POSTGRES_DSN=... go run ./cmd/cargo-api -dev-tokens -addr 127.0.0.1:0
```

- A command is a `POST` with an `Idempotency-Key` (its command id: sent again, it answers its first receipt; with another body, 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_MISMATCH`), `If-Match: "n"` when it changes an existing version (428 without it, 409 `VERSION_CONFLICT` when stale), and `occurred_at`, its business time, in the body: a retry sends the same body. It answers a receipt (`command_id`, `subject_id`, `new_revision`) with `ETag: "n"`, 201 and `Location` for a create.
- Errors are `application/problem+json` (RFC 9457) with `code` (a business code such as `RATE_VERSION_OVERLAP` where the context names one) and `correlation_id`, from the context's error kinds only: 400, 401, 403, 404, 405, 409, 413, 415, 422, 428, 503 `COMMIT_UNCERTAIN` (send the request again with the same key), 500 with nothing described.
- `GET /healthz` and `GET /readyz` (the database) take no token. `X-Correlation-ID` is taken when well formed, otherwise made, and returned.

### Local PostgreSQL 16 on IDP-Cargo's ports

Ports 25430-25439 are reserved for IDP-Cargo on developer machines: 25432 for integration runs (`dev`), 25433 for upgrade runs (`upgrade`), 25434 for empty-database checks (`empty`), 25435 for the long-lived test tenant of `tools/demo/eii-test.sh` (`demo`), 25436 for a second developer's integration runs (`dev2`), 25437 for a third developer's integration runs (`dev3`), the rest reserved. They lie below the default ephemeral port ranges of Linux and Windows. `tools/localpg/localpg.sh` serves each port only from its own labelled container (`idp-cargo-pg16-<role>`, PostgreSQL 16.15) and refuses to start, print a DSN or stop anything when a port is held by another program, so tests and migrations never reach someone else's server.

```sh
tools/localpg/localpg.sh up dev            # start, or confirm, idp-cargo-pg16-dev on 127.0.0.1:25432
eval "$(tools/localpg/localpg.sh env dev)" # IDP_CARGO_MIGRATOR_DSN and IDP_CARGO_POSTGRES_DSN, as CI sets them
go run ./cmd/cargo-migrate -manifest migrations/manifest/contexts.txt -apply
tools/localpg/localpg.sh grants dev        # the application role and the grants of the CI workflow
go test -tags integration -count=1 ./...
tools/localpg/localpg.sh down dev          # remove the container and its data
```

## Important boundaries

- The approval actor and tenant identity are **not trusted merely because they are inside a Go struct**. The HTTP adapters take them from the verified token's principal; the development tokens of `cmd/cargo-api` are not an identity platform, and permissions in a token stand in for the IDP policy service until it is connected.
- `app.tenant_id` RLS protects against accidental missing/wrong query scopes. SQL injection that can execute arbitrary `set_config` is out of its security boundary.
- A missing or legacy `result_revision=0` receipt does **not** count as execution success.
- Callers branch on the `api` error kinds (`ErrConflict`, `ErrRejected`, `ErrUnauthorized`, …), never on ORG or Bento causes; `org.ErrConflict` and Bento `repository.ErrConflict` both classify as `api.ErrConflict`.
- No direct business writes across Cargo bounded contexts.
- `internal/bootstrap` gives every context its own Bento transaction owner. A context that calls another context inside its own transaction gets `application.ErrTransactionConflict`, so cross-context queries run before the caller opens its transaction.
- `EventID` is not a substitute for CommandID deduplication. No Exactly-Once guarantee.
- The relay delivers at least once: a crash between publish and settle re-delivers after the lease expires. Sinks and consumers deduplicate by event ID, which the webhook sends as `Idempotency-Key`. Leases are wall-clock timestamps, so worker hosts need synchronized clocks.
- RATE keeps customers as ORG party references only; legal names stay in ORG. Quote costs and margins never leave RATE.
- RATE's rules follow the 2026-11-06 business review and the owner decisions of 2026-11-09; the margin floor is supplied by `MarginPolicy`, not hard-coded. Approval for non-standard payment terms and non-functional currencies (R2) waits for their reference sources, and rate cards (R6) for S2.
- COM keeps the customer's legal name and country only as the transaction snapshot frozen with the order (MD-05). The obligation snapshots carry identifiers, packs, weight and volume, not names, because FUL does not consume legal names.
- The reservation is COM's own fact, set and cleared through COM's api, so the COM → FUL relationship keeps its direction. In S1 an API client drives the handshake (rule T6); a release that FUL has not recorded first is caught by FUL's own re-checks, not by COM.
- COM's rules follow the 2026-11-06 business review (C3–C7), the owner decisions of 2026-11-09 (C1, C2, C8, C9 as amended, C10) and the decisions C11–C14 of 2026-11-10, taken by the assessor on the owner's delegation to follow CargoWise practice. Once an order is closed or cancelled its contacts are restricted and kept until the retention period ends, then erased (P1; the period is to be confirmed by legal counsel). Package type codes and weight and volume units are checked for form only until reference data exists (MD-25).
- FUL's rules are a candidate design pending business review: one transport mode per shipment (AIR, SEA, ROAD or RAIL), additions only while the plan is a draft, one weight unit per shipment, and volume declared by all of its obligations or by none. Splitting obligations into parts follows owner decision F1; locations follow F2 option 4 (UN/LOCODEs for every mode, airport-level locations with an IATA code for AIR, IATA airport codes accepted as input); a shipment's mode must be the one its orders request (C11). The customer's requested origin, destination and dates are not on the order yet, so planners enter them in FUL.
- The location reference data of S1 is a subset of the UNECE UN/LOCODE release of 2026-11-01 for the first lines (M1). Its IATA airport codes and the aliases carriers use (CNSHA, CNNGB, CNTAO and CNXMN for the ports of Shanghai, Ningbo, Qingdao and Xiamen) are the assessor's compilation and must be verified against IATA and carrier data, or replaced by the full import, before the pilot (E3).
- ORG keeps the official codes of organizations (unified social credit code, customs code, AEO, EORI, EIN, registration numbers); these are public registration data, not personal data, and leave ORG only through its queries, never in events. Identifiers of natural persons (identity cards, passports) are not accepted.
- ORG keeps addresses and contacts under privacy assessment V1.1 (an assessor's opinion awaiting the data protection owner's sign-off). Contacts are personal data: reading them is authorized separately, they never appear in events or receipts, and they can be erased. Contact fields are stored in plaintext under row level security; encryption at rest waits for KMS. Development and tests use simulated data only (M1); real personal data needs the steps of section 6 of the assessment before the pilot (E3).
- The earliest ORG master data cannot be signed off until a Cargo business owner validates authoritative identity, legal status and partner role semantics.

## Dependency lock qualification

`go.sum` is the complete module graph produced by Go 1.26.5 `go mod tidy`. On GitHub Actions it resolved with `GOPROXY=direct` and `go mod verify` passed. Promotion to an approved supply-chain baseline still needs review.
